# Pick ledger & hit rate

Claude's picks, logged pregame, graded by the 7:30am run. **Sam's bets are logged from what he reports — there is no account link, no feed, no sync. ## Standing rules

1. **Every pick logged the day it's made, pregame**, with estimate, price, book and verification status.
2. 🔴 **Re-log a card every time it is revised.**
3. 🔴 **Tag plays `[pre-correction]` when the model changes underneath them**, keep them out of calibration, **and state how many were excluded.**
4. **Unverified plays get tagged, not hidden.**
5. **Fabricated or non-existent lines are logged as errors, never as plays.**
6. **Track Sam's actual bets separately from Claude's card.** TABLE C on the date, graded, **never merged** into Claude's hit rate or calibration. The comparison is the point.
7. **Never omit a pick for being shaky.**
8. **Cross-check any surprising grading result against a second source.**
9. **Every logged play gets a W or an L. There is no void.**
10. **Grade from the boxscore endpoint** *when a browser is available*. 🔴 **WITHOUT a browser the boxscore is UNUSABLE.** **Fall back to the flat `gameLog` endpoint and CONTROL IT** — 🆕 **preferably by summing the WHOLE log against the season total, not just checking one delta.** Run the historical control before trusting an empty response.
11. **Check the market rows sum to the card size, and the W/L counts too** — 🔴 **and check the pre-computed split against the table itself.**
12. **Calibration before price comparison.**
13. **ER and hits-allowed plays are `[unmodelled]`** — flag on sight.
14. **Line value: no trend under ~50 paired observations**, never quoted as CLV.
15. 🔴 **CROSS-CHECK EVERY PLAY AGAINST THE RAW HIT RATE AT THAT EXACT THRESHOLD.** Gap >10 points → the model is the suspect. 🆕 🔴 **AND LOG THE MODEL NUMBER AND THE RAW RATE AS THEIR OWN COLUMNS — see rule 41.** ⚠️ **Debut 1/4. Rule stands; do not re-tune off one slate.**
16. 🔴 **A MISSING GAME IN THE ODDS FEED IS NOT A MISSING GAME.**
17. 🔴 **BEFORE REPORTING A BOOK AS ABSENT, CHECK THE REGION YOU REQUESTED.** Hard Rock is in `us2`.
18. 🔴 **ONE CARD, ONE TABLE, ONE NUMBERING.** 🆕 **And ONE LABEL — TABLE A is the live card, B is superseded, C is Sam's. An unlabelled Result table is a STOP condition, not a guess.**
19. 🔴 **CARD ONLY PRIZEPICKS AND HARD ROCK.**
20. 🔴 **BOTH LEGS OF A PAIR MUST CARRY THE SAME BOOK NAME.**
21. 🔴 **ON A PRICED LADDER, CARD THE LOWEST RUNG THAT CLEARS BREAK-EVEN.** ⚠️ **AMENDED 8/21: this rule is evaluable ONLY on a real sportsbook ladder.** ⛔ **A PrizePicks rung has no price at all (rule 46), so "clears break-even" cannot be computed there and must never be asserted.** ➡️ **On PrizePicks the rule becomes: RANK BY PROBABILITY AND TAKE THE LOWEST RUNG.** ✅ **The justification survives on its own footing — the 2-pick MULTIPLIER barely moves with rung difficulty** (Sam's real slips returned 1.8x, 1.9x and 2.0x), **so climbing the ladder buys hit-rate loss for almost no extra payout.** ~~"flat-priced"~~ **struck: there was never a flat price, only a flat-ish multiplier.**
22. **Every play states its moneyline and first pitch.**
23. 🔴 **LOG EVERY BET WITH ITS STAKE AND ACTUAL PAYOUT.** **And update the RUNNING RECORD block at the top on every grading run.**
24. 🔴 **A RECOMMENDATION MADE IN CONVERSATION IS A PICK. LOG IT.** ⚠️ **There is no account link — Sam's word is the only source.** 🆕 **And verify his reported results at the next grading run; do not leave a graded play resting on a text message.**
25. 🔴 **NO ODDS NUMBER EVER COMES THROUGH `WebFetch`.**
26. 🔴 **EVERY CARDED RUNG MUST APPEAR IN A RAW PULL**, quoted with its book and price.
27. 🔴 **PULL THE ALT LADDERS ON EVERY CARD** and deliver **≥5 pairs out of 10 props.**
28. 🔴🔴 **CURRENT BANDS, SAM 2026-09-22 — FLOOR ONLY, NO CEILING: 2-LEG TICKETS PAY AT LEAST 1.80x; 3- AND 4-LEG TICKETS PAY AT LEAST 3.00x.** Sam: *"we can make it 1.8- unlimited, but we still have to make sure the bets we provide have a high % to hit based on data from our model"*, then *"i want the minimum for 2 mans to be 1.8 for anything over 2man parlays i want the minimum to be 3x"*. ➡️ **Code: `PARLAY_BANDS = {2:(1.80,None), 3:(3.00,None), 4:(3.00,None)}` in `card.py` and `card_fb.py`; `verify_card.py` checks the floor only.** ✅ **"High % to hit" is the RANKING, which is unchanged: tickets are ordered by joint model probability, so removing the ceiling does not promote long shots over likely hits.** ⛔ **A ticket below its floor is still NEVER SHOWN (the rule-53 exception below stands).** ⚠️ **"Above band" no longer exists as a label for new cards; grading of cards before 2026-09-22 keeps their inside/above split as recorded.** ~~The 2026-08-26 bands (2 legs 1.80–2.20x, 3–4 legs 3.00–6.00x) and the 2.1x soft target below~~ **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-22 — kept as the record.**
    ~~🔴 **THE BAND IS ASYMMETRIC: 1.8x IS A HARD FLOOR, 2.1x IS A SOFT TARGET.**~~ ~~**EVERY PAIR LANDS IN 1.8x–2.1x.**~~ **AMENDED 2026-08-22 IN SAM'S OWN WORDS — AND THE ASYMMETRY IS THE POINT, NOT AN INCONSISTENCY.** Print the two decimals and their product on every pair.
    🔴 **BELOW 1.8x — A PAIR IS NEVER SHOWN. NOT PRINTED, NOT LABELLED "BELOW BAND", NOT LISTED AS DECLINED.** Sam, asked directly whether sub-band pairs should be printed with a "below band" label: *"i dont want to see them at all"*.
    ⛔ 🔴 **THIS IS AN EXPLICIT, DELIBERATE EXCEPTION TO LEDGER RULE 53**, which otherwise says a play that fails a check is SHOWN WITH ITS FAILING NUMBER rather than removed. 🔴 **SAM SET IT HIMSELF, IN HIS OWN WORDS, AFTER RULE 53 ALREADY EXISTED.** ⛔ **A FUTURE SESSION MUST NOT "FIX" THIS BACK TO RULE 53'S DEFAULT.** **It is not an oversight and it is not a contradiction — it is the owner of the rule overriding his own general principle for one specific case, knowingly.** ⚠️ **Rule 53 governs plays CLAUDE dislikes. Rule 28 governs pairs SAM does not want to see. Different authorities, and only the second one removes anything.**
    ✅ **ABOVE 2.1x — SHOWN, WITH ITS MULTIPLIER LABELLED, WHENEVER IT IS A GOOD BET.** Sam, asked whether an above-band slip should be excluded: *"if its a good bet its a good bet still show it, i mostly dont want anything belo 1.8"*. ➡️ **Walk the rungs first and say so if no walk was possible — but an above-band pair is NOT suppressed and is NOT a rule violation.**
    ⚠️ 🔴 **CONSEQUENCE FOR GRADING, RECORDED NOW SO IT IS NOT DISCOVERED LATE: Sam's 8/21 slip 2 was 2.20x — ABOVE BAND AND LEGITIMATELY PLAYED.** **Above-band plays are graded normally and stay IN THE RECORD.** ➡️ **When the band's own hit rate is eventually measured, REPORT INSIDE-BAND AND ABOVE-BAND SEPARATELY AND SAY WHICH IS WHICH — otherwise "does 1.8–2.1 work" measures nothing.** ⛔ **Excluding an above-band play from the RECORD is forbidden. It is excluded only from the INSIDE-BAND SUBTOTAL.**
    🔴 🆕 **WIDENED 2026-08-26 BY SAM, AND A SECOND AND THIRD TICKET SHAPE ADDED — RECORDED HERE 2026-08-31 BY THE MONDAY SWEEP, WHICH FOUND IT IN THE CODE FIVE DAYS AFTER IT SHIPPED.** `[measured 2026-08-31 from `card.py`'s `PARLAY_BANDS` and from the `parlay_rule` string the published cards carry]` **Sam, 2026-08-26: two-mans in 1.8x-2.2x, three- and four-mans in 3x-6x.**
    ➡️ **THE STANDING BANDS ARE THEREFORE: 2 LEGS → 1.80x-2.20x · 3 LEGS → 3.00x-6.00x · 4 LEGS → 3.00x-6.00x.** ⛔ **THE 1.80 HARD FLOOR IS UNCHANGED AND IS STILL NEVER CROSSED, and everything above about a sub-1.8x pair never being shown is untouched.** ⚠️ **What is new is an UPPER bound on the two-leg list — a 4x two-man is not a better two-man, it is a different bet — and the fact that THREE- AND FOUR-LEG TICKETS EXIST AT ALL, which no doc in this project had ever said.**
    ✅ 🔴 **THE CODE CONTRADICTED ITSELF FROM 2026-08-26 TO 2026-09-11, AND IT IS NOW FIXED.** ~~**AND THE CODE HAS CONTRADICTED ITSELF ON THIS SINCE THE DAY IT SHIPPED, REPORTED BY EVERY GRADING RUN AND STILL NOT FIXED: `card.py`'s `parlay_rule` prose says 1.8x-2.2x while its `in_band` flag is computed against a 2.1x ceiling. ⛔ THIS RULE DOES NOT SETTLE THAT. AN INTERACTIVE SESSION MUST PICK ONE CEILING AND MAKE THE CODE SAY IT ONCE** — a headless run can neither test nor push.~~ **A pair at 2.15x was ABOVE BAND to the flag and INSIDE BAND to the same file's own prose, for sixteen days.**
    ✅ **IT WAS NOT A JUDGMENT CALL, AND THIS RULE DID SETTLE IT.** This rule already owned the bands and already recorded Sam's 2026-08-26 instruction; `PARLAY_BANDS` already matched it. **Only the flag was stale.** ➡️ **The flag is now computed FROM the band it prints** (`lo <= mult <= hi`), so the two cannot drift apart again — see rule 185.
    ⚠️ **AND A SECOND, QUIETER BUG WENT WITH IT (rule 186):** the three- and four-leg flag read `(size != 2) or …`, **unconditionally true**, so a 3-leg at 2.5x — below its own 3.00 floor — was labelled IN BAND. **Every size is now judged against its own band.**
    ⛔ **NO HISTORY WAS RE-GRADED, AND THAT IS DELIBERATE. Every INSIDE-BAND / ABOVE-BAND subtotal in this document is computed on the 2.1x flag and STAYS THAT WAY**, stated on the rows rather than silently reconciled. **2.10 remains a SOFT TARGET and never a cap; the 1.80 hard floor is untouched.**
    ⚠️ 🔴 **`claude/card-blueprint.md` STEP 6 STILL CARRIES THE OLD SENTENCE** — *"AN INTERACTIVE SESSION MUST PICK ONE CEILING"* — **and its 2.1x ceiling prose.** ⛔ **It is STALE as of 2026-09-11; THIS RULE IS THE AUTHORITY and the blueprint already points here.** ➡️ **Next session that receives `card-blueprint.md` as a FILE rather than inline: strike it** (rule 43 — a 48KB retype for one sentence is the risk that rule exists to prevent).
    ⛔ **NO GRADED ROW, NO SUBTOTAL AND NO PUBLISHED IN-BAND/ABOVE-BAND SPLIT WAS RECOMPUTED BY THIS AMENDMENT.** **It records what Sam instructed; it does not re-grade history against a ceiling history was not graded on.**
29. 🔴 **LADDER DIRECTION: an OVER gets safer going DOWN, an UNDER gets safer going UP.**
30. 🔴 **BAND ARITHMETIC BEFORE BUILDING: any leg at +110 or longer is 2.10x by itself.** ⚠️ 🆕 **AMENDED 2026-08-22 TO MATCH RULE 28'S ASYMMETRY.** **That arithmetic tells you the pair CANNOT LAND INSIDE 1.8x–2.1x. It does NOT tell you the pair is suppressed.** ➡️ **A leg at +110 or longer still clears the 1.8x HARD FLOOR, so if the pair is a good bet it is SHOWN, labelled with its multiplier and marked ABOVE BAND.** ⛔ **Only a pair BELOW 1.8x is never shown.** ✅ **Walking the rungs to pull it inside the band is still the preferred move and is still attempted first.**
31. 🔴 **THE FEED IS NOT THE BOOK. AN ABSENCE IN AN API RESPONSE IS EVIDENCE ABOUT THE API, NEVER ABOUT THE SPORTSBOOK.**
    ~~"Hard Rock alt ladders are overs-only — the only under on Hard Rock is the standard line's under side."~~ **STRUCK 8/21 — Sam bets there and confirms alt unders are bettable.** The measurement was real and reproducible — the Odds API returns only `Over` outcomes on `pitcher_strikeouts_alternate` — **and the inference was wrong: "the feed has no under side" got written down as "the book has no under side."**
    ✅ **STILL TRUE, Sam-confirmed at the book and not a feed artifact: Hard Rock posts NO alternate OUTS market at all.** Outs on Hard Rock is one two-way standard line.
    ➡️ **PROCEDURE: when the band math wants an alternate UNDER on Hard Rock, NAME the rung, say the feed does not carry its price, and ASK SAM to read it off the app** — the same procedure already in place for PrizePicks in-app multipliers. ⛔ **This does not loosen the phantom-rung rule: never assert a PRICE you have not seen.** **The Bradish u4.5 Sam asked for was real; only its price was invisible.**
    🔴 ~~**THIRD TIME.**~~ **FIFTH TIME — count reconciled 8/21 evening against the list in rule 47.** ~~"Hard Rock posts no alt ladders"~~ → ~~"the ladders are two-way"~~ → ~~"they're overs-only"~~ → WebFetch's 27 invented events → **the feed omitting Luzardo's entire pitcher market while carrying his game** → **and the worst, ~~"PrizePicks charges a flat −137"~~, an ENCODING read as a price.** **Every one was a fact about a query reported as a fact about the world.**

Count STARTS.** 🆕 **And ITEMISE the starts — an un-itemised pass cannot be deduplicated and therefore cannot be accumulated into.** Table in `claude/calibration-accumulators.md`.

Both are honest; a phantom task is not.**

40. 🆕 🔴 **DOMAIN-CHECK EVERY FETCHED VALUE AGAINST WHAT THE FIELD CAN PHYSICALLY HOLD.** `[measured 8/21]` A flat `gameLog` pull returned **`1.3` innings pitched.** **IP fractions are thirds — only `.0`, `.1`, `.2` exist**, so the value was fabricated by the summarising fetch, and the season sum was short by exactly the 2 outs that proves it. ✅ **No graded line was affected** (relief appearance, wrong date, K column clean). 🔴 **The lesson is bigger than the row: "the flat game log is a shape a summariser cannot merge" is TRUE ABOUT BLOCKS AND FALSE ABOUT SCALARS.** ➡️ **Range-check every field with a known domain — IP fractions, outs ladders below 9, pitch counts over ~130 — and treat a violation as fabrication until proven otherwise.** ⚠️ **Related: trust ENUMERATED rows, never a fetched summary's own count of them — one claimed 1000 and enumerated 816 the same morning.**
41. 🆕 🔴 **A CARD MUST LOG THE MODEL NUMBER AND THE RAW HIT RATE AS SEPARATE COLUMNS, BESIDE THE BLEND.** `[measured 8/21]` **Rule 15's scoreboard has never been executable**, because every card to date logged the blend alone. The 8/20 numbers had to be **reconstructed from first principles** — it worked, reproducing the published blend to ≤0.2 points on 9 of 11 plays, **but a reconstruction is not a log and the next slate may not be rebuildable.** ⚠️ **And the "which was closer" tally is a BIASED statistic** — on an 8/10 day it just rewards the higher number. **Accumulate a proper scoring rule (Brier) instead. At n=12 the blend-weight surface is flat to four decimals: the weight is UNIDENTIFIABLE and the 50/50 must not be touched.**
42. 🆕 🔴 **RESULTS TABLES ARE APPEND-ONLY. "PRUNE" NEVER APPLIES TO A GRADED ROW.** `[measured 8/21 — TABLE B]` This doc carries a standing instruction to **prune**. Combined with the fact that **`project_write` replaces the entire file**, pruning silently deleted a five-play graded table and left only a dangling sentence pointing at it. ⛔ **Prune PROSE. Never a graded row, a money row, or a dated card table.** ➡️ **If a results table genuinely must go, its rows move somewhere else in the same write — they are never simply absent.**
43. 🆕 🔴 **BEFORE WRITING ANY DOC THAT CONTAINS GRADED RESULTS: COUNT THE GRADED ROWS BEFORE AND AFTER. THE COUNTS MUST MATCH, OR THE DIFFERENCE MUST BE STATED IN THE CHANGELOG.** **`project_write` gives no diff.** A dropped section leaves **no trace at all** — no error, no warning, nothing but a reference that no longer resolves. ⛔ **A rewrite from context is a retype, and anything not retyped is deleted.** ✅ **The working method is: `project_read` the current version → save it to a local file → patch it programmatically → upload with `local_path`.** **That is how tonight's edits were made and why they lost nothing.**
44. 🆕 🔴 **EVERY RESULTS TABLE CARRIES A LABEL, AND THE LABELS ARE INDEXED AT THE TOP OF THIS DOC.** A missing table must be detectable by **reconciling against an index**, not by noticing that one sentence mentions something no longer present. **TABLE B was gone for at least a day before a scheduled labelling audit caught the dangling reference.**

    ➡️ 🆕 **THIS NUMBERED LIST IS THE CANONICAL INSTANCE COUNT AND THE ONLY ONE. ⛔ DO NOT RE-ENUMERATE IT ANYWHERE ELSE.** `[corrected 2026-08-21 late]` It had drifted into **three disagreeing copies** — here, the changelog below, and pre-publish check 28 — **which is this rule failing on itself.** ✅ **Both of those now point here.**

46. 🆕 🔴 **A PRIZEPICKS PLAY MAY BE RANKED BY PROBABILITY BUT MUST NEVER CARRY A BREAK-EVEN, AN EDGE, OR AN EV FIGURE.** `[measured 2026-08-21]` 🔴 **`−137` IS NOT A PRICE. It is the Odds API's ENCODING for "goblin" (the easier rung); `+100` encodes "demon" (the harder one).** Across **7 games × 4 markets PrizePicks returned exactly two distinct values in 145 outcomes — `−137` ×77 and `+100` ×68 — while the same pull returned 80 distinct prices from hardrockbet, 73 espnbet, 28 betparx, 27 underdog, 23 fliff, 18 ballybet, 1 pick6.** **No sportsbook prices 145 props at two numbers.** Sam caught it from the app: *"sean burkes 3.5 is not priced at −137… you can't keep giving me false info."*
    ⛔ **CONSEQUENCE: every "break-even 57.8%", every "edge +X" and every EV figure ever computed on a PrizePicks play is arithmetic performed on a constant. Those numbers are MEANINGLESS — not merely imprecise.**
    ⚠️ **PrizePicks does not price rungs at all.** It is a DFS product: **the SLIP is priced by pick-count and multiplier, and a goblin reduces the multiplier.** **There is no per-leg American price to break even against.**
    ➡️ **PROCEDURE: quote `model / raw / blend / matched class`, then ASK SAM FOR THE IN-APP MULTIPLIER.** ⛔ **Do not derive one from the feed.**
    🆕 ✅ **THE LINE IS DRAWN AT THE LEG / SLIP BOUNDARY — AMENDED 2026-08-21 (late). THIS IS THE RULE, NOT AN EXCEPTION TO IT.**
    ⛔ **PER-LEG break-even, edge and EV on a PrizePicks play are banned ALWAYS.** There is no per-leg price to compute them from — **`−137` and `+100` are the feed's goblin/demon LABELS.**
    ✅ **SLIP-LEVEL EV IS PERMITTED ONCE SAM REPORTS THE IN-APP MULTIPLIER, because that multiplier is a REAL, QUOTED PAYOUT.** `EV = stake × (joint × multiplier − 1)`; the slip break-even is `1 / multiplier`. ⛔ **Never a multiplier derived from feed values, and never a per-leg figure rebuilt out of a slip one.**
    ➡️ **Consequently the 8/21 TABLE C slip-EV table, the 8/20 `break-even at 2.0x = 50.0%` line and the 8/19 `55.6% at 1.8x` line are all LEGAL under this rule** — every one of those multipliers came from Sam, not from the feed. **Mirrored in `claude/card-blueprint.md` STEP 6 item 4 and pre-publish check 31.**
    ✅ **WHAT SURVIVES, precisely: the model probabilities are untouched; every HARD ROCK price, edge, break-even, de-vig and pair multiplier stands; and "at a flat payout take the lowest rung that clears" stands — but its justification is now that the 2-pick MULTIPLIER barely moves with rung difficulty (Sam's real slips: 1.8x, 1.9x, 2.0x), not that PrizePicks charges −137 everywhere.** ⚠️ **The DFS thesis survives in WEAKENED form and can no longer be quantified from the feed.**
    ⚠️ **STILL OPEN, DO NOT RESOLVE BY GUESSING: whether Burke's 3.5 rung exists in the app at all.** Sam's message proves the price is fake and **says nothing about the rung.** The feed has shown rungs that did not exist and hidden rungs that did.
47. 🆕 🔴 **BEFORE TREATING ANY BOOK'S PRICES AS REAL, COUNT ITS DISTINCT VALUES IN THE PULL.** **A book returning 1–2 distinct prices across dozens of outcomes is emitting LABELS, NOT QUOTES.** **This is a one-line check and it would have caught the −137 error on day one.** ⚠️ **`pick6` returned ONE distinct value on 8/21 — treat it as an enum until proven otherwise.** 🔴 **FIFTH INSTANCE of this project's most repeated failure: a fact about a QUERY written down as a fact about the WORLD** — after ~~"Hard Rock posts no alt ladders"~~, ~~"Hard Rock's alt ladders are overs-only"~~, WebFetch's 27 fabricated events, and the feed omitting Luzardo's entire market while carrying his game. **This one is the worst: it was load-bearing for the project's stated premise and it survived every audit for days.** ⚠️ **And it is the first where the artifact was an ENCODING rather than an ABSENCE — a value whose shape is legal can still be the wrong KIND of thing.**

FOOTBALL (NFL + CFB) = THREE BOOKS — Hard Rock, FanDuel, DraftKings.** Sam, 2026-09-22: *"just use those three"*. ➡️ **Code: `collect.FB_BOOK_KEYS` / `league_books(league)`, and `card_fb.GL_BOOKS`; the two Hard Rock feeds count as ONE book in any "N books" figure.** ⛔ **`us_dfs` stays banned; PrizePicks is never a source of a number (rule 46).**

NEVER FROM PRIZEPICKS.** Sam, 8/21: *"from now on i want you to pull all odds from sportsbooks not prizepicks, i see the pattern of you messing up is when you are pulling odds from alt lines on prizepicks. to avoid this let's just pull our odds from hardrock and other sports books."*

HARD ROCK ONLY, EVERY PROP, EVERY CARD.** **Sam, 2026-08-22:** *"i onlt want to see hardrock odds from now on for all props"* ➡️ **`us_dfs` was already banned and stays banned; `us` is now dropped as well.** **Hard Rock lives in `us2` ONLY, so `regions=us2` alone is correct and sufficient.** ✅ **The principle is unchanged and is why this works: omitting a region is the fix at the source — a price that never enters the working set cannot be quoted.** `us_dfs` is the region that carries PrizePicks and pick6, the two books that returned 2 and 1 distinct values in the 8/21 pull (ledger rules 46–47).

NEW: 4 markets × 1 region = 4 credits per game.**

The `us2` siblings are the remaining check.**

49. 🆕 🔴 **HARD ROCK'S APP LABELS ALT STRIKEOUT RUNGS AS "TO RECORD N+ STRIKEOUTS". THE FEED CALLS THE SAME BET "OVER N−0.5". THEY ARE THE SAME WAGER — DO NOT TREAT THE LABELS AS DIFFERENT RUNGS.** `[measured 2026-08-21 — Sam's screenshot vs the raw pull, Sean Burke, ALL NINE RUNGS]`

    | App label | Feed outcome | Price (both) |
    |---|---|---|
    | To Record 3+ | Over 2.5 | −4000 |
    | **To Record 4+** | **Over 3.5** | **−1000** |
    | To Record 5+ | Over 4.5 | −400 |
    | To Record 6+ | Over 5.5 | −185 |
    | To Record 7+ | Over 6.5 | +115 |
    | To Record 8+ | Over 7.5 | +220 |
    | To Record 9+ | Over 8.5 | +425 |
    | To Record 10+ | Over 9.5 | +800 |
    | To Record 11+ | Over 10.5 | +2000 |

    ✅ **NINE OF NINE MATCHED TO THE DOLLAR.** 🔴 **This is the first end-to-end confirmation in the project that a Hard Rock feed price equals the app price**, and it is the direct evidence for rule 48: **the feed's Hard Rock numbers are real; the feed's PrizePicks numbers were never prices at all.**
    ⚠️ **THE OFF-BY-ONE IS A LIVE HAZARD.** Reading "4+" as "over 4.5" turns a −1000 quote into a −400 one and moves the break-even by **10.9 points.** ➡️ **When quoting a Hard Rock alt rung back to Sam, give BOTH forms** — "o3.5 (the app's *4+ strikeouts*) at −1000" — so a mismatch is visible on the page instead of being discovered after the bet.
    ✅ **Sam's original correction is confirmed exactly as he stated it:** *"see 3.5 is actually −1000 like i said."* **o3.5 = −1000.**


50. 🆕 🔴 **WHEN THE PITCHER'S PROFILE AND THE LINEUP'S PROFILE POINT OPPOSITE WAYS, GO TO THE DIRECT HEAD-TO-HEAD — THIS SEASON AND LAST SEASON ONLY, NOTHING OLDER.** **Sam's rule, stated 8/21:** *"when you see 2 contradictions, a pitcher who doesn't strike out batters and a lineup who strikes out a ton, i want you to look at the matchups between the two… we only look at this season and the last season to get the most recent stats on the matter."*
    ➡️ **A CONTRADICTION IS THE TRIGGER, NOT A ROUTINE STEP.** Run it when the two inputs disagree — a low-K arm against a high-K lineup, an elite arm against a lineup that does not whiff. ⛔ **Do not run it on every play and do not fold it into the blend.** **It is a TIEBREAKER, and on most nights it is a sample of one or two starts.**
    ⚠️ **STATE `n` ON THE ROW, ALWAYS, AND SAY "NONE" WHEN THERE IS NONE.** `[measured 8/21]` **Of the TEN ARMS CHECKED on the 8/21 board — not ten carded plays; the ten were Rodríguez, Dobbins, Melton, Lodolo, Cameron, Ginn, Burke, Prielipp, Manaea and Chandler — FOUR had NEVER faced that night's opponent in 2025–26: Burke, Prielipp, Manaea and Chandler.** 🔴 ~~"four of ten carded arms"~~ **corrected 8/21 evening: TABLE A is eight plays and contains no Prielipp, and Chandler was a declined play, so no set of ten CARDED arms contains all four names (rule 45).** **"No head-to-head" is a finding and must be printed, not omitted.**
    ✅ **ITS FIRST RUN RESOLVED A LIVE CONTRADICTION AND IT IS THE REASON FOR TONIGHT'S BET.** **Eduardo Rodríguez (6.4 K/9, lowest on the slate) vs Cincinnati (the most strikeout-prone lineup in baseball).** Head-to-head: **2025-06-06 — 3 K in 5.0 IP · 2026-06-12 — 3 K in 2.2 IP on 85 pitches.** 🔴 **The second row is the strong one: he was knocked out in the third and still struck out only three.** **Both under a 5.5 line, joining his own 21/25 and his peers' 30/41.**
    🔴 **AND IT CONTRADICTED CLAUDE TWICE THE SAME RUN — RECORDED BECAUSE IT CUTS AGAINST THE CARD, NOT FOR IT:**
    - **Schlittler vs Toronto.** The matched class read **18/29** and the play was written down as a doubt Sam shared. **His own head-to-head is 7 K, 7 K, 7 K in three 2026 starts, the last of them six days ago** (2025 was 3 K and 2 K). **The number that scared us both off was measuring other pitchers.** ⚠️ **Ranking deliberately NOT changed mid-card — 5 starts, 3/5 across both years, and re-weighting evidence the moment it says something better is the failure this project has rules against. Logged for the next card.**
    - **Sonny Gray vs San Francisco.** Called "the weakest of the six" off a 12/23 season rate and a 30/60 peer rate. **He faced these Giants twice in Sept 2025 for 6 K and 7 K, both clearing 4.5.** ⚠️ **Still not a bet at −230, but "coin flip" was too dismissive and the check should have been run BEFORE the verdict was published.**
    ✅ **Yamamoto vs Pittsburgh CONFIRMED the read** — one start, 2025-04-25, **5 K against a 5.5 line. A loss.**
    ⛔ **TWO-SEASON WINDOW IS THE RULE.** Older meetings describe a different pitcher and a different lineup. **Do not widen it to rescue a small sample.**


51. 🆕 🔴 **A PITCHER WHO FACED THIS LINEUP INSIDE THE LAST 30 DAYS IS A DIFFERENT BET FROM ONE WHO DID NOT. HAIRCUT THE HIGH THRESHOLDS.** `[measured 2026-08-21 — prompted by Sam: "your not worried about dobbins facing the phillies 2 starts ago"]`
    **296 rematches inside 30 days this season, mean gap 15.2 days.**
    ⚠️ **THE PRE-REGISTERED TEST FAILED, AND THAT IS RECORDED FIRST.** Spec was *mean K drop ≥ 0.5 with |t| ≥ 2.0*. **Result: 5.04 → 4.74, a drop of 0.30, t = −1.78. FAILS.**
    🔴 **THE THRESHOLD ANALYSIS, RUN AFTERWARD, IS SIGNIFICANT — AND IT IS EXPLORATORY, NOT CONFIRMED.** 4+ K fell **221/296 → 182/296**; paired McNemar **χ² = 12.78** (76 hit-then-miss against 37 miss-then-hit). ⛔ **Looking a second way after the primary fails IS specification shopping. Labelled as such, and NOT a fitted input.**
    **Conditional on a strong first meeting (≥5 K) — the number that actually applies:**

    | Next start gets 4+ K | |
    |---|---|
    | vs **anyone** | **1363/1826 = 74.6%** |
    | vs the **same team**, ≤30 days | **116/173 = 67.1%** |

    ➡️ **A dominant outing against a lineup buys NOTHING when you see them again two weeks later — 67.1% is just the league base rate.**
    ✅ **THE EFFECT IS THRESHOLD-DEPENDENT, WHICH IS THE OPERATIONALLY USEFUL PART.** At **3+ K** the same split is **87.5% → 84.4%, about 3 points**, against **~8 points** at 4+. ➡️ **The lower the bar, the less a second look costs.** **A rematch is a reason to DROP A RUNG, not to drop the pitcher.**
    ⛔ **DO NOT FIT THIS.** Pre-registered for the September re-fit with the threshold specification written into `claude/owed-tests.md` before the data is touched again.
    🔴 🆕 **2026-08-23 — THIS RULE WAS NOT RUN ON THE 8/22 CARD, AND PRE-PUBLISH CHECK 36 THEREFORE FAILED ON TWO OF ITS THIRTEEN ROWS.** **Ryan Weathers had faced Toronto on 2026-08-16 and Dylan Cease had faced the Yankees on 2026-08-16 — SIX DAYS in both cases — and neither card row stated the gap.** ⚠️ **Rule 50's head-to-head WAS run and printed on both arms; rule 51's ≤30-day check was not. Reading the same game log for one is not running the other, and that is the specific failure to guard against.** ✅ **Both instances are now logged in `claude/calibration-accumulators.md`, giving this rule its FIRST CLEAN INSTANCES — and they point OPPOSITE ways: Weathers MISSED at a 4+ K bar after a 4-K meeting, Cease HIT at a 7+ K bar after a 10-K meeting.** ⛔ **n=2. Nothing is re-graded, no carded number moves, and the pre-registered specification stays FAILED and closed.**

52. 🆕 🔴 **A TABLE C ROW IS CREATED ONLY FROM SAM'S OWN REPORT — A SCREENSHOT, OR HIS WORDS NAMING THE SLIP HE PLACED. UNTIL THEN A PROPOSED SLIP LIVES IN PROSE, NEVER IN THE BET TABLE.** `[measured 2026-08-21 — the table was wrong TWICE in one evening]`
    **5:00pm — Rodríguez + MANAEA and Rodríguez + BURKE were written into TABLE C as placed bets.** They were **Claude's recommendations.** Sam had reported nothing.
    **5:05pm — he corrected the first: *"i actually bent melton not manea sorry."*** Logged as Rodríguez + Melton.
    **5:41pm — his screenshots arrived and BOTH rows were still wrong.** The real slips are **Manaea + Rodríguez (PrizePicks, 1.80x, $30)** and **Dobbins + Melton (PrizePicks, 2.20x, $20)**. **Burke was never bet, and neither slip was on Hard Rock.**
    ⛔ **TABLE C IS A REPORT OF WHAT SAM DID. It is not a place to pre-write what he is expected to do.** 🔴 **This is ledger rule 24 in the other direction: rule 24 says a recommendation IS a pick and must be logged — it belongs in TABLE A or in prose. Rule 52 says it is NOT a bet and must never reach the bet table.**
    ⚠️ **Nothing was graded off the wrong rows and no money figure moved. On a slower night this is exactly how a fabricated BANKROLL row gets written**, and the bankroll is the one table in this project that cannot be reconstructed from any other source.

53. 🆕 🔴 **SAM DECIDES WHICH BETS HE IS COMFORTABLE WITH. CLAUDE'S JOB IS TO PROVIDE THE DATA. NEVER REMOVE A PLAY FROM HIS VIEW.** **Sam, 2026-08-22:** *"i do agree, i dont want you to stop doing this tho, it should be up to me to decide whcih i feel comfortable with your job is to provide me the data"*
    **WHAT IT CHANGES, CONCRETELY — ALL FOUR:**
    1. ⛔ **A PLAY THAT FAILS A CHECK IS SHOWN WITH THE FAILING NUMBER ATTACHED, NOT DELETED FROM THE BOARD.** **"Cut", "declined on the carding floor" and "killed by rule 15" must become LABELS ON A VISIBLE ROW — never a reason the row is absent.**
    2. **THE CARDING FLOOR IS A LABEL, NOT A GATE.** A sub-floor play still gets printed, with its blend, its matched class and the floor it failed.
    3. **CLAUDE'S RECOMMENDATION IS ONE LINE AMONG THE DATA, NEVER A SUBSTITUTE FOR IT.** State the read, state the number, and let Sam choose.
    4. ✅ **THIS DOES NOT MEAN WITHHOLDING JUDGMENT.** Sam asked for the data *and* the read — **flagging a bad price is still the job.** **What is not Claude's call is whether Sam sees the play at all.**
    🔴 **THE EVIDENCE THAT PRODUCED IT, AND IT IS STATED HERE BECAUSE IT CUTS AGAINST CLAUDE:** on **2026-08-21 Sam named six plays and FIVE WON.** **Claude argued against four of them — Yamamoto o5.5 K (9 K), Sonny Gray o4.5 K (6 K), Burke o3.5 K (4 K) and Chandler u15.5 outs (12 outs)** — **every one on PRICE grounds** (−300 needs 75%, −230 needs 70%, −1000 needs 90.9%, −160 needs 61.5%). **The table is in the 8/21 TABLE C section of this doc; no row of it is restated here (rule 45).**
    ⚠️ **THE PRICE LOGIC WAS AND IS DEFENSIBLE: a bet can win and still be bad value.** 🔴 **BUT THE REJECTIONS WERE DELIVERED AS REMOVALS RATHER THAN AS DATA, AND THAT IS THE PART THAT WAS NOT CLAUDE'S TO DECIDE.**
    ⚠️ **n=6. ONE SLATE. THE RULE IS ABOUT WHOSE DECISION IT IS, NOT ABOUT WHO WAS RIGHT.** ⛔ **It does not license lowering the bar on price, and it re-tunes nothing.**
    ➡️ **Mirrored in `claude/card-blueprint.md`** — STEP 4B's matched-class check, the carding floor and rule 15's model/raw gap are all **labels on a printed row**, not gates that remove one.

54. 🆕 🔴 **TWO LEGS ARE IN DIFFERENT GAMES ONLY IF THEIR GAME IDENTITIES DIFFER. NEVER COMPARE OPPONENT NAMES.** `[measured 2026-08-22 — a live card shipped FOUR IMPOSSIBLE PAIRS, including the #1 recommendation]`
    🔴 **THE FAILURE: Jared Jones' opponent read `Los Angeles Dodgers`; Tarik Skubal's read `Pittsburgh Pirates`. Two opponent strings that differ were read as two games that differ. They are the two halves of ONE game — PIT@LAD.**
    ⛔ **CHECK THE EVENT ID, or equivalently the ordered `(away_team, home_team)` pair. An opponent name proves nothing: in every game on every slate, the two starters have DIFFERENT opponents and the SAME game.**
    ⚠️ **The identical trap exists on any slate and it is not rare — it fires whenever two carded arms happen to be opposing starters. On this one slate it applied to THREE separate pairings: Jones/Skubal (PIT@LAD), Irvin/E. Pérez (WSH@MIA) and Lowder/Soroka (CIN@ARI).**
    ⛔ **WHY IT MATTERS TWICE OVER: (1) Hard Rock CORRELATION-REPRICES same-game parlays, so the quoted multiplier DOES NOT EXIST — this doc already recorded that for the 8/21 Yamamoto + Chandler slip in this same ballpark; and (2) the joint probability is invalid regardless of price, because two opposing starters' lines are not independent events.**
    🔴 **IT WAS CAUGHT BY SAM, NOT BY THIS PROJECT — *"skubal is on the dodgers"* — and every pre-publish check had already passed on a table containing four bets that cannot be placed.** ⚠️ **Rule 32 already requires resolving each starter's TEAM from `schedule?hydrate=probablePitcher`; the teams here were CORRECT. ⛔ The defect was downstream of rule 32, in the PAIRING step, which had no check at all. Resolving the team is not the same as resolving the game.**
    ➡️ **Mirrored in `claude/card-blueprint.md` STEP 6.**

55. 🆕 🔴 **A MARKET-DERIVED NUMBER IS NEVER PRESENTED AS A MODEL PROJECTION.** `[written 2026-08-22 — BEFORE a line of the product exists, because this failure becomes trivially easy the moment sportsbook data reaches a public page]`
    **THREE KINDS OF NUMBER, AND EVERY NUMBER THIS PROJECT PUBLISHES IS EXACTLY ONE OF THEM:**
    - 🟢 **MODEL** — **fitted, backtested, calibrated.** **May carry a confidence %.**
    - 🔵 **MARKET** — **de-vigged implied probability, a posted run line, a posted total.** **It is the BOOK'S opinion.**
    - ⚪ **DESCRIPTIVE** — **records, live game state, game logs.** **No modelling claim at all.**
    ⛔ **A MARKET NUMBER NEVER CARRIES A GIZMO'S CONFIDENCE %, IS NEVER CALLED A PROJECTION, AND IS NEVER BLENDED INTO ONE WITHOUT SAYING SO.**
    🔴 **WHY NOW:** **adding sportsbook data to a public product makes this failure trivially easy — a de-vigged consensus probability looks IDENTICAL to a model output on a page.** **Presenting one as the other claims credit for the book's work and destroys the only thing the track record is for.**
    ✅ **DISPLAYING MARKET NUMBERS IS ENCOURAGED AND HONEST — labelled as market.** ⛔ **What is banned is the LABEL, not the number.**
    **DE-VIG ALWAYS.** **Raw two-way implieds sum over 100%; normalise PROPORTIONALLY.** `[measured 2026-08-22]` **NYY −550 raw 84.6% + TOR +350 raw 22.2% = 106.8%; de-vigged 79.2% / 20.8% — the favourite is inflated 5.4 points if you skip it.** ✅ **Sam's own example is exactly right: −115 both sides = 107% raw, de-vigs to precisely 50/50.**
    ⚠️ **THIS RULE IS A GENERALISATION OF THE DISCIPLINE THAT PRODUCED LEDGER RULES 25, 46 AND 47 — every one of those was a number whose PROVENANCE was misunderstood** (a summarising fetch's invention read as a quote; a goblin ENUM read as a price; a book's label set read as a price set). **Rule 55 is that lesson applied to a PRODUCT SURFACE instead of a card.**
    ➡️ **Full detail in `claude/gizmos-picks-spec.md`.**

56. 🆕 🔴 **THE DAILY CARD IS GENERATED BY `card.py` ON THE GITHUB ACTIONS RUNNER — `python collect.py card`. IT SPENDS ZERO ODDS API CREDITS BECAUSE IT CALLS NOTHING AT ALL**, reading `data/latest/pitchers.json.gz` and `data/latest/props.json.gz` that the collector has already stored, and writing `picks/<date>.json`. `[2026-08-23]`
    **WHAT IT COMPUTES:** 🟢 **the v4.0 strikeout model (`E[K] = 4.939 + 0.673×(trailing-8 mean K − 4.949) + 0.575×(oppK − C) ± 0.151`, Poisson)** · 🟢 **the v4.0 outs model (`mu = 15.899 + k×(trailing-8 mean outs − 15.903) + 0.0371×(previous start pitch count − 86.6) ± 0.189`, k tiered 0.638 / 0.759 / 0.317, Normal with SD = cvO × season mean outs)** · ⚪ **the raw hit rate at the exact threshold** · **the 50/50 `blend`** · ⚪ **the matched class (STEP 4B)** · ⚪ **the head-to-head (STEP 4C)** · **and Hard Rock pairs.**
    🔴 **WHAT IT REFUSES TO DO, AND EACH REFUSAL IS A RULE ON THIS PAGE:**
    - ⛔ **IT DOES NOT PICK.** **Every check is a LABEL on a printed row, never a gate (rule 53).**
    - ⛔ **IT DOES NOT ADOPT AN UNADOPTED TEST.** **T21 and T22 are flags on `carried`, never on `blend` — see rule 57.**
    - ⛔ **IT DOES NOT FOLD THE MATCHED CLASS INTO THE BLEND.** **STEP 4B is a FLAG. Folding it in — even a third of the way — would be a NEW MODEL CHOICE WITH NO PRE-REGISTERED TEST.** ⚠️ **THIS IS A DELIBERATE DIFFERENCE FROM THE 8/22 HAND-BUILT CARD, WHICH NUDGED SOME ROWS BY HAND. The automated card does not, and that is stated rather than left to be noticed.**
    - ⛔ **IT DOES NOT SHOW A PAIR BELOW 1.8x** (rule 28 — Sam's own instruction, and the ONLY thing it withholds).
    - ⛔ **IT DOES NOT INVENT A PRICE.** **Every quoted number traces to a raw stored pull (rules 26 and 31).**
    ✅ **WHAT IT APPLIES THAT THE HAND-BUILT CARDS DID NOT: the raw rate is ALL STARTS with NO 4+IP filter (T23), and the matched class is selected on the DURABILITY axis for outs props and the K axis for strikeout props, point-in-time (T24).** 🔴 **NEITHER IS AN ADOPTION — both were findings about how to MEASURE, not new model terms. See `claude/owed-tests.md`, where both entries remain OPEN.**
    ⚠️ **Pairs: two legs, ONE book (Hard Rock only — it is the only one that multiplies), TWO DIFFERENT GAMES CHECKED ON GAME ID (rule 54), product ≥ 1.80, ranked by joint probability, labelled IN BAND / ABOVE BAND (rule 28).**

57. 🆕 🔴 **`blend` VERSUS `carried` IS NOW A MACHINE-ENFORCED DISTINCTION, VERIFIED BY A CHECK THAT FAILS THE BUILD.** `[2026-08-23]`
    - ✅ **`blend` IS THE PLAIN 50/50 OF MODEL AND RAW, AND IS THE ONLY COLUMN THAT ENTERS CALIBRATION.**
    - **`carried` is the blend AFTER the T21 flag (perfect-record shrinkage — 88.8% on 9+ prior starts, 78.0% on 3–8) and the T22 flag (shuttled starter, −14.4 points, point-in-time), and it enters NO DENOMINATOR.**
    - 🔴 **`verify_card.py` CONTAINS A CHECK THAT `blend` IS THE PLAIN 50/50 AND EXITS NON-ZERO IF IT IS NOT.** ⛔ **That check is what PROVES T21 and T22 were not silently adopted — it is not decoration, it is the enforcement.**
    ⚠️ **This mirrors exactly what the 8/22 hand-built card declared pregame. The difference is that a declaration can drift and a failing build cannot.**
    ⛔ **A FUTURE SESSION MAY NOT "IMPROVE" `blend` BY FOLDING IN A FLAG.** **Adopting T21 or T22 is a September re-fit decision with a pre-registered pass criterion, and the way to adopt one is to run its test — not to edit the blend.**

58. 🆕 🔴 **A CARD THAT FAILS `verify_card.py` IS NOT PUBLISHED. THE VERIFICATION RUNS BEFORE THE COMMIT STEP AND EXITS NON-ZERO ON ANY FAILURE.** `[2026-08-23 — 21 checks, all passing on the first real run]`
    **What it re-derives rather than reads back:** **Poisson CDF against 200,000 simulations (max gap 0.307 points)** · **the Normal outs probability rebuilt from the raw game log, `mu` and SD both recomputed (max gap 0.0400 points)** · **every raw hit rate re-counted by hand off the game log (32/32 exact)** · **every multiplier re-derived from the two American prices** · **every joint re-derived as the product of the two blends.**
    **What it refuses to let through:** **a start dated on or after the slate date inside any denominator (point-in-time)** · **model over + under ≠ 100% on a two-sided number (32/32)** · **a same-game parlay, checked on GAME ID** · **a pair below 1.8x** · **a pair leg not priced at Hard Rock, or a pair reusing a pitcher** · **a quoted price with no raw stored pull behind it (0 invented)** · **a `blend` that is not the plain 50/50** · **a row with no calibration band label** · **a `picks` row shorter than −700** · **a higher over rung more likely than a lower one (monotonicity)** · **an over rung missing its "To Record N+" app-label form (rule 49's off-by-one).**
    ⚠️ 🔴 **BELOW-FLOOR ALT RUNGS ARE KEPT AND SHOWN, NOT DELETED — 118 rungs on the first card, 23 of them below −700.** **The floor is a LABEL on the rung and a GATE only on what the card STARS (rule 53).**
    ⛔ **A CHECK THAT READS A NUMBER BACK OUT OF THE FILE IT IS CHECKING VERIFIES NOTHING.** **Every check above either re-derives from source or asserts an impossibility.**

59. 🆕 🔴 **HARD ROCK'S OWN PRICE IS THE PRICE THAT IS QUOTED. A BEST-PRICE-ACROSS-BOOKS FIGURE IS NEVER QUOTED AS SAM'S PRICE.** `[2026-08-23 — they had been the SAME FIELD]`
    ✅ **Every prop now keeps Hard Rock's own price and bet-slip link in an `hr` field, and the card quotes that number.**
    ✅ **WHEN HARD ROCK DID NOT POST THAT MARKET IN THE PULL, THE PLAY IS STILL PRINTED (rule 53), THE ROW SAYS SO, AND THE OTHER BOOK IS NAMED.**
    ⛔ **Such a play is BARRED FROM PAIRS — only Hard Rock multiplies (rules 20 and 54).**
    ⚠️ **"Hard Rock did not post it" always means IN THIS PULL. It is a fact about the response, never about the book (rule 31).**
    🔴 **This is `claude/mlb-data-stack.md`'s "best-price boards cannot measure a market" one level down: a best-of-each-side figure is a shopping number, and quoting it as the number Sam will actually get is a different claim.**

60. 🆕 🔴 **THE CARD IS DATED BY THE SLATE, NOT BY THE WALL CLOCK. THE DATE IS THE ET DATE OF THE EARLIEST GAME THAT HAS NOT STARTED.** `[2026-08-23]`
    ⚠️ **At 11pm ET the board already holds tomorrow's games and none of today's. Dating off the clock would file tomorrow's card under today, and the page would never find it.**
    ✅ **This is what makes the board roll to the next day by itself, and it is why finished games drop off every tab.**
    ⛔ **Do not "fix" this to a clock date. A card is about a slate, and the slate is what names it.**

61. 🆕 🔴 **A RULE WRITTEN IN A DOC IS NOT A RULE IN THE CODE. WHEN A RULE CHANGES SOMETHING THE COLLECTOR DOES, CHANGE THE COLLECTOR AND SAY YOU DID.** `[measured 2026-08-23 — TWO defects found, both of this shape]`
    1. 🔴 **THE PITCHER-PROPS PULL WAS STILL ON `regions=us,us2`.** **Ledger rule 48 narrowed the standing pull to `us2` — HARD ROCK ONLY — on 2026-08-22, the doc was amended in place, and THE COLLECTOR NEVER GOT THE CHANGE.** ✅ **Fixed to `REGIONS_CHEAP` (`us2`), which also halves that pull: 3 markets × 1 region × ~16 games = 48 credits per pull instead of 96.**
    2. 🔴 **THE ALT LADDER WAS BEING PULLED AND THEN DROPPED.** **`pitcher_strikeouts_alternate` was in the request and never survived into `props.json.gz`, so a rung-walk into the 1.8x band had nothing to walk — the ladder was bought and thrown away (rule 27).** ✅ **Ladders are now stored per pitcher, Hard Rock only, DEDUPED across `hardrockbet` and `hardrockbet_oh` (they post the same rung and it was producing duplicate plays), each rung carrying BOTH forms — the feed's `o2.5` and the app's `To Record 3+` — per rule 49.** ⛔ **`pitcher_outs_alternate` is deliberately NOT requested: Hard Rock posts no alt outs market at all, Sam-confirmed (rule 31).**
    🔴 **NAME THE PATTERN, BECAUSE THIS PROJECT ALREADY HAS IT RECORDED IN ANOTHER FORM: ledger rule 37 says A DOC ASSERTING A FIX IS NOT A FIX, and it was written about SCHEDULED-TASK PROMPTS.** **Rule 61 is the same failure with CODE as the second copy: a rule can be perfectly written, in the right doc, agreed by Sam, and still be false about what runs.**
    ➡️ **VERIFY AT THE SOURCE — `list_triggers` for tasks, `project_read` for docs, AND THE REPOSITORY FOR ANYTHING THE COLLECTOR DOES.** ⚠️ **Both defects had been live since 2026-08-22 and neither was found by a check; they were found by reading the collector while building the card generator.**

62. 🆕 🔴 **GITHUB'S SCHEDULER CAN STOP FIRING WITH THE WORKFLOW ENABLED, ACTIONS ALLOWED, THE REPO PUBLIC AND EVERY RECENT RUN GREEN. MANUAL DISPATCH KEEPS WORKING THROUGHOUT, SO "ACTIONS IS FINE" IS NOT AN ANSWER TO "NOTHING RAN".** `[measured 2026-08-27 — 21 hours of total silence]`
    🔴 **WHAT WAS ACTUALLY OBSERVED, from the Actions page rather than from the repo:** last run of ANY workflow `Aug 26 3:30 PM EDT`; **no "this workflow was disabled" banner**; Settings → Actions → General on **"Allow all actions and reusable workflows"**; the repo **Public**, so Actions minutes are free and unmetered; the last scheduled runs (#163/#164, 1:50 PM EDT) **green**. **A `workflow_dispatch` fired immediately and succeeded.**
    ⛔ **THE CAUSE WAS NEVER CONFIRMED. Do not write one into this doc later from memory.** ⚠️ **The standing suspicion is the SIZE OF THE CRON LIST — 29 entries in one workflow, most 5–15 minutes apart, on a free account — but that is a suspicion and it is labelled as one.**
    ➡️ **DIAGNOSTIC ORDER, cheapest first, and it is only four steps:** (1) Actions tab → disabled banner? (2) Settings → Actions → General → permissions; (3) is the repo still public; (4) **dispatch the workflow by hand** — if it runs, the failure is the SCHEDULE alone and every repo-side check above is a dead end.
    ⚠️ **AND THE MEASUREMENT THAT MATTERS FOR PLANNING: even when it was working, this schedule was landing roughly 4 of 16 hourly `gamelines` runs.** **GitHub drops scheduled runs as ordinary behaviour. Any design that needs N specific runs to land is already wrong.**

63. 🆕 🔴 **A MODE IS A LIST. ONE CRON DOES A WHOLE TIME BLOCK, AND A FAILED PULL FAILS THE JOB *AFTER* THE COMMIT, NEVER BEFORE IT.** `[2026-08-27 — the response to rule 62; 29 cron entries → 16]`
    ✅ **`.github/workflows/collect.yml` now maps one schedule to `props-pitcher-hr props-batter-hr props-board`, and the Collect step loops over `$MODES`.** ⛔ **Via `env:`, not interpolated into the `run:` body — `${{ }}` inside `run:` is pasted as raw shell before bash sees it, and the dispatch input reaches that string.**
    🔴 **THE DEFERRED FAILURE IS THE POINT.** **A block runs several pulls; if the third dies, the two that already SPENT CREDITS must still reach the repo. The loop records `rc=1`, the commit runs, and a final step fails the job.** ⛔ **Do not turn this back into a bare `python collect.py` — that aborts the step, skips `Commit snapshots`, and throws away paid data to report an error the run would have reported anyway.**
    ✅ **THE CARD KEEPS ITS OWN CRON** (`42 8`, `46 14`, `48 20`) **so a `verify_card.py` failure can never discard the props pull that paid for it.** ⚠️ **This is the same reasoning as rule 58, applied one level out.**
    📊 **Credit cost is UNCHANGED at 606/day — batching changes the number of RUNS, never the number of PULLS.**
    🆕 **A CARD NOW BUILDS AT 4:42am ET.** **Sam, 2026-08-27:** *"i thought we changed all of the upload times from later in the day to 4am"* — **the props pulls had moved to 4am on 8/26; the CARD had not, so at 4am the lines were fresh and the picks were still yesterday's.** ⚠️ **The trade, stated: the 4:42am card is built BEFORE lineups are posted, and the 10:46am build replaces it.**

64. 🆕 🔴 **A PROJECTION IS SUPPRESSED ONLY WHERE THE MATH IS DEGENERATE — AND "DEGENERATE" IS A PROPERTY OF THE DISTRIBUTION, NOT OF THE PROBABILITY.** `[measured 2026-08-27 — 52 pitcher rows had no projection and the guard was the reason]`
    🔴 **The coin-flip guard in `project()` refused to invert at a displayed 50% ±0.5. That is CORRECT for the Poisson, whose lumpiness makes `P(X > 2.5) = 0.5` need `lam = 2.67` — an UNDER 2.5 would have printed a projection ABOVE its own line.** ⛔ **It is WRONG for the NORMAL, which is continuous: inverting it at 50% returns the line itself, which contradicts nothing.**
    ✅ **The guard now applies to `poisson` and `negbin` only, and those fall back to the MODEL'S OWN CENTRAL VALUE (`lam` / `mu`) — which is not an inversion and so carries none of the artifact.** **Landen Roupp's `outs 16.5` at a blend of 49.6/50.4 went from blank to 16.46.**
    🔴 **AND ROUNDING NEVER PUTS A PROJECTION ON ITS OWN LINE.** **Andrés Chaparro's total-bases mean is 1.469 against a line of 1.5; at one decimal it printed "1.5" beside "under 1.5" and read as the card arguing with itself.** ✅ **`proj_round()` keeps the second decimal in exactly that case and nowhere else.** ⚠️ **A display artifact, not a disagreement — but a reader cannot tell those apart, so the display is what changes.**
    ✅ **EVERY ROW `card.py` PRICES NOW REACHES THE PROJECTION INDEX — alternate rungs included.** **Indexing only the started-games pass left alt lines blank; the index went 1,316 → 1,400.**

65. 🆕 🔴 **A CHECK THAT COMPARES AGAINST THE *FILTERED* BOARD IS ASKING THE WRONG QUESTION. VERIFY AGAINST WHAT THE CODE DID, NOT AGAINST WHAT SURVIVED THE DISPLAY CUT.** `[measured 2026-08-27 — two false failures on one card, both correct plays]`
    🔴 **`verify_card.py` traced every top-10 row to `picks[] + below_price_floor[]`. Those two lists are the board AFTER `BOARD_MAX` truncation, so a top-10 row drawn from a play `card.py` priced but did not CARD failed a check it should have passed** — Sean Manaea `strikeouts over 3.5` and Ryan Waldschmidt `RBI under 0.5`, both fine.
    ✅ **The projection index now carries a one-byte `"p"` flag meaning "card.py priced this exact pid / market / side / line", and the check traces against that.** ⛔ **The flag is pinned from BOTH ENDS so it cannot become a rubber stamp: every carded row must carry it, AND it must cover strictly fewer entries than the index (1,185 of 1,400).**
    ⚠️ **The alternative — publishing the pool's 2,196 keys — was rejected at ~48KB on a file the PAGE DOWNLOADS.**
    🔴 **THE GENERAL SHAPE, because this project has now hit it twice:** **a false FAIL costs a morning and trains the reader to ignore the verifier, which is the more expensive failure of the two.** ✅ **Per `CLAUDE.md`, the old check was REPLACED, not deleted.**

66. 🆕 🔴 **ONE PROJECTED NUMBER PER PLAYER PER STAT. THE SAME NUMBER EVERYWHERE ON THE SITE. A PROJECTION IS A STATEMENT ABOUT THE PLAYER, NOT ABOUT THE ROW IT SITS ON.** `[measured 2026-08-27 — 51 of 608 combos carried more than one]`
    🔴 **SAM FOUND IT:** *"your projections in the gizmos picks for plays fo the day dont match your projections for the gizmos picks and the player props, why? you should have the same numbers across the entire website if your talking about the same stat or projction."* **He is right, and the old design could not deliver it.**
    ⛔ **THE CAUSE WAS THE DESIGN, NOT A BUG.** **A projection was an INVERSION of the row's own confidence, and confidence is `50% model + 50% his RAW HIT RATE AT THAT LINE`. The raw rate is line-specific and thin at extreme rungs, so EVERY RUNG OF ONE LADDER IMPLIED A DIFFERENT CENTRAL VALUE.** 📊 **Worst: `pitcher_strikeouts`, 9 of 11 pitchers, a 2.1 K spread. Sean Manaea read 6.3 K on the Top-10 row (his 3.5 rung) and 5.3 K on his carded row (4.5).**
    ✅ **WHAT REPLACES IT IS LINE-INDEPENDENT BY CONSTRUCTION: pitchers get the model's own central value (`lam` / `mu`), 🟢 MODEL; hitters get their own per-game mean, ⚪ DESCRIPTIVE.** ⛔ **This RETIRES the standing instruction "DO NOT print `central`" — that instruction was written when a projection only ever appeared beside its own carded row, where per-row inversion IS coherent. Once one stat appears at many lines on one site, per-row inversion is provably incoherent. Sam owns the call and he made it.**
    ⚠️ **WHAT IT COSTS WAS NOT ASSUMED. It was pre-registered as T37 (`research/t37_spec.md`) with the bar fixed BEFORE measuring: ≤5% contradictions at 70%+, ≤2% at 80%+, over ALL PRICED ROWS.** ✅ **RESULT: pitchers 0.0% and 0.0%; hitters 5.0% (n=80) and 0.0% (n=5); across six published cards hitters 2.59% (n=116) and 0.00% (n=50).** 🔴 **MY PRE-REGISTERED PREDICTION WAS WRONG AND IS RECORDED AS SUCH: I predicted pitchers would contradict 3–8% and they contradicted not at all.**
    ⚠️ 🔴 **HITTERS LANDED ON THE BAR, NOT INSIDE IT — 5.00% against 5%. That is not slack.** **If a later slate pushes it over, the pre-registered fallback is written down — invert ONCE at the primary line and reuse it — and THE BAR DOES NOT MOVE.**
    🔴 **THE POPULATION IS PART OF THE BAR.** **Measured over the CARDED rows only the same card reads 7.7%, because carding selects high-confidence rows.** ⛔ **`verify_card.py` therefore measures over every PRICED row — which is why each priced index entry now carries its confidence. Swapping the population under a pre-registered bar is the same error as moving the bar.**
    ⛔ **`apply_projections()` IS THE ONLY WRITER OF `projection`. `build_play` and `hitter_play` still compute a per-row inversion on their way to other things and it is OVERWRITTEN. TWO WRITERS IS HOW THE SITE ENDED UP QUOTING TWO NUMBERS FOR ONE PITCHER.**
    🔴 **AND THE ROUNDING DECISION IS MADE ONCE PER PLAYER PER MARKET, NOT PER ROW.** **Rule 64 keeps a second decimal when one would land on the line; rounding that against a SINGLE row made the same mean print 1.5 on a 0.5 rung and 1.51 on a 1.5 rung — the very incoherence being removed.** ⚠️ **`index.html` was throwing the digit away again with `toFixed(1)`; fixed in the same pass.**

67. 🆕 🔴 **A TEST THAT NEVER RUNS IS WORSE THAN NO TEST. THE GATE DISCOVERS ITS TESTS; IT DOES NOT NAME THEM.** `[measured 2026-09-01 — the repo held SIX test files and CI ran ONE]`
    ⛔ **THE FOUR THAT NEVER GATED ANYTHING WERE THE FOUR THAT MATTERED:** `test_regions.py` (props region pricing), `test_p4.py` (the Power 4 name matcher, 18 real feed strings), `test_cfb.py` (pace and the CFB parsers), `test_board_match.js`. 🔴 **THOSE COVER THREE OF THE PLACES THIS PROJECT HAS ACTUALLY BEEN BURNED — the five-card props mispricing, the `Arkansas Pine Bluff Golden Lions -> Arkansas` false positive, and the `re.I` receiver-name bug.** ⚠️ **They passed locally the whole time. Passing locally is not gating.**
    ✅ **THE FIX IS DISCOVERY, NOT A LONGER LIST** — `for t in test_*.py` and `test_*.js`. **Same rule as `budget.py`: never put a list anywhere you cannot auto-update.** ➡️ **A new test file now gates by existing, with no workflow edit.**
    ⚠️ **AND A SUITE THAT SHRINKS BELOW FOUR FILES FAILS THE RUN.** ⛔ **A glob that matches nothing is exactly how a suite disappears unnoticed — the discovery fix without the floor just moves the silent failure.**
    ⚠️ **This is rule 61's shape again** (*a rule in a doc is not a rule in the code*): the tests existed, the intent existed, and **the enforcement did not.**

68. 🆕 🔴 **A BUDGET TOOL THAT UNDER-COUNTS STILL PRINTS ✅ FITS. UNDER-REPORTING IS THE WORST FAILURE AVAILABLE TO A COST TOOL, AND IT HAS NOW HAPPENED TWICE.** `[measured 2026-09-01]`
    ⛔ **(1) `[2026-08-27]`** the mode pattern was `[a-z-]+`, which cannot match a batched mode list (rule 63) — **every batched cron read as UNMAPPED and the props vanished. Reported 126/day against a true 606.**
    ⛔ **(2) `[2026-09-01]`** `fires()` tested for `*` and for `-` and fell through to *one fire a day* on everything else. **Step syntax is neither, so `*/6` counted as ONE run instead of FOUR.** Both football line-movement crons use `*/6`. **Under-reported by 108 credits a week; 13,406 should have read 13,871.**
    🔴 **NOTHING ABOUT 13,406 LOOKS WRONG NEXT TO 13,871.** ⛔ **That is the whole problem — a plausible wrong number is invisible, and the tool's verdict line says ✅ either way.**
    ✅ **THE ARITHMETIC IS NOW PINNED BY HAND** (`test_budget.py`, 22 cases written from cron's grammar, not from what the code does) **and the test states the money consequence, so it says what it protects.**
    ➡️ ⚠️ **GENERAL FORM: a tool whose only output is a number needs a test that knows the number independently. Reconciling its own output against itself proves nothing.**



69. 🆕 🔴 **A CASE-SENSITIVE TEXT SEARCH AGAINST A RENDERED PAGE IS NOT A CHECK. THE PAGE IS FULL OF `text-transform:uppercase`, AND `inner_text()` RETURNS WHAT THE READER SEES, NOT WHAT THE SOURCE SAYS.** `[measured 2026-09-01/02 — FOUR false failures, all on correct code]`
    ⛔ **THE FOUR:** `'no moneyline' in body` (the cell said **NO MONEYLINE POSTED**), the `NORTHERN SUN` / `GLIAC` D2 leak probe, and `'one-sided market' in body` on the football props tab — where the note **was rendering on all 41 Anytime TD rows** and I spent a turn hunting a renderer bug that did not exist.
    🔴 **THE COST IS NOT THE WASTED TURN. IT IS THE DIRECTION OF THE ERROR:** a false FAILURE invites a "fix" to working code. **My first instinct was to edit `fbProps()`.** ⛔ **`index.html` is the file the MLB product ships from — a speculative edit there to chase a phantom is exactly the risk Sam ring-fenced when he said *"i dont want to mess up a good thing we already have going on."***
    ✅ **THE RULE, TWO PARTS.** **(1) Lowercase both sides, always** — there is no case in which the assertion wanted case-sensitivity. **(2) Prefer a STRUCTURAL assertion to a text one.** ➡️ The check that actually settled it was *"column 5 of every Anytime TD row equals the note, and 0 rows draw a blank or a dash"* — **that counts rows and would have caught a note rendering on only half of them, which the substring search never could.** ⚠️ **A substring search over a whole view passes if the string appears ONCE. It is the weakest question you can ask of a page.**
    ⚠️ **Rule 65's shape** *(a check that asks the wrong question)*, now on the rendering side rather than the data side. ➡️ **This does not soften "verify by driving the rendered page" — it sharpens it. Drive the page, then assert on CELLS AND COUNTS, not on prose.**


70. 🆕 🔴 **A GAME LOG ANSWERS "GAMES IN WHICH HE RECORDED A STAT", NOT "GAMES HE PLAYED" — AND ONLY ONE OF THOSE IS A DENOMINATOR.** `[measured 2026-09-03, and it stopped a wrong number reaching a live page]`
    ⛔ **THE MEASUREMENT:** NFL receivers show **25.8%** of logged games with zero catches; college receivers show **3.5%**. 🔴 **THE MECHANISM, CONFIRMED NOT ASSUMED:** nflverse carries **snap counts**, so an NFL receiver is logged for every game he was ON THE FIELD (**79.1%** of his zero-catch games have `snaps > 0`); CFBD carries **none** — its own `consumer_contract` says *"NO SNAP DATA EXISTS FOR COLLEGE FOOTBALL"* — so a college player is logged **only when he touched the ball**.
    ⛔ **THE COST: the median college receiver is missing SIX of his team's THIRTEEN games, 69.6% are missing three or more, and the missing half is the QUIET half.** A hit rate over that reads **too high on every over**, and ⚠️ **nothing about the resulting number looks wrong** — it renders beautifully beside a price.
    ⚠️ **AND THE TWO CAUSES CANNOT BE SEPARATED:** a missing college game is *"played and did not touch the ball"* **or** *"did not play"*. **Assuming the first inflates unders; assuming the second inflates overs. There is no third option in this data.**
    ✅ **SO THE COLLEGE BOARD SHIPS WITH A PRICE AND NO RATE, AND SAYS WHY IN ONE SENTENCE.** ⛔ **A number wrong in a KNOWN DIRECTION is worse than no number, because it gets bet.** 📌 **T52** is what would reopen it, and it is blocked on a participation source, not on analysis.
    🔴 **THIS IS THE ELEVENTH INSTANCE OF "A FACT ABOUT A QUERY IS NOT A FACT ABOUT THE WORLD"** — the project's most-repeated failure — **and the first one caught BEFORE it shipped rather than after.** ➡️ **What caught it was asking what the denominator MEANS before dividing by it.**

71. 🆕 🔴 **A SHARED COMPONENT IS THE RIGHT ANSWER TO "MAKE IT LOOK THE SAME", AND THE WAY TO MAKE THAT SAFE IS TO PROVE THE GENERALISATION IS A NO-OP *BEFORE* MAKING IT.** `[2026-09-03 — Sam: "same layout as the mlb section, literally the same everything just for football"]`
    ⛔ **THE TEMPTING WRONG MOVE WAS A SECOND COPY OF `pickCard`.** It would have shipped faster and drifted from MLB within a month — the project already has this lesson as *"a second implementation of the selection rule is a second thing to drift"* (top10) and *"do not compute a projection in JavaScript; that is a second copy of the model"*.
    ✅ **WHAT MADE THE SHARED PATH SAFE WAS ONE QUERY RUN FIRST:** across **533 published picks in 12 cards**, `kind === 'hitter'` and `confidence_basis === 'RECORD'` **never disagree once** — so widening `isH` to read the basis is *provably* a no-op on every MLB row that has ever shipped. ➡️ **Then an A/B render of `main` against the working copy, same data, same minute: all 8 MLB tabs identical TO THE CHARACTER.**
    🔴 **AND THE GENERALISATION IMMEDIATELY EARNED ITSELF — DRIVING THE PAGE FOUND THREE DEFECTS INVISIBLE IN THE JSON**, the worst being a college row rendering the chip **"MODEL"**: ⛔ **a rule 55 violation printed on screen**, because a MARKET row with no confidence fell through to the pitcher branch. Also *"Under null Outs"* on a one-sided Anytime TD (which states the OPPOSITE of the bet) and *"Own record undefined (undefined% smoothed)"*.
    ⚠️ **THE COMPONENT NOW CARRIES THREE PROVENANCES, NOT TWO — MODEL · RECORD · MARKET.** The old binary silently assumed every non-model row still had a number.

72. 🆕 ⚠️ **RULE 69 AGAIN, TWICE MORE IN ONE SESSION — AND THE SECOND TIME IT WAS A *WORD* BOUNDARY, NOT A CASE.** `[2026-09-03]`
    ⛔ **`'nan' in text` MATCHED "HER**NAN**DEZ".** ⛔ **`'model' in chip` MATCHED "RECORD — NO ***MODEL***"** — a check for a rule-55 violation that fired on the string proving compliance, i.e. **exactly inverted**.
    ✅ **THE FIX IS THE SAME BOTH TIMES AND IT IS STRUCTURAL, NOT COSMETIC: ASSERT ON A SET OR A COUNT, NOT ON A SUBSTRING.** `chips ⊆ {"record — no model", "market — price only"}` cannot be fooled; `'model' in chip` cannot be fixed by adding case-insensitivity. ⚠️ **A substring search over rendered text is the weakest question available** — it passes if the string appears once, anywhere, inside anything.
    📌 **SEVEN FALSE FAILURES ON CORRECT CODE ACROSS TWO SESSIONS — THE SEVENTH ON 2026-09-04, AND IT WAS `'nan'` A SECOND TIME.** ⛔ **Jo*nan*thony Hall**, a real Anytime TD row on the college board, failed the walk on both Gizmo's Picks and Player Props. **Zero bare `NaN` tokens were on the page.** ➡️ **`walk.py` now searches with `(?<![A-Za-z0-9])<token>(?![A-Za-z0-9])` for `undefined`, `nan` and `null`**, and keeps `[object Object]` as a literal because no name can contain it. ⚠️ **The harness was fixed AGAIN rather than the page — which is the correct direction and also the tell that the harness, not the page, is the thing that keeps being wrong.** 📌 **SIX FALSE FAILURES ON CORRECT CODE ACROSS TWO SESSIONS.** 🔴 **The danger has never been the wasted turn. It is that a false FAILURE invites a "fix" to working code** — and twice now that code was in `index.html`, the file MLB ships from.


73. 🆕 🔴 **A GREEN RUN IS NOT A PUBLISHED CARD, AND A HEALTHY-LOOKING PAGE IS NOT A FRESH ONE. `card_gate` MAKES THE RUN GREEN; IT NEVER MAKES THE CARD SHIP.** `[measured 2026-09-04 — I told Sam the build was red, then told him I had been wrong, and the SECOND statement was the wrong one]`
    ⛔ **THE MISREADING.** `card_gate.py` exists so an accepted failure stops screaming every fifteen minutes. Its own header says what it does not do: *"The card is still reverted either way."* I read a green run plus a card sitting on `main` plus a page that looked fine, and concluded nothing was wrong. **All three of those are compatible with the card being refused on every pass**, which is precisely the state the file was designed to produce.
    📊 **THE ACTUAL STATE.** `picks/2026-09-03.json` published normally at **14:08:47Z** — Sam's instinct that the site looked fine was correct. But `data/latest/card-verify-failure.txt` had been rewritten at **21:49, 22:04, 22:19, 23:36, 23:52, 00:05 and 00:27Z** with the same T37 failure. The board on screen was **frozen at 14:08 prices** and the next day's card would have been refused too.
    ✅ **THE ARTIFACT THAT ANSWERS "IS THE CARD PUBLISHING?" IS NOT THE RUN STATUS AND NOT THE PAGE.** It is **the mtime of the card file** and **whether `card-verify-failure.txt` is still being written.** ➡️ Both are one `git log` away and neither can be argued with.
    ⚠️ **THE ERROR WAS EXPENSIVE IN THE DIRECTION THAT MATTERS.** A false alarm costs a turn. **A false all-clear costs however long it takes Sam to notice on his own** — and the whole reason `card_gate` was built was that he had already had to do that once.
    📌 **AND IT IS RULE 26 IN A NEW COSTUME:** *a fact about a query is not a fact about the world.* "The run is green" is a fact about the gate. "The card published" is a fact about the repository. **Twelfth instance.**


74. 🆕 🔴 **A TEST SHIPPED IN COMMIT N THAT ASSERTS ON COMMIT N+1's CONTENT TURNS A CORRECT CHANGE INTO A RED BUILD. THE TEST GOES WITH THE THING IT TESTS, OR IT GOES LAST.** `[2026-09-04 — Sam: "commit 1 run failed"]`
    ⛔ **WHAT I DID.** The college logo self-heal was split into two commits because one file lives in `.github/workflows/` and needs a different upload path on GitHub. **`test_team_directory.py` went in commit 1. Its section 3 reads `collect.yml` and asserts a cron names `cfb-teams` — which is commit 2.** So commit 1 was red the moment it landed, on a repository whose code was entirely correct.
    ✅ **THE ASSERTION ITSELF IS RIGHT AND MUST NOT BE SOFTENED.** It is the good kind of check — it reads the deployed workflow instead of asserting from memory, which is exactly what rule 68 and the budget tool exist to enforce. ⛔ **The fix is NOT to make it tolerant of a missing cron.** That would delete the only thing proving the backstop is wired.
    ✅ **THE RULE, AND IT IS ABOUT ORDER, NOT ABOUT THE CHECK.** When a change spans two commits, **the test belongs in the LATER one** — or the two files ship together. ➡️ **Ask before splitting: does anything in commit 1 make a claim about commit 2?** If yes, the split is in the wrong place.
    ⚠️ **THE COST WAS SMALL ONLY BECAUSE SAM ASKED FIRST.** He said *"commit 1 run failed should i run commit 2 while you review it and fix"* — **the right instinct, and it was the fix.** ⛔ Had he instead waited for a patch, or reverted commit 1, a working change would have been unwound to chase a phantom — the same failure shape as rules 69 and 72, where a false red invites an edit to correct code.
    📌 **AND THE UPLOAD DOC SHOULD HAVE SAID SO.** It described two commits as independent. **They were not: commit 1 was red until commit 2 landed.** ➡️ **A multi-commit drop must state the order and say plainly if an intermediate state is red.**


75. 🆕 🔴 **TWO SOURCES DO NOT DISAGREE UNTIL THEY HAVE BEEN ASKED THE SAME QUESTION. A GAP BETWEEN TWO DIFFERENTLY-CONSTRUCTED DENOMINATORS IS A FACT ABOUT THE TWO CONSTRUCTIONS.** `[measured 2026-09-04 — and the finding it overturned was one of ours]`
    ⛔ **WHAT WE BELIEVED.** T52, and the football checklist's headline box: *NFL receivers have zero catches in 25.8% of logged games, college receivers in 3.5%* — read as proof that CFBD hides the quiet half of a college player's season, and used to justify **stripping every rate off every college row**.
    🔴 **WHY THE COMPARISON COULD NOT SUPPORT THAT.** nflverse logs a game when a player took **a snap**; CFBD logs a game when he **recorded a stat**. A file that logs snap-only games *necessarily* shows more zero-catch games. **The 25.8-vs-3.5 gap is produced by the two file formats.** ⚠️ Both numbers were correct. Neither answered the question they were put to.
    ✅ **THE LIKE-FOR-LIKE MEASUREMENT — share of the player's TEAM's games, both leagues, one filter.** College WR **0.750**, NFL WR **0.706**; TE 0.615 vs 0.657; RB 0.857 vs 0.882. ➡️ **College is not meaningfully worse than the NFL, and on receivers it is BETTER.** ⛔ Every number is a query over files already in the repo: **no fetch, no credit, no new source.**
    ✅ **AND THE ERROR WAS THEN MEASURED WHERE TRUTH EXISTS.** In the NFL the true denominator is snaps, so the same rate can be computed both ways and differenced: pooled **median |bias| 0.21 points, p90 3.79**, against a bar of 3.0/8.0 fixed before the numbers printed. ⚠️ **One cell fails and is reported, not buried:** WR receptions over 2.5, p90 **8.58**, max 19.44 — exactly where theory says it must land, at the low receiving lines.
    📌 **THE GENERAL FORM, AND IT IS RULE 26's TWELFTH INSTANCE:** *a fact about a query is not a fact about the world.* ⚠️ **The new lesson is the sharper one: this failure survives longest when the query is a COMPARISON**, because a difference between two numbers feels like evidence in a way a single number does not. ➡️ **Before believing a gap between two sources, write down the sentence each one is actually answering. If the sentences differ, the gap is not a finding.**
    ⚠️ **THE COST OF NOT CATCHING IT WOULD HAVE BEEN A PRODUCT DECISION MADE ON A NON-FINDING** — a whole league shipping without rates on the strength of a comparison that was never valid.


76. 🆕 🔴 **A CHECK THAT ASSERTS SOMETHING MUST *EXIST* IS ASSERTING A FACT ABOUT THE WORLD, NOT ABOUT THE CODE — AND THE WORLD IS ALLOWED TO CHANGE.** `[measured 2026-09-04 on unmodified `main`; the SECOND instance, and the first was already written into CLAUDE.md]`
    ⛔ **THE CHECK.** `verify_card` check 38: *"the diagnostics are still being written to the card"*, implemented as `len(flags) > 0`. Its intent was regression protection — a clean page must never become a card that stopped writing its diagnostics.
    🔴 **WHAT IT ACTUALLY ASSERTED.** The only flag that reaches the page is *"Hard Rock didn't post this one."* So `len(flags) > 0` requires that **some row on every card be missing from Hard Rock.** ⚠️ **That is a fact about the SPORTSBOOK.** On 2026-09-04 the card came out **45 picks, every one priced at `hardrockbet`** — zero flags was the correct answer, and the build went red on a perfectly good card.
    ⛔ **ITS ESCAPE HATCH HAD NEVER FIRED EITHER:** `all(r.get('flags') == [])` compares `None` to `[]`, and a row with no flags has no `flags` key at all. **The "nothing has flags, that is fine" branch was dead from the day it was written** — and nobody noticed, because until this slate the condition it guarded had never been reached.
    ✅ **THE REPLACEMENT, AND IT IS STRICTLY HARDER: RECONSTRUCT, DO NOT COUNT.** The flag is recomputed **a second way, from the stored board** rather than from `card.py`'s own field, and checked in **both directions on every row** — Hard Rock posted it → the row must NOT carry the flag; Hard Rock did not → it MUST. Plus the row's own `on_hardrock` must agree with the board. **43 rows reconciled on the live card.**
    ⚠️ **AND THE REPLACEMENT WAS TESTED AGAINST A BROKEN CARD, because a check that only ever passes is decoration.** A **FALSE** Hard Rock flag on a row the book *did* post now fails. ⛔ **The old check could not catch that at all** — it counted flags and never asked whether any of them were true.
    📌 **THE GENERAL FORM:** *existence is not correctness.* ➡️ **When a check's failure message would be a sentence about the outside world — a book, a feed, a vendor, a slate — it is the wrong check.** Replace counting with reconstruction. ⚠️ CLAUDE.md already carried this lesson from the `-700` alt-rung check; **it recurred anyway, in a different file, three weeks later.**


77. 🆕 🔴 **A FEATURE IS NOT VERIFIED UNTIL YOU REACH IT THE WAY A READER DOES. "THE DATA IS CORRECT" AND "SOMEBODY CAN SEE IT" ARE TWO DIFFERENT CLAIMS, AND ONLY ONE OF THEM WAS EVER BEING CHECKED.** `[measured 2026-09-04 — the bug was found ONLY because the check was done, not because anything reported it]`
    ✅ **HOW IT SURFACED.** T53 passed perfectly — 285 of 285 NFL games, quarters summing exactly, overtime agreeing across two files. **Every number was right.** Rather than stop there, the page was driven **as a reader**: pick NFL, click Scores, click a game card.
    🔴 **WHAT THAT FOUND.** The Scores tab opened on **2025 week 22 — the Super Bowl — five days before the 2026 season.** College was worse: **2025 week 16, a bowl game**, while **455 games sat in week 1 with 283 of them playing that very weekend.**
    ⛔ **THE CAUSE: ONE GLOBAL SERVING TWO TABS WHOSE DATA ARRIVES ON DIFFERENT SCHEDULES.** `fbSeason` was shared by Trends and Scores. Picking a league lands on Trends, which renders FIRST; its newest NFL table is 2025 because 2026 has no box scores until games are played — so it set `fbSeason = 2025` and Scores inherited it. `fbCurrentWeek` then returns the last week of an all-final season. ⚠️ **A schedule exists MONTHS before a box-score table does.** The codebase already knew that — `fbSeasonsAvailableSched` is a separate function from `fbSeasonsAvailable` for exactly this reason — **and the season variable had never been split to match.**
    📌 **THE TELL, WORTH RECOGNISING NEXT TIME:** two data sources deliberately kept separate, feeding one piece of shared state. **If the availability differs, the state has to differ.**
    ⚠️ **NOTHING WOULD EVER HAVE REPORTED THIS.** Every test passed. The walk passed — it asserts a tab *renders*, not that it renders *the right season*. `verify_card` never sees the page. ⛔ **A tab that renders last February perfectly is indistinguishable, to every check we own, from a tab that works.**
    ➡️ **SO: WHEN A DATA FEATURE SHIPS, OPEN THE THING AND LOOK AT IT.** Not the artifact, not the probe report — **the surface, from a cold load, by the path a reader takes.** ✅ That is what turned "T53 passed" into "T53 passed *and* is visible", and it is the only reason the season bug was caught before a weekend of college football.


78. 🆕 🔴 **A BUILDER THAT ONLY RUNS CHAINED TO SOMETHING EXPENSIVE RUNS APPROXIMATELY NEVER — AND THE CODE BEING ON `main` PROVES NOTHING ABOUT WHAT IS ON THE PAGE.** `[measured 2026-09-04 during a full audit; the THIRD instance of this shape in four days]`
    ⛔ **WHAT WAS LIVE.** `picks/fb-ncaaf-latest.json`, generated **2026-09-03T22:36:18Z: 0 of 50 rows rated, 50 of 50 Anytime TD, top row +5000.** **The exact board Sam complained about**, still serving, with **three shipped fixes sitting unused on `main` behind it** — the +400 ceiling and market mix, college rates, and `own_mean`.
    🔴 **THE CAUSE.** `card-fb` was named by **no cron at all** and ran only chained inside the **PAID** `props-player` pull, which fires Thu/Fri/Sat. ⛔ **AND `refresh` COULD NOT COVER IT** — the push trigger is MLB-only — **so a football code change had no path to the page whatsoever until the next paid pull.**
    📌 **THE THIRD INSTANCE IN FOUR DAYS, AND THE SHAPE IS ALWAYS THE SAME:** `cfb-teams` (existed only because one ad-hoc run wrote it), football `news` (feeds adopted, never collected), `card-fb` (chained to a paid pull). ⚠️ **Each was built, wired to a mode, verified by hand once, and then never run.** **Every one passed every test in the repo.**
    ✅ **THE FIX FOR THIS INSTANCE:** `card-fb` is FREE and a clean no-op with no board, so it is safe on every football cron — attached to the line-movement crons AND the daily news crons, so the board rebuilds **at least once a day whatever else happens.**
    🔴 **BUT THE ROOT CAUSE IS BIGGER AND IS NAMED HERE RATHER THAN QUIETLY PATCHED: FOOTBALL HAS NO ROWS IN THE FRESHNESS CONTRACT.** `freshness.survey()` returns **13 rows, every one MLB.** The whole architecture — *each artifact declares a due time, any run builds whatever is late, and the page says when something is stale* — **covers MLB only.** ⛔ **A football artifact can rot indefinitely and nothing notices, nothing says so, and no run repairs it.** ➡️ **All three of these bugs would have been caught automatically by a contract row.**
    ⚠️ **AND THE DETECTION LESSON:** this was found by an audit that compared **the committed artifact** against **what the current code produces** — not by any test, not by the walk, not by reading the diff. **`git log` on the code says nothing about whether the artifact was rebuilt.**


79. 🆕 🔴 **TWO SPORTS IN ONE DIRECTORY MEANS A GLOB IS A CROSS-LEAGUE BUG WAITING FOR A DATE COLLISION — AND THE COLLISION CAME IN FOUR DAYS.** `[measured 2026-09-04; it turned every run red and made the site unable to reproduce its own track record]`
    🔴 **WHAT HAPPENED.** `picks/` holds MLB cards named by date **and** football cards named `fb-<league>-<date>.json`. Both college cards carried `"date": "2026-09-03"` — a **college** date that happens to collide with a real MLB slate. `collect_record()` globs the whole directory, so each football card fell through to the hand-built-card branch and wrote **2026-09-03 into `skipped`, twice, described as "a hand-built baseball card"**.
    ⛔ **AND THEN THE VERIFIER BELIEVED IT.** `verify_record` took its exclusions from `record.json`'s own `skipped` list, dropped a legitimately graded MLB day, and reported **300/499 against a published 333/549**. ⚠️ **The published record was RIGHT the whole time.** Two correct artifacts and one wrong list, and the site could not reproduce its own headline number.
    🔴 **THE SHARED DIRECTORY WAS ALREADY KNOWN TO BE A HAZARD AND THE HAZARD WAS FILED UNDER THE WRONG NAME.** The workflow's own comment says football writes to `data/nfl/` and `picks/nfl/` and must not be graded against baseball's deadlines. **The `picks/` collision was the same fact and it was not carried across.**
    ✅ **THE FIX IS AN IDENTITY, NOT A FILENAME.** `collect_record()` skips any card naming a league other than `mlb`, keyed on the **card's own `league` field** — a rename cannot re-open it — and **silently**, because ⛔ **a football card is not a *skipped baseball card*; it is not that function's sport at all.** A `skipped` entry is a claim about MLB grading and must stay one.
    ⚠️ **THE SAME GUARD WENT INTO `verify_card`'s CALIBRATION POOL, WHERE IT CHANGES NOTHING TODAY** — football cards carry no `projections` key. **That is exactly why it went in now.** The Track Record breaking this way also "couldn't happen".

80. 🆕 🔴 **A VERIFIER THAT TAKES ITS EXCLUSIONS FROM THE ARTIFACT UNDER TEST CANNOT CATCH A WRONG EXCLUSION — IT CAN ONLY BE MISLED BY ONE.** `[2026-09-04, and the file's own docstring already forbade this in a different place]`
    🔴 `verify_record` derived **settledness** itself, from the stored box scores, precisely because *"this file's own docstring forbids reusing the number it is checking"*. ⛔ **And then it read `skipped` straight out of the file it was checking.** One exclusion was independent and the other was inherited, in the same loop, ten lines apart.
    ⚠️ **THE INHERITED ONE IS WHAT BROKE.** A wrong `skipped` entry is invisible to a verifier that trusts `skipped`: the day simply stops existing, the totals stop matching, and the failure points at the totals rather than at the list.
    ✅ **EVERY REASON TO SKIP IS NOW DERIVED IN THE VERIFIER** — not this sport, not a machine card, slate not settled — and each is **narrower** than "the file said so". ⛔ **And it was proved not to be toothless:** an inflated denominator and a wrong day are both still caught, in the same test.

81. 🆕 🔴 **WHEN ONE KIND OF ROW GETS A WARNING AND ANOTHER KIND SILENTLY CANNOT, THE FIELD IS RIGHT AND THE SENTENCE IS MISSING — AND ONLY A CHECK THAT RECONSTRUCTS THE SENTENCE FINDS IT.** `[measured 2026-09-04, one day after rule 76 built exactly that check]`
    🔴 On the 09-04 card, **all 8 pitcher rows Hard Rock had not posted said so. All 3 hitter rows Hard Rock had not posted said nothing.** `hitter_play()` returned a dict with **no `flags` key at all**, so the branch could not exist — a whole class of row could never carry the one flag that reaches the page.
