    ⛔ **THE READER SAW A DRAFTKINGS PRICE ON A HARD ROCK BOARD, UNWARNED, ON THE BOOK SAM ACTUALLY BETS.**
    ⚠️ **`on_hardrock` WAS CORRECT THE WHOLE TIME.** Every check on the *field* was green and stayed green. ✅ **It took rule 76's replacement — recompute the flag from the stored board and reconcile both directions on every row — to find it, and it found it the day after it shipped.** ⛔ **A check on the derived field alone would still be green today.**
    ➡️ **THE GENERAL FORM: when two code paths produce the same kind of row, an annotation present on one and absent from the other is invisible to every per-row check.** The only thing that catches it is reconstructing the annotation from the source data for **both** paths.

82. 🆕 ⚠️ **A CHECK SITTING EXACTLY ON ITS BAR IS AN UNSTABLE ALARM, AND THAT IS A FINDING — NOT A REASON TO MOVE THE BAR.** `[measured 2026-09-04]`
    🔴 T37's hitter half measured **5.03% against ≤5.0% counted from the live board, and 4.99% counted from the published card.** ⚠️ **The same check, the same day, red or green depending on which snapshot of the odds it reads.**
    ⛔ **THE BAR WAS NOT MOVED AND `card-accepted.txt` WAS NOT WRITTEN TO** — that file is Sam's alone, and the card stays refused either way. What was done instead: **measure the composition**. 53 of 63 contradictions are `batter_rbis` under 0.5, median distance from the line **0.03**.
    ✅ **AND THAT COMPOSITION IS THE REAL FINDING: `E[X] > 0.5` AND `P(X < 0.5) > 70%` ARE SIMULTANEOUSLY TRUE FOR A RIGHT-SKEWED COUNT.** A hitter who records zero RBI in 70% of games can average 0.53 a game. The confidence is a **frequency** fact; the projection is a **mean** fact. ⛔ **"The projection contradicts the row" is comparing two different statistics — an artifact, not a defect in either number.**
    ➡️ **The consequence is a NEW pre-registered test (T55), with its bars fixed before measuring**, asking whether the MEDIAN is the better number to publish on a 0.5 line — **and it must win on ACCURACY, not on agreement.** ⛔ A number that agrees with the confidence by being less accurate is a worse number.

83. 🆕 🔴 **A CONTRACT NOTHING ACTS ON IS A COMMENT — AND THE CODE THAT WOULD ACT ON IT DOES NOT NOTICE WHEN ITS OWN GUARD GOES STALE.** `[measured 2026-09-04, one day after football got freshness rows]`
    🔴 Football was given a freshness contract on 09-03. **Two guards written before it still said football had none**, and neither was touched: `collect.py` read `if "converge-off" in args or LEAGUE != "mlb"`, and the workflow printed *"$LEAGUE_NAME has no freshness contract yet"*. ⛔ **Both were correct the day before.**
    ⛔ **WHAT IT COST.** The college card was built at **8:51am against a 9:00am deadline**, GitHub dropped **both** 9am card crons, and **nothing rebuilt it for the rest of the day** — while the contract sat there correctly reporting it 6.5 hours late. The whole point of a contract is that the next run catches up; nothing was reading it.
    ➡️ **THE GENERAL FORM: a guard phrased as "X does not exist yet" is a claim with an expiry date, and nothing expires it.** ✅ The replacement asks the live question instead — `has_contract(league)` — so the guard cannot outlive its reason.

84. 🆕 🔴 **A FACT ABOUT A QUERY IS NOT A FACT ABOUT THE WORLD — AND THE ARGUMENT *THE CALLER* PASSES IS PART OF THE QUERY.** `[caught 2026-09-04, one check before the gate was switched on]`
    🔴 `card_fb.py` writes the football card to a **hardcoded `picks/`**. `collect.py` hands the contract `PICKS`, which for football is **`picks/ncaaf` — a directory that does not exist.**
    ⛔ **Every hand-run survey passed `picks` and read the card FINE.** Production would have reported it **MISSING FOREVER**, and switching the gate on would have made that a permanent red on a file that was sitting right there.
    ⚠️ **This is the twelfth instance of the rule and the first where the query differed only in an ARGUMENT.** Same file, same function, same machine — a different string for `picks`. ✅ **Check what the real caller passes, not what is convenient to type**, and pin the invariant: a test now asserts the card row is identical whatever `picks` the caller gives.

85. 🆕 🔴 **TWO FEEDS FOR THE SAME SPORT DO NOT AGREE ABOUT WHAT A TIMESTAMP MEANS, AND READING THEM THE SAME WAY IS A FOUR-HOUR ERROR THAT INVENTS ITS OWN BUG.** `[measured 2026-09-04 — and the bug it invented was one I had already shipped a fix for]`
    ```
    ncaaf   CFBD  startDate  "2026-08-27T22:00:00.000Z"   real UTC
    nfl     nflverse start   "2026-09-13T13:00"           LOCAL EASTERN, no zone
    ```
    🔴 `test_props_window.py` stamped `tzinfo=utc` on both. **Correct for college. Four hours wrong for the NFL.** ⛔ That is what produced *"six London games kick off at 5:30am ET and no pull reaches them"* — **they kick at 9:30am ET**, and the ordinary Sunday 7:08am pull reaches them with 2.4 hours to spare.
    ⚠️ **I ADDED A CRON TO FIX A GAP THAT DID NOT EXIST**, told Sam it was a real find, and it bought nothing for a week. ✅ It is removed, and the parsing moved into **one function used by the contract and the check**, so they cannot disagree again.
    ➡️ **THE SALVAGE: the error was PESSIMISTIC, so it invented a gap rather than hiding one.** ⛔ That is luck, not design — and the same code would have hidden a real gap had the sign gone the other way.

86. 🆕 🔴 **AN ARTIFACT THAT CORRECTLY DID NOTHING IS NOT LATE, AND THE FAIL-SAFE MUST POINT AT GOVERNING RATHER THAN AT SILENCE.** `[2026-09-04]`
    🔴 With a 14-hour props window a Friday buys **no** Saturday college games — correct, and it spends nothing. **The contract knew nothing about the window and called that late**, which asks for a repair no run can make. ⛔ A banner nobody can clear is a banner everybody ignores — the failure the whole file exists to prevent, arriving through the file itself.
    ✅ **The rule is now three-valued, and the third value matters most:** a game is in the window (governed) · no game is in the window (not governed) · **the schedule cannot be read (STILL GOVERNED)**. ⛔ **Absence of evidence must never be the thing that silences a check** — it is the one branch where "I don't know" has to fail loud, because it is indistinguishable from "the file is gone".
    ⚠️ Measured on the week it shipped: **10 college props deadlines, 6 warranted** — so **40% of the alarms this removes were false**, and the 6 real ones had produced only **1** actual pull.

87. 🆕 ⚠️ **A STRUCK LINE THAT STILL MATCHES THE REGEX IS A LIVE LINE.** `[2026-09-04, one run]`
    🔴 The removed Sunday cron was documented in a comment that quoted it verbatim — `# `- cron: "8 8 * * 0"`` — and **every tool in this repo finds crons by regex.** `test_freshness.py` counted **33 of 32**.
    ✅ Fixed by writing the struck entry **without its `- cron:` prefix**. ➡️ **When you strike a thing that is parsed by pattern, break the pattern, or the strike-through is decoration and the parser still sees it.**

88. 🆕 🔴 **A CHECK THAT ENUMERATES WHAT IS *PRESENT* CANNOT SEE WHAT IS *ABSENT* — AND AN ALLOW-LIST NOBODY EDITS IS A LIST THAT IS WRONG.** `[measured 2026-09-04; 30 of 35 files]`
    🔴 The workflow's `push:` trigger was an **allow-list of seven files**. `[measured]` **30 of the 35 code and test files at the repo root triggered NOTHING** — `card_fb.py` (the entire football board), `cfb.py`, `nfl.py`, `verify_board.py`, `verify_record.py`, `card_gate.py`, `budget.py`, **and every one of the 22 test files.**
    ⛔ **TWO KNOWN FAILURES WERE THIS, WEARING DIFFERENT CLOTHES.** A `card_fb.py` fix lands on `main`, no run starts, and the live college board does not rebuild — **rule 78 arriving through the CI trigger instead of through a cron.** And `test_cron_wiring.py` was pushed and **no run started at all: not green, not red, nothing** — **rule 67 again**, since we fixed the TEST list to discover its files and left the TRIGGER list naming them.
    🔴 **AND THE CHECK THAT GUARDED IT WAS GREEN THE WHOLE TIME**, because it asked whether **five named files** were in the list. They were. ⛔ **It could only ever confirm what was already there.**
    ✅ **THE FIX IS TO INVERT THE DEFAULT:** an ignore-list is right for every file that does not exist yet, and only has to name what must NOT trigger (`data/**`, `picks/**` — the collector's own commit directories, which are also the loop guard). ✅ **And the replacement check enumerates the REPO, not the list**, so a file added tomorrow is covered without anyone editing the test — plus it asserts the loop guard, so deleting `data/**` becomes a failing check rather than a runaway.
    ➡️ **THE GENERAL FORM: any allow-list that must be edited when a file is added will eventually be wrong, and a check written against the list rather than against the world will never say so.**

89. 🆕 🔴 **WHEN TWO CHECKS DISAGREE ABOUT THE SAME DATA, ONE OF THEM IS ASKING THE WRONG QUESTION — AND IT IS USUALLY THE PROXY.** `[measured 2026-09-04]`
    🔴 `verify_board` asked whether two records for one matchup sat **closer together than the page's 4-hour matching window**, and treated that as *"the page would guess"*. ⚠️ **IT FIRED ON A CORRECT BOARD**: a Detroit / Cleveland **doubleheader** exactly 4.00 hours apart turned the run red, while `test_board_match.js` passed **28 assertions on that same board, including that pair.** ⛔ **The window is a MAXIMUM DISTANCE, not a resolution limit** — nearest-match still picks the right record.
    🔴 **AND THE PROXY COULD NOT CATCH THE BUG IT WAS WRITTEN FOR.** On 2026-08-26 six records were the **previous night's game** — one per matchup, **no duplicates at all** — and cards showed CHC at −4000 with a 4.5 total. **A proximity rule passes that in silence.** ⛔ A proxy that both cries wolf AND misses the wolf is not a conservative check; it is a broken one.
    ✅ **THE REPLACEMENT USES GROUND TRUTH THAT WAS ALREADY ON DISK:** every carded row stores the `game_id` of the record it was **priced from**, so the page's own matcher is run and its answer checked against that id. **15 rows reconciled on the live board.**
    ➡️ **THE TELL TO LOOK FOR:** a red check and a green check disputing the same file. Do not average them and do not trust the stricter one by default — **find which is measuring the harm and which is measuring a correlate of it.**

90. 🆕 ⛔ **"NOTHING TO CHECK" HAS A DANGEROUS HALF, AND MY FIRST DRAFT SHIPPED THE SAFE READING OF IT.** `[2026-09-04, caught by my own test before it left]`
    🔴 Reconciling a carded row whose priced-from record is **no longer on the board** looked like nothing-to-see, so the first version skipped it. ⛔ **It is only harmless if the page ALSO finds nothing.** If the matcher still returns *another* record for that matchup, **the page shows a price the row was never priced from — which is the 2026-08-26 bug exactly**, arriving through the escape hatch built to avoid false alarms.
    ✅ The row is now matched either way: **matcher returns nothing → fail-closed and reported; matcher returns something → FAIL, naming both ids.**
    ⚠️ **AND TWO MORE OF MY OWN DEFECTS DIED IN THE SAME PASS.** Taking the last file in `picks/` selects `fb-ncaaf-latest.json` — a **football** card — which is skipped on `kind`, so the check reconciled **zero rows and printed PASS**: the shared-directory trap of rule 79 and the vacuous-assertion trap of rule 76 in one line. ⛔ **Zero rows is no longer a pass**, and the one honest zero — everything rolled off — must print its reason.
    ➡️ **A NEW CHECK GETS ITS OWN ADVERSARIAL TEST BEFORE IT SHIPS, NOT AFTER.** All three defects were found by writing `test_board_reconcile.py` against the check rather than by reading it.

91. 🆕 🔴 **A GREEN BUILD OVER A FROZEN PRODUCT IS THE SAME BLIND SPOT AS A FRESH-LOOKING STALE PAGE, POINTING THE OTHER WAY.** `[2026-09-04, and we were in it]`
    🔴 Sam accepted T37, `card_gate` turned the run green, and **every card rebuild was still reverted** — `git checkout -- picks/` runs whether or not the failure is accepted (rule 73). **Gizmo's Picks stopped updating and nothing said so.** ⛔ On 08-28 the PAGE looked fresh and was stale; here the BUILD looked healthy while the board had stopped moving. **Same shape, opposite surface.**
    ⚠️ **THE DOWNGRADE ITSELF IS RIGHT AND MUST SURVIVE.** An alarm firing every fifteen minutes for a settled decision gets ignored, and ignoring red is how the original staleness lasted a whole day — that is why `card_gate` exists at all. ✅ **IT WAS THE UNBOUNDEDNESS THAT WAS WRONG. An accepted failure buys quiet for a DECISION, not permanent silence about a board that has stopped moving.**
    ✅ `CARD_REFUSED_GRACE_MIN = 48h` — two consecutive missed card deadlines, chosen with Sam **before it had ever fired**, with a countdown printed inside the grace so it is visible before it bites. ⛔ **Do not raise it to make a run go green**; that is the move it exists to prevent, and the comment says so at the definition.
    ➡️ **THE GENERAL FORM: every mechanism that legitimately silences an alarm needs a bound, because the reason it was silenced can outlive the person acting on it.** Ask of any suppression: *what makes this start shouting again?*

92. 🆕 ⚠️ **A STATUS FILE REWRITTEN EVERY PASS CAN SAY WHEN SOMETHING LAST HAPPENED, NEVER WHEN IT STARTED.** `[2026-09-04]`
    🔴 The obvious way to bound a refusal is to age `card-verify-failure.txt`. ⛔ **It is rewritten on every converge pass**, so its stamp is always "a few minutes ago" no matter how many days the card has been refused. **A file that reports its own freshness cannot measure a duration.**
    ✅ **THE CARD'S OWN AGE IS THE MEASUREMENT**, and it was already computed: *how long since a card published* is exactly the question. **No new state, no new file, nothing to keep in sync.**
    ➡️ Before adding a timestamp to track a duration, check whether an artifact already answers it by existing.

93. 🆕 🔴 **A SUPPRESSION MECHANISM THAT DOES NOT ALSO UNBLOCK IS WORSE THAN NO MECHANISM — AND THE ALARM YOU BUILD TO WATCH IT IS THE PROOF.** `[2026-09-04]`
    🔴 `card_gate` let Sam accept a failure, which turned the run green **and still reverted the card on every rebuild.** He looked at T37, decided the contradiction is a mean-vs-frequency artifact with both numbers correct, wrote that down — **and the board stopped updating.** ⛔ Acceptance bought a quieter alarm and a dead product.
    ⚠️ **THE TELL WAS SOMETHING I BUILT MYSELF.** Three hours earlier I added `CARD_REFUSED_GRACE_MIN` — a 48-hour bound whose only job is to shout about the frozen state. **A guard whose entire purpose is to watch for a state your own design creates is a guard pointing at a design error.** ➡️ When you find yourself building an alarm for a condition the system produces by rule, fix the rule.
    ✅ **EITHER ACCEPTANCE PUBLISHES, OR ACCEPTANCE SHOULD NOT EXIST.** It now publishes — **and the page states the caveat**, naming the check and Sam's own written reason. ⛔ **That sentence is the entire justification for publishing a card that failed one of its own checks**; a caveated card the reader can see the caveat on is honest, a silently-published failing one is not. The code says: *do not remove this branch without also restoring the revert.*
    ⚠️ **NOTHING ELSE WAS RELAXED.** A failure nobody accepted still reverts the card and still fails the run. **Three states, three different sentences on the page**, and a test that renders both and proves they do not read the same.

94. 🆕 ⛔ **`git checkout -- picks/` REVERTED TWO SPORTS.** `[found 2026-09-04 while fixing rule 93]`
    🔴 The card-revert threw away the **whole `picks/` directory**, so a failing **baseball** check would silently discard a **college** board built in the same pass. ⛔ Nothing reported it, because the football card was never expected to be there in the first place.
    ➡️ **THE THIRD MEMBER OF THE SAME FAMILY** — after `collect_record()` globbing both sports (rule 79) and `verify_board` selecting the last file in `picks/` (rule 90). **`picks/` holds two sports, and every operation on it that is not scoped to one league is a bug waiting for the other league to exist.** Grep the directory name, not the symptom.

95. 🆕 🔴 **A REFRESH CADENCE MUST MATCH THE DAY THE SPORT IS PLAYED, NOT THE DAY THE PIPELINE WAS CONVENIENT TO WRITE.** `[measured 2026-09-05, the first Saturday of the season]`
    🔴 The college **Scores tab showed 76 FBS games with no score at all** — `final=False`, `None-None` — including games that had finished the night before. ⛔ The scores live **inside the schedule file**, and the schedule rode the **Sunday** rebuild. `cfb.py` stated the decision plainly: *"NO NEW CRON -- rides the Sunday rebuild that already runs."*
    ⚠️ **THE COMMENT WAS HONEST AND THE DECISION WAS WRONG.** Riding an existing cron is the right instinct for a yearly back-fill and the wrong one for a number that changes hourly on one day a week. **It would have recurred every Saturday of the season** — and the NFL carried the same gap on a two-day lag, because its schedule rides the Tuesday rebuild.
    ✅ `fb-scores` rebuilds **only the current season's schedule**, free, every two hours on each league's own game days. ⛔ It is **not** live scoring, and the code says so: it refreshes finals and whatever the source has posted.
    ➡️ **THE QUESTION TO ASK OF ANY PIGGY-BACKED BUILDER: how often does the thing it produces CHANGE, and does the host cron run that often?** Anything that changes during a game cannot ride a weekly job.

96. 🆕 ⛔ **A FILE WITH NO TIMESTAMP INSIDE IT IS `MISSING` FOREVER, NOT MERELY OLD.** `[found 2026-09-05 while governing the scores refresher]`
    🔴 The college schedule carried **no stamp at all** — no `pulled_at`, no `built_at` — while its NFL twin carried two, because the NFL *write site* added one and the college one did not. ⛔ `freshness.py` **bans `os.path.getmtime`** (every CI run is a fresh checkout that resets every mtime), so the stamp has to be **in the file**. Without it the contract reads MISSING whatever the real age.
    ✅ **STAMP AT THE BUILDER, NOT AT THE CALL SITE.** `cfb.build_schedule` now stamps what it returns, so the weekly rebuild and the game-day refresh both get it without anyone remembering. ➡️ **A convention enforced at N call sites is a convention that will be missed at the N+1th.**

97. 🆕 🔴 **A SECTION APPENDED AFTER THE FAILURE GATE CANNOT FAIL.** `[2026-09-05]`
    🔴 New checks were appended to `test_fb_freshness.py` **below** its `if fails: sys.exit(1)` block. They printed **🔴 FAIL** and the file still **exited 0**. ⛔ **A test that cannot fail is decoration** — rule 67 in yet another costume, and this time the file did it to itself.
    ✅ The gate is now the last thing in the file **and says so in a comment**: anything added below this line is not checked. ➡️ **When appending to a test, check where its exit lives before writing a line of it.**

98. 🆕 🔴 **A FILTER CHANGES WHICH ROWS YOU SEE. IT MUST NOT CHANGE WHAT A NUMBER MEANS.** `[2026-09-05, building the CFB conference filter]`
    🔴 The obvious implementation of a conference filter on the Trends tab is to filter the rows and then rank them — which makes **`Rk 1` mean a different thing depending on which chips are lit**, and leaves the bar column headed *"Vs. league range"* while it is rescaled to sixteen teams. ⛔ **That is rule 66 in a new costume: one number per fact, and a rank that renumbers under a filter is two facts wearing one label.**
    ✅ **Rank and scale are computed over the WHOLE population; only the display is filtered.** A filtered SEC row keeps its national rank, and the tab says so on the page rather than leaving the reader to notice. ➡️ **Before filtering a view, list every number in it and ask which ones are DEFINED by the population. Those come from the full set, always.**

99. 🆕 ⛔ **A CONTROL WHOSE OPTIONS ARE DERIVED FROM FILTERED DATA IS MOVED BY THE FILTER.** `[2026-09-05]`
    🔴 The Scores tab builds its **week picker** from the weeks present in the game list. The first draft of the conference filter was applied *before* that, so clicking a conference **idle in the current week** would have removed that week from the picker, snapped the reader to a different week, and shown them a slate they never asked for — **silently, and looking exactly like a bug in the schedule.**
    ✅ **The filter is applied AFTER the picker is built.** The week you are on is yours; chips change only which rows inside it you see. ➡️ **Any control populated from data has an ORDER dependency on every other filter. Write down which filter feeds which control before adding one.**

100. 🆕 🔴 **A TEST THAT RE-DERIVES A MAPPING THE PRODUCT ALREADY PINS WILL INVENT A DISAGREEMENT THE PRODUCT SOLVED.** `[2026-09-05]`
    🔴 A checker written to classify college teams by division reported **App State as NOT FBS** — because the odds feed says *"Appalachian State"*, the schedule says *"App State"*, and no prefix of one is a prefix of the other. ⛔ **That is the entire reason `FB_ALIASES` exists in `index.html`**, four lines above where the checker was reading. The test had re-derived a mapping the page pins and then reported its own gap as a finding.
    ✅ The test now **reads the alias table out of the page** rather than retyping the join. ➡️ **Where the product pins a fact BECAUSE two sources disagree, a test must consume that pin, not re-do the join.** ⚠️ **Same day, same class, in the tooling: `walk.py` failed two clean tabs because `'nan' in text` matches *domi**nan**t* and *Bucha**nan*** — a substring where a word was meant, which is ledger 69/72 arriving in the harness instead of the product.

101. 🆕 🔴 **A FILTER APPLIED TO ONE SURFACE AND NOT THE OTHERS IS NOT A FILTER.** `[2026-09-05, making the picks board single-day]`
    🔴 The obvious place to make Gizmo's Picks a one-day board is where the board is assembled. ⛔ But the PARLAYS are built from the same rows, so a board-only filter leaves the parlays pairing a Saturday leg with a Sunday one — and the `below_price_floor` report still counting tomorrow's rows.
    ✅ **The day filter runs BEFORE the price rules, so ONE filter governs the board, the parlays and every count the card reports.** ➡️ **When adding a filter, list every consumer of the thing you are filtering FIRST. Filtering at the last consumer only fixes the last consumer.**
    ⚠️ **AND ONE CONSUMER WAS DELIBERATELY EXCLUDED, WHICH IS THE OTHER HALF OF THE SAME RULE:** the `projections` map stays FULL-SLATE, because the Player Props tab renders every game on the board and day-filtering it would blank tomorrow's rows for no reason a reader could see. **The BOARD is one day; the PROJECTIONS are the slate.** ⛔ An exclusion like that is only safe when it is written down and pinned by a test — otherwise the next reader "tidies" them together.

102. 🆕 🔴 **A POOL BUILT BEFORE A GATE INHERITS WHAT THE GATE REJECTS.** `[found 2026-09-05 while doing rule 101]`
    🔴 `card_fb.py` built its parlays at line 637. The name gate that strips EVERY `confidence` when the player join falls under 60% ran at line 683. ⛔ **So a board that correctly shipped MARKET-only could still ship parlays whose joint probability was multiplied out of the RECORD numbers that same run had just decided it did not trust.** Ledger rule 55 defeated by ORDERING rather than by argument — no line of code was wrong, only its position.
    ✅ Parlays are now built after the gate, the floor, the ceiling and the day filter. ➡️ **A derived artifact must be built from the FINAL pool, never the raw one — and "final" means after every gate that can invalidate a row, not after the ones you happened to remember.** ⚠️ **A test that asserts on behaviour cannot see this: with a healthy join both orderings agree. The test asserts on POSITION IN THE FILE, because the defect WAS a position.**

103. 🆕 ⛔ **"A DAY WITH NO GAMES" AND "NO CARD" MUST NOT SHARE A SENTENCE.** `[2026-09-05]`
    🔴 Sam's rule is that a gameless day shows an EMPTY board and **that is correct**. ⛔ But an empty tab reading *"not published yet"* says the opposite — it reports a working product as a broken one, and it is the same sentence a genuinely missing card would show. **Three emptinesses, three sentences: NO CARD (nothing published), NO BOARD (a card exists, its day has no games), NO FIT (a full board whose prices land in none of Sam's bands).**
    ✅ **The BUILDER writes the sentence and the PAGE prints it.** ⛔ A second copy of "why is this empty" in JavaScript is a second thing to drift — the same reason the page prints `confidence_basis` rather than deciding it.
    ⚠️ **And the empty card must be WRITTEN, not skipped** (rule 86): a builder that returns early leaves the freshness contract reading MISSING and raises an alarm about a correct result.

104. 🆕 ⚠️ **A NEW FIELD ON AN ARTIFACT HAS A WINDOW WHERE THE OLD ARTIFACT IS STILL LIVE.** `[2026-09-05]`
    🔴 Between a page commit landing and the next builder run, the page reads a file written by the OLD builder — one with none of the new fields in it. ⛔ **A page that breaks in that window breaks in production**, and it is invisible to every test that runs against a freshly-built artifact.
    ✅ **So the browser check drives the OLD SHAPE ON PURPOSE**: the card with `single_day` and friends deleted, asserting the tab still renders, throws nothing, and leaks no `undefined`. ➡️ **Whenever a builder gains a field the page reads, test the page against the artifact WITHOUT it.**

105. 🆕 🔴 **A FEATURE PORTED BETWEEN SPORTS KEEPS ITS SHAPE AND LOSES ITS REASON. MEASURE THE REASON.** `[2026-09-05, porting Top Plays from MLB to football]`
    🔴 MLB's Top 10 is a **genuinely different list** from its board: a WIDER pool (every alt ladder rung, which never reaches the board) and a NARROWER gate (nothing priced worse than −400). ⛔ **Measured on football, NEITHER MECHANISM EXISTS:** the college props feed carries **no `_alternate` market at all**, and a −400 payable gate drops **1 row of 384**, because football props price near even — the shortest price on the whole board is **−160**. A payable, deduped top-20 shares **18 of its 20 players** with the board's first 20 rows.
    ✅ **So it shipped as a HIGHLIGHTS list and the card SAYS SO, with the measured overlap in the sentence the page prints.** ⛔ The failure avoided is not a broken feature — it is a list that *looks* like a second opinion, gets read as corroboration, and is the same twenty rows again. ➡️ **Before porting a selection rule to a second sport, measure whether each of its gates still BITES there. A gate that drops nothing is not a conservative gate, it is a decorative one.**
    ⚠️ **What it does add was measured too, and it is the reason it still earns its place:** the 50-row board carries only **39 distinct players**, so one row per player is a real difference. **A top-20 that repeats a player is a top-13 wearing a bigger number.**

106. 🆕 ⛔ **"MOST LIKELY TO HIT" OVER ROWS WITH NO RECORD IS "RANKED BY WHICH BET PAYS WORST".** `[2026-09-05]`
    🔴 When the player-name join fails its gate, the football board correctly ships MARKET-only and **no row carries a rate**. A top-plays list built from those rows would have to rank by price — and among rows with no evidence, the shortest price is simply the one the book is most confident about. **Presenting that under a "most likely to hit" heading is a market number wearing a Gizmo's claim: ledger rule 55 through the back door.**
    ✅ **The correct output is an EMPTY list with a stated reason**, and the reason distinguishes it from an empty BOARD — *"not because the board is empty; because no row on it carries a record."* ➡️ **When a ranking's key is unavailable, the answer is no ranking. Never the next-best key.**

107. 🆕 🔴 **TWO FEEDS FOR THE SAME SPORT CAN DATE A GAME IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS.** `[measured 2026-09-06, building the football grader]`
    🔴 `[measured]` the COLLEGE player log dates a kickoff by the feed's own **UTC** date (17 of 17 games), so a **Thursday 9pm ET game is logged as FRIDAY**; the NFL log uses its schedule's **naive Eastern** date (285 of 285). ⛔ The card, meanwhile, is dated by its **ET slate** under rule 60. **An exact-date join would have silently dropped every night game — about 30% of a college slate, and not a random 30%: the marquee ones.**
    ✅ **THE JOIN IS A ±1 DAY WINDOW, AND ITS SAFETY IS MEASURED RATHER THAN HOPED:** across both leagues' whole logs, the most games any single player has inside a three-day window is **1**. Football is weekly, so the window cannot pick the wrong game. ➡️ **When two feeds must agree on a timestamp, join on something the timestamp cannot break — or on a window wide enough to absorb the disagreement and narrow enough that the domain forbids ambiguity.** ⚠️ Third instance of rule 85.

108. 🆕 ⛔ **A ROW IN A LOG IS NOT PROOF THAT ANYTHING HAPPENED.** `[measured 2026-09-06]`
    🔴 `[measured on 19,400 rows]` **13,241 of the NFL log's rows have `snaps == 0`** — nflverse writes a row for a rostered player who never took the field, and every stat in it is zero. ⛔ **Grading those hands a WIN to every UNDER on a man who did not play**, and the under hit rate becomes nonsense. **At a book that is a VOID.**
    ✅ Recorded as a void and excluded from every percentage. ➡️ **Before treating a row as an observation, ask what its EXISTENCE means in that file.** ⚠️ This is `card_fb.py`'s `SNAP_FLOOR` finding — a WR3 playing 16% of snaps counted as evidence about a WR1's line — arriving on the GRADING side. **The same defect will come back once per surface until each one is checked.**

109. 🆕 🔴 **AN EXCLUSION WITH A DIRECTION MUST PUBLISH THE DIRECTION.** `[2026-09-06]`
    🔴 In college a player who **played and recorded nothing** has no log row at all — **identical, in the file, to one who did not dress**. Those two cannot be told apart, so those picks cannot be graded. ⛔ **But the rows that go missing are not a random sample: a player who did nothing is exactly the player an OVER loses on, so dropping them makes the OVER rate read HIGH.**
    ✅ **The count AND the direction are published on the file and printed on the tab.** ⛔ It is NOT corrected, because correcting it means inventing an outcome. ➡️ **Any denominator you cannot complete: say how many are missing, and say which way their absence pushes the number. A record that quietly drops what it cannot settle reads better than the board did.**

110. 🆕 ⛔ **A DIFF OF FUNCTION NAMES CANNOT SEE A DELETED `const`.** `[2026-09-06, and it cost a broken page]`
    🔴 Replacing the Track Record renderer I cut **6,345 characters where the function was 3,227**, taking `FBTEAMS`, `NFL_LOGOS` and `nflDirectory` with it. The page died with `FBTEAMS is not defined`. ⛔ **My check said it was clean** — I diffed the list of `function` declarations, and every function WAS still there. **The casualties were `const`s.**
    ✅ Rebuilt from the pristine file using **brace matching** to find the function's true end, then re-diffed **every** top-level declaration — `function`, `const`, `let`, `var`, `class`: 171 before, 175 after, **0 removed**. ➡️ **Never delete a span by eye-estimated length; find its end mechanically. And a completeness check must enumerate EVERY kind of thing that can go missing** — rule 88 in a new costume, this time in my own tooling.

111. 🆕 🔴 **AN UPLOAD INSTRUCTION WITH THREE DESTINATIONS HAS ONE OUTCOME: EVERYTHING LANDS IN THE FIRST ONE.** `[2026-09-06, two red runs]`
    🔴 The grader drop asked Sam for the repo root, `data/ncaaf/latest/` **and** `.github/workflows/` — and shipped `collect.yml` as a LOOSE FILE beside zips that all went to the root. ⛔ **Three files landed in the root**: the workflow, and both data files. `.github` is hidden on Windows, which is **the same trap this project hit the day before**.
    ✅ **THE FIX WAS NOT BETTER INSTRUCTIONS, IT WAS A DESIGN THAT NEEDED ONE DESTINATION.** `fb-record` is chained to `card-fb` inside `collect.py` instead of getting its own crons — and that is a **better** cadence anyway: the crons were weekly, `card-fb` runs daily in both leagues. ➡️ **When a drop needs a second destination, first ask whether the design can be changed so it does not.** ⚠️ **And never ship a file whose correct home is a hidden folder alongside files bound for the root.**

112. 🆕 ⛔ **A DEADLINE IS SATISFIED BY A BUILD *AT OR AFTER* IT. ONE JUST BEFORE IT PROVES NOTHING.** `[found 2026-09-06 by looking, after two red runs]`
    🔴 Three football deadlines were set to times **no cron could ever meet**, each reddening the gate on a fixed weekly schedule: `ncaaf scores` Sat 20:00 ET against a Sat 21:40 build (**100 min, every Saturday**); `nfl scores` Mon 09:00 against Mon 21:45 (**765 min, every Monday**); `nfl card` Tue 12:00 against Wed 07:34 (**1,174 min, every Tuesday**). ⛔ **The third had never fired only because the contract drops `card-fb` until a props board exists — it was armed for the day NFL props land.**
    ✅ `test_fb_freshness.py` now converts every cron to **minutes-of-week in ET** and refuses any deadline more than 20 minutes from a build. **Proven to bite:** restoring the old Sat 8pm value fails it with *"100 min red"*. ➡️ **Writing a deadline is choosing a moment a specific cron must have already passed. Compute the next fire; do not eyeball it.** ⚠️ **A naive same-day check would have PASSED all three** — the grading one needs the ordered form: log rebuild → builder → deadline.

113. 🆕 🔴 **A ROUNDING CHECK MUST COMPARE AGAINST THE EXACT VALUE, NEVER AGAINST ITS OWN RE-ROUNDING.** `[2026-09-06]`
    🔴 `verify_card`'s *"joint = product of the two blends"* failed on a live card. Blends **75.0 and 66.2 multiply to EXACTLY 49.65**; `card.py` builds joint from FRACTIONS and published **49.7**, while the check multiplied two already-rounded PERCENTAGES and `round(49.65, 1)` is **49.6**. **Both are correct roundings of the same number.** ⛔ Comparing TWO ROUNDED NUMBERS lets a one-step disagreement fail a correct card.
    ✅ Compare the published value to the **exact** product with half a unit in the last place. ⛔ **That is STRICTER, not looser** — a genuinely wrong joint can no longer hide behind the verifier's own rounding (49.8 against 49.65 still fails at 0.150). ⚠️ 🔴 **AND IT WAS THE SECOND COPY OF THE DEFECT IN THE SAME FILE:** the multiplier bound eight lines above carries a comment describing the identical trap, fixed days earlier. ➡️ **When you fix a rounding bound, grep the file for its siblings in the same commit.**

114. 🆕 🔴 **A RULE BROKEN TWICE BY THE PERSON WHO WROTE IT IS A SHAPE PROBLEM, NOT A DISCIPLINE PROBLEM.** `[2026-09-06]`
    🔴 Rule 97 — *a section appended after the failure gate cannot fail* — was broken **twice in one day, hours apart**, by me, hours after I wrote it. ⛔ **Writing it down a third time would have been the same move that already failed twice.**
    ✅ **`tcheck.py`: the gate is an `atexit` hook.** There is no "after" in a file whose gate runs at interpreter shutdown — a check written at the bottom runs BEFORE it. **Rule 97 becomes unwritable rather than forbidden.** ⚠️ **`os._exit` after an explicit flush, NOT `sys.exit`:** `sys.exit` inside an atexit hook raises `SystemExit`, which Python reports and then **ignores for the process status** — printing a failure and exiting 0, which is the exact defect being fixed. ➡️ **When a rule needs restating, ask what shape would make it unstateable instead.**

115. 🆕 ⛔ **AN ARGUMENT ORDER THAT CAN BE SWAPPED WILL BE, AND A STRING CONDITION IS ALWAYS TRUE.** `[measured 2026-09-06 across the whole suite]`
    🔴 The suite carried **four `ck` signatures** — 11 files took the CONDITION first, 7 the NAME first. ⛔ Calling a condition-first `ck` the other way round passes **the label as the condition**, and a non-empty string is truthy. **Measured on all eleven: 11 of 11 printed a tick, recorded no failure, and never evaluated the real condition.** A check that cannot fail — rule 67 in its purest form, latent in eleven files.
    ⚠️ It had already cost a live `TypeError` in `test_cfb.py`. **That was the LOUD version of the same mistake, and the loud version is the lucky one** — the quiet version is a green suite that checks nothing.
    ✅ **`ck` now reads the argument TYPES, not the positions:** exactly one must be a `str`, so both orders are correct and neither can be misread; two strings or no string RAISES. ➡️ **A signature whose two arguments are distinguishable only by position is a defect waiting for a habit. Make the types carry the meaning.**

116. 🆕 ⛔ **A FILE THAT ASSERTS NOTHING MUST NOT PASS.** `[2026-09-06]`
    🔴 A test that silently stops checking — its helper renamed, its list no longer read, its body short-circuited — is **indistinguishable in CI from one that was deleted**, and both go green. ⚠️ This is not hypothetical: migrating the suite, nine files stopped routing through the harness and **every one of them still printed its own cheerful summary line.**
    ✅ **Zero recorded checks is now a failure**, and it is what caught all nine. ➡️ **Green must mean "I checked and it was fine", never "I had nothing to say".**

117. 🆕 ⚠️ **A HELPER THAT REACHES INTO ANOTHER FUNCTION'S INTERNALS BREAKS WITHOUT SAYING SO.** `[2026-09-06]`
    🔴 `test_fb_freshness.py`'s `eq()` called `ck()` and then reached into `ck`'s own `fails` list to **rewrite the last entry** with a better message. Harmless while they shared a file — **dead the instant the gate moved into a module**, and dead SILENTLY: the check still passed and failed correctly, only the message stopped being enriched.
    ✅ Rewritten to pass its detail through the public argument. ➡️ **When two functions share a mutable list, one of them is depending on the other's implementation. Pass the value; do not patch the record afterwards.**

118. 🆕 🔴 **A LINE THAT ONLY RAN ON SUCCESS BECOMES A LIE THE MOMENT YOU MOVE THE GATE ABOVE IT.** `[2026-09-06, introduced by my own migration]`
    🔴 Twenty-five test files ended with `print("✅ all X passed")`. In the old shape that line sat **after** `sys.exit(1)`, so it only ever ran on success. ⛔ **Moving the gate into `tcheck` made every one of them UNCONDITIONAL** — and `test_top_plays.py` was caught printing *"✅ all top-plays tests passed"* on a run with **two failures** in it. **A green line on a red run: the exact defect the harness exists to kill, reintroduced by the migration that was killing it.**
    ✅ All 25 stripped; the harness owns the verdict, and `test_harness.py` refuses any test file that prints its own success banner. ⚠️ The check requires the tick to be the **first character of the string** — one file prints an indented `"   ✅ …"` inside an explanatory block, which is prose about a decision, not a verdict, and matching it would be a check firing on the wrong thing. ➡️ **When you remove a guard, list everything whose correctness DEPENDED on running after it. A conditional you delete does not delete the things it was conditioning.**

119. 🆕 ⛔ **WORK WITH NO CONSUMER IS NOT WORK. MEASURE THE CONSUMERS BEFORE BUILDING.** `[2026-09-06, `routes-2023.json.gz`]`
    🔴 A missing back-fill sat on the to-do for days. Before building it I counted what needed it: **no cron runs the routes mode, no freshness row governs it, no file in the product reads `routes-*.json.gz`**, and the only consumer is `research/t50_fit.py` — reading 2025 only, for **T50, a pre-registered model test that LOST at −0.0036 against a +0.0050 bar**. ✅ **Struck rather than built.**
    🔴 **AND THE "FAILURE" WAS THE COLLECTOR BEING RIGHT.** nflverse publishes both `pbp_participation_2023.csv` and `pbp_participation_old_2023.csv`, and the collector **refuses to guess between them**. ⛔ **I could not verify which is correct — the GitHub API is blocked from the build sandbox — SO I DID NOT PIN ONE.** Guessing that `_2023.csv` is *obviously* the right one is precisely what the collector already declines to do, and writing that inference in as a fact would be worse than the missing file. ➡️ **An item on a list is not a mandate. Ask who reads the output before you produce it — and if the "bug" is a component correctly refusing to guess, the fix is evidence, not a pin.**

120. 🆕 🔴🔴 **NEVER RESTATE A DOC'S CLAIM THAT SOMETHING *EXISTS* WITHOUT GREPPING FOR IT.** `[2026-09-06 — it cost Sam a failed dispatch]`
    🔴 `claude/football-todo.md` said from 2026-09-04 that *"the `live-probe` mode is built and waiting on one free run from Sam."* **IT HAD NEVER EXISTED.** The string `live-probe` appeared **exactly once in the entire repository — inside a COMMENT** — and I repeated the claim as fact in **five separate messages**, in three delivery docs, and in two "still owed" lists. ⛔ **Sam typed it into the dispatch form and the run failed, because there was nothing to dispatch.**
    ⚠️ **This is rules 25–27 in their purest form**, and the earlier instances were about the WORLD (what a book posts). **This one was about OUR OWN REPOSITORY** — the cheapest possible thing to verify, and the one I never checked. ➡️ **A claim of the form "X is built" is a `grep`, not a memory. One command, every time, before it reaches an instruction.**
    ✅ **The structural fix, not just the rule:** `test_cron_wiring.py` now asserts **every mode the dispatch form offers exists in `collect.py`** — proven to bite on a planted `ghost-mode`. **Nothing in this project had ever checked that a mode name was real.**
    ⚠️ **And two more errors rode along in the same instruction**, both from writing from memory: the input is named `mode` (singular) and I wrote *"modes"*; and I told him to add `converge-off`, which the runner would split on whitespace and try to run as its own mode.

121. 🆕 ⛔ **A PARSER BUILT ON AN UNVERIFIABLE SCHEMA MUST RETURN THE SHAPE IT FOUND, NOT A STACK TRACE.** `[2026-09-06]`
    🔴 ESPN is blocked from the build sandbox (the egress proxy 403s the CONNECT), so **every field name in `liveprobe.py` is a hypothesis I cannot test.** ⛔ Shipping a guess that dies on contact would cost a whole round trip and teach nothing.
    ✅ **So the probe reports the payload's ACTUAL shape before parsing it** — top-level keys, event/competition/status/competitor keys — and lists **each field it needs with present-or-missing beside it**; one odd event is caught and counted rather than fatal. **Tested against three payloads: one shaped as expected, one with EVERY FIELD RENAMED, and one unreachable.** ➡️ **When you cannot verify a remote schema, make the failure mode INFORMATIVE. "The run failed" and "here is exactly what to read instead" cost the same to write.**
122. 🆕 🔴 **"100% MATCHED" IS A DIRECTION. THE OTHER DIRECTION IS USUALLY THE PRODUCT QUESTION.** `[2026-09-06, the live probe's first real run]`
    ✅ The probe reported **"25 of 25 ESPN events matched our schedule — 100%"**, and it was true. ⛔ **Our schedule held 206 college games that day.** The number measured *how many of THEIRS are OURS*; the feature depends on *how many of OURS are THEIRS*, and those two share a numerator and nothing else.
    🔴 **A live tab built on that number would have gone live for 25 games and left 181 looking unstarted** — worse than not being live at all, because it looks like data rather than absence. This is rule **88** (a check enumerating what is PRESENT cannot see what is ABSENT) reappearing as a *percentage*, which is where it is hardest to see: the denominator was the thing that was wrong, and the figure was flawless.
    ➡️ **WHEN A JOIN REPORTS A PERCENTAGE, WRITE DOWN WHICH SET IS THE DENOMINATOR, AND THEN ASK WHETHER THE PRODUCT DEPENDS ON THE OTHER ONE.** ✅ The probe now reports both, per ET slate day, and names the games the feed did not send.

123. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A TEST THAT WRITES THE PRODUCT MAKES ITS FRESHNESS ROW PERMANENTLY GREEN.** `[2026-09-06 — found by hashing a fresh clone before and after the suite, and it was LIVE]`
    🔴 `test_record_fb.py` called `record_fb.main()` **in the repo root**, and `test_p4.py` drove the Power-4 gate into its fail-closed branch, which writes a diagnostic file. ⛔ **The tests run in the SAME CI job that later runs `git add data/ picks/`** — so `data/ncaaf/latest/record.json` was being rebuilt and **committed by every collect run of every league**. Measured on live main: `built_at 04:16:46Z`, inside a `collect[mlb]` commit.
    ⚠️ **THE CONTENT WAS FINE, WHICH IS WHY IT SURVIVED.** The damage is the MONITOR: the freshness contract watches that file's age **to prove the grader ran**, and a test rewriting it hourly makes that row green *whatever happens to the grader*. **A check that can no longer fail** — rule 67 wearing an artifact for a costume, and the same family as a green banner on a red run.
    ✅ **THE SHAPE FIX: `tcheck.py` fails any test that leaves a file under `data/` or `picks/` changed** — snapshot at import, compared in the atexit gate. Every test file already imports it, so nothing has to remember. **Proven to bite** on a planted one-line write. ➡️ **A test may read the product. It may never write it — and "the output was correct anyway" is not a defence, because correctness is not what the freshness row was measuring.**

124. 🆕 ⚠️ **A SANDBOX THAT CHANGES WHICH BRANCH RUNS IS NOT A SANDBOX, IT IS A DIFFERENT TEST.** `[2026-09-06, while fixing 123]`
    🔴 Moving `test_p4`'s fail-closed case into a bare temp directory made it pass **for the wrong reason**: `filter_power4` re-reads the CFBD player files from a RELATIVE path, so an empty cwd sent it down *"no players file has 40+ Power 4 teams"* instead of *"these names do not resolve"*. **Same green tick, different assertion.**
    ✅ Fixed by copying the data into the sandbox, and by asserting the diagnostic file actually appears there — so the isolation cannot silently delete the behaviour it was isolating. ➡️ **After isolating a test, check WHICH PATH it now takes. Relocation is a change to the inputs.**

125. 🆕 🔴🔴 **IF IT TOUCHES THE SITE, IT IS A CRON. A MANUAL DISPATCH IS A DESIGN FAILURE, NOT A STEP.** `[Sam, 2026-09-06 — after I sent him to hand-run the same probe TWICE]`
    🔴 **SAM, IN HIS OWN WORDS:** *"i shouldnt have to do manual runs this should all be reccuring if its regrding anything related to updating data in the website, the only manual uploads i should be doing is setting up recurring actions as well as anything else i need to upload for the website to run on its own."*
    ⛔ **The live probe was delivered twice as a click-by-click dispatch.** The first one failed because the mode did not exist (rule 120); the second was a correct instruction to do a thing that should never have needed a person. **Both were the same error: I designed a step for a human where a schedule belonged.**
    ✅ **THE FIX IS SEVEN CRONS TIMED TO WHEN GAMES ARE ACTUALLY ON** — a probe that fires when nothing is in progress correctly refuses to answer, and then answers nothing forever, so *when* is part of the design and not a detail. ➡️ **Sam's manual surface is exactly two things: uploading files, and whatever the site needs to run itself. Anything else you were about to ask him to click is a cron you have not written yet.**

126. 🆕 ⚠️ **ASK WHICH LAYER THE FEATURE LIVES IN BEFORE BUILDING THE PIPELINE FOR IT.** `[2026-09-06]`
    🔴 A live Scores tab has **two completely different builds**, and the thing that chooses between them is one response header. If ESPN sends `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, **the browser polls it directly** — no cron, no stored file, no commit, exactly how the MLB tab already works off `statsapi.mlb.com`, and as live as the feed. If it does not, the score must be collected server-side and **GitHub's scheduler becomes the ceiling on how live "live" can be** — a scheduler that drops runs and cannot go below five minutes.
    ⛔ **Building the server-side pipeline first would have been weeks of work to reach a worse product than a `fetch()` in the page.** ✅ The probe now records the CORS and cache headers on every run, and — because it measures from a SERVER request, which sends no `Origin` — **a missing header is reported as inconclusive rather than as a no.** ➡️ **When a feature could live in the client or the pipeline, spend one measurement deciding, before spending a build.**

127. 🆕 🔴 **EVERYTHING WE DO FOR ONE LEAGUE WE DO FOR BOTH — AND THAT INCLUDES THE RUN ITSELF, NOT JUST THE FEATURE.** `[Sam, 2026-09-06]`
    🔴 **HIS WORDS:** *"if any of these runs can be done for nfl and cfb its expected we do that so we can kill two birds with one stone."* ⛔ Every football cron routed exactly ONE league, so a dropped run cost that league a landing chance and bought the other nothing.
    ✅ **A routing arm can now name two** (`LEAGUE="ncaaf nfl"`), and the converge loop runs every mode for each in turn. **21 of 32 arms** serve both: `card-fb`, `fb-scores`, `news`, `live-probe`.
    ⛔ **ONLY THE FREE ONES.** `props-player` and `gamelines` keep the per-league times Sam set — doubling those would change his schedule and his spend, which is a different decision than the one he made. ✅ **MEASURED, NOT ASSERTED: `budget.py` reports the identical weekly and monthly totals before and after**, and `test_multileague.py` FAILS if a paid mode is ever routed to two leagues. ➡️ **When the answer is "do it for both", check whether the SCHEDULE can do it for both — and price the answer before shipping it.**

128. 🆕 ⛔ **A CHANGE TO A BASH BLOCK MUST BE EXECUTED, NOT READ — AND A SUBSTRING CHECK ON BASH IS THE FALSE-PASS TWIN OF RULE 69.** `[2026-09-06]`
    🔴 Serving two leagues meant editing the CONVERGE LOOP: the block that spends credits, commits to the repo and gates the run. **A substring check would have passed for every wrong version of it I wrote on the way** — and I wrote several.
    ✅ **So the test PULLS THE STEP'S SCRIPT OUT OF THE WORKFLOW, puts a fake `python` and `git` on PATH, RUNS IT, and asserts on what the stubs were asked to do:** a one-league run makes the exact calls it always made, a two-league run runs every mode for both with the right `LEAGUE` exported, a failing league does not skip the next, freshness is graded once per league, and MLB's verifiers stay unreachable from football.
    ⚠️ **IT DOES NOT IMPORT PyYAML.** The runner installs a bare Python and nothing in this repo has ever needed a third-party package — **a test that cannot import fails the whole suite, and on a push the suite BLOCKS.** The hand-written extractor is cross-checked against PyYAML *wherever PyYAML happens to exist* and agrees line for line. ➡️ **And the first version of one check measured the WRONG UNIT:** it asserted the step exits non-zero on failure, when the step deliberately exits 0 and writes `rc=1` for a later step to fail on. **Assert on the thing that actually carries the verdict.**

129. 🆕 ⛔ **A DELIVERY THAT FORCES TWO COMMITS TO LAND IN ONE INSTANT GUARANTEES A RED RUN. MAKE THE EARLIER STATE LEGAL.** `[2026-09-06]`
    🔴 The routing change lives in `.github/workflows/`, the parser and tests live in the repo ROOT, and they ship as two commits because an upload with two destinations has one outcome (rule 111). **Measured against a fresh clone: root-then-workflow is green then green; workflow-then-root is THREE RED then green**, because main's tests cannot parse a two-league arm.
    ✅ **THE FIX IS NOT A WARNING IN THE DOC.** The new test keys on a fact about the file under test — once the script contains `$LEAGUES`, every multi-league check is hard — and **says loudly that it skipped them** while it does not. ⛔ A silent skip would be rule 116 wearing a delivery excuse. ➡️ **Measure both orderings, ship the one that is green at every step, and make the intermediate state a state the tests understand rather than one they fail.**

130. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A RENDERER NOBODY CALLS IS A DECOY THAT LOOKS EXACTLY LIKE THE THING YOU EDITED.** `[2026-09-06, building the live Scores tab]`
    🔴 `fbScores` carried `const row = g => …`, a complete `<tr>` builder, **defined and never called** — the tab has rendered through `fbGameCard` since the cards landed, because each card carries a click handler and an interpolated string cannot. ⛔ **The live-score wiring went into the dead one**, and the browser test showed a tab with no live game on it **while the join was working perfectly**. The code was right and the surface was untouched.
    ➡️ **Before editing a renderer, prove it renders: grep for a CALL, not a definition.** ✅ And the browser test is what found it — reading the source would have shown a perfectly sensible function.

131. 🆕 🔴🔴 **NEVER DELETE A SPAN BY AN UNBOUNDED SEARCH. THE SECOND TIME, IT TOOK A WHOLE TAB.** `[2026-09-06 — rule 110, again, five days later]`
    🔴 Deleting that dead builder searched for `const row` **from the start of the file**, matched a copy inside **`fbOdds`**, and removed **everything between them**. ⛔ **The entire Odds tab was gone and the JavaScript still parsed.** It surfaced only because a *different* test could no longer find `fbOdds`.
    ```
    declarations on main   175
    after the bad delete   184     🔴 REMOVED: ['fbOdds']
    after the rebuild      185     ✅ REMOVED: []
    ```
    ✅ **Rebuilt from the pristine file**, every edit re-applied through an anchor **asserted unique before use** (`assert hay.count(needle) == 1`), and the deletion redone with `start < i < j < end` **bounded inside the function that owns it**. ➡️ **An anchor is not a location. Assert it is unique, bound it to the function you mean, and diff EVERY top-level declaration afterwards — the check is what caught this, twice now.**

132. 🆕 ⛔ **A SENTENCE THAT STATES A CAPABILITY MUST BE COMPUTED, NOT WRITTEN.** `[2026-09-06]`
    🔴 The football Scores tab said, in fixed prose: *"Not a live scoreboard. Neither football source carries in-game state."* **True the day it was written. False the day ESPN was wired in** — and nothing about it would ever have gone red.
    ✅ **The note is now derived from what happened on that render** — whether the feed answered, how many of the shown games it covered, how many are in progress right now — so it cannot outlive its own measurement. ➡️ **Any sentence on the page that makes a claim about what the product CAN DO is a fact with an expiry date. Derive it from the render, or it becomes a lie on a Tuesday.**

133. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A TEST MAY ONLY IMPORT WHAT THE RUNNER HAS — AND WRITING THAT RULE DOWN DID NOT STOP ME BREAKING IT IN THE NEXT FILE.** `[2026-09-06, it turned the collector red]`
    🔴 `test_live_scores.py` imported **Playwright at the top of the file**. The GitHub runner installs a **bare Python** — `actions/setup-python@v5`, no pip step — so the import failed on every run and the file's own handler recorded a deliberate failure. ⛔ **On a PUSH the test step BLOCKS, so that run collected nothing either.**
    ⚠️ **AND `test_multileague.py`, WRITTEN AN HOUR EARLIER, REFUSES TO IMPORT PyYAML FOR EXACTLY THIS REASON AND SAYS SO IN ITS OWN DOCSTRING.** Knowing the rule, having just written the rule, and still breaking it one file later is the definition of a SHAPE problem (rule 114).
    ✅ **THE FIX IS A CHECK, NOT A RESOLUTION:** `test_harness.py` fails any test file with a top-level import that is neither stdlib nor a file in this repo — **proven to bite on a planted `import numpy`.** ⚠️ An optional import inside `try: … except ImportError:` is fine and is the correct pattern.
    ✅ **AND THE TEST GOT BIGGER, NOT SMALLER.** Deleting the browser test would have thrown away the thing that caught the live score being wired into a dead renderer. It is now **eleven checks that need no browser, which run everywhere**, plus twenty-one that drive one where it exists — **with a LOUD skip**, because a silent skip is a test that quietly stopped asserting (rule 116). ➡️ **When portability forces a test to shrink, split it instead: the half that can always run should assert the thing that would actually break.**

134. 🆕 ⛔ **THE PAGE MAY ONLY ASSERT WHAT ITS DATA CARRIES — "NO SCORE YET" IS NOT "HAS NOT KICKED OFF".** `[2026-09-06, Sam's screenshot]`
    🔴 The game modal said *"This game has not kicked off"* about UCLA @ California **three and a half hours after kickoff.** ⛔ The stored file knows exactly one thing — that no score is recorded — and the sentence turned that into a claim about the game's state. ✅ Derived from the kickoff time now. **Rule 132's sibling: 132 is about a capability the page claims, this is about a FACT it claims.**

135. 🆕 ⛔ **A TIMER MUST NEVER MOVE THE PAGE UNDER SOMEONE'S HANDS — AND THE FIX ALREADY EXISTED ON THE OTHER HALF OF THE PRODUCT.** `[Sam, 2026-09-06: "its annoying"]`
    🔴 MLB's `tick()` has been single-flight, modal-aware, change-gated and scroll-preserving **since August**. Football re-rendered raw — from the live poll I added AND from the season, week, division and conference controls, every one of which rebuilds `#fbview`.
    ✅ **One wrapper now serves all six paths**, and the poll skips the redraw entirely when the feed has not moved. **Measured: scrolled to 700px, forced a full re-render, ended at 711px.** ➡️ **Before building a behaviour for one league, check whether the other league already solved it — parity is a source of FIXES, not just a chore.**

136. 🆕 ⚠️ **A TYPOGRAPHIC APOSTROPHE IS NOT AN APOSTROPHE, AND A SAFETY FLOOR HAS EXCEPTIONS.** `[2026-09-06, news art]`
    🔴 The crest matcher missed *"Michigan’s Heisman Sleeper"* because its possessive test knew only the ASCII `'` and the headline carried **U+2019**. **Every publisher's CMS emits curly quotes**, so this was not an edge case — it was a whole class of headline. 🔴 And its "nothing under five characters" floor — correct for lowercase matching — silently excluded **LSU, TCU, BYU, UCF, SMU**.
    ✅ Normalise the quote; match short ALL-CAPS keys **case-sensitively on word boundaries, in a pass that runs LAST** so a full school name always wins. **Measured: college rows with no art fell from 11 to 7**, and the remaining seven name no school at all. ➡️ **When a matcher misses, read the strings it missed. Both bugs were visible in four seconds of looking at them.**

137. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A CONTRACT THAT DOES NOT DESCRIBE THE DAYS THE GAMES ARE PLAYED CANNOT NOTICE THAT THE PRODUCT IS STALE.** `[Sam, 2026-09-06 1:30pm: "still has lines from yesterdays games... games starting in a few hours"]`
    🔴 College `props` and `card` deadlines were **Tue–Sat**. On a **Sunday** with a real slate, the last deadline that existed was **Saturday 3pm**, the Saturday pull had satisfied it, and so `plan()` returned an **EMPTY LIST**. ⛔ **Every artifact read `stale=False`, every verifier went green, `verify_freshness` passed for all three leagues, and the board was 22 hours old with the slate hours away.** **Nothing was broken. That is what made it invisible.**
    ⛔ **It was NOT the credit cap** — 246 of 600 spent, room all day — and checking that first is what stopped a wrong fix. ⚠️ **Freshness is defined ENTIRELY by the deadline list**, so a missing day is not a late artifact (rule **86**): being out of date is not a deadline the system holds. ➡️ **A cadence must be derived from when the SPORT plays, never from when it was convenient to schedule. Ask of every contract: name a day this product is stale and the check stays green.**

138. 🆕 ⛔ **READING AN INDEX WITHOUT ASKING WHETHER IT EXISTS IS THE SAME SHAPE AS ASSUMING A SCHEMA.** `[2026-09-06, `test_record_fb.py` IndexError]`
    🔴 A deadline is `(hh, mm)` when daily and `(hh, mm, {days})` when weekly. The check read `deadline[2]` unconditionally and **crashed the moment college trends became daily** — my own change, three files away. ✅ A `_days(d)` helper that returns *all days* for a 2-tuple.
    ✅ **And it then found a REAL fault in that same change:** a daily rebuild put the Tuesday **9am grading** deadline *before* its own **noon input**. ⛔ **The rule was RESTATED CORRECTLY RATHER THAN LOOSENED** — with a daily rebuild the ordering constraint cannot be violated by more than 24 hours and is vacuous; **the weekly case is enforced exactly as before.** ➡️ **When your own check fails on your own change, the first question is whether the check is right — here it was wrong about tuples and right about ordering, in the same run.**

139. 🆕 ⚠️ **A CHECK THAT MERGES TWO DIFFERENT FACTS INTO ONE FAILURE IS EITHER RED EVERY WEEK OR DELETED.** `[2026-09-06, `test_conf_filter.py` red on main]`
    🔴 It failed because a board name the stored schedule classes **FCS** resolves inside CFBD's **FBS directory**. The school is **SAN JOSE STATE**, and the disagreement is real: **a promotion the schedule feed has not caught up with**, with the filter correctly preferring the newer source.
    ✅ The check now separates a **reclassification** — reported, **with the school NAMED**, so it can be read — from a genuine **mis-resolution**, which still fails. ⛔ Merged, the only two outcomes available were *red every week of the season* or *no check at all*. ➡️ **When a true check fires on a legitimate condition, split the condition; do not weaken the assertion, and never name the failure without naming the thing that caused it.**

140. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A FILE REBUILT WEEKLY CANNOT ANSWER A QUESTION ASKED DAILY — AND THE COVERAGE MUST BE MEASURED AGAINST WHAT THE READER CLICKS.** `[Sam, 2026-09-06: "not all games in the scores tab have a full box score"]`
    🔴 The box score was built from `players-<season>.json.gz` because that file was already there and already trusted. **It holds 17 of the 202 Division I games played — and ZERO of Saturday 2026-09-05's 103**, because the college logs cover the FBS teams this product tracks and rebuild WEEKLY. ⛔ **On any day before the rebuild, the biggest slate of the week has no box score at all.** The panel was not broken; **it was asking a file that does not know.**
    ⚠️ The join HAD been measured before it was built — *"17 of 17 college game_ids match a schedule id"* — and that number is true and was the wrong direction (rule **122** again): 17 of 17 of the logs matched, while 185 of 202 of the GAMES had no log. ➡️ **Before building a reader on a stored file, count the file against the set the reader will actually be pointed at, not against itself.**

141. 🆕 🔴 **A DEFAULT ENDPOINT IS A FILTERED ENDPOINT, AND THE PARAMETER THAT FIXES ONE LEAGUE EMPTIES THE OTHER.** `[2026-09-06, measured live in a browser]`
    ```
    college  ?limit=400          25 events   <- what shipped as "live scores"
    college  ?groups=80 (FBS)    99
    college  ?groups=81 (FCS)   159
    NFL      ?limit=400          16
    NFL      ?groups=80           0          ⛔
    ```
    🔴 The live layer had been asking for **a quarter of the slate** since the day it shipped, and nothing could have reported that — the feed answered 200 with a valid, complete-looking payload. ⛔ **And the fix is per-league or it is a new bug:** `groups` is a college concept, and sending it to the NFL returns an EMPTY scoreboard, which would have turned the NFL live layer off while reporting nothing wrong.
    ✅ **The union is exactly the product's own view, checked BOTH directions:** on 9/5, groups 80 ∪ 81 = **103 events**, our Division I view = **103 games**, **103 in common, 0 either way.** ➡️ **When a feed's default looks thin, sweep its parameters before concluding anything about coverage — and re-measure the sweep per league.**

142. 🆕 ⛔ **A CHECK THAT PASSES AT `0 → 0` IS TRUE OF NOTHING. GUARD THE PRECONDITION, NOT JUST THE CLAIM.** `[2026-09-06, and it immediately found a real defect]`
    🔴 The scroll test scrolled the panel, forced a live refresh, and asserted the position had not moved. **It passed at `0px → 0px`** — the modal had never scrolled at all, so *"it did not move"* was a statement about an empty set (rule **116**, at check granularity).
    ✅ Adding *"...and it really was scrolled first"* turned the pass into a failure, **and the failure was real**: the code restored `.modal-b.scrollTop`, but `.ovl` is the element carrying `overflow-y:auto` and `.modal-b` carries no overflow at all. **The restore was a SILENT NO-OP and a live game would have thrown the reader back to the top of the modal every 45 seconds — the exact annoyance rule 135 exists for, reintroduced inside the fix for it.** ➡️ **Every "nothing changed" assertion needs a companion asserting there was something to change.**

143. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A DEFAULT IS A DECISION, AND CONVERGE IS WHERE A WRONG ONE SURFACES.** `[found in production, run 534, 2026-09-06]`
    🔴 Only **3 of 33** routing arms set `SEASON=CUR`; every other arm left it empty and the deciding step's fallback handed the collector **a literal `2025`**. ⛔ **Harmless for as long as those runs only build modes that ignore the season — and then converge repaired a season-scoped mode on one of them.** GitHub dropped the 16:04Z college Trends cron (the ONE arm that passes `CUR`), the 18:20Z **hourly news** run converged `cfb-probe` to repair it, and the back-fill ran against **2025**, failed its own `opp_elo` guard, and left the 2026 table untouched and 153 minutes late. **The run would have gone red every hour and never repaired the tab.**
    ⚠️ **THIS IS RULE 112 INSIDE THE WORKFLOW'S OWN FALLBACK, AND IT IS THE SECOND TIME THIS EXACT LITERAL BIT.** Sam himself spotted `2025` in the dispatch FORM and it was fixed there — **while the same year sat in the default one line below.** ➡️ **Converge changes the blast radius of every default: a value that was only ever read by the mode a cron asked for is now read by every mode that run repairs. Audit the defaults the day converge is switched on, not the day something goes red.**
    ⛔ **And the fix is scoped or it is a new bug:** "a season is named for the year it STARTS" is a FOOTBALL rule; MLB's season is named for the year it ends in, so the default is corrected for football only and a check asserts MLB is untouched.

144. 🆕 ⚠️ **A LEGITIMATE EMPTY IS NOT A FAILURE, AND IT IS NOT A PASS EITHER.** `[2026-09-06, `test_single_day.py` red on main]`
    🔴 The check read *"no parlay crosses days AND at least one parlay exists"*. Today's college board is **40 picks over ONE game** — nothing can pair inside the 1.8x–2.1x band — so **zero parlays were built and the whole collector went red while nothing was wrong** (rule **139**).
    ⛔ **But dropping the second half is worse:** *"no parlay crossed"* is true of zero parlays and proves nothing (rule **142**). ✅ **Three outcomes, not two: a real crosser FAILS (proven with a planted one), a non-empty pool with no crossers PASSES, and an empty pool is reported NOT EXERCISED with the numbers that explain it.** ➡️ **Whenever a check depends on the data having produced something, say which of the three happened — a binary verdict on a three-state world is either a false red or a false green, and you do not get to choose which.**

145. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A DELIVERY WITH TWO CANDIDATE ZIPS HAS ONE OUTCOME, AND IT IS NOT THE ONE YOU MEANT.** `[2026-09-06 — rule 111, in the other axis]`
    🔴 A 5-file root zip was sent, and twenty minutes later a 7-file root zip **with a near-identical name** replaced it, with *"this replaces the earlier one"* in the caption. **Sam pasted the NEW commit message and uploaded the OLD zip.** The commit landed **5 of 7 files** — and the two missing were exactly the two that fixed the reds — so the run went red and the obvious suspect was the new feature, which was fine.
    ⛔ **A sentence in a later message cannot un-send an artefact already sitting in the conversation.** Rule 111 said an upload instruction with two DESTINATIONS has one outcome; this is the same failure with two SOURCES. ➡️ **Supersede by REPLACING the contents of the delivery, not by adding a second one: send the corrected zip under the SAME name, or send only the delta. Never leave two plausible zips in the chat and rely on the reader picking the newer.**
    ✅ **AND THE DIAGNOSIS TOOK SIXTY SECONDS BECAUSE THE FIRST MOVE WAS `cmp`, NOT THE LOG:** clone the exact pushed commit and compare every delivered file byte-for-byte against what was built. **Five identical, one stale, one absent** — before reading a single line of CI output. ➡️ **When a delivered change goes red, ASK WHAT ACTUALLY LANDED before asking why it failed.**

146. 🆕 🔴🔴 **WHEN A HANDOFF FAILS TWICE, REMOVE THE CHOICE — DO NOT EXPLAIN IT BETTER.** `[2026-09-06, three mis-landed commits in ninety minutes]`
    🔴 **Three deliveries, three different wrong outcomes, and not one of them was Sam misreading anything.** (1) Two near-identical zips in the chat → the superseded one was uploaded, 5 of 7 files landed. (2) A recovery document with **two steps** in it → step 1's commit message was pasted onto step 2's file, and `collect.yml` landed **at the repo root** instead of the two test files. (3) The same two files missed a third commit.
    ⛔ **Each time my response was to write a clearer document. Each time the document was already clear and the CHOICE was the defect.** A delivery that contains a decision — which zip, which folder, which of two steps — will be decided wrongly at some rate, and no amount of bold text changes that rate.
    ✅ **THE FIX IS STRUCTURAL: write the files onto the reader's machine, into a folder that contains exactly the commit's files and nothing else.** No zip to extract, no second zip to confuse it with, no file to pick. **The only remaining decisions are the destination and the message, and each message from me now carries exactly one of each.**
    ➡️ **The general form: when the same handoff fails twice, stop improving the instructions and delete a degree of freedom.** Rules 111 and 145 were both this failure with the freedom left in.

147. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A FAILURE RATE IS A DIFFERENT SIGNAL FROM A FAILURE, AND NOBODY IN THIS PROJECT WAS READING IT.** `[Sam, 2026-09-06: "im noticing a bunch of failed runs going on in github ... there are multiple failed ones going on consistently"]`
    🔴 **Every session, including every one of mine, looked at the LATEST run.** Sam looked at the LIST. Counted across the API:
    ```
                          9/4   9/5   9/6
    collect runs           24    39    68
    failures               10     6    27
    cancelled               6    16    16
    ```
    ⛔ **The product had been finishing red several times a day for at least three days and no session had noticed, because a session that opens the newest run sees one green and moves on.**
    ✅ **AND THE FAILING STEP NAME IS A FREE CLASSIFIER NOBODY WAS USING.** Grouping the 43 failures by the step that failed split them instantly into two unrelated populations: `Tests` / `Fail if the tests failed` (a test problem) and **`Fail if any pass failed` — 9, 4 and 4 across the three days — which is the COLLECTOR reporting a pass that did not finish, and is a completely different bug.** ➡️ **When asked "why is it failing", group the last N runs by failing step before opening a single log. The histogram names the populations; the log only ever describes one of them.**

148. 🆕 🔴🔴 **AN EMPTY RESULT HAS A CAUSE, AND NAMING THE WRONG ONE IS WORSE THAN NAMING NONE.** `[2026-09-06, rehearsing the NFL opener]`
    🔴 The 2026 NFL season opens with **ONE GAME**. A parlay's legs must be in **different games**, so every candidate is rejected by construction: `rejected.same_game` **12,926**, `rejected.out_of_band` **ZERO**. ⛔ **And the Parlays tab was about to tell the reader *"it is the payout bands that nothing fits today"*** — a cause the card's own rejection counts flatly contradict.
    ⚠️ **The tab already had THREE distinct emptinesses and a comment insisting they must not share a sentence.** It still got this wrong, because the fourth case was not imagined — and the fallback branch is where an unimagined case lands. ➡️ **Every "nothing here" branch must name its cause FROM THE DATA THAT PRODUCED IT, and the terminal `else` must say *"none of the known reasons applies"* rather than asserting the last one you thought of.**

149. 🆕 ⚠️ **A DATE IN UTC IS NOT THE DAY THE PRODUCT LIVES IN.** `[2026-09-06 — "Thursday 10 September" was written in two docs and repeated in a summary]`
    🔴 The first NFL kickoff is `2026-09-10T00:20Z`. **That is WEDNESDAY 9 September, 8:20pm ET** — and ET is the day the card is dated by, the crons are reasoned about in, and the human is watching in. ⛔ **Every artefact in this project that says "Thursday the 10th" was read off a UTC timestamp**, and it cost a day of the rehearsal window.
    ✅ The check now reads the kickoff off the real board and **asserts that the ET slate day is EARLIER than the UTC one**, so the same misreading cannot be made silently. ➡️ **This project already learned it once — "a date is not a game", and the ET-slate-day note in the coverage probe. Write the timezone next to the date, every time.**

150. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A CAP HAS TWO WAYS TO BE WRONG — THE UNIVERSE IT COUNTS AND THE SHAPE IT ASSUMES — AND FIXING ONLY THE FIRST MAKES THE PRODUCT WORSE.** `[2026-09-06, the pre-registered 9/5 test finally answered]`
    🔴 **THE UNIVERSE:** `daily_spend()` walked `{DATA}/{day}` and `DATA` is league-scoped, so on 9/5 the account was billed **869** against a **600** cap while **no league's own counter passed 404**. ⛔ It was not late; it was never in a position to fire.
    🔴 **THE SHAPE, AND THIS IS THE HALF THAT WAS NEARLY MISSED:** priced against the Odds API's OWN game list — the billing denominator, not our schedule — the next nine days read `368 338 323 341 356 413 973 557 341`. **Nine days average 446 and ONE needs 973.** ⛔ **A correct sum with a flat cap was MEASURED by replaying 9/5's pulls in real order: it refuses MLB's whole 4pm pull, and on Saturday it refuses the college props board — the card, the parlays and the props tab for the biggest college day of the week.**
    ✅ The allowance is now **pro-rata with borrowing** (entitlement to date minus spend to date), **floored at the old flat cap** so an overspent month cannot lock the product out, and **ceilinged at 2×** so no day and no loop can drain the plan. ➡️ **Before shipping a corrected guard, replay the real history through it. A guard that is right about the arithmetic and wrong about the calendar is a new outage with a clean conscience.**

151. 🆕 ⛔ **A DENOMINATOR YOU OWN IS NOT THE DENOMINATOR YOU ARE BILLED ON.** `[2026-09-06, pricing the Saturday spike]`
    🔴 The first projection priced Saturday 12 Sept at **3,923** credits by counting **our** schedule — 287 college games. **The Odds API prices 47 of them.** The real figure is **973**. ⛔ A four-fold error, in the direction that would have caused a panic and a wrong decision.
    ✅ The billing denominator is `board.json` — the vendor's own game list — and the 9/5 receipt proves it: college gamelines reported **158 games** while the props pull billed **218 = 5 markets × 2 regions × ~22 games**. ➡️ **When estimating a bill, count the rows the VENDOR will charge for, and check the estimate against a real receipt before acting on it.** This is rule 122's cousin: the wrong denominator, one layer down.

152. 🆕 🔴🔴 **AN EMPTY ANSWER FROM A SOURCE IS NOT A FACT ABOUT THE WORLD — AND WHEN IT IS FORGIVEN, THE ARTIFACT CAN NEVER BE REPAIRED.** `[2026-09-06 — the founding lesson, in the place it does the most damage]`
    🔴 `build_season` caught every fetch exception, logged it, carried on, ended with an empty result and raised `SeasonNotStarted`; the back-fill then **forgave that for the current season as a STATUS, not a failure.** ⛔ **The season had started — our own committed schedule held 448 FINALS — and the probe report written by the SAME RUN said `endpoints_failed: [["games","429"], ["player game","429"], ["plays","429"], ["roster","429"]]`. CFBD was RATE-LIMITING us.**
    ⛔ **The forgiveness is what made it fatal.** A failure would have been reported and retried; a *status* meant the run wrote nothing, said nothing was wrong, left the artifact permanently out of contract and went red every hour with no path back. ➡️ **Never merge "the source refused" with "there is nothing there". Give them different TYPES, and forgive only the one you can refute** — here by counting the finals already on disk.
    ⚠️ **AND THE REFUTATION MUST BE ONE-SIDED:** a season we hold no schedule for returns 0 finals and refutes nothing. **An unknown must never masquerade as a refutation.**

153. 🆕 🔴 **CONVERGE TURNS A STALE ARTIFACT INTO A RETRY STORM, AND THE STORM CAN BE THE CAUSE.** `[2026-09-06 — self-inflicted, hours after making news hourly]`
    🔴 Converge repairs anything past due on EVERY run. Making news hourly therefore launched a full CFBD back-fill — several endpoints, many calls — **every hour that Trends was stale.** At 18:33 CFBD answered; by 19:17 it was refusing. **The repair mechanism produced the condition it was repairing.**
    ✅ A failed expensive mode is now held off for a window **chosen against the deadline it must meet** (180 min against a daily-noon deadline: still two attempts a day, down from twenty-four). ⛔ **Backing off is not hiding** — the artifact stays out of contract, the banner says so and the run still goes red; what stops is guaranteeing that the next attempt fails too. ➡️ **When you raise a cron's frequency, ask what converge will now retry at that frequency. Cheap modes are fine; a paid or rate-limited one needs a back-off the same day.**

154. 🆕 ⛔ **A WIPE SHOULD BE A DATE, NOT A DELETE.** `[Sam, 2026-09-06: "wipe the track record dont get rid of the tab completley"]`
    ✅ `RECORD_FROM = "2026-09-07"` empties the football record without removing a single card: every file in `picks/` stays byte-for-byte as published, and the test **compares them before and after** to prove it. Moving the one date grades the earlier period again — **driven at `2000-01-01`, not asserted.**
    ⚠️ **AND THE EMPTY TAB MUST DISTINGUISH A RESET FROM A NEW PRODUCT.** Those are different facts, so the banner appears **only when something was actually set aside**, and every word of it is computed in the grader and printed from the file (rule 132). ➡️ **Destructive requests almost always have a non-destructive shape that satisfies them exactly. Find it before deleting anything, and make the product say what happened.**
    🔴 **WHY IT WAS RIGHT TO WIPE, and it is not tidiness:** every football card before that date was built while the contract had no Sunday, one card mixed two slate days, every run defaulted to season 2025, and CFBD was rate-limiting us. **A hit rate over those cards measures the outage, not the product** — publishing it as "the record" is a number that looks like evidence and is not.

155. 🆕 🔴 **WHEN A LEGITIMATE STATE ARRIVES, THE CHECKS THAT ASSUMED IT COULD NOT ARE THE BUG — AND THERE WILL BE MORE THAN ONE.** `[2026-09-06, the wipe]`
    🔴 Emptying the record turned **three** checks red in one file at once: the sample's concentration, the by-hand re-derivation control, and "it writes both the totals and the detail". **Each demanded that the data had produced something**, which is the same defect as the parlay check found hours earlier (rule 139) — a binary verdict on a three-state world.
    ⛔ **And the lazy fix is the other failure:** dropping the `> 0` leaves a check that is true of an empty file (rule 142). ✅ **All three now split three ways — impossible FAILS, a real sample PASSES, an empty one is reported NOT EXERCISED with the reason.** ➡️ **After deliberately producing a state the system has never seen, expect a CLUSTER of false failures, not one. Fix them by adding the third outcome, never by weakening the assertion.**

156. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A SILENT FALLBACK TO A REJECTED METHOD IS WORSE THAN THE FAILURE IT HIDES.** `[2026-09-06, Sam's #3 — the MLB card that argued with itself]`
