    🔴 T37's pre-registered fallback moved `HITTER_PROJ` markets from the MEAN to an INVERSION at the primary line. **When the inversion cannot be built, `coherent_projections` falls through to `hitter_mean` — the very statistic T37 measured at 5.43% against a 5.00% bar and replaced — and says nothing.** The row then reads *"his own per-game average"*: true of the value, silent about the method having failed.
    ⛔ **That is what made the red run undiagnosable.** The mean legitimately sits on the far side of a skewed line, so the gate correctly refuses it, and nothing on the row, in the log or in the failure file records where the number came from. ➡️ **A fallback path must record that it was taken. Three labels, not two: "no distribution", "the distribution failed today", and "it worked" are different facts, and only the middle one is a defect.**

157. 🆕 ⚠️ **WHEN A GATE FAILS, TEST WHETHER THE GATE IS WRONG — AND THEN TEST WHETHER YOUR TEST OF THE GATE IS WRONG.** `[2026-09-06, and I nearly deleted a correct pre-registered check]`
    🔴 My first read was that the gate was too strict: RBIs are negative-binomial, so a player can be 75% under 0.5 and average 0.6, and both numbers are true. **I measured it and it held** — Chaparro 75.4% zero / mean 0.493 / max 8, and 8 of 472 hitters clear both bars at once. ⛔ **All correct, and all beside the point: the card is not supposed to publish a mean on that market at all.** The evidence for "the gate is wrong" was real and answered a question nobody had asked.
    ✅ **What caught it was reading the check's own justification** — *"stricter than T37's 5%"* — and following it to `research/t37_spec.md`, where the fallback and the reason for zero tolerance were already written down before any data was seen. ➡️ **Before removing a check, find the decision that created it. A check that cites a pre-registered test is not an opinion, and "my measurement disagrees with it" usually means the measurement is answering a different question.**

158. 🆕 ⛔ **A PARITY RULE THAT LIVES IN A SENTENCE WILL BE BROKEN; MAKE IT A CHECK.** `[Sam, 2026-09-06: "verify and test that we have … the same recurring tasks for the nfl tabs as well as the cfb ones"]`
    ✅ **33 crons, 55 routed arms, and every shared mode carries the SAME cron count in both leagues** — `fb-scores` 6, `gamelines` 2, `props-player` 2, `card-fb` 8, `news` 3, `live-probe` 7. ⛔ **Read from the workflow and the contract themselves, never from a doc** (rule 120).
    ⚠️ **The two divergences are ALLOWED BY NAME AND CHECKED AGAINST THE DATA, not by assertion:** Trends is daily for college and weekly for the NFL because `nfl.py` reads nflverse's *weekly* drop — the test greps that word — and team logos are college-only because the 32 NFL crests are a hard-coded `NFL_LOGOS` map the test confirms exists. ➡️ **An allow-list entry must carry the evidence for itself, or it becomes the place divergence hides.**

159. 🆕 🔴 **EQUALITY AT A SINGLE MOMENT MEASURES THE CALENDAR, NOT THE PROPERTY.** `[2026-09-06 — my parity check was wrong twice before it was right]`
    🔴 **v1** compared the two leagues' ungoverned sets TODAY and failed: the NFL's `card-fb` and `props-player` are correctly ungoverned because no NFL game is inside the window (rule 86). 🔴 **v2** compared them 13 hours before the NFL opener and **also** failed — at that instant COLLEGE has no game in its window either. **Each league is at a different point in its own arming sequence, always.**
    ✅ **What is actually invariant is the SHAPE:** exactly one mode is permanently ungoverned (a diagnostic that writes no product artifact), and everything else ungoverned is waiting on a **stated precondition**. ➡️ **When two systems arm on different calendars, never assert they are equal at a time — assert what is true of both at every time.**


160. 🆕 🔴 **A COMMENT JUSTIFYING A DEADLINE IS A CLAIM ABOUT THE SCHEDULE, AND IT MUST BE MEASURED LIKE ONE.** `[2026-09-06 — I wrote it, shipped it, and it was false]`
    ⛔ Moving college Trends to Sam's 3am, I wrote into `freshness.py`: *"3am ET is 07:00Z, which is after even a west-coast night game has finished and its box score has settled."* **It reads like a fact and it was a guess.** Measured against the real 2026 schedule: **2 of 62 slate days finish AFTER their own 3am rebuild** — 2026-09-12 and 2026-10-03, both by **29 minutes**, both because **Hawai'i kicks off at 5:59pm HST = 11:59pm ET** and a 3h30m game ends at 3:29am.
    🔴 **The danger is not the 29 minutes; it is that a future session reads the comment and stops checking.** A deadline's justification is exactly the kind of sentence nobody re-derives. ➡️ **If a comment asserts something about when games end, when a feed publishes, or when a run lands, it is a measurement — write the number and the date beside it, or do not write the sentence.** Struck in place, with the measurement in its own words. Sibling of rule **83**.

161. 🆕 🔴 **"DOES IT FINISH IN TIME?" IS ALMOST NEVER THE PRODUCT'S QUESTION. "IS IT THERE BEFORE THE NEXT READER?" IS.** `[2026-09-06 — the check that was failing was asking the wrong thing]`
    ⛔ `test_trends_schedule.py` v1 asked *"does every slate finish before its own 3am rebuild?"* and went red on two days. **Both answers were correct; the question was too narrow.** A rebuild that misses one night is only a defect if a board reads the table before the next rebuild catches up — and the next college slate after each Hawai'i night is **4–5 days later**.
    ⛔ **THE TEMPTING FIX WAS TO MOVE THE DEADLINE TO 3:30AM AND GO GREEN.** That is tuning a contract Sam chose to a scheduling artifact, and it buys 29 minutes on two nights of a season. **CLAUDE.md's one rule forbids weakening a check; it permits replacing one that asks the wrong question with a STRICTLY HARDER one.** ✅ v2 asserts coverage across **all 62 slate days** — first rebuild at-or-after the last whistle must land before the next slate's first card — and the tightest margin on the calendar is **5.9 hours**.
    ➡️ **Before relaxing a schedule to satisfy a check, write down which reader is harmed if you do not. If you cannot name one, the check is measuring the clock instead of the product.**

162. 🆕 🔴 **PIN THE REASON FOR AN EXCEPTION, NEVER THE LIST OF DATES.** `[2026-09-06 — the exception list would have been deleted by the 2027 schedule]`
    ⛔ The obvious way to keep the two late nights honest is to write `EXPECTED_LATE = {"2026-09-12", "2026-10-03"}`. **That check goes red the day the 2027 schedule loads, for no reason anyone can act on, and the next session deletes it** — rule **139** exactly.
    ✅ **What is durable is WHY: Hawai'i is six hours behind Eastern.** So the check asserts *every slate that runs past its own rebuild has a Hawai'i home game as its last kickoff.* **A late slate for any other reason goes RED instead of being absorbed into a known exception** — which is the entire point of recording an exception at all.
    ➡️ **An exception list is a cache of a reason. Store the reason.**


163. 🆕 🔴 **A CHECK THAT CAN ONLY BE RUN ON THE DAY IT PASSES IS NOT A CHECK.** `[2026-09-08 — the live scores layer, verified 103 of 103 and broken three days later]`
    ⛔ The football Scores tab fetched ESPN's scoreboard with no `dates` parameter. **That returns the week ESPN is CURRENTLY on**, not today and not the reader's week. On 2026-09-05 those were the same thing, so the coverage check read **103 of 103 both directions** and the source comment said "today". On 2026-09-08 the identical code answered for **09-11 to 09-13** and had nothing to say about the days Sam was looking at.
    🔴 **The verification was correct AND worthless**, because its precondition — "the reader's week is ESPN's current week" — held on the day it ran and on no other day. ➡️ **When a check depends on today, either move the clock inside the test or assert the property that does not move.** ✅ Now measured per day: `dates=20260906` → 6 events, 6 final; `dates=20260907` → 1 event, 1 final; the 7 stored games that rendered blank resolve **7 of 7**.

164. 🆕 🔴 **A DATE RANGE IS NOT A DATE LOOP.** `[2026-09-08 — measured, and it would have looked like it worked]`
    ⚠️ ESPN's scoreboard accepts `dates=A-B`, and it **resolves the range to the WEEK it starts in and silently drops the rest**: `dates=20260905-20260907` returned only 09-05 and 09-06; `dates=20260906-20260907` returned only 09-06. A single `dates=YYYYMMDD` was exact both times.
    ⛔ **The failure mode is the dangerous one: mostly-right.** A range request returns plenty of games, so it looks fine, and the day it loses is the most recent one — the day a reader is most likely to check. The per-day loop is in the source with the measurement beside it precisely because "simplify this into one request" is the obvious next edit.

165. 🆕 🔴 **A CRON IS NOT A CONTRACT.** `[2026-09-08 — rule 137 for the second time, on the mode that looked most covered]`
    ⛔ `scores` had **six** game-day crons and a deadline of **Sat 21:30 + Sun 09:30** only. Because the crons were dense, nobody asked whether the DEADLINE described the sport. `[measured]` the college schedule went **two days** without a rebuild — 6 games on 09-06 and 1 on 09-07 showing no score — and **nothing was red**: the last deadline had been met, the next was days away, `plan()` saw nothing stale and every verifier passed.
    ⚠️ **A dropped cron with no deadline behind it is never repaired.** Converge only repairs what the contract says is late, so crons without a matching deadline are best-effort, and best-effort is what the contract exists to replace.
    ✅ And the check that was supposed to catch this **pinned the defect as the expectation** — it asserted the days were exactly `{Sat, Sun}`. It now measures the game days **from the stored schedule** and requires the deadline to cover them: strictly harder, fails on the old shape, and names the uncovered days (college Mon–Fri, NFL Wed–Sat).

166. 🆕 🔴 **A CHECK WITH AN EXPIRY DATE, AND NOTHING EXPIRES IT.** `[2026-09-08 — red on every run for a day before anyone noticed]`
    ⛔ `test_record_reset` asserted `cards_before_record_from == len(dated)` — *"every published card is set aside"*. True when written, and true for **exactly one day**: the moment a card dated ON the floor was published — correctly counted, exactly as designed — it read **3 of 4** and stayed red forever.
    🔴 **The product was right; the check was measuring the calendar** (sibling of rule 159). ✅ It now asserts the number set aside equals the cards dated **strictly before** the floor, which tests the boundary instead of a coincidence, and carries a second check that the floor is a real boundary rather than a catch-all.

167. 🆕 🔴 **A FIXTURE THAT CANNOT EXERCISE THE CHECK FAILS IT INSTEAD.** `[2026-09-08 — `int(round(1 * 0.5))` is 0]`
    ⛔ `test_single_day` shifted 50% of the props board to the next day and asserted the card held those rows back. The college board holds **one game**, and Python rounds a half to even, so **nothing was shifted** — the card correctly held back nothing and the check failed as though the BUILDER were broken.
    ⚠️ **The failure pointed at the wrong file**, which is worse than a check that does not run: it accuses working code. ✅ The fixture now **synthesises** the second day when the split would be degenerate, and **guards the precondition** before asserting on it. **11 checks → 24**, because the filter is finally exercised on every board instead of only on a fat one.

168. 🆕 💰 **AN EMPTY ANSWER STILL COSTS A CALL, AND WE WERE BUYING 29 OF THEM.** `[2026-09-08 — CFBD 429 on every endpoint]`
    ⛔ `build_pace` looped `range(1, 17)` for **both** season types: a flat **32 CFBD calls per rebuild whatever the date**. In week 2 of the season, **29 of those 32 could only return nothing** — 14 unplayed regular weeks and all 16 postseason weeks. Meanwhile converge was dragging the whole rebuild into every hourly run because the Trends deadline had moved to 3am and its cron had not.
    ✅ A season has no gaps, so **two consecutive EMPTY weeks means the played part is over**: 32 calls → **6**, an 81% cut.
    🔴 **AND THE DANGEROUS HALF IS THE ERROR CASE.** If a 429 counted as "empty", a rate-limited fetch would read as *"the season is over"* and silently truncate a real season — the same confusion `SourceUnavailable` exists to prevent, one layer down. **An error is never an empty**, and a driven test proves it: 32 calls when every fetch raises, 6 when they return nothing.

169. 🆕 🔴 **NAME THE STATUS CODE OR NAME NOTHING.** `[2026-09-08 — "HTTPError" for two days]`
    ⛔ `build_schedule` stored `f"{st}: {type(e).__name__}"`, so the committed probe read `["regular: HTTPError", "postseason: HTTPError"]` while the Scores tab sat frozen. **That cannot distinguish 401 (our key is rejected) from 429 (we are over quota) from 5xx (CFBD is down) — three problems with three different owners.** The sibling function two hundred lines away already captured the code; only this one dropped it.
    ⚠️ **And the host was fine the whole time** — measured from a browser: `api.collegefootballdata.com` served 200 at the root and a clean 401 with no key. The one field that would have said whose problem it was, was the field being thrown away. Rule 148, paid for again.

170. 🆕 🔴 **A STALE NUMBER THAT ANNOUNCES ITSELF IS A DIFFERENT PRODUCT FROM ONE THAT DOES NOT.** `[2026-09-08 — Sam: "the trends tab is still not updated"]`
    ⛔ The Trends tab drew a full, confident table of numbers **built on 2026-09-04** and said nothing whatever about the date. Sam could only find out by asking. While CFBD answers 429 **no code can make the numbers newer** — but the page can stop presenting four-day-old numbers as current.
    ✅ The tab now reads `built_at` **from the file** and says how old it is, loudly past each league's own cadence (2 days college, 9 days NFL). ➡️ **When the fix for the data is out of our hands, the fix for the READER never is.**


171. 🆕 🔴 **A PERMANENTLY RED RUN IS NOT AN ALARM, IT IS A COST.** `[2026-09-09 — Sam: "i keep getting emails saying the collect run is failing"]`
    ⛔ Every college run AND every NFL run was red on a loop — **46 football runs a day** — for two states that were already diagnosed, already recorded, and had **no action left in them**: CFBD 429 (quota spent, key already upgraded) and nflverse not having published `stats_player_week_2026` (the season opened that morning).
    🔴 **THIS PROJECT HAD ALREADY LEARNED THIS AND WRITTEN IT DOWN.** `verify_freshness.py` carries the argument verbatim about the refused card: *"22 consecutive red runs for a decision that had already been made and recorded. AN ALARM THAT FIRES EVERY FIFTEEN MINUTES GETS IGNORED, AND IGNORING RED IS EXACTLY HOW THE ORIGINAL STALENESS SURVIVED A WHOLE DAY."* **The mechanism existed, bounded by `CARD_REFUSED_GRACE_MIN`, and was applied to exactly one state.**
    ✅ So the fix was not a new policy but the SAME one, extended to the two other states this system can actually recognise — **downgrade to a warning, and BOUND IT**: 7 days for a refusal, 14 for a not-yet-published season, then red again saying *"this is a decision now, not an outage."*
    ➡️ **When you invent a mechanism for a known state, ask immediately what the OTHER known states are.** One instance of a pattern is nearly always the first of several.

172. 🆕 🔴 **A STAND-DOWN IS A DECISION; A FAILED FETCH IS AN EVENT. THEY MUST NOT SHARE AN EXIT CODE.** `[2026-09-09]`
    ⛔ The CFBD back-off skip called `sys.exit(1)` under the banner *"SKIPPING IS NOT HIDING — the artifact stays out of contract and the run still goes red."* **Right about hiding, wrong about the exit code.** It reddened every run by itself, before the freshness gate even ran, so the back-off that existed to *reduce* noise was the loudest thing in the log.
    ✅ A stand-down now exits 0 and says so; a fetch that was ATTEMPTED and returned nothing still exits 1. **Nothing is hidden**: the artifact stays out of contract, the gate warns every run and fails past the grace, and the tab prints its own age.
    ⚠️ **AND A STAND-DOWN MUST NOT WRITE ITS PROBE REPORT.** Writing an empty one would overwrite a REAL diagnosis — the status code, the response body, the quota block — with the absence of one. *We did not ask* and *we asked and got nothing* are different rows in the same file.

173. 🆕 🔴 **`ast.parse` IS NOT `compile`. VERIFYING WITH IT IS A FALSE GREEN.** `[2026-09-09 — I nearly shipped a SyntaxError]`
    ⛔ I restructured two back-off branches to use `continue`, ran `ast.parse` on the file, saw ✅, and moved on. **`continue` outside a loop is legal to PARSE and illegal to COMPILE** — the check happens in the compiler, not the parser. Both branches sat inside an `if LEAGUE == …`, not a loop.
    ✅ `python3 -m py_compile` on every file caught it instantly. The suite would have caught it too, at import — **but only because two tests import `collect.py`**, which is luck, not design.
    ➡️ **Syntax-check with `py_compile`, never `ast.parse`.** And a structural edit is not verified until the thing has been RUN.

174. 🆕 🔴 **RULE 86 WAS WRITTEN FOR THE NFL CARD AND NEVER APPLIED TO THE NFL LOGS.** `[2026-09-09 — a red nobody had looked at]`
    ⛔ `allowed-by-position-2026.json.gz` **has never existed for the NFL** — only 2021–2025. The contract falls back to the 2025 file and demands a weekly rebuild of it, but `nfl-logs` for the CURRENT season legitimately writes nothing while nflverse has no 2026 data, so the watched file is never touched and the row is red every Tuesday forever.
    ⚠️ **The collector was right all along and said so**: `not yet : [2026] (season not started — not a failure)`. The CONTRACT was not reading its own builder's answer.
    ➡️ **"An artifact that cannot exist yet is not late" is only a rule if the contract can SEE that it cannot exist yet.** The evidence has to be wired in, not just written down.

175. 🆕 🔴 **A COLUMN THAT IS CONSTANT BY ARITHMETIC IS NOT A BROKEN JOIN, AND THE GUARD ACCUSED THE CODE FOR TWO DAYS.** `[2026-09-10 — every NFL run since the season opened]`
    ⛔ `nfl-logs` died on every build from 2026-09-09 with *"ahead_out is CONSTANT ZERO across 66 player-weeks. That is a join failure, not a result."* **It was not a join failure.** `share_before(pid, wk)` averages games with `week < wk`; on a week-1-only log that list is EMPTY for everyone, so it returns `0.0` for every player, `share_before(q) > mine` is `0.0 > 0.0`, and `ahead_out` is **necessarily** 0 on every row.
    ⚠️ **THE GUARD ASKED A QUESTION THE CALENDAR GUARANTEED IT COULD NOT PASS** — and answered it with an accusation. No amount of correct joining could have moved it before week 2.
    ➡️ **Before a constancy check accuses a join, ask whether the quantity CAN vary on this input.** A point-in-time feature needs a prior period to exist; on the first period, constant IS the correct answer.

176. 🆕 🔴 **THE BAR WAS COUNTING ROWS WHEN THE QUESTION WAS WEEKS.** `[2026-09-10 — 1,848 rows, one week, three columns accused at once]`
    ⛔ `cfb.py` carried `CONST_MIN = 500` with a comment reasoning that "a real season is tens of thousands of player-weeks", so a degenerate slice could never reach the bar. **1,848 rows clears 500 comfortably while still being a SINGLE WEEK**, and the college build failed with three constant trailing columns — one cause, and the message named none of them correctly.
    ✅ A point-in-time column is now judged only when **at least two distinct weeks** exist, in BOTH leagues, and the failure message carries the WEEK SPAN rather than only a row count.
    ➡️ **Size the guard in the unit of the thing being measured.** A trailing feature is a claim about periods; bounding it by rows measures the wrong axis and passes exactly the case it was built to exclude.

177. 🆕 🔴 **THE REPLACEMENT FOR A FALSE ALARM MUST BE A CHECK THE OLD ONE COULD NOT MAKE.** `[2026-09-10 — how not to relax a guard]`
    ⛔ Loosening a constancy check because it fired wrongly is exactly the move `CLAUDE.md`'s first rule forbids. **The defensible version is a strictly harder question**, and it existed: **a week the SOURCE says was played that produced no rows in the log.**
    ⚠️ **No column-constancy test can see that.** Every column can vary perfectly across the weeks that ARE present, while the table is silently built on half a season and every trailing number in it is wrong.
    ➡️ **When you narrow a guard, ship the harder check in the same commit.** Both leagues now fail by name on a missing completed week — a fault neither could detect before.

178. 🆕 🔴 **"IS ANYTHING THERE?" IS NOT "IS ANYTHING WRONG?", AND FOUR TEST FILES CONFLATED THEM ON THE SAME DAY.** `[2026-09-10 — a correct card, four red checks]`
    ⛔ The college card for 2026-09-10 held **zero picks** — a Thursday with nothing priced — and `test_top_plays`, `test_conf_filter`, `test_record_fb` and `test_single_day` all failed. `0 < len(T)`, `c["n"] > 0`, `days == [card_date]`, `{basis} == {"RECORD"}`, `cards_seen == len(eligible_files)`. **Every one demanded data EXIST.**
    ✅ `== {"RECORD"}` became `<= {"RECORD"}`; `days == [d]` became "no day differs from `d`". **On any non-empty input these are the IDENTICAL assertion**; what was dropped is a claim the checks never owned. Each now reports **NOT EXERCISED** when the input is empty, so an empty board can never pass silently (rule 144).
    ⚠️ **This is `CLAUDE.md`'s own founding example** — *"at least one alt rung is below −700"* tested that data existed rather than that data was correct — **recurring in four files at once, two years of lesson notwithstanding.**
    ➡️ A check that cannot survive a legitimately empty input will eventually go red on a correct product, and **the day it does is the day someone stops reading red.**

179. 🆕 🔴 **A GUARD THAT CROSSES A FALSE ALARM OFF ALSO HIDES A REAL SENTENCE.** `[2026-09-10 — found by the test being fixed, not by the fix]`
    ⛔ While correcting `test_top_plays`, the empty-board branch of the card was read for the first time. On a board with **zero priced rows** it printed, to a reader: *"No top plays. ⛔ Not because the board is empty — because no row on it carries a record."* **On a board that WAS empty.**
    ⚠️ Two distinct states — *nothing was priced* and *rows exist but none carries a record* — shared one sentence, and it gave the wrong reason for the more common one.
    ➡️ **The defect was invisible until an empty board occurred AND a check looked at the words.** Both branches are now asserted. ⛔ When two states share a message, the message is wrong for one of them.

180. 🆕 🔴 **A TRAILING GAP IS PUBLISH LAG; ONLY AN INTERIOR HOLE IS A DEFECT — AND I SHIPPED THE WRONG ONE INSIDE THE FIX FOR 175-177.** `[2026-09-10, caught by the mock test Sam asked for, before it ever ran]`
    ⛔ Drop 16's replacement check asked *"is any week the schedule says was played missing from the log?"* **An NFL week is Thu/Sun/Mon.** The schedule reports week N played the moment Thursday's game ends; nflverse publishes that week's player stats on its WEEKLY drop. **So the check is RED FROM FRIDAY TO MONDAY, four days in seven.** The college side had the same shape for a different reason: a week is `completed_per_source` as soon as ONE of its games finishes, so a Thursday-night college game would trip it every Thursday.
    🔴 **THAT IS RULES 175-177 REINTRODUCED BY THE COMMIT THAT WROTE THEM** — a guard firing on a state the calendar guarantees, written by someone who had just spent a session removing two of exactly those.
    ✅ **A missing week is a defect only when it is missing from BELOW the log's own highest week**: the source published a LATER week and skipped this one. That cannot be lag, and it is still the fault no column-constancy test can see. **A gap at the END is staleness, which `built_at` and the freshness contract already own** — one question per check (rule 161).
    ➡️ **When you replace a false alarm, ask what the new check does on every day of the week, not just today's.** The old one was wrong on one day a season; its replacement was wrong on four days in seven.

181. 🆕 🔴 **READING A STORED ARTIFACT TESTS ITS AGE, NOT THE CODE THAT WRITES IT.** `[2026-09-10 — a check that failed for the right reason and the wrong one]`
    ⛔ The mock test's first form read `picks/fb-ncaaf-latest.json` to check the empty-board sentence, and it FAILED — on a fix that was correct. **That card was written by the pre-drop builder and is not rewritten until the next `card-fb` run** (rule 104: a code change reaches the page only through a rebuild).
    ⚠️ **The failure looked exactly like a broken fix.** Nothing in the output distinguished "the code is wrong" from "the file predates the code".
    ✅ The check now REBUILDS the card in a sandbox and reads what the builder produces today, and states in the same breath that the committed card still carries the old sentence and correctly so.
    ➡️ **If a check reads an artifact, name what would make it stale, or rebuild it.** Otherwise a green means the file is fresh and a red means nothing in particular.

182. 🆕 🔴 **I WROTE THE TEST, SHIPPED IT, AND NAMED IT SOMETHING THE DISCOVERY LOOP CANNOT SEE.** `[2026-09-10 — Sam: "did we run our test to verify that our files are working"]`
    ⛔ `mocktest_fixes.py` went to live main in drop 17 and **never ran once.** The workflow discovers tests with `for t in test_*.py`; that name does not match, so every run since reported green on **47** files while the file sat in the repo as decoration.
    🔴 **THE WORKFLOW ALREADY SAYS THIS IN ITS OWN COMMENTS** — *"A TEST THAT NEVER RUNS IS WORSE THAN NO TEST: it is a false sense of cover, and it rots silently"* — which is why the loop DISCOVERS files instead of listing them. **The discovery mechanism was fine. I handed it a name outside its glob.**
    ⚠️ **AND THE GREEN WAS REAL, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES IT DANGEROUS.** Sam read "green runs" as "the mock test passed". It had never been executed.
    ➡️ **A new test is not shipped until you have seen the runner COUNT it.** The step prints `ran N test file(s)`; read N. ⛔ And any test carrying a machine-specific path must degrade to NOT EXERCISED rather than die on import, or it is a test that can only run in one place — which is rule 163 wearing a different hat.

183. 🆕 🔴 **A RE-FIT THAT UPDATES THE DOC AND STOPS HAS SHIPPED NOTHING, AND IT SAT THAT WAY FOR TEN DAYS.** `[2026-09-11 — `claude/mlb-projection-model.md` published v5.0 on 09-01; `card.py` ran v4.0 until 09-11]`
    ⛔ **13 of the card's 16 model constants were stale for ten days** while the doc that owns them said v5.0 and the CODE-DEBT block inside it said, in its own words, *"⛔ The re-fit is therefore NOT complete."* **Nobody read it, because a handover written into a doc is not a task.**
    🔴 **THE ONLY THING THAT NOTICED WAS A SCHEDULED GRADING RUN RE-REPORTING IT INTO A LIST NOBODY WAS READING** — rule 171 again, in the other direction: a permanently-red line item stops being information.
    ⚠️ **`claude/owed-tests.md` step 14 had already written the rule down**: *"the card is the surface Sam reads."* Having the rule is not the same as the rule firing.
    ✅ **THE FIX IS A GUARD, NOT A RESOLUTION.** `test_model_version.py` fingerprints the 16 constants and pins the fingerprint beside `MODEL_VERSION`. Change a coefficient without bumping the stamp — or bump the stamp without changing a coefficient — and CI is red.
    ➡️ **A re-fit is now THREE deliberate acts: update the constants, bump the stamp, paste the fingerprint.** The doc still owns the VALUES; the test owns only that the two halves ship together. ⛔ Do not copy the coefficients into the test — that is the second-source-of-truth pattern that once put v4.0's numbers into `card-blueprint.md` where a card builder read them first.

184. 🆕 🔴 **I RANKED IT COSMETIC. THE STAMP WAS CORRECT AND THE CODE WAS STALE, WHICH IS THE OPPOSITE PROBLEM AND THE WORSE ONE.** `[2026-09-11 — my own triage, wrong in the direction that matters]`
    ⛔ The grading run's one-liner read *"model_version v4.0 nine days after v5.0 published"*, and I filed it as **"a label; no math reads it."** It reads like a stale string. **It was not a string problem at all.**
    🔴 **`card.py` GENUINELY RAN v4.0, so the stamp was TELLING THE TRUTH.** The defect was ten days upstream of the label.
    ⛔ **AND THE "OBVIOUS FIX" WOULD HAVE BEEN THE WORST AVAILABLE MOVE** — stamping `v5.0` onto v4.0 coefficients is a **false provenance claim on a page Sam bets real money against.** It would have silenced the only signal pointing at the real bug.
    ➡️ **WHEN A VERSION STRING DISAGREES WITH A DOC, ASK WHICH ONE IS LYING BEFORE EDITING EITHER.** A stamp that matches the code is evidence; a stamp that matches the doc may be a fiction. **The coefficients are the fix and the stamp follows them, never the reverse.**

185. 🆕 🔴 **ONE CEILING, STATED TWICE, DRIFTED — AND THE CARD CONTRADICTED ITSELF ON ITS OWN PAGE FOR SIXTEEN DAYS.** `[2026-08-26 → 2026-09-11]`
    ⛔ `PARLAY_BANDS` printed the prose **"1.8x – 2.2x"** while the `in_band` flag was computed against `TARGET` = **2.10**. **A pair at 2.15x was ABOVE BAND to the flag and INSIDE BAND to the same card's own sentence**, and every scheduled grading run re-reported it.
    🔴 **THE TWO NUMBERS ARE NOT BOTH BAND EDGES.** 2.10 is the **soft target** (the 47.6% break-even landmark); 2.20 is the **band edge** from Sam's 2026-08-26 instruction, which rule 28 owns. **Using the landmark as an edge WAS the bug.**
    ✅ The flag is now computed **from the band it prints** (`lo <= mult <= hi`), so the two cannot separate again.
    ⛔ **WHAT DID NOT MOVE:** the **1.80 hard floor** still suppresses (Sam: *"i dont want to see them at all"*), 2.10 is still never a cap, and **no graded row or published INSIDE/ABOVE subtotal was recomputed** — history was graded on the 2.1x flag and stays that way.
    ➡️ **A CONSTANT THAT APPEARS IN BOTH A SENTENCE AND A COMPARISON MUST BE READ FROM ONE PLACE.** Two copies of a number are two things to drift.

186. 🆕 🔴 **`(size != 2) or ...` — A BOOLEAN THAT CANNOT BE FALSE IS NOT A CHECK, AND IT RODE ALONG BESIDE A REAL BUG FOR SIXTEEN DAYS.** `[2026-09-11 — found while fixing rule 185, not by looking for it]`
    ⛔ The three- and four-leg `in_band` flag was `(size != 2) or <band test>`. For every size that is not 2 the first clause is **unconditionally true**, so **a 3-leg at 2.5x — below its own 3.00 floor — was labelled IN BAND.**
    ⚠️ **IT WAS NEVER REPORTED, BECAUSE A FLAG THAT IS ALWAYS TRUE NEVER LOOKS WRONG.** Nothing in the output distinguishes "judged and passed" from "not judged."
    ✅ Every size is now judged against its own band from `PARLAY_BANDS`, in one expression.
    ➡️ **WHEN A GUARD IS SHORT-CIRCUITED BY A TYPE OR SIZE TEST, ASK WHAT IT EVALUATES TO IN THE SHORT-CIRCUITED CASE.** "Not applicable" and "passed" must not be the same value.

187. 🆕 🔴 **A FLOOR STATED AS A VALUE MUST INCLUDE THAT VALUE, AND `>` SILENTLY EXCLUDED THE EXACT NUMBER SAM NAMED.** `[2026-09-11 — both the pitcher and the hitter path]`
    ⛔ Sam's rule is **"−700, nothing shorter."** The code read `price > -700`, which **rejects −700 itself** — the single most common posted price at that end of a ladder, and the exact rung his instruction was about.
    ⚠️ **A BOUNDARY BUG NEVER LOOKS LIKE A BUG.** The board simply had slightly fewer rows, and every one that was dropped was dropped for a reason that reads correct in the log.
    ✅ Both paths now read `price >= -700`.
    ➡️ **WHEN A RULE IS QUOTED AS A NUMBER, THE COMPARISON IS `>=` OR `<=` UNLESS SOMEONE SAID OTHERWISE.** And when two files implement the same floor, fix both in the same commit — the hitter path had drifted from the pitcher path because the rule was set on pitchers.

188. 🆕 🔴 **AN mtime IS NOT CONTENT, AND FOR THREE DAYS THE INTEGRITY GUARD ACCUSED TESTS OF WRITING FILES THEY HAD ONLY TOUCHED.** `[2026-09-08 → 2026-09-11 — the "unexplained failure" Sam asked me to find]`
    ⛔ `tcheck.py` snapshots `data/` and `picks/` as `(st_size, st_mtime_ns)` — **1,066 files in 19ms**, which is why it is fast enough to run on every test. But a file that is **read-then-rewritten byte-identical**, or merely touched, moves its mtime and keeps its size. **The guard called that a mutation and failed the run.**
    🔴 **THE GUARD WAS RIGHT TO FAIL CLOSED.** It could not tell the two apart, and "I cannot tell" must never resolve to "fine" on the directories that hold the permanent record.
    ✅ **THE FIX IS AN ADJUDICATOR, NOT A RELAXATION.** On a suspected change it now asks **`git status --porcelain -- data picks`**, which compares CONTENT. Content changed → fail, as before. Content identical, mtime moved → a **note**, not a failure. Git unavailable or erroring → **the old behaviour: fail.**
    ➡️ **AN INTEGRITY CHECK MAY USE A CHEAP PROXY TO DECIDE WHETHER TO LOOK, NEVER TO DECIDE WHAT IT FOUND.** The proxy screens; something that reads the bytes adjudicates.

189. 🆕 🔴 **THE CARD SAID HOW MANY TIMES AND HOW MANY IT CLEARED, AND NEVER **WHEN** — FOR AS LONG AS RULE 51 HAS EXISTED.** `[2026-09-11 — pre-publish check 36, reported for weeks]`
    ⛔ `claude/card-blueprint.md` STEP 4C is not ambiguous — *"State the gap in days on the card row."* The card printed `3 start(s) vs TOR -- cleared 2/3` and **nothing about when the last one was.**
    🔴 **THOSE ARE DIFFERENT QUESTIONS AND RULE 51 IS ABOUT THE SECOND.** Conditional on a first meeting of 5+ K, the next start reaches 4+ K **74.6% (1363/1826) vs anyone** and **67.1% (116/173) vs the same lineup inside 30 days** — **which is just the league base rate. Whatever edge he had, they have seen it.**
    ⚠️ **A CHECK THAT NAMES A MISSING FIELD IS NOT A CHECK THAT ADDS IT.** Check 36 said this every run and every run was headless — it could neither test nor push. **A defect reported by a run that cannot fix it needs an interactive session to be listed FOR, not just reported BY** (rule 171's other face).
    ✅ The sentence now appends `-- last one N days ago` and, inside 30 days, *"and they have seen him inside a month"*; `h2h_gap_days` and `h2h_rematch` are stored as their own fields so the calibration record gets a NUMBER rather than a sentence to re-parse.
    ⛔ **AND IT TOUCHES NO NUMBER, ENFORCED BY TEST.** Rule 51's PRE-REGISTERED specification **FAILED** (0.30, t=−1.78); only the exploratory threshold split survived. `test_rematch_gap.py` asserts no `h2h_gap` value reaches `blend`, `confidence`, a band, a pair or an edge. ➡️ **A rematch is a reason to DROP A RUNG (~8 points at a 4+ K bar, ~3 at 3+ K), not to drop the pitcher.**

190. 🆕 🔴 **RULE 160 WAS WRITTEN ABOUT A COMMENT IN THIS FILE, AND A DIFFERENT COMMENT IN THE SAME FILE WENT ON BEING WRONG FOR TWO WEEKS.** `[2026-09-11 — the header said 16 crons against FORTY]`
    ⛔ `collect.yml`'s header explains why the schedule was cut from 29 entries to 16 after **GitHub stopped firing every cron in the file for 21 hours on 2026-08-26/27 while manual dispatch kept working.** **The file then grew back to forty and the sentence never moved.**
    🔴 **WRITING THE RULE DID NOT MAKE THE RULE FIRE.** Rule 160 says a comment justifying a schedule is a claim about the schedule and must be measured like one — **and it was written about a comment three lines away from this one.** ⚠️ Same family as `CLAUDE.md`'s standing ban on writing a credit total into a comment: **a number has gone into a comment in this project three times and been wrong twice.**
    ✅ **THE NUMBER STAYS AND IS PINNED, RATHER THAN DELETED** — the 08-26/27 outage is a real reason to know it. The header carries `# CRON COUNT: 40`; `test_cron_wiring.py` counts the entries and asserts it. **Proved by flipping it back to 16 and watching the run exit 1.**
    ⛔ **FORTY IS NOT ENDORSED BY THE CHECK.** It pins the comment to the file. **Whether forty is too many is Sam's question, not a test's** — and if this ever fails, the answer is to count the crons, never to edit the comment.
    ➡️ **A CLAIM IN A COMMENT EITHER GETS A CHECK OR GETS DELETED. Leaving it is choosing the third option, which is that it rots.**

191. 🆕 🔴 **I SHIPPED A TEST AND THE FILE IT ASSERTS ON IN TWO SEPARATE COMMITS, AND THEN WROTE THE INSTRUCTIONS THAT GUARANTEED THE RED.** `[2026-09-11 — drop 26, run 1]`
    ⛔ `test_cron_wiring.py`'s new check asserts on a `# CRON COUNT:` line that exists only in the NEW `collect.yml`. **The web uploader can only target one folder per commit, so the drop was two commits — and my UPLOAD.md put the ROOT FILES FIRST.** ➡️ **The intermediate commit was red before it ever ran, and I authored the order.**
    ⚠️ **I HAD ALREADY MEASURED THE CHECK BITING** — flipped the number to 16, watched it exit 1 — **and read that as proof it worked rather than as a description of the state I was about to create.** The same evidence said both things and I only heard one.
    ✅ **THE RULE: WHEN A TEST AND ITS SUBJECT SHIP IN TWO COMMITS, THE SUBJECT GOES FIRST.** A test landing on a tree that cannot satisfy it is not a transitional inconvenience — it is a red run on `main`, and rule 171 says a red run nobody can act on decays into noise.
    ⛔ **AND DO NOT "FIX" IT BY MAKING THE CHECK TOLERATE THE OLD FILE.** That is weakening a check to make an ordering mistake invisible. **Order the uploads instead.**

192. 🆕 🔴 **RULE 166 FOR THE SEVENTH TIME — AND I WROTE THE FILE THE DAY AFTER RECORDING THE OTHER SIX.** `[2026-09-11 — Sam: "both failed"]`
    ⛔ `test_drop16_fixes.py` asserted **"only ONE college week has completed games."** True when written on 09-10; **false on 09-11 the moment week 2's Thursday games finished.** Two checks went red on a tree where nothing was wrong.
    🔴 **AND THE CODE WAS NEVER WRONG — IT WAS THE GUARD DOING ITS JOB.** Two completed weeks is the constant-column guard **ENGAGING**, which is the entire point of rules 175–177. **The test's premise expired; the thing it was guarding behaved correctly.** ⚠️ **A red that means "the world moved" is indistinguishable, in the log, from a red that means "the code broke" — which is what makes this expensive rather than merely wrong.**
    ✅ **THE REPLACEMENT ASKS A QUESTION THE CALENDAR CANNOT ANSWER FOR US (rule 177):** not *"how many weeks have played today"* but *"does the guard's decision FOLLOW the week count, whatever it is"* — driven through the real `cfb.verify()` at the **ONE-to-TWO week boundary**, the exact edge the 09-10 defect lived on and one the old check could never reach, because it could only observe the day it ran on.
    ⚠️ **THREE MORE TICKING CHECKS IN THE SAME FILE WERE FIXED IN THE SAME PASS** — *"the live college card really is empty"* (×2) would have gone red the first time a college board priced, **on a perfectly good card.** Demoted to notes; the subprocess and sentence checks that carry the content still run, and a non-empty board now reports **NOT EXERCISED** rather than silently passing.
    ➡️ **BEFORE PINNING A FACT, ASK WHAT MAKES IT STOP BEING TRUE — AND IF THE ANSWER IS "THE CALENDAR", PIN THE RELATIONSHIP INSTEAD OF THE VALUE.**

193. 🆕 🔴 **I WROTE "THIS IS COVERED BY test_card_fb.py's SYNTHETIC FIXTURE", CHECKED, AND IT WAS NOT.** `[2026-09-11 — caught before upload, inside the fix for 192]`
    ⛔ Writing a NOT EXERCISED note, I reassured the next reader that another file covered the gap. **`test_card_fb.py` has no such fixture**, and `test_top_plays.py`'s copy of the check **branches on the same live card** — so on a day with rows, the empty-board sentence is exercised by **NOTHING**.
    🔴 **A FALSE "THIS IS COVERED ELSEWHERE" IS WORSE THAN THE GAP IT DESCRIBES**, because it is the sentence that stops anyone looking. Rule 37 — a doc asserting a fix is not a fix — applies to a COMMENT INSIDE A TEST exactly as it applies to a project doc.
    ✅ The note now names the real state: **nothing covers it today**, the fix is a synthetic empty-board fixture that does not depend on the calendar, and it is **recorded rather than invented in the same hurried pass.**
    ➡️ **EVERY "X ALREADY HANDLES THIS" IS A CLAIM. GREP FOR IT BEFORE YOU WRITE IT.**

194. 🆕 🔴 **A CLIENT TIMEOUT IS NOT A FAILED WRITE, AND I DELETED CONTENT ON THE STRENGTH OF ASSUMING IT WAS.** `[2026-09-11 — caught by re-reading `project_info` instead of trusting my own inference]`
    ⛔ `project_write` on this doc returned `timeout of 30000ms exceeded` four times. `project_info` then listed **32 docs with `claude/pick-ledger.md` ABSENT**, and `knowledge_size` read **788,603 of 2,000,000**. I added the doc's raw size to that figure, got **2,008,392**, and concluded the project had hit its knowledge cap **by 8,392 bytes.**
    🔴 **EVERY STEP OF THAT WAS WRONG, AND THE ARITHMETIC LOOKED PERFECT.** The write was **succeeding server-side** while the 30-second client timeout fired; the doc was absent from the listing because it was mid-write; and **`knowledge_size` IS NOT THE SUM OF RAW DOC BYTES** — with the doc restored it reads **1,244,271**, so a 1.2 MB file contributes about **456 KB** to it. **There was never a cap problem.**
    ⚠️ **AND THE COST WAS REAL: I CONDENSED ~17 KB OF CHANGELOG NARRATIVE TO "MAKE IT FIT".** Restored in the same session from a local copy taken before the first trim — ⛔ **which is the only reason this is an anecdote and not a loss** (rule 43: a `project_write` gives no diff, so a deletion leaves no trace).
    ➡️ **BEFORE ACTING ON A FAILURE, RE-READ THE THING ITSELF.** A timeout describes the CLIENT'S PATIENCE, not the server's outcome; `project_info` immediately after says whether the write landed. ⛔ **And never infer a quota from a number you have not confirmed is measured in the units you are adding to it** — *a fact about a query is not a fact about the world*, applied to my own tooling.
    ⚠️ **THE ONE THING WORTH KEEPING FROM THE FALSE ALARM:** this doc is **1.2 MB** and every write on it takes longer than the client waits. **That is not a cap, but it is a reason to expect timeouts here and to verify rather than retry.**

195. 🆕 🔴 **I VERIFIED EVERY VALUE AND NEVER LOOKED AT THE RANKS. 92% OF THEM WERE ARBITRARY.** `[2026-09-11 — Sam asked for the cross-reference and it found something I had not gone looking for]`
    ✅ **THE VALUES WERE FLAWLESS.** Rebuilt independently from the player log, all ELEVEN stats rather than the rushing column I had checked before: **6,116 of 6,116 cells reproduce exactly.** ⚠️ And my own first pass flagged 5 mismatches at USC and UNLV — **the table was right and MY DENOMINATOR was wrong** (both played 2 FBS games; a tight end simply did not record in one, and dividing by "games a TE appeared in" would punish a defence for shutting tight ends out).
    ⛔ **BUT 5,613 OF THE 6,116 PUBLISHED RANKS BROKE A TIE ARBITRARILY.** Both builders ranked with a bare ordinal — `enumerate(rows)` → `i + 1` — so **equal values came out of `sorted()` in whatever order the dict yielded them and each was handed a different rank.** Offense side 91%. The NFL runs the same code.
    🔴 **WHOLE COLUMNS, NOT EDGE CASES: 142 defences ALL at 0.0 interceptions allowed to running backs, ranked 1 through 142.** One team was told it was best in the country and another worst at a statistic where they are IDENTICAL. ⚠️ And not only the junk columns — **QB rushing yards had 93 tied-but-differently-ranked rows, RB rushing 53, WR receiving 50.**
    ➡️ **"ARE THE NUMBERS RIGHT?" AND "IS WHAT WE SAY ABOUT THE NUMBERS RIGHT?" ARE DIFFERENT QUESTIONS, AND A DERIVED COLUMN IS AS PUBLISHED AS A MEASURED ONE.** A rank, a percentile, a bar width and a sort order are all claims. ⛔ Verifying the inputs and stopping is how a page can be perfectly accurate and still tell a reader something false.
    ✅ **THE FIX HAS TWO RULES, AND THE SECOND IS DERIVED FROM THE DATA:** ties share the BEST rank; and **a column where every value is identical publishes NO rank at all.** ⛔ The alternative was a hard-coded list of impossible football — running backs do not throw — which would be a second source of truth about the sport, wrong the first time a running back throws a touchdown, and blind to any column that goes degenerate for a reason nobody predicted.

196. 🆕 🔴 **THREE SECOND COPIES IN ONE DAY, AND THE THIRD WAS INSIDE THE FIX FOR THE FIRST.** `[2026-09-11]`
    ⛔ **(1) THE RANKING.** Fixed in `cfb.py` and `nfl.py` — and `index.html` **ranked again in JavaScript**, same ordinal, same defect. Fixing only the builders would have left the reader looking at the bug.
    ⛔ **(2) THE METRIC LIST.** The fix for (1) filtered the picker to metrics the loaded file actually carries — and `fbControls()` **built its own `fbMetricsFor(fbPos)`**, so the TABLE used the filtered list and the DROPDOWN offered everything. *"Total yards"* appeared in the picker against a table that had no such column.
    ⛔ **(3) THE BUILDERS THEMSELVES** were byte-identical blocks in two files, which is how the rule got to be wrong in both at once.
    🔴 **THIS IS RULE 66 — one number per fact, the same everywhere — AND IT KEEPS BEING BROKEN IN THE SAME DIRECTION:** the SOURCE gets fixed and a DISPLAY keeps its own copy. `card-blueprint.md` once held v4.0's coefficients where a card builder read them first; the page once computed its own projection.
    ➡️ **WHEN YOU FIX A DERIVED VALUE, GREP FOR EVERY PLACE IT IS DERIVED — INCLUDING THE PAGE.** ✅ And where two files held the identical block, the fix was **one shared module both import**, so "everything for CFB we do for NFL" is structural rather than remembered.

197. 🆕 🔴 **THE SOURCE CHECKS WERE GREEN AND THE RENDERED PAGE WAS WRONG.** `[2026-09-11 — found by loading the deployed site in a headless browser, after the test file already passed]`
    ⛔ `test_ranking.py` asserted the metric filter **EXISTED**. It did. **Asserting a filter exists is not asserting that anything USES it** — the same gap as *"is anything there?"* versus *"is anything wrong?"* (rule 178), in a new costume.
    🔴 **WHAT CAUGHT IT WAS RENDERING THE PAGE AND READING THE DOM**: the dropdown offered *"Total yards"* against a table built the previous night with no such column. **No source check in the repo could see that, because every one of them was reading the same file that contained the correct filter.**
    ✅ **A PAGE THAT RENDERS IS A DIFFERENT TEST FROM SOURCE THAT READS CORRECTLY.** The same pass also produced the only real proof the fix worked: **Fresno State and Tennessee State both at 410.0, both rank 7, next row rank 9** — competition ranking, visible on screen.
    ➡️ **FOR ANY CHANGE A READER CAN SEE, RENDER IT BEFORE CALLING IT DONE.** ⚠️ Chromium and Playwright are already in the container and the repo is a static site — `python3 -m http.server` over a worktree at `origin/main` is the whole setup.

198. 🆕 🔴 **TWO CHECKS COVERED THE SENTENCE AND BOTH READ THE LIVE CARD, SO ONLY THE CALENDAR DECIDED WHICH BRANCH RAN.** `[2026-09-11 — the fix for rule 179, a day later]`
    ⛔ The 09-10 defect — an EMPTY board told *"Not because the board is empty"* — was fixed the same day. **But `test_drop16_fixes.py` §6 branched `if _empty:` and `test_top_plays.py` branched `elif not C["picks"]:`, both on the live card.** ➡️ **On a day with rows priced, the empty-board sentence was exercised by NOTHING**, and the only thing between that and a repeat was which day of the week it was. **The college season had just started.**
    ⚠️ **AND I NEARLY PAPERED OVER IT** — the first NOT EXERCISED note I wrote said the branch was *"covered by `test_card_fb.py`'s synthetic fixture."* **I checked. It is not** (rule 193).
    ✅ **THE SENTENCE IS NOW A NAMED FUNCTION** — `empty_top_plays_sentence(slate, n_board)` — drivable with no board, no data tree and no date. **Proved to bite by reintroducing the 09-10 defect: three checks went red, including the one that quotes the false sentence back.**
    ➡️ **A RULE THE PRODUCT PRINTS SHOULD BE A RULE A TEST CAN DRIVE.** Third extraction for this exact reason after `nfl.check_ahead_out()` and `card.h2h_gap_days()`. ⛔ **It does not replace the rebuild-and-read check** — that one tests the PLUMBING, this one tests the RULE, and the plumbing test can only ever run the branch the calendar hands it.

199. 🆕 🔴 **A t-STATISTIC IS NOT A PREDICTION. THE FOURTH FOOTBALL MODEL LOST, AND THE ONE TERM THAT REACHED SIGNIFICANCE IS WHY THE RULE IS WORTH WRITING.** `[2026-09-11 — T57, pre-registered in `claude/owed-tests.md` before the data was seen, CLOSED-FAILED]`
    ⚠️ **Sam's idea was good and it was tested properly.** `research/t57_fit.py` is `research/t47_fit.py` with three predictors added and **nothing else touched**, so the sample is identical — 2,874 rows, 1,361 fit, 1,513 test, and **100% of T47's rows carried a game line**, so the join cost nothing.
    ```
                                              Brier      MAE
    T57 (T47 + spread + implied + game script)  0.2555    17.20
    naive season-to-date                        0.2545    17.10   <- beat us
    PRIMARY Brier −0.0010 vs +0.0050 · SECONDARY MAE worse · SUBGROUP RB −0.0174
    ```
    ✅ **`own_implied` WAS SIGNIFICANT — +0.84 receiving yards per implied point, t = +2.73.** The market line genuinely says something about the matchup and it is measurable. ⛔ **AND IT GAVE NOTHING OUT OF SAMPLE.** In-sample significance with no held-out gain means the information is already inside trailing form, or is too small to beat a player's own season average. **The t-statistic was the most persuasive number produced all session and it predicted nothing.**
    🔴 **THE GAME-SCRIPT MECHANISM GOT TWO SHOTS AND IS NULL BOTH TIMES:** `spread × RB` t = −1.08 and `spread × WR` t = −0.66 in the primary; then the pre-registered secondary tested it head-on on **RB rushing yards**, where "the favourite runs more late" should bite hardest, and it **failed harder** (Brier −0.0087, MAE worse by 1.91).
    ⚠️ **TWO THINGS CORRECTED OR IMPOSSIBLE, BOTH RECORDED RATHER THAN SKIPPED:** the spec said 2021–2025 and that was **wrong — fixed BEFORE any fit**, which made the sample identical to the prior tests rather than merely similar; and the line-timing arm **could not run at all**, because only CLOSING lines are archived and card-time lines were never stored (moot here — the friendlier closing line already failed).
    ➡️ **THE PRE-COMMITTED CONSEQUENCE FIRED: THE MARKET-LINE ROUTE IS CLOSED.** T46 +0.0010 · T47 −0.0041 · T50 −0.0036 · T57 −0.0010 — **four specifications, all beaten by a player's own season average.** The reopening condition returns to charted data this project does not own, and the spec forbids a college replication unless the NFL passes. ⛔ **The value of a pre-registration is that it decides this BEFORE the number is seen; a fifth specification chosen after four failures is a search, not a test.**

200. 🆕 🔴 **HE ASKED FOR "THE SAME MODEL" AND THERE IS NO SUCH MODEL — SO THE LIST RANKS SOMETHING MEASURABLE INSTEAD OF INVENTING A FORECAST.** `[2026-09-11 — Game Lines shipped, drop 32]`
    `[Sam]` *"i also would like you to start adding moneyline, team total, spread... you would use the same model to make these picks."* ⛔ **Football has no model at all** (rule 199), **and MLB's prices pitcher strikeouts and outs — it has no opinion about who wins a game.** A *"model's best 15"* here would have been **a number nobody measured, on a page bet against with real money.**
    ✅ **WHAT SHIPPED RANKS HOW MUCH MORE THE BEST BOOK PAYS THAN A TYPICAL ONE, ON THE SAME WAGER** — `[measured on the live college board]` **276 comparable quotes, best gains 3–5%, median 0.93%.** It is arithmetic on quotes already stored, **it makes no forecast, so it cannot be wrong about a game**, which is precisely why it is allowed to carry a number when nothing else on the football side is.
    🔴 **THE EXACT SIGNED NUMBER, ALWAYS.** Groups are keyed `(market, side, SIGNED point)` — the live MLB case where eleven books posted ATL −1.5 and two posted it inverted means matching on `|point|` pairs **opposite bets** and advertises a bargain that does not exist. **3+ books at the same number or it claims nothing; one row per game; every row labelled MARKET** (rule 55), and the test asserts no row carries a confidence, probability or projection.
    ⚠️ **AND THE HONEST CAVEAT IS ON THE PAGE, NOT JUST HERE:** Sam is right that these markets are more popular — **popularity and sharpness are the same thing.** They carry the most money and are the most efficiently priced. The section is useful and it is **not a route to an edge over the book.**
    🔴 **THE BROWSER CAUGHT A BUG THE SOURCE COULD NOT, FOR THE SECOND TIME IN TWO DAYS** (rule 197): on an **empty props board** the picks tab returned before reaching the new section, so **Game Lines never drew.** Invisible reading the source, obvious the moment the page loaded. ⛔ **A props board and a game-line board are DIFFERENT MARKETS** — a Thursday with no props priced still has 98 games with spreads and totals.
    ➡️ **WHEN THE HONEST ANSWER IS "THERE IS NO MODEL", SHIP THE MEASUREMENT AND LABEL IT — DO NOT DRESS A PRICE AS A PROBABILITY.** ⚠️ **And if a team-bet model is ever wanted, the bar is not "beat a naive average": for game lines the MARKET is the benchmark, and the test would have to beat the CLOSING LINE.**

201. 🆕 🔴 **A THRESHOLD FOR WHEN A COLUMN CAN BE JUDGED IS A PROPERTY OF THE COLUMN, NOT OF THE CHECK.** `[2026-09-11 — the collect run red again, one week after rule 175, on a healthy build]`
    ```
    RuntimeError: ahead_out_lastwk is CONSTANT 0 across 1,870 rows
    spanning 2 week(s) [1, 2] — a join failure, not a result
    ```
    ⛔ **It was not a join failure**, and the 09-10 fix had set the bar at **two distinct weeks for all three point-in-time columns. One of the three needs THREE.** `ahead_out_lastwk` counts players **ahead of you** who **did not play last week**; to be *ahead* you must have a trailing usage, which requires having played a **strictly earlier** week; in week 2 the only earlier week is week 1, **so anyone ahead of you necessarily PLAYED week 1 and cannot have been out last week.** ➡️ **Mutually exclusive — the column is zero BY CONSTRUCTION until week 3, and no amount of real data changes that.** Proved with a fixture rather than argued: 2 weeks → all zero; 3 weeks with the ahead player missing the MIDDLE one → reaches 1; 3 weeks missing the LAST one → still zero, correctly lagged.
    ⛔ **THE FIX IS PER COLUMN, NOT A BLANKET BUMP.** `depth_rank` and `trailing_usage` **do** vary on a two-week log, and they are where a genuinely broken join shows up **first**; raising all three to 3 would stop judging them for an extra week **to silence one column.** Their floor of 2 is unchanged.
    ⚠️ **AND SAM'S STANDING RULE — *"everything we do for CFB we do for NFL"* — CORRECTLY DID NOT APPLY, WHICH IS THE HARDER HALF.** The NFL's `out_set` is an **injury report keyed (player, week)**: a player can be OUT in week 2 **and** have played week 1, so the two conditions are **independent** and that column varies from week 2. College publishes **no injury report and no snap data**, so absence is *inferred* from "did not play last week" — which is what creates the exclusion. **Same name, different question, different floor: a documented RE-SPECIFICATION, not a copy.** ⛔ **Mirroring it would have broken a working guard**, and the only way to know was to open `nfl.py` and read what its column actually measures (rule 174 in reverse).
    ⚠️ **ONE EXISTING CHECK WENT RED FOR THE RIGHT REASON:** `test_drop16_fixes.py` asserted **three** findings at two weeks — correct when written the day before, made wrong by this fix. ✅ **Rather than loosen it to `>= 2`, it now names WHICH columns engage at WHICH week** — two at week 2, three at week 3 — **strictly more precise than the count it replaced, and exactly the knowledge the failure bought.**

202. 🆕 🔴 **TWO FIXTURES MADE THEIR CHECKS PASS FOR A REASON OTHER THAN THE ONE THEY CLAIMED, AND BOTH WERE CAUGHT ONLY BY MAKING THEM FAIL FIRST.** `[2026-09-11 — writing `test_game_lines.py` and `test_ahead_out_weeks.py`]`
    ⛔ **(1) "ONLY SAM'S FIVE BOOKS COUNT"** priced all three eligible books **identically**, so the row was dropped for having **no price gap** — and the check asserted on an **empty list**. It could not distinguish *"the outside book was correctly excluded"* from *"there was nothing to rank at all"*, which is the entire question it exists to answer. Fixed by giving the eligible books a real gap.
    ⛔ **(2) "A CONSTANT COLUMN IS STILL JUDGED AT TWO WEEKS"** built a fixture whose `depth_rank` **varied by player**, so the guard had nothing to catch — and the check **failed on correct code.** A constant column is what the guard is for, so the fixture has to contain one.
    ⚠️ **THESE ARE THE SAME DEFECT IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS — a false green and a false red — and both come from writing the assertion before checking what the fixture actually produces.**
    ➡️ **A CHECK IS NOT WRITTEN UNTIL IT HAS BEEN SEEN TO FAIL ON THE THING IT IS ABOUT.** ✅ This is the discipline that proved rule 185's band flag (flip a digit), rule 190's cron count (flip it to 16) and rule 198's empty-board sentence (reintroduce the 09-10 defect) — **and it is cheap enough that there is no excuse for skipping it.** ⛔ Green is not evidence; **green after a deliberate red is.**

203. 🆕 🔴 **A DELIBERATE STAND-DOWN EXITS 0 AND WRITES NOTHING, SO FROM OUTSIDE THE REPO IT IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM A CRON THAT NEVER FIRED.** `[2026-09-11, watching the first unattended CFB rebuild live from 07:18Z to 09:36Z]`
    ⚠️ **This one is an OBSERVABILITY hole, not a defect — the back-off behaved exactly as designed**, and the design is right: `cfb-probe` holds an **unconditional 3-hour stand-down after a failed back-fill** because a rebuild is ~15 CFBD calls and retrying our own bug hourly would spend the month (the 09-06..09 storm: 11 attempts, every one a 429).
    ```
    05:58:38Z   cfb-probe FAILED (pre-fix code)  -> consecutive failures: 1
    06:23Z      the FIX landed
    07:04Z      the 3:04am ET cron  -> SKIPPED, exit 0, NO COMMIT, no log we can read
    08:58:38Z   back-off expires
    09:32Z      the hourly converge attempts it  ->  written: [2026]
    ```
    ⛔ **THE FIX LANDED 25 MINUTES AFTER THE FAILURE THAT STARTED THE CLOCK**, so the cron everyone was waiting on fired into a closed door — and because the stand-down was correctly demoted from `exit 1` on 09-09 (*"46 football runs a day, every one red"*), **it left no commit, no red run and no artifact.** ➡️ **"Fired and stood down" and "never fired" produce byte-identical evidence**, on a project whose entire architecture rests on *"the crons are just chances to land"* (rule 137/165).
    ⚠️ **AND THE PREVIOUS FAILURE'S REPORT WAS STILL THE NEWEST THING ON DISK, WHICH IS A TRAP** — reading `backfill-report.txt` at 07:30Z showed `failed: [2026]` stamped **25 minutes before the fix existed.** ⛔ **That is rule 181 again: a stored artifact tests its AGE, not the code.** Anyone checking "did the fix work?" against it would have concluded the fix failed.
    ➡️ **A DECISION NOT TO ACT IS AN EVENT, AND EVENTS BELONG IN THE REPO.** ✅ The three things that DO say so — out of contract, the `::warning::` from `verify_freshness.py`, the Trends tab printing its own age — all describe the ARTIFACT's staleness; **none of them records that a run considered the mode and declined.** ⚠️ **The cheap fix is a line in the freshness payload** (`stood_down_until`, written by the stand-down branch), so the page and the next session can both tell the difference. ⛔ Until that exists, **never infer "the cron did not fire" from an absent commit.**

205. 🆕 🔴 **THE SECTION SHIPPED WITHOUT A LABEL, ON THE ONE PAGE WHOSE WHOLE DISCIPLINE IS LABELLING.** `[2026-09-11 — found by reading the rendered DOM, after `test_game_lines.py` was already green]`

    every other section on that tab  ->  a badge
    the only badge on the picks tab  ->  "Record", on the block ABOVE
                                          a numbered list of percentages

    ⛔ **LEDGER RULE 55 REQUIRES EVERY NUMBER ON THE SURFACE TO BE LABELLED MODEL, MARKET OR DESCRIPTIVE**, and a list of price gaps sitting under a *Record* badge is the exact confusion that rule exists to stop. ⚠️ The panel *did* say **"Market, not a model"** in prose — **but a prose sentence is not the badge the rest of the page trained the reader to scan for.**
    🔴 **AND THE NOTE CARRIED THE WRONG GLYPH.** It read `⚪ Market, not a model.` — **`⚪` is this page's DESCRIPTIVE marker**, introducing the word *"Descriptive."* in four other places; `🔵` is what it uses twice for *"these are the sportsbooks' numbers, not ours"*, which is precisely this claim. ➡️ **One vocabulary, or it is not a vocabulary.**
    ⚠️ **WHY THE TEST WAS GREEN THROUGH ALL OF IT:** it asserts **no row carries a confidence** and **the section draws.** ⛔ **Neither asks whether the drawn section is LABELLED.** ➡️ **ASSERTING A WRONG THING IS ABSENT IS NOT ASSERTING THE RIGHT THING IS PRESENT** — rule 197's third instance in two days, and the sharpest phrasing of it yet.

206. 🆕 🔴 **A CHECK THAT REASONS IN OFFSETS FROM ONE ANCHOR ABOUT A WINDOW MEASURED FROM ANOTHER WORKS BY LUCK, AND THE LUCK RAN OUT.** `[2026-09-11 — three test files red on live main, none of them a code bug]`
    ⛔ **`test_parity.py` PROBED THE PROPS CONTRACT AT FIXED OFFSETS FROM THE FIRST KICKOFF (−3d, −13h). The window is anchored to the props DEADLINE.** `_props_warranted` asks whether a kickoff falls inside `[last_due(now), last_due(now) + 14h]`, so an offset measured from a KICKOFF lands wherever the deadline grid happens to put it. **Measured:**
    ```
    −3d   Thu 17:00Z  -> deadline Thu 15:00Z, window to Fri 05:00Z
                         THURSDAY NIGHT FOOTBALL at 00:35Z is INSIDE it
                         -> warranted      (the check demanded ABSENT)
    −13h  Sun 04:00Z  -> last deadline was SATURDAY 15:00Z, 26h out
                         -> not warranted  (the check demanded ARMED)
    ```
    ➡️ **BOTH ASSERTIONS WERE BACKWARDS AND THE CODE WAS RIGHT.** ✅ The probes are now **derived from the deadline grid and the real schedule**, so no offset anyone chose is load-bearing.
    🔴 **`test_nfl_opener.py` ASSERTED SOMETHING TRUE ONLY OF A NIGHT KICKOFF** — *"the first NFL slate day is EARLIER in ET than in UTC."* True while week 1's Thursday opener (00:20Z) led the board; **false the moment a Sunday 17:00Z game did.** ✅ The lesson it protected is kept and made permanent — **the slate day is obtained by CONVERTING, never by slicing the UTC string** — now driven through **`freshness.et_date`, the repo's own converter**, on a constructed case, **in both directions.** ⛔ **Its first replacement asserted arithmetic written on its own line, which proves nothing about the code**; driving the real converter is the difference between a check and a restatement.
    ⚠️ **AND *"the opening slate is a SINGLE game"* IS NOW REPORTED, NOT PINNED** — with 13 games on it, **"the parlay pool is empty by construction" is no longer an available explanation**, which is information the check should surface rather than assert away.
    🔴 **`test_drop16_fixes.py` ASSERTED THE EMPTY-BOARD REFUSAL CLAUSE UNCONDITIONALLY — THREE LINES BELOW THE `if _empty:` GUARD THE SAME FILE ALREADY APPLIES.** `top_plays_rule` is that sentence **only while the board is empty**; the first college board to price made it the real rule and the needle correctly vanished. **A straggler outside the branch written for it.** ✅ Moved inside, and the priced branch now asserts the two sentences are **NOT interchangeable** — which is the 09-10 defect's actual shape.
    ➡️ **RULE 166's EIGHTH, NINTH AND TENTH INSTANCES, ALL IN ONE MORNING.** ⛔ **The pattern is not "dates are tricky" — it is that a check's PREMISE is itself a claim, and an unexamined premise expires silently while the check keeps reporting green.** ✅ **ASK OF EVERY ASSERTION: what would have to change in the world for this to stop being true, and is that thing allowed to change?**

207. 🆕 🔴 **THE VERIFIER KEPT ITS OWN COPY OF THE MODEL, AND BLOCKED A CORRECT CARD EVERY FIFTEEN MINUTES FOR SEVEN HOURS.** `[2026-09-11 — Sam: *"the recurring collect run is failing on the hour"* and *"drop 33 failed"*; ONE cause, and drop 33 was fine]`
    ```
    FAIL Normal outs probability recomputed from the raw log
         max gap 1.8258 pts        (the bar is 0.06)
    ```
    ⛔ **`verify_card.py` RECOMPUTES THE OUTS PROBABILITY FROM THE RAW LOG, AND TO DO IT IT HARD-CODED EIGHT OF THE OUTS COEFFICIENTS. THEY WERE v4.0's.** `card.py` shipped **v5.0 at 01:11Z**; from **11:13Z** every MLB converge pass wrote `card-verify-failure.txt`, **reverted the card and exited 1** — about thirty passes in a row.
    ✅ **THE CARD WAS RIGHT, AND IT WAS MEASURED RATHER THAN ARGUED** — same rows, same code path, only the constants swapped:
    ```
    verify_card.py's literals (v4.0)   max gap 1.8258 pts   FAIL
    card.py's own constants (v5.0)     max gap 0.0424 pts   PASS
    ```
    **All eight differ; the tier-mid trailing slope alone moved 0.647 → 0.759**, which is most of the gap. `verify_card.py` now reports **ALL CHECKS PASSED, 92 of 92.**
    🔴 **AND IT DID MORE THAN TURN THE RUN RED — A FAILING VERIFY REVERTS THE MLB CARD**, so Gizmo's baseball card sat at **14:07Z for four and a half hours. RED BUILD, FROZEN PRODUCT**, which is the exact state the workflow's own 09-04 comment was written about.
    ⚠️ **IT SHIPPED WITH v5.0 AND STAYED INVISIBLE FOR TEN HOURS.** The gap only clears 0.06 when the board prices an outs row in the tier where the stale slope bites hardest. ➡️ **A SECOND COPY DRIFTS SILENTLY UNTIL THE DATA HAPPENS TO EXPOSE IT** — which means the interval between shipping the defect and seeing it is set by the market, not by the code.
    ⛔ **THIS WAS NOT A CHECK TO WEAKEN, AND THE DISTINCTION IS THE WHOLE POINT** (`CLAUDE.md`'s first rule). **The tolerance is UNCHANGED at 0.06** and the recomputation is **still fully independent** — mu, the prior SD, the z-score and the Normal CDF are all still rebuilt from the raw log by a second and different route, reading no computed value back off the card. **What changed is that the COEFFICIENTS are no longer restated**: they are the model's SPECIFICATION, owned by `claude/mlb-projection-model.md`. ⚠️ **AND NO PROTECTION IS LOST — this check never guarded the coefficients' VALUES**, because a wrong coefficient moves the card and the recomputation together and was always invisible here. `test_model_version.py` guards the values, by fingerprinting them beside the stamp.
    🔴 **RULE 66's THIRD PLACE, AND THE MOST POINTED ONE:** `card-blueprint.md` held v4.0's coefficients where a builder read them first; `index.html` ranked a second time in JavaScript; **and the VERIFIER kept its own model — the one file whose entire job is to disagree with the card.**
    ✅ **SO THE COPY IS STRUCTURALLY BANNED, NOT JUST DELETED.** `test_model_version.py` now takes **`card.py`'s CURRENT values** and fails if any other source file contains one of their literals. ➡️ **The needles are DERIVED, so the search re-aims itself at the next re-fit** instead of going stale the way the copy did; struck lines in comments are excluded, because keeping a superseded coefficient visibly beside its replacement is this project's own convention. **Proved to bite** by reintroducing the three tier slopes. ⛔ It also asserts `verify_card.py` **still rebuilds mu itself**, so the fix can never be "simplified" into `mu = card.outs_mu(...)`: **importing a CONSTANT is not importing a CALCULATION**, and the second one would make the verifier reuse the arithmetic it exists to check.
    ➡️ **THE RULE THIS LEAVES BEHIND: A CHECK THAT RECOMPUTES A PUBLISHED NUMBER MUST IMPORT THE SPECIFICATION AND REBUILD THE ARITHMETIC.** ⛔ Restating the specification is not independence — it is a second model, and it will eventually disagree with the first about a card that is perfectly correct.

208. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A CARD THAT FAILED ITS OWN VERIFICATION WAS PUBLISHED, BECAUSE `git checkout` CANNOT REVERT A FILE GIT HAS NEVER SEEN.** `[2026-09-11 — found while verifying drop 34, in the same commit that carried the failure report]`
    ```
    9ab382f 14:07Z   picks/2026-09-11.json                  ADDED
                     data/latest/card-verify-failure.txt    written
    ```
    ⛔ **SAME COMMIT.** The workflow had already set `rc=1` and logged *"nobody has accepted -- reverting the card"*. **The revert did nothing:**
    ```
    git checkout -- "picks/$(date -u +%Y-%m-%d).json" 2>/dev/null || true
    ```
    🔴 **`git checkout` CANNOT REVERT AN UNTRACKED FILE.** On the **FIRST card build of any day** the card is brand new, so the line exits 1, changes nothing, and the `git add -A` a few lines below **stages it as an addition and publishes it.** ⛔ **`|| true` converted "I could not withhold this card" into silence.** Measured in a throwaway repo rather than assumed: `error: pathspec ... did not match any file(s)`, exit 1, file untouched, then staged as `A`.
    ⚠️ **TODAY'S CARD WAS NOT WRONG** — the failing check was the stale-coefficient one (rule 207) and the card's own numbers verify at a 0.0424 pt gap. ⛔ **BUT THAT IS LUCK, NOT A SAFETY PROPERTY.** `CLAUDE.md`'s first rule is that a card failing its checks does not reach a page Sam bets money against, and **for the first build of every single day that rule was not in force.**
    ➡️ **A REVERT IS A STATE TRANSITION, AND "THE FILE DOES NOT EXIST YET" IS ONE OF THE STATES.** ✅ The fix asks git which state it is in: a card git KNOWS is restored to its committed bytes; a card git has NEVER SEEN is **deleted**; and **a revert that cannot be done sets `rc=1` and logs `::error::`** — failing to withhold a card is the most important thing that step can report.
    ✅ **AND THE TEST EXECUTES THE SHIPPED SHELL RATHER THAN A DESCRIPTION OF IT.** `test_card_revert.py` lifts the `for _c in ... done` block **out of `.github/workflows/collect.yml`** and runs it in throwaway repos, so what is proved is the deployed block and not a paraphrase that can drift (rule 66). **Both branches driven, and the STRUCK LINE driven too** on the identical untracked case — observed to leave the unverified card in place and then be staged as `A` (rule 202).
    ⚠️ **ITS FIRST FIXTURE WAS WRONG IN A WAY WORTH KEEPING:** it put both cases in one repo keyed on the block's own two dates, and **for ten hours of every day those are the SAME date** — `date -u -d '10 hours ago'` does not roll over until 10:00Z — so both cards were written to one path, the untracked case never existed, and the check failed on correct code. **Rule 202's fixture half, twice in two days.**
    ⛔ **AND ONE CORRECTION OF MY OWN, RECORDED RATHER THAN QUIETLY DROPPED:** I told Sam the MLB card was *"frozen at 14:07Z for four and a half hours."* **I had not checked, and it was false** — the card's deadline is 10:00 ET, it was built at 10:07am ET, and the freshness planner reports it **inside contract**. The damage was the red run, not a stale card (rule 193 again).

209. 🆕 🔴 **A SECOND, STALE COPY OF THE WORKFLOW IS SITTING IN THE REPO ROOT.** `[2026-09-11]`
    `collect.yml` at the repo root is **771 lines and pre-drop-26**. The live file is `.github/workflows/collect.yml` at **809 lines**, and the root copy still carries the struck sentence *"the count is 16"* against forty crons — **the exact claim rule 190 was written about.**
    ⚠️ **Nothing reads it.** `test_cron_wiring.py` reads `.github/workflows/collect.yml`; GitHub runs only the one under `.github/`. ⛔ **Which is precisely what makes it dangerous:** it is a plausible-looking second source of truth that no check will ever contradict, sitting one directory away from the file it disagrees with.
    ➡️ **DELETE IT.** ⛔ A GitHub file upload cannot remove a file — this one needs the trash-can button in the UI, so it is on Sam's list rather than in a drop.

210. 🆕 🔴 **THE BUDGET TOOL ADDED A CEILING TO A MEASUREMENT, CALLED THE SUM THE TOTAL, AND PRINTED A RED STOP-INSTRUCTION OFF IT.** `[2026-09-11 — found while pricing the halftime markets Sam parked]`
    ```
    printed:   TOTAL 42,773/month of 20,000  (214%)
               🔴 ABOVE 90% OF PLAN. ⛔ Do not add a second props pull per game day.
    actual:    TOTAL 11,670/month of 20,000  ( 58%)  -- 8,330 of headroom
    ```
    ⛔ **THE MEASURED-SPEND READER GLOBBED `data/20*/*/*.json.gz`, WHICH IS MLB ONLY.** Football snapshots live at `data/ncaaf/20*/...` and `data/nfl/20*/...` and carry the **same `credits_used` field** — 58 of them this month. **Nothing was looking at them**, so the football half was a **cron CEILING** while the MLB half was a **MEASUREMENT**, and the two were **added together** and compared with the plan as if they were one kind of number. **Football ceiling 34,073/month against a measured 2,970 — FOURTEEN TIMES.**
    ⚠️ **THE CEILING IS NOT WRONG AS A CEILING.** It prices every props cron as if it fires on a full slate, and `freshness._props_warranted()` stands most of them down because a pull with no kickoff inside the window buys nothing. ➡️ **THE DEFECT WAS PRESENTING IT AS A FORECAST.**
    ✅ **BOTH NUMBERS ARE PRINTED NOW, BOTH LABELLED, AND THE VERDICT IS TAKEN FROM THE MEASUREMENT** — with the ceiling kept on screen and the condition attached, because **the headroom is conditional and deleting the ceiling would trade one misleading number for another.**
    🔴 **THIS IS THE PROJECT'S FOUNDING LESSON IN A COST TOOL: a fact about a QUERY — the cron schedule — written down as a fact about the WORLD, which is what the account is billed.** ⛔ And `CLAUDE.md` names this script as the budget authority, so the wrong number had standing.
    ⚠️ **AND MY OWN FIX HAD A BUG THAT THE EXIT CODE HID.** The first form indexed the day from the **start** of the path, which reads a segment of the ABSOLUTE path: every football snapshot filed under the day **"claude"**, and the report printed `817/day` off one bucket. **The script ran and exited 0.** ➡️ **READ THE OUTPUT, NOT THE EXIT CODE** — rule 202's other half.

211. 🆕 🔴 **THE STAMP WAS RIGHT AND THE PROSE WAS LYING — TWO OF THE THREE STRINGS WERE ON THE PAGE.** `[2026-09-11]`
    `card.py` has shipped `MODEL_VERSION = "v5.0"` since 01:11Z. **Three strings still said v4.0:** `confidence_note` (*"v4.0 model blended 50/50 with his own rate at this line"*), the MODEL parlay label (*"Every leg is a v4.0 model estimate"*), and the module docstring. ⛔ **THE FIRST TWO REACH THE READER** — a false provenance claim on a page real money is bet against.
    ⛔ **THIS IS RULE 184 INVERTED.** There a version stamp was stale and the code was right; here **the stamp was correct and the sentences beside it were not.** ⚠️ **And I had filed these as *"prose only, no math reads them"* and left them to ride along with the next `card.py` change. That was the wrong call**: no number was wrong, and a reader checking which model produced their number got the wrong answer for ten hours after v5.0 shipped.
    ✅ **A VERSION IS A FACT ABOUT THE CODE. READ IT, NEVER RETYPE IT.** All three interpolate `MODEL_VERSION`, confirmed on a real card build — **`v4.0` appears nowhere in `picks/<date>.json` and `v5.0` appears 26 times.**
    ✅ **AND THE COPY IS STRUCTURALLY BANNED:** `test_model_version.py` **walks `card.py`'s AST** and fails if any string that can reach the card file or the page carries a version in prose. ⚠️ **It walks the AST rather than grepping lines ON PURPOSE — docstrings and comments are EXEMPT**, because they are where this project records what a number used to be, and **a line search would punish the very convention that documents the fix.**
    ⛔ **AND ONE CHECKLIST ITEM WAS WRONG AND IS STRUCK.** ~~*"stale `calibration_warning` figures"*~~ — **there are none.** `calibration_sentence()` is built from `CAL`, which `load_calibration()` reads out of `data/latest/record.json` at runtime, and its own docstring already says it *"fails to silence, not to a stale number."* ➡️ **I wrote that item down without checking it and carried it for two days** (rule 193, a fourth time).

212. 🆕 🔴 **A CHECK READ AN EXIT CODE THAT THREE UNRELATED MODES ALSO WRITE TO, AND WENT RED ON UNCHANGED CODE.** `[2026-09-11]`
    `test_source_block.py` asserts that a back-off **stand-down exits 0** — the 09-09 change that ended *"46 red football runs a day"* for a decision the collector had correctly made. ⛔ **It read that exit code from `collect.py <mode>`, which routes through `converge(explicit=<mode>)` and BY DESIGN also rebuilds every other overdue artifact for the league.** So `gamelines`, `props-player` and `props-board` ran in the same process and **their exit landed in the number the check reads.**
    ⚠️ **IT PASSED FOR HOURS AND THEN FAILED WITH NOTHING IN THE REPO CHANGED**, purely because those three fell out of contract during the day. The stand-down itself was working perfectly throughout: *"SKIPPING cfb-probe … 179 min of back-off left. NOTHING FETCHED."*
    ➡️ **RULE 166 IN ITS SUBTLEST FORM SO FAR: NOTHING WAS PINNED TO A DATE. The check and three other modes were SHARING ONE SIGNAL, and the signal stopped meaning what the sentence claimed the moment the other writers changed state.**
    ✅ **THE FIX IS STRICTER, NOT LOOSER:** `collect.py <mode> converge-off` runs the named mode **alone**, so the exit code asserted is the exit code of the thing the sentence is about, and the pass can no longer mask or manufacture it.
    ⚠️ **It is almost certainly not why any live run went red** — on the runner those modes have a network and a key. ⛔ **But a check that can go red for a reason unrelated to its own sentence is a check nobody will trust the next time it fires**, which is how the 09-10 red runs cost a whole day.

213. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A TEST WAS SPENDING REAL MONEY, AND THE SANDBOX IT SPENT IT INTO WAS DELETED THREE LINES LATER.** `[2026-09-11 — found chasing the red collect run Sam was emailed about]`
    ⛔ **`test_source_block.py` ran every sandbox with `env=dict(os.environ, ...)`, WHICH INHERITS `ODDS_API_KEY` ON THE RUNNER** — and `collect.py <mode>` routes through `converge(explicit=...)`, which by design rebuilds **every other overdue artifact for the league**. ➡️ **So a check about a CFBD BACK-OFF was firing real `gamelines` and `props-player` pulls into a `tempfile.mkdtemp()` it then removed. The credits were spent and the data was thrown away.**
    🔴 **AND THE SPEND IS INVISIBLE TO THE BUDGET.** `daily_spend()` measures the snapshots on disk, and these snapshots never survived the test. **`daily_spend()`'s own docstring already called itself a FLOOR for exactly this reason** — *"a paid call that fails to write a snapshot is invisible here"* — **and this is an instance of it.** ⛔ **Rule 210's corrected "measured" figure is therefore a floor too, and this sits underneath it.** ⚠️ **The magnitude is NOT stated anywhere, because it is not knowable from outside the runner. The mechanism is certain; putting a number on the bill would be the exact error rule 210 exists to stop.**
    🔴 **IT IS ALSO WHAT TURNED THE RUN RED, AND THE INTERMITTENCY IS THE PROOF.** Measured on `origin/main` with **nothing in the repo changed**: **green 18:58Z · RED 19:05Z · green 19:34Z**, as `gamelines`, `props-player` and `props-board` fell out of contract and the 19:16Z pull brought them back in. **The stand-down under test was working perfectly throughout** — *"SKIPPING cfb-probe … 179 min of back-off left. NOTHING FETCHED."*
