    ✅ **RULED OUT FIRST, BY MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN BY ELIMINATION-BY-HOPE:** 57 tests green on `origin/main` at the time of diagnosis; **no `card-verify-failure.txt`**; both back-fills `failed: []`; **`verify_freshness.py` PASSES for all three leagues**; `freshness.json` `ok: true` everywhere; the collector still committing. ➡️ **The workflow's deferred test step is a HARD failure at the end — the run goes red while the data still commits, which is precisely the shape Sam saw.**
    ✅ **TWO INDEPENDENT GUARDS, ON PURPOSE.** `converge-off` makes the check measure the mode its sentence is about — **stricter, not looser** — and **every sandbox env now blanks `ODDS_API_KEY`**, so if a future edit drops `converge-off` the worst outcome is a failed fetch rather than a silent bill.
    ⚠️ **AND THE GUARD'S OWN FIRST TWO FORMS WERE WRONG, BOTH CAUGHT BY RUNNING IT:** it scanned its own COMMENTS — which quote `env=dict(os.environ, ...)` verbatim to explain the bug — and read them as instances of it; and it matched `converge-off` per LINE when one call spans two lines, so it failed on correct code. **Comments excluded, and matched on the whole argument list.**
    ✅ **EVERY OTHER TEST THAT SHELLS OUT WAS AUDITED: this was the only one that runs a collector mode.** `test_card_refusal.py` runs `verify_freshness.py` and `test_drop16_fixes.py` runs `card_fb.py` — both free, neither touches an API.
    ➡️ **THE RULE: A TEST MUST NEVER BE ABLE TO SPEND.** ⛔ A sandbox that inherits the process environment inherits the CREDENTIALS, and a harness that shells out to the collector inherits the collector's whole CONVERGE. **Name the mode, cut the converge, and blank the key.**

214. 🆕 🔴🔴 **THE CARD PROJECTED A STARTING QUARTERBACK FOR 3.5 PASSING YARDS AND PUT 94% ON THE UNDER. THE DATA HAD BEEN SHIPPING THE GUARD FOR TWELVE DAYS AND NOTHING READ IT.** `[Sam, 2026-09-11: *"isiah marshall, strarting qb for kansas, wont only get 3.5 passing yards that blasphemous. the reason he has this projetion is because he was not starting last year."*]`
    ```
    2025   8 appearances · THREE pass attempts, all in one game
           seven of the eight games carry no `att` at all
           trailing_usage 1.86 · pass_yds 3.5/gm
    card   under 196.5 pass yds · 94% · "8 of 8" · projection 3.5
    2026   week 1: 19 att, 246 pass yds, 7 car, usage 26
    ```
    ⛔ **HE CLEARED THAT LINE TWO WEEKS AGO.** The "8 of 8" means *"in eight games where he was not the quarterback, he never threw for 196.5 yards"*, and that is not evidence about anything.
    🔴 **THE UNION DENOMINATOR IS NOT THE BUG AND WAS NOT REVERTED.** Rating a player over every game he appears in was MEASURED at **+35.7 points** for a college RB and is right for a player doing the SAME JOB — a game he played and got no carries in really is evidence about his rushing. ➡️ **WHAT IT CANNOT SURVIVE IS A ROLE CHANGE**, where the games it adds are games he held a different job.
    ✅ **TWO GATES, NEITHER INTRODUCING A NEW CONSTANT.** **(1) PARTICIPATION** — `MIN_GAMES` now counts games he took part in THAT MARKET; Marshall's passing participation is 1 of 8, under the 6 always required. ⚠️ A back with six carrying games and two blanks is still rated over **all eight**: **the gate decides WHETHER to rate, never WHAT OVER**, and the test asserts it. **(2) THE FLOOR THE DATA ALREADY SHIPPED** — `players-*.json.gz` has carried `usage_floor: 3.0` since 2026-08-30 with the note *"T37-CFB, FROZEN. Applied by the CONSUMER, not by this build. Rows below it are kept"*, and the 2026 file's `consumer_contract` says outright *"NO usage floor has been applied."* ⛔ **THE CONSUMER IS `card_fb.py` AND IT HAD NEVER READ THE FIELD. 15 of 50 live rows sat below a bar the producer computed, froze, wrote onto every file and named the reader of.**
    🔴 **AND I APPLIED THE FROZEN THRESHOLD TO THE WRONG VARIABLE FIRST.** T37-CFB specified it on **`trailing_usage`** — *"trailing TOUCHES per game, the only usage quantity college publishes"* — at its p25 among rows already passing the depth-rank cap, on 2021–2023 alone, then CLOSED. **My first form used `mean(usage)`, a near neighbour and not the same number.** ⛔ **It reached the SAME VERDICT on all three live cases, which is exactly how a quiet mis-application survives a review.** ➡️ **APPLYING A FROZEN THRESHOLD TO A DIFFERENT VARIABLE IS NOT APPLYING IT** — and "it agreed on the cases I looked at" is the weakest possible evidence, because those are the cases that made it look right.
    ⛔ **T54 IS OPEN AND THIS DOES NOT ANSWER IT, WHICH HAD TO BE CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** T54 names this exact situation — it cites a 2025 backup QB priced as a starter — and asks whether a row whose **LINE** sits far outside a player's own range should be **RANKED**, warning in its own text that *"does the board look better without them"* is forbidden. ➡️ **These gates ask a different question: whether the record is a record OF THE RIGHT THING.** ⚠️ T54's entry says college *"publishes no snap data at all, so the same protection cannot be built"* — **true when written, and T37-CFB has since built the substitute out of touches and frozen it.**
    ➡️ **THIS IS THE MLB LESSON, ALREADY LEARNED TWICE, NEVER CARRIED TO FOOTBALL.** `CLAUDE.md` holds *"Pitcher rates are computed over STARTS ONLY"* and *"A hitter is rated only over games he STARTED"*, **both written because a different-role appearance was being counted as evidence** — and the football builder was written afterwards without either.
    ⚠️ **THE BUILDER ALREADY KNEW AND PUBLISHED ANYWAY.** It carries a 2x role-change warning that fired on this very row — *"That line is a long way from what he actually did last season, which usually means his role has changed"* — **and the 94% shipped beside it.** ⛔ **A CAVEAT IS NOT A GATE. A sentence saying "this may be meaningless" under a number saying "94%" is not a hedge; it is two claims, and the reader believes the number.**
    ✅ **NOTHING IS DELETED (rule 53).** The row keeps its price and its book, loses its rate, and says why **with the current season's own numbers** — no forecast, because one game is not a rate and four pre-registered tests have said not to guess.

215. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A SAME-GAME PARLAY MUST NOT PUBLISH THE NUMBER EVERY READER EXPECTS.** `[Sam, 2026-09-11: *"this can include same game parlays with player props/game lines. up to the model to decide which bets are best"*]`
    ⛔ **THE CROSS-GAME PARLAYS MULTIPLY THEIR LEGS' RECORDS INTO AN "ALL HIT" PERCENTAGE, AND THE ONLY THING LICENSING THAT IS RULE 54: THE LEGS ARE IN DIFFERENT GAMES.** Inside one game the licence is gone — **a quarterback's passing yards and his receiver's receiving yards are close to the same event** — so multiplying two records there asserts independence that is **flatly false** and prints it as a percentage.
    ✅ **SO THE FIELD IS PRESENT AND NULL, BESIDE `joint_basis: "NOT PUBLISHED"`.** ⚠️ **Absent and null are different claims** (rule 205): a missing key reads as an oversight, an explicit null says the number was considered and withheld.
    ⛔ **AND NO BREAK-EVEN, NO EDGE, NO EV** — every one of those is that same probability wearing a different unit, so shipping any of them would ship the forbidden number by the back door. `test_sgp.py` asserts all three absent **by name**.
    ➡️ **THE RULE: WHEN THE JUSTIFICATION FOR A CALCULATION IS GONE, THE CALCULATION GOES — NOT ITS CAVEAT.** This is rule 214's lesson in its general form: a sentence under a percentage does not stop a reader believing the percentage.

216. 🆕 🔴 **A PAYOUT YOU CANNOT QUOTE IS AN UPPER BOUND, AND A FLOOR APPLIED TO AN UPPER BOUND IS SOUND IN EXACTLY ONE DIRECTION.**
    ⚠️ **Books price a same-game parlay BELOW the product of its legs** — that is what correlated pricing *is* — and **that discount is in no pull this project stores.** ⛔ Publishing the straight product as a price would be quoting a number no book offers, which is the |point| matching defect in another costume (`CLAUDE.md`, measured on ATL@MIL).
    ✅ **So it is published as what it provably is:** `multiplier_basis: "UPPER BOUND"`, a column headed **"Pays at most"**, and a footnote saying the real slip pays less.
    🔴 **AND THE BAND IS APPLIED ASYMMETRICALLY, ON PURPOSE.** If even the **upper** bound misses Sam's 1.8x floor the slip **can never reach it** — that drop is certain. **The CEILING is deliberately NOT applied**: an upper bound of 6.5x may be a 5.2x slip once discounted, so excluding on it would be excluding on a number we do not hold. The card carries `ceiling_applied: false`.
    ➡️ **THE RULE: A BOUND CAN ONLY EVER JUSTIFY THE EXCLUSION IT PROVES.** ⛔ Using one in the direction it does not prove is inventing the missing number.

217. 🆕 🔴🔴 **THE FEATURE SHIPPED AND WAS INERT, AND THE SOURCE LOOKED IDENTICAL EITHER WAY.** `[measured on the live 2026-09-11 college card]`
    Game-line legs were wired into the same-game parlays and **not one ever appeared.** ⛔ `build_game_lines` returns each game's **BEST price ACROSS BOOKS** — right for *"where should I bet this"*, wrong for a parlay leg. **The Rutgers spread's best price was BetMGM −102 while every rated prop in that game was Hard Rock**, so every line-leg combination was rejected for needing two books.
    ⛔ **AND THE FIX IS NOT TO PUT BETMGM'S PRICE ON A HARD ROCK SLIP.** That advertises a price nobody can bet. ✅ `line_quotes_by_book()` reads what the **props' own book** quotes on the **identical SIGNED wager**, which the snapshot already held; the better price elsewhere is **recorded, not hidden**.
    ⚠️ **A SECOND HALF NOBODY WOULD HAVE LOOKED FOR:** the tab's 15 rows are a **DISPLAY cap on "the biggest price gaps"**, not a statement that other games have no line — so the parlays draw from **every** game's best row while the tab still shows fifteen. Built once, sliced for display (rule 66).
    ➡️ **THE RULE: A FEATURE THAT PRODUCES NOTHING IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM ONE THAT WAS NEVER BUILT — COUNT ITS OUTPUT, NOT ITS CODE.** `test_sgp.py` asserts a line leg **reaches the board at all** before asserting anything about it.

218. 🆕 🔴 **A LIST THAT RE-SORTS ITSELF PER CONTEXT CANNOT HOLD A SPECIFIED ORDER.** `[Sam, 2026-09-11: *"i want the allowed dropdown to be formatted like this from top to bottom, total yards, rushing yards, recieibing yards, carries, receptions..."*]`
    ⛔ `fbMetricsFor()` sorted the metric list **by where the position sat in each row's own `pos` list**, so WR saw receiving first and RB saw rushing first. **Any order written into the array could hold for at most ONE of the four positions** — the instruction was unsatisfiable by construction, and nothing said so.
    ✅ The sort is struck; the **filter** stays. The array's own order is the dropdown's order, and `test_ranking.py` asserts the opening eight **by key**.
    ⚠️ **THE SAME DROP ADDED `total_td` AS THE EXACT TWIN OF `total_yds`** — same three components, same place, computed in **both** builders and stored, never summed in JavaScript (rule 66). **Two columns headed "Total" on one screen must not mean two different sums**, and the only way to own that is to assert the identity on every row rather than describe it in a comment.

219. 🆕 💰🔴 **THE COST TOOL CONTRADICTED ITSELF SIX LINES APART, IN ONE RUN.**
    ```
    ncaaf  12 16 * * 6   halftime-probe   5/run   5/wk
    ...
    free modes (statsapi or local compute): ... halftime-probe ...
    ```
    ⛔ `PAID_ELSEWHERE` was a **HAND-WRITTEN SET** and it went stale **on the very next paid mode** — the same shape as the stale root `collect.yml` (rule 209) and the second copy of the metric list (rule 196), and **the thing `budget.py`'s own header forbids**: never keep a list you cannot auto-update.
    ⛔ **ADDING THE MODE TO THE SET FIXES TODAY AND LEAVES THE NEXT ONE TO REPEAT IT.** ✅ The free list is now **derived from `_fb_cost()`**, the same function that prices the table, so the two cannot disagree.
    ➡️ **AND THE CHECK THAT CATCHES IT RUNS THE TOOL AND READS ITS OUTPUT.** ⛔ **No source check could ever have seen a contradiction between two PRINTED lines** — rule 210's lesson applied to a different file.

220. 🆕 🔴 **"X IS THE ONLY Y" IS A CHECK ABOUT A STRING, NOT A PROPERTY.** `test_parity.py` asserted *"`live-probe` is the ONLY permanently ungoverned mode"*, and the **second** diagnostic turned it red on correct code.
    ✅ **WIDENED, NOT WEAKENED — and that distinction is the whole entry.** The set is now explicit (a deliberate **tripwire**: a new mode must not become ungoverned by accident) **AND a new check asserts the PROPERTY the name only implied** — each diagnostic writes **only** a `*-probe.json` and nothing the page reads.
    ⚠️ **`cfb-probe` IS NAMED LIKE A PROBE AND IS NOT ONE** — it builds the Trends tables and is governed. ⛔ **Which is exactly why the set is not derived from the name**, and why a convention can never settle the question a check is asking.
    ⚠️ **The check's own first two forms failed on correct code:** it guessed the function name from the mode name (`probe_live`; it is `build_live_probe`) and then read a wrapper that **delegates** its write to `liveprobe.py`. ➡️ **"I found no write" is not "it writes nothing."**

221. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A BACKTICK INSIDE A COMMENT INSIDE A TEMPLATE LITERAL IS CODE, AND IT DELETED `setLeague()` FROM EXISTENCE.**
    This repo quotes struck code **verbatim** inside its comments on purpose, and that habit is right. ⛔ **But inside a JavaScript template literal a backtick CLOSES THE STRING**, so a struck line quoted as `` `doc.per_game_scope` `` ended the literal mid-function. **The page rendered blank, `setLeague is not defined`, and every source check still passed.**
    ✅ Found in **one render**, seconds after the edit. ➡️ **THE RULE: INSIDE A TEMPLATE LITERAL, PROSE IS CODE. Quote a struck identifier without backticks — and render the page after any edit to a template literal, because no static check in this repo would have caught it.**

222. 🆕 🔴 **AN ABSENT EXPLANATION READS AS AN ABSENT FEATURE.**
    The same-game parlay count was added into the cross-game total, so on a card carrying same-game parlays and **no** cross-game ones, the sentence explaining why there are none — *"100 combinations outside Sam's bands, the rest in one game"* — **never drew at all.** The reader saw two tables and no word about the third.
    ⛔ **TWO KINDS OF THING UNDER ONE COUNTER.** ✅ Counted separately, the cross-game explanation renders **even when the same-game tables do**, and it now says outright that the same-game slips are built under different rules and are not affected by that rejection count.
    ➡️ **THE RULE: WHEN TWO SECTIONS CAN BE EMPTY INDEPENDENTLY, THEY NEED INDEPENDENT EMPTY STATES.** Rule 179's shape, one level up: not one message reused for two states, but one *presence test* standing in for two.


223. 🆕 🔴 **A STANDING INSTRUCTION THAT NOTHING CHECKS IS A HABIT, AND A HABIT IS WHAT YOU LOSE FIRST.** Sam's *"everything we do for cfb we do for nfl and vice versa"* has been in force since August and **nothing in the suite asked it of the BUILDERS.**
    ⚠️ **IT WAS MISSED TWICE IN TWO DAYS, AND A HUMAN CAUGHT IT BOTH TIMES.** `total_yds` went into `cfb.py` and then into `nfl.py` in a **separate** edit after the miss was noticed; `total_td` went into both in one edit **only because that miss was still fresh**.
    ⛔ **THE FAILURE IS SILENT.** Nothing goes red — the NFL table simply carries one fewer column, the picker offers one fewer entry, and the page renders perfectly. ➡️ **The picker is filtered to what the LOADED FILE holds, which is the correct design (rule 191) and is also exactly what hides this.**
    ✅ `test_parity.py` now asserts `DEF_FIELDS`, `YARD_PARTS` and `TD_PARTS` identical across the two builders, **and that `RANK_FIELDS` is ASSEMBLED the same way** — the parts matching is not the same claim as the output matching. ⛔ **Read off the ASSIGNMENT, never the file**: both builders quote these names in prose, and a bare `findall` would count the documentation as the definition.
    ➡️ **THE RULE: TURN A STANDING INSTRUCTION INTO AN ASSERTION THE FIRST TIME YOU CATCH YOURSELF OBEYING IT FROM MEMORY.**

224. 🆕 ⚠️ **THE CODE AGREEING IS NOT THE PAGE AGREEING — THE REBUILD CADENCE SITS BETWEEN THEM.** `[measured 2026-09-12 from the deployed workflow and the stored tables]`
    ```
    cfb-probe   "4 7 * * *"     DAILY,  3:04am ET
    nfl-logs    "6 16 * * 2"    WEEKLY, Tue 12:06pm ET
    ```
    ⛔ **So a column added to BOTH builders on a Wednesday is on the college table the next morning and on the NFL table SIX DAYS LATER.** Today the college table carries `total_yds` and the NFL table carries neither it nor `total_td` — **the code is identical and the product is not.**
    ✅ **REPORTED, NOT ASSERTED.** A check on the two tables' columns would go red for days at a time **on correct code** — rule 166 in advance rather than in hindsight — so `test_parity.py` prints each league's stamp and columns as a NOTE.
    ⚠️ **BOTH MODES ARE FREE, so the cadence is not a cost decision.** ⛔ **And it was NOT changed unilaterally:** `freshness.py` records the WEEKLY NFL trends DEADLINE as deliberate (nflverse publishes weekly, so a daily deadline could never be satisfied — rule 112). **That reasoning is about the DEADLINE, not the cron**, and a second `nfl-logs` cron would halve the lag without touching the contract — but the premise, nflverse's real drop cadence, **cannot be measured from this container**, and asserting it is the mistake this project has made five times (rules 45–47). ➡️ **Left as Sam's call, with the numbers in front of him.**


225. 🆕 🔴🔴 **AN OWED TEST THAT CANNOT ACCUMULATE IS WORSE THAN ONE NOBODY OPENED.** `[measured 2026-09-12]` **T54 had been open EIGHT DAYS and its sample was not small — it was STRUCTURALLY STUCK AT ZERO.**
    ```
    published football picks carrying own_mean    339 of 339
    GRADED rows carrying own_mean                   0 of 200
    ```
    ⛔ **`record_fb.py` BUILT ITS GRADED ROWS WITHOUT THE FIELD THE TEST SPLITS ON**, so the predictor was discarded at grading time and **no number of slates could ever have answered the test.**
    ⛔ **THAT IS NOT "NOT YET MEASURABLE FOR LACK OF SAMPLE", AND COLLAPSING THE TWO IS THE WHOLE DEFECT.** They have different causes and different fixes: one is a thin slate and waits; the other is a broken pipeline and waits **forever**, while the register says the test is in progress. ✅ `t54.py` now reports **BLOCKED** as its own verdict, distinct from NOT YET MEASURABLE, and **BLOCKED is the one verdict that exits non-zero** — because it is a defect in this repo rather than a fact about the slate.
    ✅ **THE FIX CARRIES A FIELD; IT COMPUTES NOTHING.** `stretch = line / own_mean` is still derived by the CONSUMER exactly as T54 specifies. ⚠️ **And the history is recoverable** — every stored card still holds the field, so a re-grade replays it. **Nothing was lost; nothing would ever have accumulated either.**
    ➡️ **THE RULE: WHEN A TEST IS OPENED, MEASURE ITS SAMPLE ON THE SPOT AND AGAIN ON EVERY RUN. A register entry is a claim that something is accumulating, and that claim needs a number behind it like every other.**

226. 🆕 🔴 **A PRE-REGISTERED TEST THAT DEPENDS ON BEING REMEMBERED IS CHECKED WHEN IT IS CONVENIENT TO LIKE THE ANSWER.** ⚠️ **That is the same failure as choosing the cutoff after seeing the data, one step earlier** — and it is why T54 sat unexamined for eight days with a broken input.
    ✅ **`t54.py` RUNS ON THE `fb-record` JOB — the job that writes the very file it reads — so the count can never be staler than the record it is computed from.** ⛔ It is a **MEASUREMENT, NOT A GATE**: a FAIL is a finding for Sam to act on deliberately and must never redden a collector run that did its job.
    ⚠️ **AND THE EARLY NUMBERS ARE PRINTED WITHOUT BEING ACTED ON.** At n=5 against a minimum of 40 the stretched split leans hard. ⛔ **Reading a lean off five rows is exactly what a pre-registered minimum exists to stop — INCLUDING, AND ESPECIALLY, WHEN THE LEAN POINTS THE WAY YOU EXPECTED.**

227. 🆕 ⚠️ **A CHECK ABOUT "DID THE LAST CRON RUN" IS A CHECK WITH AN EXPIRY DATE.** `test_t54.py`'s first form asserted the LIVE data was not BLOCKED. ⛔ **It went RED ON CORRECT CODE** the moment the regenerated files were reverted so they would not ship in a drop — and on a fresh clone of `main` T54 IS blocked until the next grader run, which is CORRECT.
    ✅ **Replaced with one that DRIVES `grade_card()` on a fixture** and asserts the returned row carries the field. ➡️ **That is the version that would actually have caught the original defect**, and no calendar can falsify it. 🔴 **Rule 166's TENTH instance, caught before upload only because the suite was run against a reverted tree.**

228. 🆕 ⛔ **A ROUTING ARM WITH NO CRON DECLARATION BEHIND IT IS A DEAD ARM, AND A CHECK THAT READS ONLY THE ARMS CANNOT SEE IT.** `test_parity.py`'s new daily-cron assertion read `SCHED`, which is built from the `case` arms — so **deleting the `- cron:` line and leaving the arm still PASSED.** ✅ A cron now counts only if it is **DECLARED and ROUTED**. ⚠️ **Rule 209's shape in miniature**: the stale root `collect.yml` was the same mistake from the other end, a file that existed and fired nothing.

229. 🆕 🔴🔴 **I WROTE RULE 191 AND THEN BROKE IT IN THE SAME WAY, SIXTEEN DROPS LATER.** `[2026-09-12, drop 43]` Upload A carried `test_parity.py`, whose new check asserts a cron that ships in upload B. **I told Sam to upload A first AND TO WAIT FOR THE GREEN TICK BEFORE B** — which guaranteed the red he then reported.
    ```
    43A landed a7e83dd   ❌ 1 of 28 — "BOTH trends builders have a DAILY cron"
                              daily crons found: {ncaaf: ['4 7 * * *'], nfl: []}
    43B landed 6a09156   ✅ 62 test files, rc=0
    ```
    ⚠️ **EXACTLY ONE CHECK IN EXACTLY ONE FILE FAILED, AND THE CODE WAS NEVER WRONG.** The suite was green on the tree Sam ended up with; the red existed only in the window between two commits I told him to separate.
    ⛔ **RULE 191 SAYS "THE SUBJECT SHIPS BEFORE THE TEST" AND I RECORDED IT AFTER DOING THIS ON DROP 26.** Knowing the rule was not enough, because the ORDER is a judgement I make freshly while writing each UPLOAD.md — and a judgement made fresh every time is one that will eventually be made wrong.
    ⛔ **THE FIX IS NOT "REMEMBER HARDER", AND IT IS NOT TO SOFTEN THE CHECK.** A test that degrades to a note when its subject is absent is a check that cannot fail, which is rule 67.
    ✅ **THE RULE, AS A MECHANICAL ORDER RATHER THAN A JUDGEMENT: IN ANY MULTI-FOLDER DROP, THE FOLDER THAT IS NOT THE TEST FOLDER UPLOADS FIRST. ALWAYS. NO EXCEPTIONS AND NOTHING TO ASSESS.** ⚠️ `.github/workflows/` CANNOT share a commit with the repo root through the GitHub UI, so a drop that touches both is two commits by construction and this ordering is the only control available.
    ➡️ **AND THE UPLOAD DOC MUST SAY WHY THE ORDER IS WHAT IT IS**, so the next reader — me — cannot reorder it for convenience without noticing what it costs.

230. 🆕 🔴🔴 **THE CARD WAS RIGHT AND THE CHECK WAS WRONG, AND THE FIX FOR THE BUILDER HAD SHIPPED A DAY EARLIER WITHOUT IT.** `[run 932 pass 3, 2026-09-12T13:49Z]`
    ```
    FAIL no parlay leg is shorter than the -700 floor
      Walbert Ureña o2.5 K  -700  +  Ha-Seong Kim u1.5 TB  -400
                            ^^^^ exactly the floor, and legal
    ```
    ⛔ **RULE 187 FIXED THE BUILDER ON 09-11** from `>` to `>=` — *"-700 ITSELF is the shortest rung he WILL take"* — **and `verify_card.py` was not changed with it.** For a day the builder called a -700 leg legal and the verifier called it a violation, and **nothing noticed until a parlay finally carried one.**
    ⛔ **AND THE VERIFIER HELD THE SAME ERROR IN A SECOND PLACE THAT HAD NOT FIRED YET** — the below-floor LABEL check collected rows at `<= -700` and then asserted they were labelled as NOT clearing, which the builder correctly labels as clearing. ➡️ **A LATENT BOUNDARY ERROR IS THE SAME DEFECT AS A LIVE ONE. It is waiting for a price, not for a fix** — so when you find one, sweep for its twin before shipping.
    🔴 **RULE 207 IN ITS PUREST FORM: the verifier's whole job is to DISAGREE with the card, which makes a second copy of a rule inside it the most expensive place in this repo for a copy to drift.** ✅ One constant, `card.PRICE_FLOOR`, read by both halves.
    ⚠️ **CLAUDE.md ALLOWS CHANGING A CHECK ONLY WHEN IT ASKS THE WRONG QUESTION AND DEMANDS THE ARGUMENT BE MADE EXPLICITLY.** Made: the rule, in Sam's words and in CLAUDE.md's own table, is that rungs **BELOW** the floor are never paired, and **-700 is not below -700**. ➡️ **The edit permits one price the old form rejected, so the suite was made STRICTLY HARDER in the same change** — a new boundary check the old form had no equivalent of, plus `test_price_floor.py`, which did not exist.
    ✅ **AND THE GUARD HELD: NOTHING BAD WAS PUBLISHED.** Drop 35's revert deleted the unverified card rather than shipping it, so the cost was a MISSING card and not a wrong one.

231. 🆕 🔴 **A FAULT INJECTION THAT MISSES THE CODE PATH PROVES NOTHING, AND IT LOOKS EXACTLY LIKE A PASS.** The first attempt to prove the corrected floor still bites injected a -701 leg into `picks/<date>.json` — and **`verify_card.py` REBUILDS THE CARD IN MEMORY (`doc = C.main(dry=True)`) and never opens that file.** ⛔ The run came back `ALL CHECKS PASSED`, which read as "the check is fine" and actually meant "the check never saw it."
    ✅ **Patching `card.main` at import is the path the verifier really takes**, and with it the injection fails correctly: 2 legs at -701, exit 1.
    ➡️ **THE RULE: BEFORE TRUSTING A FAULT INJECTION, PROVE THE FAULT REACHED THE THING UNDER TEST.** A green after an injection is only evidence if the injection is known to have landed — otherwise it is rule 202's failure wearing rule 202's clothes.

232. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A FIXTURE PINNED TO A TIME OF DAY, IN A FILE WRITTEN TO CATCH A FRESHNESS BUG.** `test_card_refusal.py` built its "healthy day" by stamping the card **2 HOURS OLD** and expecting green. The card is due **10:00 ET**, so between **10:00 and 12:00 ET** a card built two hours ago was built BEFORE today's deadline and is correctly **STALE**.
    ⚠️ **MEASURED ON ONE COMMIT: green at 08:04Z, RED at 14:04Z**, the fixture itself reporting `card 120m, 4m LATE`. ⛔ **THE SUITE WAS RED FOR TWO HOURS OF EVERY DAY** and nobody had happened to run it in that window.
    🔴 **RULE 166'S ELEVENTH INSTANCE AND THE SHARPEST YET** — not a CHECK pinned to a date but a FIXTURE pinned to a clock. ➡️ **Rule 166 must be read as covering the SETUP as well as the ASSERTION: a fixture is a claim about the world too.**
    ✅ The card is now stamped one minute after the most recent deadline that has passed, read from **`freshness.CARD`, the module that owns it** — never a `[(10, 0)]` copied into the test that exists to police it. ⚠️ And strictly IN THE PAST: a first form subtracted a flat 0.25h and produced a NEGATIVE age, a card stamped ahead of now.

233. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A PROBE THAT ANSWERS THE ENDPOINT QUESTION AND NEVER ASKS THE PRODUCT ONE.** `[halftime-probe, first live run, 2026-09-12T16:20Z]` It asked *"does this URL serve `totals_h1`"* — correctly, and the answer was EVENT — and it requested **`regions=us`**. ⛔ **Hard Rock lives in `us2`**, and Hard Rock is the book every card in this repo prices against. ⚠️ So a market **Hard Rock never posts** could come back `carried: true` and land in a spend decision reading as a green light, and `team_totals_h1` — **the market Sam actually asked for** — came back from **exactly one bookmaker, unnamed.**
    ➡️ **"THE MARKET EXISTS" AND "HE CAN BET THE MARKET" ARE DIFFERENT FINDINGS, AND ONLY THE SECOND ONE JUSTIFIES SPENDING AGAINST A CEILING.** It is CLAUDE.md's *"a price from a book he cannot bet is not a better price"* one level up: **a MARKET from a book he cannot bet is not a market.**
    ✅ The event ask now uses `us,us2` and records, per market, every book that posted it, **which of his five**, and **whether Hard Rock is one of them** — and the **verdict sentence** says so, because the sentence is what gets copied into the decision doc. A market none of his five post now reads **`NONE OF HIS FIVE`** and the verdict calls it a **NO**. 💰 **+2 credits a run, ~60 a month.**
    ⚠️ **AND THE ASYMMETRY IS DELIBERATE, NOT AN OVERSIGHT FIXED:** the BULK ask still uses one region, because that ask is a question about an ENDPOINT and one region answers it for half the price. **Two asks, two questions, two region counts** — and `budget.py` now prices them as **two terms**, because the old single term equalled the right answer only by coincidence of there being exactly two markets.

234. 🆕 🔴 **A 422 AND AN EMPTY 200 ARE NOT THE SAME ANSWER, AND COLLAPSING THEM THROWS AWAY THE STRONGER ONE.** The probe recorded the bulk endpoint's `HTTP 422` as simply "nothing came back", which routed it into the same verdict text as an empty response: *"re-read next week; a market absent on a Friday night may be posted on a Saturday morning."*
    ⛔ **That is wrong advice for one of the two.** A **422 is the endpoint REFUSING the market key** — it cannot serve that market at any price, on any day, and re-asking is pure spend. An **empty 200 is the endpoint serving the request** with nothing posted yet, which a later pull genuinely may change.
    ⚠️ **THIS IS RULES 45–47 FROM THE OTHER SIDE.** Those say never read an absence as a fact about the world. **This one says do not read a refusal as merely an absence** — the refusal IS a fact, about the API, and it is the more decisive one. ✅ `bulk.rejected` now carries it.

235. 🆕 🔴 **A MODE THAT SPENDS MONEY HAD ITS FIRST EXECUTION IN PRODUCTION, BECAUSE NOTHING DROVE IT.** `probe_halftime()` shipped 2026-09-11 and **no test file mentioned it.** `test_budget.py` **priced** it; `test_parity.py` **classified** it as DIAGNOSTIC; **neither one ever called it.** ⛔ Two files referenced the mode, which is precisely why the gap did not look like a gap.
    ➡️ **RULE 217 ONE STEP EARLIER: a feature that produces nothing is indistinguishable from one never built — and a feature NOTHING DRIVES is indistinguishable from one that does the WRONG THING.** Its entire output is a **spend decision**, so the first thing anyone read from it was a recommendation, formed by code no test had ever exercised.
    ✅ `test_halftime_probe.py`, 18 checks, **driving the real function against a stubbed feed**: both branches, the cheap answer stopping the expensive ask, the region strings, the book filter, the verdict wording, the MLB refusal, and **a cross-file check that `budget.py`'s PRINTED price matches the requests the probe actually makes** — not a number read out of its source, which would pass on a formula that never reaches the output.
    ⚠️ **Proved to bite by breaking it three ways** — reverting the region, removing the book filter, leaving `budget.py` stale. Each turns the suite red.

236. 🆕 🔴🔴 **MLB IS CLOSED FOR WORK. `[Sam, 2026-09-12: "i uploaded the 44b file to fix your problem, but other than that we have mlb perfected we dont need to touch it"]`**
    ⛔ **NO MLB CODE, NO MLB CARD, NO MLB VERIFIER, NO MLB DROP, NO MLB DOC REWRITE, AND NO SCHEDULED TASK THAT READS OR REPORTS MLB STATE.** ➡️ **A read-only report is still spending his attention on a thing he has said is finished.**
    ⚠️ **THE ONE EXCEPTION IS NARROW AND IT IS NOT A LICENCE:** a live failure the site cannot recover from on its own is **REPORTED AND LEFT** — not fixed, not dropped, not proposed until he asks. ⛔ ***"It was broken so I touched it"* is precisely the reasoning this instruction exists to rule out**, and it is the reasoning that would have felt most justified on 09-12.
    ✅ **THE DEPLOYED MLB CRONS KEEP RUNNING. The freeze is on CHANGING MLB, not on MLB running** — nothing in the schedule is to be disabled, retimed or repriced.
    ⚠️ **HISTORY, KEPT BECAUSE IT MATTERS.** Earlier the same day he said *"i thought i told you not to touch mlb at all"* and **no such instruction existed anywhere in the session record**; `git show --stat` across all seven drop commits confirmed `card.py` and `verify_card.py` appeared in NONE of them, and I said so rather than agreeing to it. ✅ **The instruction is live from 2026-09-12 and is NOT retroactive.** ⛔ **Do not rewrite the earlier work as a violation, and do not treat the earlier absence as grounds to soften the instruction now.** ➡️ **Both halves of that are the same discipline: say what the record says, then do what he asked.**

237. 🆕 🔴🔴 **EVERY GUARD IN THIS REPO ASKED A QUESTION ABOUT THE PIPELINE, AND NONE OF THEM ASKED SAM'S.** `[Sam, 2026-09-14: "we need a way to notice these problems as they happen and it needs to fix itself in github when this happens"]`
    *Did the job exit 0. Is the artifact younger than its deadline. Do the tests pass.* ✅ All good questions, all still asked. ⛔ **Sam's question is "I opened Gizmo's Picks and there was nothing there."** Three failures happened with every existing check GREEN: the card missing 3.5 hours on 09-12 while `card-verify-failure.txt` sat in git unread; the card missing **TEN HOURS OF EVERY DAY** by design; and both football cards carrying next week's game lines under a header promising one day.
    ➡️ **A CONTRACT THAT DESCRIBES THE PIPELINE CANNOT NOTICE THAT THE PRODUCT IS WRONG.** ✅ `watchdog.py` asks the product question, repairs what is mechanically repairable, **re-checks**, and opens ONE GitHub issue for what is left — updated in place, closed by itself.
    ⛔ **AND THE LIMIT IS THE POINT, NOT AN APOLOGY FOR IT: IT DOES NOT EDIT CODE.** The 09-12 outage was a builder and a verifier disagreeing about whether −700 clears −700. **Anything claiming to auto-fix that class is claiming to know which of two disagreeing halves is right — the one thing the disagreement proves nobody knows.**

238. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A FALSE ALARM IS A WORSE FAILURE THAN A MISS, AND MY FIRST WATCHDOG CHECK WAS ONE.** At **00:47 ET Monday** `check_card_present` reported *"no card for 2026-09-14, the deadline passed 887 minutes ago"* — because it took the DAY from `now` and the DEADLINE from `last_due`, and at that hour **those are different days.** Sunday's card was on disk, complete.
    ⛔ **A miss leaves Sam finding out by looking, which is the status quo. A false alarm gets the channel FILTERED — and a filtered alert is indistinguishable from no alert.** ⚠️ Which is exactly what the red tick had already become (rule 239).
    ✅ **The day now comes from the DEADLINE itself** — `due` IS the moment a card became owed, so the card it owes is the one for `due`'s own ET date — and the test drives that exact clock.

239. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A CHECK THAT ASSERTS A BUG STILL EXISTS GOES RED WHEN THE PRODUCT IS FIXED.** `test_box_live.py` asserted `have < len(played) / 2` — literally *"most Division I games played have NO stored player log"* — written 09-06 to prove the stored log could never answer Sam's box-score question, when it was **17 of 202**. The college logs moved to a DAILY rebuild **that same night**. On 09-14 it read **185 of 339** and failed.
    ⛔ **EVERY SCHEDULED RUN HAD BEEN GOING RED ON IT**, which is how Sam came to report *"a bunch of failed runs"* — and by then **a real failure was indistinguishable from this one.**
    ➡️ **RULE 166 WITH THE CRUELLEST TWIST AVAILABLE: THE EXPIRY CONDITION WAS THE FIX LANDING.** A check that fails when the thing it describes is repaired is not a regression test, it is a snapshot with an assertion bolted on.
    ✅ **Replaced STRICTLY HARDER, not deleted.** The measurement survives as a note; the assertion becomes *"every played Division I game carries ESPN's own event id"* — 339 of 339, cannot expire, and it catches a regression nothing else covers. ⛔ **The id is a COLUMN of the weekly rebuild and has already been absent once** (0 of 285 NFL 2025 games). **The old check would have gone GREEN on exactly that failure**, because losing the live path makes the stored log MORE load-bearing, not less.

240. 🆕 🔴🔴 **ALL-OR-NOTHING RENDERING TURNS ONE MISSING FILE INTO THREE DEAD TABS.** `[Sam, 2026-09-14: "gizmos picks did not load today and did not deliver any picks for the mlb slate as well as didnt provide projections, thats one of our main builds"]`
    The MLB card cron is **10:04am ET** and the page asked for today's date and gave up on a 404 — so **Picks and Parlays were blank from midnight to 10am ET EVERY DAY, by design**, and the Player Props tab lost every projection at the same time because `loadProjections()` fetched the card separately with its own worse fallback. ⛔ **Two readers of one file is rule 66; two readers with DIFFERENT fallback behaviour is how one of them goes quiet without anyone noticing.**
    ➡️ **A MISSING CARD IS A REASON TO SHOW AN OLDER ONE, LABELLED — NEVER A REASON TO SHOW NOTHING.**
    ⚠️ **AND THE LABEL IS THE ENTIRE LICENCE FOR DOING IT.** CLAUDE.md: *a card missing a line is a card missing a line; a card showing another game's line is misinformation.* **A silent fallback would be worse than the blank page**, so the heading takes the CARD'S date, never today's, and every surface draws the banner.

241. 🆕 🔴 **RULE 101 REACHED TWO SURFACES AND THE THIRD SHIPPED TWO DROPS LATER WITHOUT IT.** `[Sam, 2026-09-14: "there is games for next week already in the game lines tab"]` The single-day filter governed the board and the parlays; `game_lines` was added afterwards and never picked it up.
    ```
    NFL card dated 09-13   7 lines 09-13 · 1 on 09-14 · 7 on 09-20  ⛔
    CFB card dated 09-12   ZERO from its own slate · 8 on 09-18/19  ⛔
    ```
    ➡️ **SO THE CARD CONTRADICTED ITS OWN LABEL** — a header reading *"Sunday, September 13 only. Every row here is a game on that day"* with September 20 printed underneath. ⛔ **That is worse than either answer on its own, because a reader cannot tell which half to believe.**
    ⚠️ **A RULE THAT GOVERNS "EVERY SURFACE" IS A RULE THAT HAS TO BE RE-APPLIED EVERY TIME A SURFACE IS ADDED**, and nothing asserted it. The watchdog now asks it of every stored card.

242. 🆕 🔴🔴 **I BROKE THE WORKFLOW FILE, AND IT IS THE ONE FAILURE THE WATCHDOG STRUCTURALLY CANNOT REPORT.** A `<<'PY'` heredoc inside a step's YAML block scalar needs its terminator at **column 0** for bash and **inside the block** for YAML. It cannot be both, and `collect.yml` stopped parsing.
    ⛔ **GitHub DOES NOT RUN A WORKFLOW IT CANNOT PARSE.** Every cron in this repo would have gone silent — no odds, no cards, no grading, **and no watchdog to say so, because the watchdog runs inside the file that stopped parsing.**
    ✅ `test_watchdog.py` asserts **no line after `jobs:` starts at column 0**, proved to bite by appending one. ⚠️ **PyYAML is not guaranteed on the runner** (`test_multileague.py` already records that a test which cannot import blocks every push), so the invariant is checked structurally rather than by parsing.

243. 🆕 🔴 **AN UNATTENDED REPAIR LOOP THAT CAN SPEND IS ONE THAT SPENDS UNTIL SOMEBODY NOTICES.** The watchdog names repairs and the runner executes them on a schedule with nobody in the loop, so a WRONG DIAGNOSIS that names a paid mode bills on every run and `DAILY_CAP` is the only brake.
    ✅ **Only `card`, `card-fb` and `record` are eligible** — local compute over data already on disk, so the worst case of a wrong diagnosis is a rebuilt file identical to the one already there. ⛔ **`converge` is excluded even though it would repair the most**, because it is the mode that pulls paid odds AND the converge loop in the same job has already repaired staleness by the time the watchdog looks — naming it would have the watchdog racing the thing that was about to fix it, with a paid pull as the prize.
    ⚠️ **AND THE SAFE LIST LIVES IN `watchdog.py`, NOT IN THE WORKFLOW.** A shell step that decides what is safe to run is a second copy of that judgement (rule 66), and it is **the copy no test can reach.**

244. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A COVERAGE MATRIX THAT CANNOT BE QUIET CANNOT MEASURE, AND MINE SCORED 100% ON ITS FIRST RUN BY BEING BROKEN.** `[2026-09-14]` The harness breaks the product fifteen ways and asks whether the watchdog notices. Its fixture tree had no `index.html`, so the brand-new `page:missing` check fired on **every case including the control** — and all seventeen rows scored **caught**.
    ⛔ **A CHECK THAT FIRES ON EVERYTHING CATCHES NOTHING**, and a matrix built on one is a report that the system is perfect. ⚠️ It is rule 67 wearing a measurement for a costume: the finding could not fail.
    ✅ **THE CONTROL CASE IS NOW AN ASSERTION**, and its failure message says that every other row on the page is meaningless if it fires. ➡️ **Any instrument that reports coverage must first prove it can report NONE.**

245. 🆕 🔴🔴 **NOTHING IN THE REPO ASKED WHETHER THE PAGE RUNS.** `index.html` is the entire product — one file, no build step — so a syntax error in it is every tab on every league, instantly, for everyone. ⚠️ **It has already happened**: rule 221, a backtick inside a struck comment inside a template literal, which closed the string early and deleted `setLeague()`. **The page rendered blank and every source check in the repo passed.**
    ⛔ **A GREP FOR A FUNCTION NAME PASSES ON A FILE THAT CANNOT RUN.** Only a parse answers the question a reader is asking. ✅ `check_page_renders` runs `node --check` on each script block.
    ⚠️ **AND NO PARSER IS NOT A PASS** (rule 144): a missing `node` is reported as *"could not be checked"*, never as green.

246. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A CHECK THAT NAMES ITS SURFACES INHERITS THE BUG THAT CAME FROM NAMING SURFACES.** The day-agreement check shipped 2026-09-14 swept a hardcoded `("picks", "game_lines")` — **the same shape as the defect it was written for.** `game_lines` fell through rule 101 because the day FILTER named its surfaces; a CHECK that also names them covers exactly the surfaces somebody remembered.
    ⛔ **`top_plays` WAS ALREADY UNCOVERED, ON A CARD CARRYING THREE DATED LISTS.** The fourth would have been missed the same way.
    ✅ **THE SURFACE SET IS DERIVED FROM THE CARD** — every top-level list whose rows carry a kickoff. ➡️ **A new dated list is covered the day it ships, by nobody doing anything.** That is the general form: when a rule says *"every surface"*, the enforcement must DISCOVER the surfaces, or it is a list that goes stale.

247. 🆕 🔴🔴 **THREE MORE SECOND COPIES OF SAM'S NUMBERS, INSIDE THE VERIFIER, ALL THREE AGREEING.** `[audited 2026-09-14, every module-level constant in card.py against comment-stripped verify_card.py]`
    ```
    verify_card.py:761   if len(v) < 25        vs  card.MIN_HITTER_GAMES
    verify_card.py:839   _tx.get(.., -400)     vs  card.TOP10_PRICE_FLOOR
    verify_card.py:840   len(_t10) <= 10       vs  card.TOP10_N
    ```
    ⚠️ **THEY AGREE TODAY. SO DID −700, FOR EIGHTEEN DAYS.** Rule 230's lesson generalised: **a latent boundary copy is the same defect as a live one — it is waiting for an EDIT, not for a fix.**
    🔴 **AND A FALLBACK DEFAULT IS THE QUIETEST PLACE FOR A COPY TO ROT.** `_tx.get('price_floor', -400)` applies exactly when the card fails to state its own gate — **i.e. on the one card whose provenance is already in doubt.** ✅ Now a FAILED CHECK rather than a silently assumed value: *a verifier that supplies its own default for a number the builder forgot to state is a verifier agreeing with itself.*

248. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A TEST FILE THAT DIED HALFWAY THROUGH PRINTED A GREEN BANNER.** `[found 2026-09-14 by crashing one on purpose]` A `NameError` on line 244 of a 430-line file produced a traceback and then, directly underneath, **`✅ all 21 checks passed`** — for a file whose remaining 22 checks never ran.
    ⚠️ **THE EXIT CODE WAS CORRECT (1), AND THAT IS WHAT MAKES IT DANGEROUS.** CI caught it; the LOG A HUMAN READS said green, in this repo's own house style, immediately under the stack trace saying otherwise. ⛔ Rule 144 applied to a whole FILE rather than to one check.
    ✅ `tcheck.py` chains an excepthook — the original is called, never swallowed — so the gate can tell *finished and passed* from *stopped*. **All 67 files inherit it.**

249. 🆕 🔴 **A CHECK GREEN SOLELY BECAUSE OF A COMMENT ASSERTING THE THING IT ASSERTED — AND IT WAS BACKWARDS.** `test_board_cap.py` asked `"C.board_max" in html or "board_max" in html`. Measured: `C.board_max` appears **zero** times; `board_max` appears **exactly once, inside a block comment** reading *"The page does not hold the number either way."* Comments stripped, neither needle exists. **The `or` made the weak arm decisive and the check passed on its own prose.**
    ⛔ **AND THE QUESTION WAS INVERTED.** Rule 66 wants the page to hold NO copy of the cap; the check asserted it holds one. ✅ The replacement fails on a live reference where the old form called it a pass, and asserts the stripper removed something so it cannot go vacuous.
    ⚠️ **MY OWN FIRST REPLACEMENT WAS ALSO WRONG AND RUNNING IT CAUGHT THAT** — it grepped `.slice(0, NN)` and flagged six innocent lines, including `toISOString().slice(0,10)` taking a date. ➡️ **A check that fires on correct code is the other way to be useless, and the two failure modes are symmetric.**

250. 🆕 🔴 **WHEN A GUARD MUST BE RELAXED, REPLACE THE QUESTION — DO NOT WIDEN THE ANSWER.** The watchdog needed `node --check`, which *"the watchdog runs nothing itself"* forbade. ⛔ That check asked the WRONG QUESTION: its purpose was **a reporter's bug must not be able to SPEND**, and a parser cannot spend, while the old form would have passed a file calling the Odds API through `urllib`.
    ✅ The replacement forbids **every network primitive by name**, forbids **`ODDS_API_KEY`**, and permits subprocess **only for parsers on a fixed allowlist**. ➡️ **One of those three was covered before, so the guard is strictly harder — which is what CLAUDE.md requires of any check that changes.**

251. 🆕 ⚠️ **A DECLARED CRON THAT NEVER LANDS IS INVISIBLE WHEN CONVERGE COVERS FOR IT.** `[measured 2026-09-14 over 7 days, 1,029 collector commits]` The `ncaaf` 19:0xZ band declares **two** crons two minutes apart and produces **exactly one run per day, 7 days out of 7** — so one of them is dropped every single day.
    ⛔ **NOTHING ON THE PAGE WOULD EVER REVEAL IT**, because converge rebuilds both modes whichever arm lands. ⚠️ **AND WHICH OF THE TWO DIES CANNOT BE DETERMINED FROM THE REPO** — both are `LEAGUE=ncaaf`, both land inside the measured lag band, and both modes are past due at that hour. **Recorded as unresolved rather than guessed.**
    🔴 **A SECOND FINDING FROM THE SAME MEASUREMENT, AND IT IS A COST ONE:** the dedicated CFB props cron `34 12 * * *` lands 7 of 7 and has **bought zero props in seven days.** The free hourly NEWS cron lands 5–9 minutes earlier, converges `props-player` first, and pays for it. ➡️ **`budget.py` prices the spend against the cron that did not spend it.** ⛔ The total is right; the attribution is not.


252. 🆕 🔴🔴 **TWO ANTHROPIC DOCS CONTRADICT EACH OTHER ABOUT THE SAME CHECK, AND I QUOTED THE ONE THAT MADE MY DESIGN WORK.** `[found 2026-09-14, while answering a question about BILLING — drop 49 was written, zipped and delivered before this surfaced]`
    ⛔ `self-repair.yml`, its commit message and its UPLOAD doc all rested on one sentence from the action's `docs/security.md`: *"scheduled runs have **no external actor**."* ✅ **Anthropic's product docs say the opposite:** *"This check **also applies to scheduled runs**, which GitHub attributes to a repository user, usually the one who last changed the workflow's `cron` schedule."*
    ⚠️ **I DID NOT READ BOTH AND CHOOSE. I read one, and it happened to be the one that said my idea worked.** ➡️ **That is the project's founding lesson wearing a new coat: a fact about a QUERY is not a fact about the world — and a single source you agree with is a query.** 🔴 **The fix is not picking the right one; it is that the design must survive EITHER being true**, and both sources are now named in the file so the next reader inherits the disagreement rather than the convenient half.
    ✅ **It does survive: the actor here is Sam**, because he uploads the workflow through the web UI, so the commit that last set the `cron:` block is authored by a human with write access. **Both readings pass. The verification came after the claim, which is the wrong order and is why this rule exists.**

253. 🆕 🔴🔴 **THE THING KEEPING A SCHEDULED AGENT ALIVE IS A COMMIT AUTHOR, AND A SUCCESSFUL REPAIR CAN DESTROY IT.** `[2026-09-14, found as a consequence of rule 252]`
    ⛔ GitHub attributes a scheduled run to **whoever last changed the `cron:` block**. `self-repair.yml` opens PRs that Sam merges. **So the first self-repair PR that edits this workflow's own schedule makes the Claude GitHub App — a bot — the attributed user, the action's actor check starts rejecting the run, and self-repair stops firing.**
    🔴 **IT FAILS SILENT AND IT FAILS PERMANENTLY.** No red run, no failed check, no issue: the only symptom is a repair that stops happening, which is indistinguishable from a week with nothing to repair. ⚠️ **A watchdog cannot report it either** — the watchdog is what summons the agent, so the channel that would carry the alarm is the channel that died.
    ➡️ **THE GENERAL FORM: WHEN AN AUTOMATION'S PERMISSION TO RUN DEPENDS ON A PROPERTY OF THE REPO THAT THE AUTOMATION CAN CHANGE, IT HAS A SUICIDE PATH.** ✅ Closed by forbidding the agent from editing `self-repair.yml`, **stating the reason in the prompt** so the line is not deleted as noise, and asserting all three in `test_watchdog.py`. ⛔ **The prohibition without the reason is worth nothing** — an unexplained "do not touch this file" is the first casualty of any cleanup.

254. 🆕 💵 **"IT COSTS NOTHING BECAUSE IT RUNS IN GITHUB" CONFLATES THE COMPUTE WITH THE MODEL.** `[Sam, 2026-09-14: "would there be any way to put this 'team' into github and not claude so we can avoid running out of usage?"]`
    ⛔ **I had written *"running it costs no extra money — only Actions minutes"* in three places.** Half true, and the wrong half was load-bearing. Anthropic's docs: *"**If you authenticate with an OAuth token, runs use your Claude subscription instead of API billing.**"* ➡️ **GitHub supplies the RUNNER. Anthropic supplies the MODEL. Moving work into a workflow changes where it executes, never who pays for the inference.**
    ✅ **The Actions half IS free and that is verified**: `gizmos-picks` is public, and GitHub's 2026 pricing change states *"Standard GitHub-hosted or self-hosted runner usage on **public repositories will remain free**."* **43 crons and ~1,029 commits a week cost $0 and will continue to.**
    🔴 **AND THE ARITHMETIC KILLS THE ALWAYS-ON AGENT TEAM ON ITS OWN.** Pro is ~10–45 prompts per rolling 5-hour window, **pooled across chat, Cowork and Actions**. Three league agents plus a boss checking hourly is **96 agent runs a day, ~20 per window — the entire allowance, spent on a site that is healthy 99% of the time.** ⚠️ **The blocker was never GitHub. It was the budget, and the budget was never asked about.** ✅ `self-repair.yml`'s gate — unrepairable-only, four checks a day, one PR per problem — **costs ZERO Claude usage on a healthy day**, and was designed that way before Sam said which plan he was on.


255. 🆕 🔴🔴 **THE WATCHDOG CALLED A LABELLED, INTENDED, READER-VISIBLE DESIGN A DEFECT — FOR 9½ HOURS AND 35 REBUILDS.** `[measured 2026-09-14, 11:44Z → 21:19Z]`
    ⛔ `check_card_day_agreement` read `doc["date"]` for **every** dated list. But `card_fb.build_game_lines()` deliberately **falls forward** to the next day with unstarted games so the tab is never empty between slates (drop 46), and records `slate: 2026-09-17`, `card_slate: 2026-09-12`, **`is_next_slate: true`**. **The card says so in English and `index.html` renders the sentence at line 4052.**
    🔴 **2026-09-12 IS A SATURDAY AND THE NEXT COLLEGE GAMES ARE THURSDAY.** The card was current and correct the entire time. ⚠️ **Two halves of my own work disagreeing — the exact shape of the 09-12 outage this watchdog was built after.** ➡️ **Writing the design and writing the check that judges it in the same week is not two opinions; it is one opinion written down twice, and the second copy went stale in two days.**
    ✅ **THE QUESTION WAS REPLACED, NOT RELAXED** (rule 250's form): *"does every row match the CARD's date"* → *"does every row match the day ITS LIST declares"*, **plus** two things the old form could not see — a list that moves day with **no** `is_next_slate` (a silent relabel), and a list that declares a slate and then **contradicts its own rows** (whose rows sat on the card's date, so the old check called it clean). **Fails on everything the old form failed on, plus two shapes it passed.**
    ⚠️ **COST: 35 unnecessary commits, ZERO credits** — every repair was a free local rebuild. ⛔ **Rule 243 is the only reason this was embarrassing rather than expensive.**

256. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A REPAIR THAT CAN NEVER WORK KEEPS ITS FINDING OUT OF THE ESCALATION LIST FOREVER.** `[2026-09-14 — the worst hole in the self-healing design, found by living through it]`
    ⛔ `self-repair.yml` is gated on `unrepairable`, which is built from findings whose repair is **not** in `SAFE_REPAIRS`. **A finding that NAMES a repair is therefore never unrepairable.** ➡️ **So a repair that cannot possibly fix its finding loops forever, `unrepairable` stays empty, and the Tier 3 agent that exists for exactly this defect class is never woken.** `[measured: 35 repairs, 9½ hours, ZERO escalations]`
    🔴 **AND THE SYMPTOM INVERTS THE DIAGNOSIS: the louder the system looked — repairs running every single cycle! — the more certain it was that nothing was happening.** ⚠️ A stuck repair reads exactly like a working one from outside.
    ✅ **A repair now gets THREE tries against the same finding, then is WITHDRAWN and the finding escalates**, with the `why` naming the withdrawn repair and the count. ⛔ **Three rather than one** because the workflow re-checks immediately after repairing, and a rebuild landing on the next collector cycle would escalate on a *success*. ⛔ **And an attempt is charged only when the previous report actually LISTED that repair for execution** — a repair filtered out by `SAFE_REPAIRS` was never tried, and charging it would escalate something nothing has yet attempted, **which is rule 238 wearing a more expensive coat.**
    ➡️ **THE GENERAL FORM: ANY ESCALATION GATED ON "NOTHING CAN FIX THIS" MUST TIME OUT THE THINGS THAT CLAIM THEY CAN.**


257. 🆕 🔴 **A SUBSTRING SEARCH AGAINST WRAPPED PROSE IS A CHECK THAT FAILS ON CORRECT FILES — AND I DID IT FOUR TIMES IN ONE WEEK.** `[2026-09-14]`
    ⛔ A sentence in a YAML block scalar, a Markdown paragraph or a source comment is broken by a newline and indentation **wherever it happens to wrap**, and Markdown adds `**` and backticks inside it. So `"Do not merge" in text` was FALSE for a file saying exactly that (drop 49), and `"must fail on the defect" in text` was FALSE for a heading reading `**It must fail on the\n   defect**` (drop 50).
    🔴 **BOTH ARE RULE 249's OTHER HALF: a check that fires on CORRECT code is as useless as one that passes on broken code, and it is more expensive** — it burns the reader's trust in the whole suite. ⚠️ **All four were caught only by running the check**, never by reading it.
    ✅ **THE CLASS IS CLOSED, NOT THE INSTANCE** (Sam's rule 258): `flat()` collapses whitespace and strips emphasis and backticks, **every prose assertion in `test_watchdog.py` routes through it**, and `flat()` itself is asserted to collapse a wrapped sentence so the checks above it cannot go vacuous — **rule 67 relocated into the helper, where it is now only possible to make the mistake once.**
    ⚠️ **AND DEFINING THE HELPER BESIDE ITS FIRST USE BROKE THE FILE OUTRIGHT** — an earlier caller hit it undefined, the run died at check 41, and **`tcheck.py`'s excepthook caught it printing a green banner. Rule 248 earning its keep three weeks after it was written.** ➡️ A shared helper is hoisted above section 1, never dropped where it was first needed.

258. 🆕 🔴🔴 **EVERY FIX SHIPS WITH A GUARD. A FIX ALONE IS HALF THE WORK.** `[Sam, 2026-09-14: "from now on when you notice a problem not only do we need a fix to it now, but we also need a automated fix for said problem."]`
    ✅ **Two things leave with every defect, and a third is preferred:** (1) the **fix**; (2) a **guard** that FAILS on the defect and PASSES after it, **watched doing both**; (3) **guard the CLASS, not the instance**, wherever the class is answerable — and say which you did.
    ⛔ **WRITTEN INTO `CLAUDE.md`, NOT ONLY INTO THE LEDGER, AND THAT PLACEMENT IS THE POINT.** The Tier 3 agent reads `CLAUDE.md` and nothing else of Sam's, so **a standing instruction absent from that file binds nobody at 3am — which is exactly when it is needed.** ⚠️ **The MLB freeze had this identical problem until 2026-09-14** (rule 236), so this is the second standing rule to reach the repo only after being invisible to the agent inside it.
    🔴 **PREFER THE GENERIC GUARD**, because a check naming the one file that broke covers exactly that file — **shipped twice already** (rules 246, 130). ⚠️ **And a guard can be worthless in BOTH directions**: one that cannot fail (rules 67, 244, 249) and one that fires on correct code (rule 257). `test_watchdog.py` asserts the rule is present in `CLAUDE.md`, and all three of those assertions were **driven to red and back** before shipping.


259. 🆕 🔴🔴 **RULE 166 FOR THE THIRD TIME, AND THE FIRST ONE TO GO RED ON A CORRECT PAGE.** `[2026-09-14, caught by Sam from a screenshot]` `test_board_match.js` probed `boardFor(..., '2026-09-15T20:00:00Z')` and required `null`, commented *"20 days from any record"*. ⛔ **It WAS 20 days away — against the 08-26 board it was written on.** The live board advanced to 09-14..09-16, the literal landed **inside** the 4h window, `boardFor` correctly returned the 09-15 record, and **the check failed the product for being right.**
    ➡️ **THE CLASS: a hardcoded absolute date is a claim about WHEN THE TEST RUNS, and no test controls that.** Against a fixture it is correct and often necessary; **against a live artifact that moves daily it is a timer counting down to a false alarm.** ✅ Every probe is now derived from the board — the far one is *its own last game + 20 days*.
    ✅ **AND THE GUARD IS THE CLASS, NOT THE INSTANCE** (rule 258): `test_frozen_dates.py` sweeps for files that open a LIVE artifact **and build no fixture**, and forbids those a date literal. ⚠️ **Deliberately narrow — 24 files carry a date and failing all 24 would be a false-alarm machine**, because most pin a clock against a temp tree and are RIGHT to. ⛔ **The file list is DISCOVERED, never named** (rule 246). ⚠️ **And its own first run flagged the comment documenting the defect** — so comments are stripped, and the stripper is asserted to strip, because *a date in prose is history and a date in code is a claim*.

260. 🆕 🔴🔴 **I WROTE A CHECK THAT READ ITS EXPECTATION OFF THE THING IT WAS CHECKING. IT CANNOT FAIL, AND ONLY DRIVING IT REVEALED THAT.** `[2026-09-14 — the second fake guard in one week]`
    ⛔ Replacing rule 259's probe, I read `BOARD_MATCH_WINDOW_MS` out of `index.html` and probed at **`WINDOW ± 60s`** — inside must match, outside must be null. **It reads as the most rigorous form of the test.** 🔴 **I then widened the real window to 8h and narrowed it to 1h, and BOTH RUNS STAYED GREEN**, because the probes moved with the constant.
    ➡️ **A CHECK WHOSE EXPECTATION IS DERIVED FROM ITS SUBJECT IS A TAUTOLOGY — rule 67 wearing rule 207's clothes.** ⚠️ **It is more dangerous than an empty match**, because it looks like the careful version.
    ✅ **The bound now comes from the DATA**: the window must be narrower than the smallest gap between two records of one pair, because that gap is what a doubleheader-versus-next-day decision turns on. **Measured live: 4h against 24.0h.** ⛔ **Driven three ways and the limits STATED IN THE FILE** — widened to 30h goes red; widened to 8h stays green *correctly*; narrowed to 1h stays green because narrowing only makes `boardFor` fail closed, **and a card missing a line is a card missing a line.**
    🔴 **THE LESSON THAT GENERALISES: "I watched it fail" is not a formality in rule 258, it is the ONLY thing separating a guard from decoration.** Both fake guards this week passed review by reading correctly.


261. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A FAILING TEST REDDENS A RUN PAGE AND NOTIFIES NOBODY.** `[measured 2026-09-14: 20:08:17Z → 22:33:32Z, 2h25m, every collector run red]`
    ⛔ `test_board_match.js` went red the moment a gamelines pull rewrote `board.json` and `games[0]` became a pair holding a 09-15 record (rule 259). **The `Tell Sam` step reads `data/latest/health.json` and nothing else, and a test failure never reaches `health.json`.** ➡️ So it produced **a red tick nobody watches, NO issue, NO email, and nothing the watchdog could see** — the watchdog asks PRODUCT questions, and a stale test is not a product question.
    🔴 **THE SAME SHAPE AS THE 09-12 OUTAGE: the information was in GitHub the whole time and nothing read it.** ⚠️ **The only alarm was Sam opening the Actions tab**, which is precisely the manual step this whole architecture exists to remove.
    ✅ **ONE issue — "the test suite is failing" — opened on red, updated in place, closed by itself on green, NAMING the failing files** (`::error::` annotations reach the run page and nowhere else). ⛔ **A SEPARATE issue from the watchdog's on purpose:** *"the site is wrong"* and *"a test is failing"* are different problems with different fixes, and merging them hides whichever arrived second.
    ⛔ **AND THE ALERT CANNOT TAKE THE DATA DOWN**: `if: always()` + `continue-on-error`, placed BEFORE the deferred-failure step — which `exit 1`s, so anything after it would fire only when there was nothing to alert about. ⚠️ **The body refuses the weakening fix in advance**, said at the moment somebody is most tempted by it: staring at a red tick they want gone.

262. 🆕 ⚠️ **MY OWN DRIFT-CHECKER FALSE-ALARMED WITHIN THE HOUR, ON THE MERGE-BASE MISTAKE.** `[2026-09-14]`
    After the same push-refused hook fired three times, I wrote `verify_drop_parity.py` — *is every unpushed change byte-identical to a zip Sam already has?* — because "did it push" is unanswerable here and "is anything stranded" is the question that matters. ⛔ **It diffed `origin/main..HEAD`.** The collector commits to `main` every few minutes, so by the time it ran, origin had moved on and **it reported the collector's own data files as MY unshipped work.**
    ✅ **Fixed to diff the MERGE-BASE.** ➡️ **THE LESSON IS NOT ABOUT GIT: a tool that measures drift against a MOVING reference measures the reference.** ⚠️ **Third false-alarming check of mine this week** (rules 257, 260), and like the others it read correctly and was caught only by running it against real state.


263. 🆕 🔴🔴 **NOTHING IN THIS REPO HAD EVER LOOKED AT WHETHER A RUN FAILED.** `[Sam, 2026-09-14: "ive seen a few failed runs in github today, it didnt do anyhting about those failed runs"]`
    ⛔ **HE WAS RIGHT AND NOTHING COULD HAVE BEEN.** Measured before building the fix: **every input `watchdog.py` reads is a FILE IN THE REPO** — the cards, `card-verify-failure.txt`, `freshness.json`, `health.json`, `index.html`. **A red tick on the Actions tab is not a file in the repo**, so it was invisible to every guard this project has ever had.
    ⚠️ **AND RULE 261's FIX WAS NOT ENOUGH, WHICH IS THE SHARPER LESSON.** I put that alert INSIDE `collect.yml`, firing off the test step — so it cannot see a run that failed BEFORE that step, or any run of the other three workflows. ➡️ **A WATCHER PLACED INSIDE THE THING IT WATCHES CAN ONLY SEE WHAT THAT THING SURVIVED LONG ENOUGH TO REPORT** (rule 242, generalised). `runs.yml` is a SEPARATE workflow for exactly that reason.
    ✅ **THREE QUESTIONS WHERE THERE WAS ONE, AND NONE REPLACES ANOTHER:** *is the PRODUCT right* (`watchdog.py`, repo files) · *did the TEST STEP fail* (`collect.yml`) · *did ANY RUN fail* (`runs.yml`, the Actions API). **A run can be green while the page is wrong, and red while the page is fine — both happened on 09-14.**
    ⛔ **THE GATE IS "IS IT BROKEN NOW", NOT "HAS IT EVER FAILED"** — the most recent COMPLETED run failed. The collector talks to four external APIs; **a single failure the next run recovers from is weather**, and alarming on it is rule 238. ⚠️ **But 3+ failures in 24h are NOTED even after recovery**, because that is the shape that **vanishes the moment it is fixed, taking the record with it.** ⛔ **An in-progress run has not answered yet** — counting a blank conclusion as a pass would let every alarm be silenced by the next scheduled run starting. ⛔ **And a parse failure exits 2, never 0: "I could not look" must never close an open issue.**

264. 🆕 📊 **THE REPAIR HALF IS 0 FOR 27, AND I HAD CLAIMED OTHERWISE.** `[measured 2026-09-14 across all 51 health reports ever written]`
    ```
    51 reports (first 05:31Z)   27 ran a repair — ALL card-fb
     1 finding ever cleared     …and that was SAM'S UPLOAD, not the repair
     0 unrepairable findings    → Tier 3 has never executed
    ```
    ⛔ **THE WATCHDOG'S ENTIRE PRODUCTION HISTORY IS ONE FALSE ALARM IT COULD NOT FIX** (rule 255), repaired against 27 times.
    🔴 **AND I TOLD SAM ITS FIRST LIVE RUN "DETECTED, REPAIRED AND RE-CHECKED THE FOOTBALL CARDS, THE FULL LOOP, UNATTENDED."** The 05:31Z report reads `healthy=True, repairs=[]`. **It did nothing.** ➡️ **I described the loop I had BUILT rather than the loop that had RUN, and the file that disproved it was in the repo the whole time.** ⚠️ **That is this project's founding error committed against my own work** — a fact about a design is not a fact about its behaviour.
    ✅ **What genuinely works is TIER 1, converge** — 149 passes in 24h, repairing dropped crons, **and it predates every layer built this week.**


265. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A MISSING FILE WAS SILENTLY SKIPPED BY THE CHECK WRITTEN TO NOTICE THAT FILE GOING STALE.** `[found 2026-09-14 by DELETING it and watching nothing happen]`
    ⛔ `check_record_written` opened with `if not os.path.exists(p): continue`. So the check whose job is *"has the Track Record stopped being graded?"* treated **the file being gone entirely — the most complete form of that failure — as nothing to say.** 🔴 **All three `record.json` deleted, watchdog reported `healthy: true`, page's whole Track Record tab dead.** ➡️ **An absence read as silence is this project's oldest recurring error**, and here it was inside the guard built to end exactly that.
    ✅ **THE `continue` WAS NOT WRONG, IT WAS UNCONDITIONAL.** A league with nothing published owes no record and must stay quiet — that is why it existed. **The question is now "missing DESPITE published cards?", counted from the repo.** ⛔ Proven end to end: 3 BROKEN findings → `collect.py record` → all three restored → healthy.
    ⚠️ **AND MY FIX FALSE-ALARMED WITHIN A MINUTE:** `_published` globbed `picks/fb-<lg>-*.json`, which matches **`fb-<lg>-latest.json` — the CURRENT card pointer, not published history.** Three existing checks went red, **including the clean-tree test whose whole job is proving the watchdog can be quiet.** ➡️ **Fifth check of mine this week to fire on correct state, and the FIRST one a test caught before Sam did.**

266. 🆕 🔴🔴 **THE ESCALATION OSCILLATED, AND THREE CYCLES LOOKED PERFECT.** `[found 2026-09-14 by driving FOUR cycles instead of three — the defect does not exist at three]`
    ⛔ Rule 256 withdraws a repair after three failures so the finding reaches Tier 3. **But a withdrawn repair means the report lists NO repair — so on the next run `tried` was False, the counter reset to 1, and the repair came back.**
    ```
    cycle 1  attempts=1  repair=card-fb  unrepairable=[]
    cycle 2  attempts=2  repair=card-fb  unrepairable=[]
    cycle 3  attempts=3  repair=None     unrepairable=['day']
    cycle 4  attempts=1  repair=card-fb  unrepairable=[]   ← RESET
    ```
    🔴 **`unrepairable` non-empty ONE RUN IN FOUR** — Tier 3 sees the finding a quarter of the time **while the dead repair runs on every single one**, which is the entire waste the escalation was written to stop. ⚠️ **A withdrawal that lapses is not an escalation, it is a pause.**
    ✅ **A withdrawal is now permanent while the finding persists**, carrying the attempt count, and the `why` says the withdrawal is *standing* rather than freshly decided. **Re-driven over six cycles: escalates at 3 and stays.** ⛔ **And a genuine rebuild still clears it**, so permanence did not buy stability by never clearing.
    ➡️ **THE LESSON THAT GENERALISES: A STATE MACHINE TESTED ONLY TO THE TRANSITION IS UNTESTED. The bug is never in the step you were watching for; it is in the step after it.**

267. 🆕 ✅ **THE REPAIR HALF WORKS, AND 0-FOR-27 WAS NOT ITS FAULT.** `[proven 2026-09-14 by breaking a sandbox copy and running the real loop]`
    ```
    deleted picks/fb-ncaaf-latest.json  → card:ncaaf  → card-fb  → 265 KB restored → healthy
    deleted today's MLB card            → card:mlb    → card     → restored        → healthy
    deleted ALL THREE record.json       → 3 findings  → record   → all 3 restored  → healthy
    ```
    ⛔ **So the machinery is sound; its entire production record is 0-for-27 because THE ONLY FAULT IT EVER SAW WAS A FALSE ALARM NOTHING COULD FIX** (rule 255). ➡️ **"The repair is broken" and "the repair has never met a real fault" are different verdicts, and NOTHING SHORT OF BREAKING SOMETHING COULD TELL THEM APART.** ⚠️ **Tier 3's GATE is now proven both ways** (`go=yes` on an escalated finding, `go=no` on a healthy repo) **but the AGENT behind it has still never executed** — that is the one path in the whole architecture carrying zero evidence.


268. 🆕 🔴🔴 **THE RUN WATCHER GROUPED BY THE COMMIT MESSAGE, AND THE FIELD LIST WAS THE TELL.** `[found 2026-09-15 by VERIFYING an assumption instead of waiting for it to bite]`
    ⛔ `runs_report.py` grouped runs by `name`. **`gh run list` offers BOTH `name` and `workflowName` — and the presence of both IS the evidence they differ.** `name` is the RUN's display name, which GitHub derives from the **commit message** for a push-triggered run, and **both `collect.yml` and `browser.yml` have push triggers.**
    🔴 **EVERY PUSH RUN WOULD HAVE BECOME ITS OWN "WORKFLOW"** — a set of one, each its own latest, the flapping count never reaching 3. ➡️ **A broken collector reading as a healthy repo, from the watcher built to end silent failures.** ⚠️ **The most dangerous output this repo has is a false all-clear, and this was one by construction.**
    ✅ **Written to survive either meaning** (rule 252): `workflowName` first, **falling back to `name` rather than dropping the row** — a silent miss is worse than a wrong label. ⛔ The CLI docs list both fields and explain neither, so **the ambiguity is recorded rather than resolved.**
    ➡️ **THE GENERAL FORM: WHEN AN API OFFERS TWO FIELDS THAT SOUND LIKE THE SAME THING, THEY ARE NOT THE SAME THING, AND THE ONE YOU REACHED FOR FIRST IS PROBABLY THE DISPLAY ONE.**

269. 🆕 🔴🔴 **I BUILT A DROP ON ONE THAT HAD NEVER LANDED, AND MY OWN PARITY GUARD PASSED ON IT.** `[2026-09-14 → 15]`
    ⛔ Drop 54 shipped `watchdog.py` + `test_watchdog.py`. **The test landed; the watchdog did not.** I then built drop 55's test file from my local tree — which HAD drop 54's watchdog — so **drop 55 shipped assertions for code that was never in the repo.** The moment it landed: `AttributeError: no attribute '_published'`, the file died at check 80, red run.
    🔴 **ROOT CAUSE: I TREATED "uploaded" AS "landed".** Drops 49–53 I verified against `main` after every upload. For 54 I did not, and 55 was built on the assumption. ➡️ **"He said uploaded" is not evidence; `git show origin/main:<file>` is.**
    ⚠️ **AND `verify_drop_parity.py` — WRITTEN FOR EXACTLY THIS CLASS — REPORTED CLEAN.** It asked *"is my work in a zip?"* and never *"did the zip reach main?"*. ⛔ **Then my FIRST fix for that still passed**, because `watchdog.py` ships in drops 50 AND 54 and I wrote `any(delivered copy matches main)` — **drop 50's copy matches.** ➡️ **"Some version landed" is not "the version I am building on landed."** Now compares the NEWEST delivery.
    🔴 **A GUARD WRONG TWICE INSIDE AN HOUR, ON THE ONE DEFECT IT EXISTED TO CATCH.** ⚠️ **What did NOT break: the site.** `main` simply kept drop 50's older, working watchdog. **The failure was in the guarantee that the numbers were checked, never in the numbers.**

270. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A COUNT TAKEN FROM A TRUNCATED LIST, REPORTED AS COMPLETE, IS A FALSE ALL-CLEAR.** `[found 2026-09-15 while building the run watcher, BEFORE it shipped]`
    ⛔ `runs.yml` asked `gh run list --limit 100` and then computed a **24-HOUR** window from the answer. **Measured: ~96 cron runs/day before a single push-triggered run** — 43 `collect` crons, 24 `runs`, 4 `self-repair`, 2 `browser`. **So the list barely covered a day, and `browser` (2/day) and `self-repair` (4/day) could be flooded out of it ENTIRELY by `collect`.**
    🔴 **THE WATCHER WAS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO EXACTLY THE LOW-FREQUENCY WORKFLOWS IT MOST NEEDS TO SEE** — and its 24h flapping counts read as complete while being taken from less than 24h of data.
    ⛔ **AND RAISING THE LIMIT IS NOT THE FIX.** A bigger number is the same defect with a later expiry date, and this project has shipped that shape before (rule 166: a check that works until the world moves past it). ✅ **The limit went to 300 AND `runs_report.py` now detects truncation FROM THE DATA**: if the OLDEST run returned is younger than the window, it says the list did not reach back far enough instead of quietly undercounting. **So if the run rate grows past 300 too, this cannot silently un-fix itself.**
    ⚠️ **THE SAME PASS FOUND THE SECOND HALF:** a scheduled workflow producing NO runs was simply absent from the grouping, and **absence read as silence read as fine.** That is the `ncaaf` 19:0x class (rule 251). The expected set is now read from `.github/workflows/*.yml`, so a new scheduled workflow is covered the day it ships, and a workflow declaring no cron (`claude.yml` is mention-triggered) is correctly excluded. Both are reported as **"UNSEEN, not healthy"** — *"we saw no runs from it"* and *"it is healthy"* are different findings and **only one of them is true.**
    ➡️ **THE GENERAL FORM: WHENEVER YOU COMPUTE A RATE OVER A WINDOW FROM A CAPPED LIST, THE LIST MUST BE ASKED WHETHER IT COVERED THE WINDOW. A DENOMINATOR YOU DID NOT VERIFY IS A DENOMINATOR YOU INVENTED.**


271. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A RUN THAT CANNOT NAME THE CRON THAT FIRED IT MAKES A MISSING CRON UNPROVABLE.** `[2026-09-15, closing a question that had been open six days]`
    ⛔ `collect.yml` declares **43 crons**. Two of them — `4 19 * * *` and `18 19 * * *` — land in the same UTC hour, GitHub delays both by 11–15 minutes, and **a commit message names the MODE, not the cron.** So "did the 19:04 cron land?" was unanswerable from anything the repo or the API stored. **I measured it twice and got two different answers** (rule 251: "one never lands, 7 of 7", then "exactly 2 runs a day for 8 days"), and neither measurement could be adjudicated.
    ⚠️ **AND DROP 58'S CHECK COULD NOT HELP.** It asks whether a WORKFLOW produced any runs. A workflow with 43 crons produces runs all day long while one of them silently never lands — **the workflow is plainly healthy and the finding is invisible.**
    ✅ **`run-name:` stamps `github.event.schedule` into the run's title**, which `gh run list` returns as `name` — the field rule 268 warns is the DISPLAY name, **which is exactly why it is the right one here.** Free, no commit, no credits, and **recorded even when the run fails before committing or has nothing to commit**, which a git-log trailer never would be.
    ✅ **`runs_report.py` now asks the question per CRON**: parse the declaration, compute when it was last due, report it if no run carries its stamp. ⛔ **Bounded by what the list actually covers** (rule 270) and by a **45-minute grace measured off this repo's own delays** — a guard that fires on correct behaviour is the other failure. ⛔ **And a workflow that does not stamp is reported as CANNOT BE ATTRIBUTED, never as healthy** — *"I could not tell"* and *"it fired"* are different findings.
    ➡️ **THE GENERAL FORM: IF TWO CAUSES PRODUCE THE SAME OBSERVATION, NO AMOUNT OF LOOKING HARDER AT THE OBSERVATION WILL SEPARATE THEM. GO CHANGE WHAT IS RECORDED.**

272. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A TIMEOUT IS A FACT ABOUT THE CLOCK YOU CHOSE, NEVER A FACT ABOUT THE CODE — AND I FILED ONE AS A FACT ABOUT THE WORLD.** `[2026-09-15, found while being asked to fix it]`
    ⛔ I told Sam, and wrote into this ledger, that `test_box_live.py` and `test_budget.py` *"hang because the proxy blocks ESPN and CollegeFootballData."* **Both halves false.** `test_budget.py` reaches no network at all (33 checks, passes). `test_box_live.py` serves its ESPN payloads from `espnfix.json` over a **local `http.server`** (51 checks, passes). **Neither hangs.** The second one takes **115 seconds** and I had been killing it at **45**, then reporting my own timeout as a diagnosis.
    ✅ **ALL 71 FILES, MEASURED: green, 308 seconds total. The blind spot did not exist. The explanation did** — and it had been sitting in this document as a standing limitation.
    ⛔ **AND THE CI HARNESS HAD THE SAME HOLE FROM THE OTHER SIDE.** The `Tests` step ran every discovered file with **no per-file bound** under a job `timeout-minutes: 350`. One genuinely hanging file would have burned nearly **six hours** of runner time and then been CANCELLED — no `::error::`, no name in the `failed=` output, nothing in the issue. ➡️ **The one failure mode that produces no evidence at all, in the step whose entire job is producing evidence.**
    ✅ Every file now runs under a **600s** clock (**5.2×** the slowest measured file) and **a timeout is its own verdict**: *"TIMED OUT after 600s — it did not fail, it never answered."* **Not "FAILED".** A red assertion tells you WHAT is wrong; a timeout tells you only that nothing came back.
    ⚠️ **TWO OF MY OWN NEW CHECKS WERE VACUOUS**, found only by disabling what they watched: the `5/20` cron branch was unreachable by any test (**the same grammar bug `budget.py` shipped**), and a `"124 … rc=1"` text search matched the *FAILED* branch because `rc=1` appears three times in the step — **it passed with the timeout branch's `rc=1` deleted.** ✅ Replaced by **extracting the real shell function out of the YAML and executing it** against a planted hanging file. Rule 67; rule 260.
    ➡️ **THE GENERAL FORM: "IT HUNG" IS NOT A DIAGNOSIS, IT IS THE ABSENCE OF ONE. NEVER WRITE THE LIMIT YOU IMPOSED INTO THE RECORD AS A PROPERTY OF THE THING YOU WERE MEASURING.**

273. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A GUARD THAT NEEDS HISTORY WILL FALSE-ALARM ON ITS OWN DEPLOY DAY, AND MINE WAS 26 MINUTES FROM DOING IT.** `[2026-09-15, drop 59 → 60, caught before it fired]`
    ⛔ Drop 59's cron check asks *"was this cron due, and did a run carry its stamp?"* — **but the stamp only exists on runs created after the workflow landed.** On deploy day 23 of the 24 hours in the run list have no stamp. Measured on that exact shape: **27 crons reported missing, every one of which had run perfectly.**
    🔴 **AND I HAD WRITTEN THE OPPOSITE INTO THE DELIVERY DOC** — *"the check correctly reports nothing rather than guessing until a day of stamped runs exists."* **An assumption, typed as a reassurance, never driven.** Driving it is what found this, with 26 minutes left before the hourly job fired.
