    ⚠️ **THIS IS `CLAUDE.md`'S WORSE FAILURE, NOT THE MILDER ONE.** *"A guard that fires on correct code is the other failure, not a safe one."* **27 false findings in the first issue a new alert channel ever opens is how that channel gets muted on day one** — and then the next real failure is silent. Rule 238's lesson, arriving in a system built after rule 238.
    ✅ **THE FIX IS AN EVIDENCE FLOOR, PER WORKFLOW.** A cron is judged only from the timestamp of its own workflow's earliest STAMPED run. Before that there is no evidence either way, and **absence of evidence reported as a finding is the false all-clear's mirror image.** ⛔ **Per workflow, not one shared clock** — `browser` runs twice a day, so its first stamped run can be twelve hours behind `collect`'s.
    ⚠️ **AND MY FIRST GUARD FOR IT WAS VACUOUS:** it used a `browser` with NO stamped runs, which is excluded either way, so **a single global floor passed it.** Rule 67, third time this week, caught by swapping the global floor in and watching the suite stay green.
    ➡️ **THE GENERAL FORM: BEFORE SHIPPING A CHECK THAT COMPARES A DECLARATION TO A HISTORY, ASK WHAT IT SAYS ON THE FIRST DAY, WHEN THE HISTORY DOES NOT EXIST YET. THE ANSWER IS ALMOST NEVER "NOTHING".**

274. 🆕 🔴🔴 **I BUILT THE FIRE-ALARM TEST THAT CANNOT REPORT A FIRE, AND THEN BLAMED THE USER FOR SEVEN RUNS.** `[2026-09-15, run #7 — Sam: "ok so ive done this mutliple times but your ssaying im not doing it"]`
    ⛔ The Tier 3 DRILL exists to answer exactly one question — *does this path work?* — because the agent had never executed. **`claude-code-action@v1` exits 0 whether or not the agent did the work.** So run #7 read `triage SUCCESS 5s / fix SUCCESS 45s / overall SUCCESS` with **no `self-repair/drill` branch, no `self-repair-drill.md`, no PR.** Sam fired it seven times. Every run went green. Nothing was ever produced.
    🔴 **THAT IS `CLAUDE.md`'S FIRST RULE BROKEN BY THE THING BUILT TO UPHOLD IT** — *a green tick that proves nothing is worse than a red one, because it gets trusted.* And I trusted it.
    ⛔ **THE WORSE HALF IS WHAT I DID WITH IT.** `api.github.com` is blocked from this container, so from the repo side *"never fired"* and *"fired and did nothing"* are **indistinguishable** — the drill's own output was the only thing that could separate them, and it was lying. **I filled that gap with three messages of click-by-click instructions telling Sam he had not ticked a checkbox he had ticked every time.** ➡️ **When an instrument reports nothing wrong and the world disagrees, suspect the instrument before the person.** It took him pasting a run URL — which put it in my fetch provenance — for me to see anything at all.
    ✅ **THE FIX: THE DRILL ASSERTS ITS OWN ARTIFACT.** A branch or a PR, or the job fails loudly and names the likely cause. `if: always()`, so it still speaks when the agent step blew up. ⛔ **The REAL repair path is deliberately NOT held to that bar** — it has a legitimate empty outcome, because `CLAUDE.md` tells the agent an MLB finding gets a comment and no code change, and failing on "no branch" would red-flag the agent for obeying the freeze.
    ⚠️ **AND DRIVING THE GUARD CAUGHT A FALSE ALL-CLEAR INSIDE THE FIX.** My first version let a blank `gh` answer PASS (`PR=""` is not `"0"`) — **the second false all-clear in the same file, caught only because `test_drill_proof.py` executes the real shell against a stub rather than reading the YAML.** ✅ One further line was deleted rather than kept: the same coercion on the branch count was **unreachable by any mutation**, and by this repo's own rule an un-bitable line is decoration, not a guard.
    ➡️ **THE GENERAL FORM: A TEST WHOSE ONLY OUTPUT IS THE EXIT CODE OF SOMETHING THAT ALWAYS EXITS 0 IS NOT A TEST. ASSERT THE ARTIFACT, NOT THE STATUS.**

275. 🆕 🔴🔴 **THE AGENT WAS BLOCKED BY ITS OWN PERMISSION GATE, NOT BY AUTH — AND I NAMED THE WRONG CAUSE THREE TIMES.** `[run #8 log, 2026-09-15]`
    ⛔ I told Sam the OAuth secret was my leading suspicion, three separate times. **The log killed it outright:** `OIDC token successfully obtained`, `App token successfully obtained`, `Verified human actor: smh0602`, Claude Code 2.1.272 installed, model `claude-sonnet-5`, `total_cost_usd: 0.105246`. **Authentication worked perfectly and the run spent real subscription money.**
    🔴 **THE ACTUAL CAUSE, IN ONE FIELD:** `"permission_denials_count": 3`, alongside `"is_error": false, "subtype": "success"`. **The agent asked to do three things, was denied all three, and reported success because nothing errored.** Without an `--allowedTools` list it can read and think and nothing else — no file, no branch, no push, no PR.
    ⛔ **AND THE REAL REPAIR STEP HAD THE IDENTICAL DEFECT.** Same action, same missing allowlist. **Tier 3 was never capable of working, from the day it shipped** — and nothing would have revealed that until a genuine outage, at which point the agent would have burned quota, changed nothing and gone green.
    ⚠️ **THE PERMISSION GATE WAS NEVER WHAT MADE THIS SAFE.** `CLAUDE.md` is: open a PR, never merge, never touch MLB, never weaken a check — and a human presses merge. A gate that blocks everything is not a guardrail, it is an off switch that reports success.
    ✅ **Fixed on both steps**, plus `show_full_output: true` on the DRILL ONLY — the log itself named that switch as the reason the denials were counted but not named. ⛔ **Not on the repair step**, whose prompt interpolates live findings into a PUBLIC run log; the test asserts both halves so nobody turns it on everywhere later.
    ➡️ **THE GENERAL FORM, AND IT IS THE THIRD TIME TONIGHT: A STATED CAUSE I HAVE NOT READ OFF AN ARTIFACT IS A GUESS WEARING A DIAGNOSIS'S CLOTHES.** The timeout was one (rule 272), the checkbox was one (rule 274), and this was one. **All three dissolved the moment a real log was in front of me, and in every case the person or the machine I was blaming was fine.**

276. 🆕 🔴🔴 **THE REPO-CONNECTED SESSION CAN PUSH WORKFLOW FILES — AND GITHUB ATTRIBUTES ITS COMMITS TO `claude`, NOT TO SAM.** `[measured 2026-09-15 06:30Z on the push probe, NOT inferred]`
    ✅ **BOTH PROBES SUCCEEDED**, including the one that matters: `probe: touch .github/workflows to test workflow scope` changed `.github/workflows/collect.yml` and pushed cleanly, so the token **does** carry the `workflow` scope. The upload round-trip is genuinely over for ordinary files.
    🔴 **BUT THE COMMIT PAGE READS `claude committed · Verified`.** Not `smh0602`. ⛔ **Rule 253 says GitHub attributes a scheduled run to the repository user who last changed the workflow's `cron:` block** — and this repo has **50 crons across 5 workflows**, every one of which depends on that attribution staying a human with write access.
    ⚠️ **WHAT IS MEASURED AND WHAT IS NOT, KEPT SEPARATE.** Measured: the push works, the scope is there, the author is an account named `claude` and not Sam. **NOT measured: whether a cron-block change under that author actually stops scheduled runs.** ⛔ **And that test is destructive** — if it fails, every cron dies silently, with no red run, and the watchdog cannot report it because the watchdog is what summons the repair agent.
    ✅ **SO THE STANDING RULE HOLDS, NOW WITH A MEASUREMENT BEHIND IT RATHER THAN A CAUTION: any workflow file containing a `cron:` block goes through Sam by hand, even from a session that can push it.** ➡️ The convenience is real and the downside is the whole product going dark; those are not comparable quantities.
    ➡️ **THE GENERAL FORM: WHEN A NEW CAPABILITY ARRIVES, THE FIRST THING TO MEASURE IS NOT WHETHER IT WORKS BUT WHOSE NAME IT ACTS UNDER.**

277. 🆕 🔴🔴 **THE DENOMINATOR WAS PRINTED ON EVERY ROW THE WHOLE TIME, AND I EXPLAINED THE BOARD WITH WHAT I KNEW ABOUT FOOTBALL INSTEAD OF READING IT.** `[2026-09-15, hours after writing T58]`
    ⛔ **WHAT I WROTE INTO T58 AS THE MECHANISM:** *"NFL is two weeks into its season... a receiver with one good game reads as a near-certain OVER on a one-game denominator."* **Both halves false.** `[measured from `picks/fb-nfl-*.json`]` `logs_season: 2025`, `min_games: 6`, `denominator: games at >= 50% of team snaps`, and the observed `raw` strings are **"8 of 8"** and **"13 of 15"**. ➡️ **The card never reads a 2026 game at all.**
    🔴 **AND THE REFUTING FIELD WAS ON THE ARTIFACT, IN PLAIN TEXT, NAMED `raw`.** Not in a log, not behind an API, not expired — **in the published pick file, on every row, since before the claim was written.** One `json.load` would have stopped it.
    ⚠️ **THE TELL, FOR NEXT TIME: THE STORY WAS ABOUT THE SPORT AND THE QUESTION WAS ABOUT THE PIPELINE.** "It is week 2 of the NFL season" is a true sentence about the calendar and says nothing whatever about which season the collector queried. **Domain knowledge is the most confident-sounding source of a wrong answer, because it is genuinely true — just not about this.**
    ✅ **WHAT SAVED IT WAS THE HEDGE, WRITTEN BY ME, IN THE SAME PARAGRAPH:** *"BUT THIS IS A STORY, NOT A RESULT... the mechanism is UNTESTED and must not be repeated as though it were established."* ⛔ **The hedge is why this cost a struck paragraph instead of a shipped feature.** ➡️ **KEEP WRITING THE HEDGE, INCLUDING — ESPECIALLY — WHEN THE STORY FEELS OBVIOUS.**
    ➡️ **THE GENERAL FORM: BEFORE EXPLAINING A NUMBER, OPEN THE FILE THAT PRODUCED IT.** The founding rule of this project was *a fact about a query is not a fact about the world*; this is its twin — **a fact about the world is not a fact about the query.**

278. 🆕 🔴🔴 **"THE CONTROL IS UNCHANGED" IS NOT "THE CONTROL HELD", WHEN `n` DID NOT MOVE EITHER.** `[2026-09-15, re-reading T58's CFB control]`
    ⛔ T58 VOIDS **if CFB develops the same over/under gap beyond ±10 points.** Re-read today, CFB is **`+0.0`, `z = 0.00`, `p = 1.0000`** — identical to the pre-registration. **It is extremely tempting to write "the control is holding."**
    🔴 **IT IS NOT HOLDING. IT IS FROZEN.** CFB `n` is **97 in both readings** — not one new CFB row was graded between them. **The number could not have moved.** ➡️ **An unchanged statistic on an unchanged sample is not evidence of stability; it is the same measurement, read twice.**
    ⚠️ **AND THE NFL SIDE HAS THE MIRROR DEFECT.** The gap widened −34.6 → −36.2 as `n` went 100 → 125 — which looks like T58 confirming itself, **and is not, because those 125 rows CONTAIN the 100 that generated the hypothesis.** T58 requires rows published after 2026-09-15. **Nothing has advanced it by a single row.**
    ✅ **THE CHEAP GUARD, AND IT IS ONE LINE: PRINT `n` BESIDE EVERY RE-READ.** A verdict quoted without its denominator cannot be told apart from a stale cache.
    ➡️ **THE GENERAL FORM: WHEN A TRACKED NUMBER HAS NOT MOVED, THE FIRST QUESTION IS WHETHER ITS INPUT MOVED** — in code, in a control arm, in a dashboard tile. **Nothing changing and nothing being measured look identical from the outside.**


279. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A MEASUREMENT IS NOT SHIPPED UNTIL THE THING THAT WILL ASK FOR IT HAS ASKED, FROM WHERE IT WILL BE STANDING.** `[measured 2026-09-15 by the narrowed audit — and this rule ITSELF was lost for a day, see 280]`
    ⛔ Drop 59 built `run-name:` stamping and `runs_report.py` to answer *which declared crons are not landing*. **Built, driven, mutation-tested, verified against `main` — every §11 bar cleared.** ✅ And **`git clone` works** from the scheduled session.
    🔴 **BUT THE ACTIONS API ANSWERS `HTTP 403` TO THAT SESSION**: *"GitHub access to this repository is not enabled for this session."* ➡️ **The instrument is readable from inside CI, which is not where the question gets asked.** Step 1 therefore still runs on commit-log band accounting — **with exactly the blind spot drop 59 was built to remove.**
    ⚠️ **AND THE SAME 403 HAS A SECOND CONSEQUENCE NOBODY ASKED ABOUT.** `runs.yml` commits nothing, so **no artifact in the repo says whether the run watcher is alive** — its own death is invisible to every check that reads files.
    ✅ **THE FIX IS (a), NOT (b): MAKE `runs.yml` COMMIT ITS VERDICT** to `data/latest/runs.json` with a `written_at`. **That closes both holes at once** — the audit reads the stamped evidence for free, and a stale `written_at` makes a dead watcher visible to `watchdog.py`. ⛔ Granting the session API access buys the answer once a day, for Claude usage, and **leaves the watcher unwatched.**
    ➡️ **IT IS RULE 272 INVERTED.** There I invented a blind spot that did not exist; here I shipped one without noticing. **The common cause is identical — neither was settled by making the call and reading the answer.**

280. 🆕 🔴🔴 **I CITED A RULE NUMBER BEFORE WRITING THE RULE, AND THE NUMBER WENT TO SOMETHING ELSE.** `[found 2026-09-15 while adding §15 to the architecture doc]`
    ⛔ `self-healing-architecture.md` §14 ends *"Ledger rule 277"*, pointing at the measurement lesson above. **Rule 277 was never written.** Hours later I filed the T58 denominator finding as 277 — **so the architecture doc now cited a real, numbered, unrelated rule.**
    🔴 **THIS IS THE WORST SHAPE A BROKEN REFERENCE CAN TAKE.** A pointer to rule 999 is obviously dangling and gets fixed. **A pointer to a rule that exists and says something plausible-but-different is followed, believed, and never questioned** — the reader has no signal at all.
    ✅ **FIXED BOTH WAYS:** the lesson is written as **279**, and §14's pointer now names it. ⚠️ Nothing was renumbered — **277 and 278 are cited elsewhere already, and silently moving a rule number is this same defect committed deliberately.**
    ➡️ **THE GENERAL FORM: NEVER CITE AN IDENTIFIER YOU HAVE NOT YET CREATED.** Write the target first, or write the pointer as prose. **A forward reference in a numbered namespace is a claim that the number is reserved, and nothing reserves it.**


281. 🆕 🔴🔴 **THE BETTER-CALIBRATED BOARD CARRIES THE HONESTY NOTE AND THE WORSE ONE DOES NOT.** `[measured 2026-09-15, comparing the two boards' own card files]`
    ⛔ **MLB PUBLISHES ITS OWN MISS, IN NUMBERS, ABOVE THE ROWS:** *"under-60 hit 47.6% against a claimed 56.5% (−8.9 pts, n=168); 60-70 hit 49% against a claimed 64.2% (−15.2 pts, n=243)… every one of them will move."* ✅ **That is the product at its best** — it tells the reader the number has been wrong and by how much.
    🔴 **`picks/fb-nfl-latest.json` AND `picks/fb-ncaaf-latest.json` CARRY NO `calibration_warning` AT ALL.** Measured, both NONE. **And football is the board that misses by more**: NFL delivers **46.4%** overall, **49.4% against a stated 75.0%** in the ≥60 band, with the 80-90 bucket at **11 of 23 (z = −4.76)**.
    ⚠️ **THE LABEL WAS NEVER THE PROBLEM — RULE 55 IS SATISFIED.** Every football row already carries the `RECORD — no model` chip, and `no_model_note` explains the number is the player's own 2025 rate. ⛔ **What is absent is any statement of HOW THAT NUMBER HAS PERFORMED.** A reader sees `84` in a CONF cell; nothing on the page says that bucket has gone 11 of 23.
    📌 **THE CLASS: A CORRECT LABEL IS NOT A TRACK RECORD.** "DESCRIPTIVE" says where a number came from. It says nothing about whether it has been right. **Both boards needed both, one got both, and nobody compared them** — because each was reviewed against its own past, never against its sibling.
    ➡️ **THE GENERAL FORM: WHEN TWO SURFACES DO THE SAME JOB, DIFF THEM AGAINST EACH OTHER, NOT ONLY EACH AGAINST ITSELF.** Sam's standing rule — *everything we do for cfb we do for nfl and vice versa* — is the same instruction one level down, and it was never extended to MLB-versus-football.
    ✅ **FIXED FORWARD, NOT BY EDITING MLB:** the football card derives its own warning from its own record, and **a league under `MIN_N` says the sample is too small to read rather than printing a percentage.** ⛔ **CFB is n=97 today — under the bar — so that branch is live on day one, not hypothetical.**


282. 🆕 🔴🔴 **I BUILT A FILE RELAY TO STOP PASTES BEING TRUNCATED, SHIPPED IT ON AN UNCHECKED ASSUMPTION, AND THEN HAD A PASTE TRUNCATED.** `[2026-09-16]`
    ⛔ **THE ASSUMPTION:** Sam said *"my desktop"*, so I took Claude Code to be running on his Windows box and wrote it a task file to `C:\Users\Senor\Downloads\`. **I never checked.** Claude Code runs in a **cloud Linux VM with only the repo cloned** — a different machine, no `/mnt/c`, no WSL bridge, no path between them. I wrote the setup doc, had Sam commit a slash command to `main`, and told him it worked.
    🔴 **AND IT WAS CLAUDE CODE, NOT ME, THAT MEASURED IT** — five candidate paths, all raw, plus the observation that no Windows filesystem was mounted at all. ➡️ **Same shape as rule 277 and rule 279 in one day: a story about the world instead of a call to the thing itself.**
    ⚠️ **ONE PROPERTY I GOT RIGHT BY ACCIDENT: IT FAILS TO SILENCE.** The command reads nothing rather than something stale, so the *announce-the-task-number-first* check can never be fooled by it. **That was luck, not design, and it is recorded as luck.**
    🔴🔴 **AND THEN THE JOKE COMPLETED ITSELF: THE FALLBACK PASTE ARRIVED TRUNCATED.** The task-9 brief was cut off mid-sentence and the lost line was *"every guard ships with the mutation that proves it — declare them for the vacuity harness."* ⛔ **`test_card_fb.py` shipped with 0 `@vacuity` declarations, exactly the omission predicted by the risk I invented the relay to remove.**
    ✅ **WHAT SAVED IT WAS THE RECEIVER, NOT THE CHANNEL.** Claude Code said the message ended mid-sentence, **named what it could not know, and refused to guess** — then asked which rule was missing rather than inventing one. ➡️ **A truncated instruction is only dangerous when the reader fills the gap.**
    📌 **THE STANDING CHANGE: EVERY BRIEF ENDS WITH AN EXPLICIT END MARKER AND A COUNT OF ITS NUMBERED REQUIREMENTS**, so a reader can tell a finished message from a severed one without having to infer it from grammar.

283. 🆕 ⚠️ **MY OWN SPEC WAS WRONG AND THE AGENT'S CHOICE WAS RIGHT — RECORDED BECAUSE THE DEFAULT IS TO ASSUME OTHERWISE.** `[2026-09-16, the football calibration banner]`
    ⛔ I specified that a league under `MIN_N`=100 graded rows must print **no percentage at all**. Claude Code instead suppressed **per BAND** at n≥20, the shape MLB already uses, and flagged the disagreement rather than silently choosing.
    ✅ **ITS VERSION IS BETTER AND MINE WOULD HAVE RE-CREATED THE DEFECT.** CFB's readable sample is **71** — under 100 — so my rule would have blanked the CFB banner entirely, leaving a CFB reader with **no honesty note at all**, which is the exact hole the task existed to close. **Silence is worse than a caveated number.**
    ⚠️ **BUT ONE SENTENCE WAS GENUINELY MISSING, AND IT IS LIVE TODAY:** `[measured]` readable bands — **NFL 106 (over the bar), CFB 71 (under it)**. The CFB banner quotes *"80-plus hit 72.4% against a claimed 87% (−14.6 pts, n=29)"* on a league whose whole readable sample sits below the bar at which `calibration.py` refuses to alarm. ➡️ **Say so, and import `MIN_N` rather than retyping 100.**
    📌 **THE CLASS: A DESCRIPTION AND AN INFERENCE DO NOT NEED THE SAME BAR.** "This band went 21 of 29" is true at any n; what n governs is whether anything may be CONCLUDED from it. **I had collapsed the two, and the bar for alarming is the wrong bar for reporting history.**

284. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A BACKTEST ASKS WHETHER A NUMBER PREDICTS. IT NEVER ASKS WHETHER THE NUMBER IS CORRECT — AND THOSE FAIL IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS.** `[2026-09-17, Sam: "im just worried that the data may be wrong but we can backtest it"]`
    ⛔ **THE WORRY IS RIGHT AND THE INSTRUMENT IS WRONG.** A systematically wrong predictor can backtest GREEN (a home/away attribution swap inverts the sign, and a sign-flipped predictor with real signal still separates outcomes), and a perfectly correct predictor backtests RED whenever the thing simply does not predict. ➡️ **A green backtest is therefore not evidence the field is right, and a red one is not evidence it is wrong.** Correctness needs its own instrument, run first, or the backtest is measuring an unknown quantity.
    ✅ **THE INSTRUMENT FOR POSSESSION EXISTS AND IS EXACT — MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED.** `[nflverse play_by_play_2025, 48,771 plays, 285 games, 6,034 drives, 0 unparseable]` Summing `drive_time_of_possession` per team per game: **269 of 269 regulation games sum to EXACTLY 60:00. Mean |sum − 60:00| = 0.0s, max 0s.** All 16 overtime games sum above 60:00 (62:27 → 71:41). ➡️ **This is an arithmetic identity, not a tolerance**, and it is assertable on every game forever at zero cost.
    ⚠️ **AND ITS BLIND SPOT IS NAMED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED LATER: THE IDENTITY IS PRESERVED BY A HOME/AWAY SWAP.** 26:14 + 33:46 sums to 60:00 in either order. ⛔ **The sum check cannot see an attribution error, which is the single most likely bug in a derived build.** ➡️ **That is what the per-team season cross-check is for**, and it is the ONLY thing it is for — a swap moves Army from 34:25 to 25:35 and is unmissable per team while invisible per game.
    📌 **THE CLASS, AND IT GENERALISES PAST POSSESSION: FOR EVERY DERIVED FIELD, FIND THE CONSERVED QUANTITY FIRST.** Possession has one (60 minutes per game). Snaps have one. Drives have one. **A derived field with a conserved quantity gets a free exact guard; a derived field without one has to be cross-checked against an outside source, and that is strictly weaker.** ⛔ Do not let a field reach a pre-registered test before its correctness check has run — `claude/possession-validation.md` is the worked instance.

285. 🆕 🔴🔴 **GIT CANNOT DELTA-COMPRESS A GZIP, SO A CUMULATIVE `.gz` REWRITTEN N TIMES A DAY COSTS ITS FULL SIZE N TIMES A DAY.** `[2026-09-17, sizing the shadow record before building it]`
    📌 **THE SAME FEATURE, THE SAME DATA, TWO ARCHITECTURES, MEASURED:** 8,000 graded rows/week carrying all eight signal scores compress to **265 KiB/week** (170 b/row raw → **34 b/row gzipped**, ratio 19.9%). Written as **dated append-only files** that is **5.17 MiB over a 20-week season**. Written as **one cumulative `.gz` rewritten on each of ~8 daily card arms** it is **2,896 MiB over the same season.** ➡️ **560× — and nothing about the feature changed, only where the bytes land.**
    ⛔ **THE MECHANISM, SO NOBODY RE-DERIVES IT: git's delta compression works on similar blobs. A one-row change to the INPUT of gzip changes essentially the whole OUTPUT**, so two consecutive versions of a `.gz` share no usable delta and git stores each in full. ⚠️ **This repo already stores most snapshots gzipped, so the rule applies broadly — the protection today is that they are DATED and written once.**
    ✅ **THE RULE: an accumulating artifact is written to a DATED, WRITE-ONCE path. ⛔ Never to a single cumulative compressed file that a cron rewrites.** `data/<league>/<date>/...` and `picks/fb-<league>-<date>.json` are already the correct shape; **`data/<league>/latest/dossiers.json.gz` is not, and is overwritten every run.**
    ⚠️ **AND THE BUDGET FINDING THAT CAME WITH IT, WHICH IS BIGGER THAN THE FEATURE:** `[measured]` `.git` is **114 MiB over 26 days = 4.4 MiB/day → 1 GiB in ~7.7 months**, and **nothing watches it.** The shadow record adds **38 KiB/day = 0.84%** of that. ➡️ **The repo-growth watcher is the real outstanding cost risk on this project; a new graded-row archive is not.**

286. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A SPEND COUNTER THAT INCREMENTS *AFTER* THE CALL SUCCEEDS CANNOT SEE THE CALLS THAT FAILED — AND THE API BILLS THEM ANYWAY.** `[2026-09-18, the narrowed audit; measured against the account's own balance]`
    ⛔ **MEASURED, NOT ARGUED: 2026-09-17 the account was BILLED 304 credits and the repo recorded 261 (86%). 2026-09-18 by 12:48Z, BILLED 350, recorded 284 (81%).** The three-day zero-gap run (9/14, 9/15, 9/16) is over.
    🔴 **THE CODE, AND IT IS ONE LINE'S PLACEMENT.** `collect.py:collect_props()` — and `collect_half_totals()` — do `spent += u` **INSIDE** the `try`, and `odds_get()` takes `used` from the `x-requests-last` **response header**, which it never reads if `get()` raises. ➡️ **A per-event request that errors is billed by the Odds API and recorded as ZERO.**
    ⚠️ **AND THE COMMENT ABOVE THE `except` IS WHAT MADE IT INVISIBLE:** *"A game with no props posted yet is normal, not an error."* **TRUE ABOUT THE DATA. It is not a statement about the BILL, and it was read as one.**
    🔴🔴 **THE CONSEQUENCE IS THE CAP.** `_spend_under()` sums only `credits_used`, so `daily_allowance()` is blind to every uncounted credit. ⛔ **THIS IS THE 2026-09-05 DEFECT RETURNING WITH A NEW CAUSE** — `_dated_roots()`'s own docstring records *"billed 869 against a 600 cap and NO LEAGUE'S OWN COUNTER EVER PASSED 404."* **The 9/6 fix taught the counter about all three LEAGUES. It did not teach it about all the CREDITS.**
    📌 **THE CLASS, AND IT GENERALISES PAST MONEY: AN ACCUMULATOR YOU MAINTAIN IS A CLAIM; A TOTAL THE COUNTERPARTY ASSERTS IS EVIDENCE.** `credits_used` is ours. `credits_remaining` is the API's. ➡️ **WHEREVER BOTH EXIST, RECONCILE THEM — THE DIFFERENCE IS EXACTLY THE SET OF EVENTS YOUR OWN CODE DID NOT SURVIVE.**
    ✅ **THE GUARD FOR THE CLASS, FREE AND READING FILES ALREADY IN THE REPO:** assert `prev_remaining − credits_used == this_remaining` across the day's paid snapshots. **It would have fired on 2026-09-10.**
    ⛔🔴 **AND THE GUARD MUST NOT SHIP BEFORE THE FIX — THIS IS THE ONE PLACE §10's "EVERY FIX SHIPS WITH A GUARD" HAS TO WAIT.** `collect.yml`'s Tests step DISCOVERS every `test_*.py` and is `continue-on-error: ${{ github.event_name != 'push' }}` — **on a PUSH it BLOCKS and nothing is collected.** ➡️ **A chain guard added today would be RED on today's data (a real defect), and the next push would therefore stop the collector.** ✅ **ORDER: land the accounting fix, watch the chain go quiet, THEN add the assertion.** 📌 **The general form: a guard for a defect that is still live is a guard that takes the product down; the guard is the SECOND commit, never the first.**

287. 🆕 🔴 **A NEW PAID LINE ITEM PUT IN ITS OWN KEY FOR GOOD REASONS IS A LINE ITEM NOTHING SUMS.** `[2026-09-18, commit `9dbfe241`, "fb: buy the first-half TOTAL", shipped 05:17Z that morning]`
    ✅ **THE REASONING WAS RIGHT AND IS QUOTED HERE SO NOBODY UNDOES IT:** *"THE PAID EXTRA IS RECORDED SEPARATELY, never folded into `credits_used`. The bulk pull's bill and the per-game bill move for different reasons, and a single total cannot be reconciled against `budget.py` afterwards."*
    ⛔ **AND NOTHING WAS TAUGHT TO READ `half_total_credits`** — not the audit sweep, not `budget.py`'s measured lines, **not `_spend_under()`, i.e. not the daily cap.** ✅ **Measured the same day: `data/ncaaf/2026-09-18/gamelines/1125.json.gz` carries `half_total_credits: 2`, and 2 is EXACTLY the residue the balance chain showed at that step.**
    ⚠️ **TWO CREDITS TODAY. IT IS PER-GAME** — a 41-game CFB Saturday is up to ~41 per gamelines pull, twice a day, against a cap that cannot see any of it.
    📌 **THE CLASS: SPLITTING A NUMBER OUT OF A TOTAL IS HALF A CHANGE. THE OTHER HALF IS EVERY CONSUMER OF THAT TOTAL** — and the consumer that matters is never the report, it is the GUARD. ➡️ **Grep the key you are moving away from before you ship the key you are moving to.**

288. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A KILLED PROCESS DOES NOT RUN `finally`, SO A HARNESS THAT REWRITES SOURCE IN PLACE LEAKS WHENEVER IT IS KILLED — AND THE GUARD FOR IT WAS BUILT INTO THE ONE WORKFLOW THAT DOES NOT NEED IT.** `[measured 2026-09-18, diagnosing issue #66]`
    ⛔ **`test_vacuity.py:202` runs the real mutation sweep against the CHECKED-OUT TREE** — its own header says so — rewriting live source and restoring it in a `finally`. **`SIGTERM` terminates without running `finally`.** `collect.yml` gives every test file 600s and the sweep needs ~19.4 minutes, so `timeout -k 15 600` fires on it every run.
    ✅ **DRIVEN, NOT REASONED: sweep started, SIGTERM at 90s, parent confirmed dead, tree re-read with no python alive and again ten seconds later → ` M cfb.py`, the coaches-probe filename mutated.** `cfb.py` is a COLLECTOR MODULE; the Tests step is `continue-on-error`; every step after it builds data, commits and pushes.
    ⛔ **THE `git add data/ picks/` ALLOWLIST DOES NOT HELP.** It correctly refuses the mutated `.py` — **and the WRONG DATA that `.py` produces is exactly what the allowlist permits.** `leaked()`, the guard built for this, never runs: the process was killed before reaching it.
    🔴 **AND IT IS ~HALF OF EVERY RUN, NOT A RARE RACE.** Sampling the tree at 1 Hz across a full sweep: **~52% of sampled seconds had a live source file rewritten** — `dossier_fb.py`, `cfb.py`, `collect.py`, `nfl.py`, `cfbd_budget.py`, two workflow files, and **a file under `data/ncaaf/latest/`**. ⚠️ **MY FIRST CLAIM WAS THAT IT LEAKS ON EVERY RUN, AND A 600s KILL CAME BACK CLEAN.** The sweep alternates mutated run / restored run, so a kill is a coin flip. **The measured rate replaced the prediction; the prediction was mine and it was wrong.**
    📌 **THE CLASS, AND IT IS THE SHARPEST PART: `vacuity.yml` ALREADY CARRIES THE TREE-CLEAN CHECK, and its comment names this exact hazard — *"a leaked mutation in a repo whose collector commits every few minutes would be committed."* `collect.yml`, the workflow that actually commits every few minutes, has no such check anywhere.** ➡️ **When you write a guard, ask which workflow carries the RISK, not which one you happened to be editing.**

289. 🆕 🔴🔴 **CONFIRMING AN ANOMALY IS REAL IS NOT EXPLAINING IT, AND STOPPING THERE FEELS LIKE DILIGENCE.** `[2026-09-18, Sam: "i actually wanted to see if youd catch on to the pattern there but you never did"]`
    ⛔ **Sam gave three line-movement examples over several days — Chase Burns 17.5 → 8.5 outs, Brady Basso 5.5 → 14.5, Kam Shanks 18.5 → 41.5. I verified all three against our own snapshots, reported that the moves were real, and NEVER ASKED WHY ANY OF THEM MOVED.**
    🔴 **His answer:** *"the book didnt know something that others didnt, it was a workload change but was reported in the news."* ➡️ **The book was not ahead of the market. WE WERE BEHIND THE NEWS.**
    📌 **This is the founding error in a new costume.** "A fact about a query is not a fact about the world", and rule 277's twin. **Here: I answered a question about our RECORDS when the question was about the WORLD.** That a move exists is a fact about our snapshots; what caused it is a fact about the world, **and only the second one is worth anything.**
    ⚠️ **AND THE FIRST USE OF THE ANSWER IS A VETO, NOT A BET.** Our projections are season-to-date, so a model seeing Burns at 8.5 against a 15.5 average **screams VALUE at exactly the moment the line is right.** ➡️ See `claude/line-movement.md`.

290. 🆕 🔴 **A PREMISE THAT IS TRUE ONLY ON THE DAY YOU WRITE IT IS RULE 67 WITH A FUSE ON IT.** `[measured 2026-09-18 — the red that turned every collector run of the day red]`
    ⛔ **`test_dossier_coverage.py`'s fixture copies the real `data/<lg>` tree and strips `latest/dossiers.json.gz` so that "a dossier came out" means THIS run produced one. The writer writes TWO paths and the strip covered ONE:** `data/<lg>/<date>/dossiers/HHMM.json.gz` is **committed**, so `copytree` carries every previous run's archive in and `len(_arch) == 1` was counting the whole history.
    ✅ **It read 1 on 2026-09-17, the day it was written, when none had been committed yet — and could never read 1 again.** Measured the next day: **4 archives under `ncaaf`, 6 under `nfl`, every one a correct write-once reading from a correct run.** **The product was never wrong.**
    ⚠️ **The comment three lines above the strip already names this failure** — *"the same premise that went stale in `test_accumulators.py`"* — **and the strip was applied to one path of two.** ⛔ **Knowing the class does not protect you from the instance.**
    📌 **AND THE OLD ERROR TEXT CLAIMED A GUARD IT DID NOT HAVE:** *"a SECOND archive path would be a second thing to drift"* — **nothing checked the path at all.** An archive named `1808-nfl.json.gz`, or under `<lg>/dossiers/`, or dated to the wrong day, all passed a bare count of 1. **Rule 249: a check must ask its own prose.** ➡️ **When a check goes stale, read its failure MESSAGE and ask whether the assertion ever asked that question.**
    ⚠️ 🔴🔴 **AND I MADE THE SAME CATEGORY ERROR IN THE BRIEF THAT FIXED IT.** `[caught by Claude Code, 2026-09-18, and verified independently]` **I wrote that `main` carried SIX nfl archives and named `1808.json.gz`. It carried FIVE, and `1808` was NEVER COMMITTED** — `git log --all -- data/nfl/2026-09-18/dossiers/1808.json.gz` is empty. **It was written by the test's own fixture run at 18:08, and I read the FIXTURE'S TOTAL — 5 inherited plus the 1 that run wrote — as the repo's inherited count.** ⛔ **That is precisely the conflation the defect is about, committed while describing the defect.** ✅ **The agent measured 5, refused to paste my number into the permanent record, said why, and cited rule 166. It was right on every count.** 📌 **KNOWING THE CLASS DOES NOT PROTECT YOU FROM THE INSTANCE — and it does not protect you from committing the instance in the fix.**

291. 🆕 🔴🔴 **I STATED THE MECHANISM CORRECTLY AND THEN PRESCRIBED ITS OPPOSITE, THREE PARAGRAPHS APART, IN ONE DOCUMENT.** `[2026-09-19, task 41's brief — caught by the repo-connected agent, driven and confirmed]`
    ⛔ **§1b of the brief said, correctly:** *"`git worktree prune` only drops registrations whose directory is already gone, and these directories are still there."* ⛔ **The fix section of the SAME brief said:** *"keep the `git worktree prune` — it is still the right cover for the SIGKILL case."* **SIGKILL leaves the directory.**
    ✅ **DRIVEN:** create a worktree → registered 1 · `git worktree prune` → **still 1** · delete the directory, prune again → 0. **A SIGKILLed run leaked a 59 MB worktree forever.**
    📌 **THE CLASS, AND IT IS NOT A TYPO: KNOWING A MECHANISM AND APPLYING IT ARE SEPARATE ACTS, AND THE FIRST ONE FEELS LIKE THE SECOND.** I had the rule in my hand, wrote it down in my own words, and then reasoned as if it said something else. ➡️ **When a document states a mechanism AND prescribes an action, check the action against the stated mechanism before shipping — the contradiction will not announce itself.**
    ✅ **The agent's replacement is better than what was asked for:** worktrees carry their owner's PID and startup reaps any whose owner is dead — exact rather than a heuristic on age, and a live sweep is never touched, so *"never run two at once"* stays discouraged rather than becoming destructive.

292. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A DRIVE THAT EXERCISES THE HELPER LEAVES THE LIVE ASSERTION UNTESTED — AND EVERY MUTATION STILL COMES BACK RED, WHICH IS WHY IT SURVIVES.** `[2026-09-19, found by the agent while implementing task 41 §2]`
    ⛔ The fix pulled the divergence condition into `_divergence()` and drove it four ways in a temp tree. **All four passed. Three mutations went red. And restoring the ORIGINAL always-true spelling on the live check — `isinstance(_today, int)` — left every one of those drives GREEN**, because the drives call the helper while the live `ck` is a **separate expression** that nothing was asserting about.
    📌 **THE SHAPE: extracting a condition into a function makes the FUNCTION testable and quietly makes the CALL SITE untested.** The refactor that improves testability is the refactor that opens the hole, and the drives all still pass, so nothing looks wrong.
    ✅ **THE FIX IS A META-CHECK THAT READS THE FILE'S OWN PARSED AST** and requires the live `ck` to consume `_divergence`'s verdict. ⚠️ **And its FIRST version reddened on correct code** — it matched a bare `Name` where the condition is `not _fires`, a `UnaryOp` — which is CLAUDE.md's other failure and was caught by driving it.
    ➡️ **This is rule 244 one level up, and rule 249 one level up, and it is the third time this week the defect has been "the guard is correct about the thing it is no longer pointed at."**

293. 🆕 ⚠️ **A NUMBER REPEATED IS NOT A NUMBER MEASURED.** `[2026-09-19 — the CFB fixture anomaly]`
    ⛔ **I wrote "0.37%" and "98x better than the real feed" in two project docs, a ledger changelog entry, and four task briefs across two days. I had never run it.** ✅ Measured on the real fixture through the real function: **`anomaly_pct 0.506`**, so the gap is **71.7x**, not 98x.
    ⚠️ **The conclusion did not change and that is exactly the danger** — a figure that supports the right answer is never audited. 📌 ➡️ **A number carried across a document boundary has become hearsay. Re-measure it or cite where it was measured; do not retype it.**

294. 🆕 🔴🔴 **`git rev-list --objects --since=<date> HEAD` ANSWERS "REACHABLE FROM THE COMMITS IN THAT WINDOW", NOT "INTRODUCED IN IT" — AND BOTH OF US HIT IT, IN DIFFERENT FORMS, ON THE SAME DAY.** `[measured 2026-09-19, task 42 / PR #81]`
    ⛔ **MY VERSION:** I sized repo growth with `rev-list --objects --since="7 days ago" HEAD`, which includes **the entire tree of the boundary commit**. ✅ **Driven side by side on one clone, one window:**
    ```
    --since="7 days ago" HEAD        7.71 MB/day   <- mine, inflated
    HEAD --not <base>                4.57 MB/day   <- the real figure
                          overcount  1.69x
    ```
    ⛔ **THE AGENT'S VERSION, INDEPENDENTLY:** its first per-day series used `rev-list --objects --no-walk <day's commits>` — the same "reachable, not introduced" error in a different spelling — producing **31–41 MB per day against a 37 MB window TOTAL, roughly 8× out.**
    🔴 **AND THIS IS THE PART THAT MATTERS: BOTH WRONG NUMBERS WERE ENTIRELY PLAUSIBLE.** Its note: *"the shape looked perfectly plausible."* 7.72 MB/day is a reasonable-sounding repo growth rate. **Neither error announced itself, and neither would ever have.**
    ✅ **THE GUARD THAT CATCHES IT IS AN IDENTITY, NOT A REVIEW: the per-day series must RECONCILE with the window total.** Driven by restoring the original bug — 1 of 25 red, exactly that check, no collateral. 📌 ➡️ **For any windowed measurement over a history, assert the parts sum to the whole. "Does this number look right" cannot catch an 8× error; a conservation check catches it on the first run.**
    ⚠️ **AND THE CORRECT INCANTATION IS WORTH WRITING DOWN, BECAUSE IT IS NOT THE OBVIOUS ONE:** `git rev-list --objects <base>..HEAD`, with sizes from `git cat-file --batch-check='%(objectsize:disk)'`. ⛔ **`--since`, `--no-walk` and a `du` diff all answer different questions, and only one of the four is the repository's actual cost.**

295. 🆕 ⚠️ **A MEASUREMENT CITED INSIDE AN ERROR MESSAGE READS AS A LIVE READING.** `[2026-09-19, two instances the same day]`
    ⛔ **Instance 1, ours:** `repo_watch.py`'s shallow-clone refusal says *"measured as 43.39 MiB against a real 105.14 MiB"* — those are the figures from the investigation, baked in as constants. **Run in a different shallow clone it reported 35.35 MiB while the message still said 43.39.** A reader takes it for this clone's numbers.
    ⛔ **Instance 2, the live product:** §6 told 90 college readers *"roughly the fourth week of the season, and this is the third"* — a hardcoded constant on a branch with **no condition on any data at all**, so in week 10 it would still have said "the third" (task 43).
    📌 **THE CLASS: a number frozen into prose is a number that stops being a measurement the moment it is written, and prose does not carry the tense to say so.** ➡️ **Either derive it at the point of printing, or mark it explicitly as the reading that motivated the check — never let a constant sit where a reading would.**



296. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A GUARD THAT SHARES THE DEFECT'S ASSUMPTION SCORES A TICK FOR A CASE IT IS NOT TESTING.** `[2026-09-19]`
    `watchdog.check_freshness` read `freshness.json["rows"]`; `collect.write_freshness()` emits `{"artifacts": rows}` — `rows` is the WRITER'S LOCAL VARIABLE NAME and has never been a field. ⛔ **The staleness branch had never once executed**, which is why `health.json` said `healthy: true` on the same run the freshness contract said NFL was failing.
    🔴 **AND `test_watchdog_coverage.py` — the matrix whose whole job is proving the watchdog catches things — PLANTED ITS FIXTURE WITH THE SAME WRONG KEY.** So the "freshness contract reports stale" row had scored a green tick every run while testing nothing. **Fixing the reader is what turned the test red.**
    📌 **THE CLASS: when a test is written from the same mental model as the code, it does not test the code — it tests the model.** ➡️ **A fixture must be built from the WRITER'S OWN OUTPUT, not from the reader's expectation of it.** `test_reader_keys.py` now derives, for every repo-written JSON artifact, every literal key any module reads off it, and requires that key to exist both in the real committed file and in the writer's own dict.

297. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A PERMISSION FAILURE SWALLOWED BY `|| <default>` BECOMES AN ANSWER.** `[measured 2026-09-19, self-repair run 35424213172]`
    The `triage` job was granted `contents: read` and `pull-requests: read` and **no `issues:` scope at all**, while its one real step calls `gh issue list`. Every call 403'd. The query ended `2>/dev/null || echo "[]"`, so the refusal became an empty list: the step printed **`open watcher issues: 0` while labelled issue #80 was open**, stood down, and **the run went green**.
    ⛔ **AN UNREADABLE QUEUE IS NOT AN EMPTY QUEUE**, and writing one as the other is this project's oldest recurring error wearing a shell idiom. ➡️ **`test_gh_scopes.py` derives the scope every `gh <noun> <verb>` needs from the job's own shell — and follows `gh api` calls inside the Python a job runs, which is where `runs.yml`'s `actions: read` requirement lives and where no reader of the workflow would ever see it.**

298. 🆕 🔴 **A STEP THAT EXITS SKIPS ITS SIBLINGS. TWO VERDICTS NEED TWO GATES THAT BOTH RUN.** `[measured 2026-09-19]`
    `collect.yml` ends in three terminal gates. The suite was red on every collector run for two days — over a missing PyYAML, on entirely correct code — so the second gate fired every time and **the third never ran once**: 94 runs, 0 success, 68 failure, 26 cancelled over 29 hours, and **not one of them ever said whether a converge pass had also failed.** ⛔ The channel that went dark was the FOOTBALL one.
    ➡️ **Every gate after the first in its job carries `always()`, and `test_gate_order.py` discovers the gates from the workflows rather than naming them** — a gate added below an existing one inherits the bug by default, because `if:` without `always()` is what you get for free.

299. 🆕 🔴🔴 **WIRING AN UNRUN VERIFIER IS NOT A ONE-LINE JOB — THREE WEEKS OF NEVER RUNNING IS THREE WEEKS OF NOBODY FINDING OUT ITS QUESTIONS WERE WRONG.** `[2026-09-19]`
    `verify_nfl.py` had been in the repo for three weeks and **nothing had ever invoked it** (rule 78). Running it produced **five failures, four of them FALSE** on a perfectly correct early season: `week` is CONSTANT, `ahead_out` is CONSTANT, `ahead_out` resolves, and vs-position has no defences — all four are what a season holding **one week** looks like.
    ✅ **MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED:** `ahead_out` is non-zero on **0 of 1,040** week-1 rows in the complete 2024 season and **0 of 1,071** in 2025 — it reads a teammate's TRAILING usage. The live schedule pulled the same morning says week 1 is **16 of 16 final** and week 2 is **1 of 16**.
    ➡️ **AND THE REPLACEMENT IS HARDER, NOT SOFTER.** What the constant-column check was accidentally standing in for — *are the logs stale?* — is now asked directly against the schedule: **the logs must cover every week the schedule reports as fully final.** A one-week season in December fails that outright, where the old check passed the moment a second week arrived. 📌 **Shipping it as a gate unexamined would have reddened every NFL run on correct data — the other failure, not the safe one.**

300. 🆕 ⛔ **AN OPTIONAL IMPORT THAT DECIDES AN ANSWER IS A REQUIRED IMPORT.** `[measured 2026-09-19 in CI]`
    `test_watch_label.py` §6 extracted the step under test inside `try: import yaml`. The runner does not install PyYAML, so `_RUN` came back `None` and **ten checks went red on a workflow that was entirely correct** — `❌ 10 of 41 checks FAILED`, every run, for days. By rule 298 that also silenced the converge gate below it.
    ⚠️ **THE EXISTING GUARD PERMITTED IT.** `test_harness.py` already forbade a top-level third-party import and explicitly allowed a guarded one with a loud skip — which is right. The defect is not the import, it is that **the file's ANSWER depended on it**. ➡️ **So the question is no longer "is the import guarded" but "does the file still PASS without it", and that is answered by RUNNING it that way**: every test carrying a guarded third-party import is re-run with that module made unimportable. ✅ An optional CROSS-CHECK survives unchanged and simply reports one fewer check.

That is not luck; it is what deriving a surface instead of listing it does.** ⛔ **Their branch shipped and mine was DELETED rather than merged** — two implementations of one fix is a guaranteed conflict on Sam's desk, and theirs was already pushed. **Verified first by merging both into a throwaway tree.**

302. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A RULE STATED FOR EVERY LEAGUE AND ENFORCED IN ONE IS A RULE ENFORCED IN ONE.** `[measured 2026-09-19 across every dated card in `picks/`]`

The permanent record of what this product advertised on the 17th is partly a different week's board. One other is 39 hours late and one is 112.

    🔴🔴 ~~**The eleven already-damaged files … the list may only shrink, because a published card may never be edited, including to repair one.**~~ **STRUCK 2026-09-19 EVENING — THE FIRST HALF IS FALSE AND I NEVER CHECKED IT.** `picks/` is committed. `[measured on a full clone, 3,475 commits]` **every recoverable card has a committed version from before its own first kickoff — 14 of 15, the one exception carrying no kickoff at all**, some within fifteen minutes:

303. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A GUARD WHOSE VERDICT IS DECIDED BY PRODUCTION DATA WILL FLIP WITHOUT A CODE CHANGE — AND TWICE IN TWO DAYS IS A CLASS, NOT A COINCIDENCE.** `[2026-09-19]`
    `test_dossier_coverage.py` passed **42 of 42** against `main` at 10:11Z and failed **4 of 42** against `main` at 16:04Z. ⛔ **Zero code change to that test or to `dossier_fb.py` between the two.** The only difference was **72 files the collector had committed under `data/`** — the §3 fixture builds from the first two games of the live board, and today's two had no prior meeting where yesterday's did.
    ⚠️ **AND THE NIGHT BEFORE, THE SAME SHAPE:** `test_credit_balance.py`'s declared mutation only bit because the live repo happened to be finding-free — and the reason it was finding-free was the very freshness-key defect being fixed. **That guard would have gone vacuous the first time anything went stale, with no code change at all.**
    📌 **THE CLASS: if a check reads a LIVE artifact to decide whether it can ask its question, the answer is a property of today, and the check is a coin that lands green most days.** ➡️ **Build the condition, do not wait for it** — a synthetic tree whose shape is known — and where the subject genuinely is the live artifact, make the question one the artifact cannot change the answer to.

304. 🆕 ⛔ **AN EXCEPTION SURFACE IS VALIDATED AGAINST THE CODE, NEVER AGAINST TODAY'S OUTPUT.** `[2026-09-19]`
    `dossier_fb.audit()` skips seven `CARRIED` subtrees because they are verbatim source data, and it **refused to write the whole dossier unless all seven appeared in that slate's output** — reasoning that a name which stopped appearing meant the walk was skipping a subtree that was not there. ⛔ **The inference is invalid.** `meetings` is present only if some game on the board has a prior meeting inside 365 days; `weather` only outdoors; `live` only mid-game. **A one-game NFL Thursday between two teams who have not met would have written NOTHING to the football tabs**, and it FAILED CLOSED.
    ✅ **THE REAL QUESTION — can this exception surface be padded with junk to silence a finding? — IS ANSWERABLE STATICALLY AND IS STRICTLY HARDER:** every `CARRIED` name must be a key the module itself emits, read from its own AST **including subscript assignments** (`d["closing"] = {…}` is written that way, and a first draft that read only dict literals accused correct code), **and** must have appeared in at least one stored dossier artifact — 20 artifacts, 3,071 distinct keys. ⚠️ A junk name fails both on a quiet Tuesday as surely as on a full Saturday. **An absence is now reported on the document as `carried_absent`.**

305. 🆕 🔴 **TWO AGENTS WORKING THE SAME LIST PRODUCE A MERGE CONFLICT, NOT TWICE THE FIX — AND THE ONE WITH PUSH ACCESS WINS BY DEFAULT.** `[2026-09-19]`
    Both this session and the repo-connected agent fixed all nine ranked audit items overnight, independently, touching **the same seven files**. ⛔ Merging both would have put the conflict on Sam's desk. **Theirs was already pushed as eleven PRs, so mine was deleted** — after verifying theirs by merging all eleven onto today's `main` one at a time (zero conflicts), confirming the result **byte-identical** to their own integration branch, and running the suite (**109 files, 0 failures**).
    🔴 **AND MINE WAS WRONG IN A WAY REVIEWING THEIRS EXPOSED.** My football card freeze locked the WHOLE dated card once its FIRST kickoff passed. **Theirs freezes PER ROW.** An NFL Sunday runs 17:00Z to 03:00Z — mine would have stopped the card repricing the 4pm and night games every week. ⚠️ **The correct rule is about a row whose game has started, and I had read it as a property of the file.** 📌 **The value of a second agent is not a second patch; it is the review neither of us could give our own work.**

306. 🆕 ⛔ **A STORE THAT CHARGES FOR A REWRITE AS IF IT WERE AN INSERT MAKES THE LARGEST DOCUMENT UNEDITABLE FIRST — AND THE LARGEST DOCUMENT IS THE RECORD.** `[2026-09-19]`
    The project knowledge cap check adds a doc's NEW size to the running total **without subtracting its old size**, so replacing the 1.72 MB ledger read as adding a second one and was refused. ⛔ **The 11:30Z MLB grading run could not append its slate**, and parked it in a second file rather than risk the only copy of every graded row — right, and a record splitting a little further every day.
    ✅ **THE SPLIT RESTORED IT:** slates before 2026-09-13 and changelog entries before 2026-09-15 moved verbatim to `claude/pick-ledger-archive.md`, `collections.Counter` reproducing the original **9,113 lines exactly**, 1.72 MB → 0.63 MB, then the parked slate merged home and writability **proved by writing three times**.
    ⚠️ **AND THE ORDER IS THE OPPOSITE OF THE OBVIOUS ONE.** Write the TRIMMED document FIRST — that write is small enough to fit — and only then the archive, because the total does not fall until the big document is gone. **The note written the night before said "archive first" and would have failed.**
    📌 **THE CAP IS ON THE TOTAL, SO SPLITTING BUYS NO RUNWAY.** ~1.61M of 2.00M against ~46 KB a slate is about a month, whatever the shape. **That is a decision, not a fix.**

307. 🆕 🔴🔴 **"CHANGED AFTER THE EVENT" IS NOT "CORRUPTED" — A FILE WHOSE SECTIONS ARE UNDER DIFFERENT CONTRACTS CANNOT BE JUDGED WHOLE.** `[2026-09-19 evening]`
    I measured *"was this card rewritten after its first kickoff"* and reported **11 of 13, 85%**. The repo-connected agent measured it **per section** and got **five**. ⛔ **Both numbers are right and only one of them is the question.** A football card carries `picks`, `top_plays` and `parlays` — published estimates, frozen at kickoff — beside `game_lines` and `sgp`, which `next_line_slate()` deliberately advances to the next unstarted slate so the tab is not empty between them.
    🔴 **AND THE EXAMPLE I LED WITH WAS THE ONE PART WORKING AS DESIGNED.** `picks/fb-nfl-2026-09-17.json`: picks 25→25 identical, top_plays identical, sgp identical, parlays identical, **`game_lines` 1→14 and nothing else.** I had read that docstring earlier the same day.
    📌 **THE CLASS: a whole-file diff answers "did the bytes move", and nobody is ever asking that.** ➡️ **Before calling a change damage, name the section's contract** — and where a file mixes contracts, the comparison is per section or it is noise. ⚠️ **It inflated a real finding by ~3x, which is worse than inventing one: the true five were buried in nine that were fine.**

308. 🆕 ⛔ **A WARNING WRITTEN BESIDE ONE LINE DOES NOT PROTECT THE LINE BELOW IT.** `[2026-09-19, found by the repo-connected agent in `freshness.py`]`
    `_football_contract()` carries eleven lines of comment explaining that **`{picks}` is the WRONG argument for football** — `collect.py` passes `picks/ncaaf`, *"a directory that does not exist"* — and that the card row must therefore hardcode `picks/fb-<league>-latest.json`. ⛔ **Fifteen lines further down, the `fb-record` row gates itself on `glob.glob(f"{picks}/fb-{league}-2*.json")`.**
    `[verified live 2026-09-19]` that glob finds **0 files** under `picks/ncaaf` and `picks/nfl` while `picks/` holds **15** — and `fb-record` is **absent from both leagues' published `freshness.json`**. **The football Track Record's freshness row has never once been added in production.** The grader could stop entirely and nothing would notice.
    📌 **I READ THAT FUNCTION TWICE ON THE DAY AND MISSED IT, because the comment reassured me the class was handled.** ➡️ **A comment describing a hazard is evidence the hazard is KNOWN, never evidence it is fixed. Check every use of the named argument, not the one the comment sits beside.**

309. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A BAR MET BY ROWS THAT CANNOT CARRY THE SIGNAL IS NOT A BAR — COUNT THE UNITS THE COLUMN IS CAPABLE OF VARYING IN.** `[measured 2026-09-19 off the live nflverse feed]`
    `nfl.py`'s `ahead_out` guard fired on **every scheduled `collect` run**: *"CONSTANT ZERO across 1,188 player-weeks spanning 2 weeks [1, 2]. That is a join failure, not a result."* **It was not a join failure.** `stats_player_week_2026.csv.gz` held **1,190 rows — 1,118 in week 1 and 72 in week 2 — and those 72 rows were two teams, DET and BUF.** nflverse had published **one of week 2's sixteen games**.
    ⛔ **Week-1 rows are zero by ARITHMETIC** (`share_before()` reads strictly earlier weeks — the code already said so), and among week-2 OUT/DOUBTFUL players on DET or BUF **not one is in a prop position**, so no row had a candidate that could be non-zero. **The column was zero BY CONSTRUCTION, and 1,118 rows that could never carry the signal are what cleared the 500-row bar.**
    ⚠️ **THIS IS THE THIRD TIME THE SAME GUARD HAS ACCUSED A HEALTHY BUILD**, and the third distinct shape: week-1-only (2026-09-10), CFB's `ahead_out_lastwk` needing a third week (2026-09-11), and now a week that is *present* but 1/16th published. `cfb.py` had already written *"the bar was counting the wrong thing: rows, when the question is WEEKS."* ➡️ **This is one turn further: rows IN THE WEEKS THAT COUNT. A week present in the log is not a week published.**
    ✅ Fixed by counting rows in every week after the log's first. **Strictly preserved:** run #194 (constant zero over a real season) still raises with 640 eligible rows; a fully published week 2 with a constant column still raises. `test_nfl_week1.py` §4c drives all six cases and **fails 3 of them when the bar is reverted**.
    📌 **THE GENERAL FORM, AND IT IS NOT ABOUT FOOTBALL:** a constancy check's minimum-N must be counted over the rows where a non-constant answer is *possible*. Anywhere else, N is padding.

310. 🆕 ⛔ **"BLOCKED" MEASURED ON ONE HOST IS NOT "BLOCKED" FOR A SOURCE — AND I HAD ALREADY WRITTEN THE WRONG ONE DOWN.** `[2026-09-19, self-corrected the same day]`
    The changelog entry directly below says *"the sandbox proxy blocks nflverse, so whether it publishes a partial week is unmeasured — the first NFL run after #91 settles it."* ⛔ **False, and it cost a day of calling the question unanswerable.** `api.github.com` is 403 from this sandbox; **`github.com/nflverse/nflverse-data/releases/download/…` is not**, and one `urlopen` returned the injuries and player-week assets immediately.
    ➡️ **I generalised a refusal from one hostname to an entire source without trying the source.** That is rule 1 in its oldest form — a fact about a query is not a fact about the world — applied to my own tooling rather than to the data. ✅ **The disagreement it left open is now SETTLED BY MEASUREMENT: nflverse does publish a partial week** (72 rows, two clubs, 1 of 16 games), so `verify_nfl`'s "any final game" reading is the one that matches the feed.

311. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A MERGE OF TWO CAPPED LISTS IS NOT A CAPPED LIST — AND TONIGHT'S FIX BROKE A DIFFERENT RULE SIX HOURS LATER.** `[measured live 2026-09-19 19:10Z]`
    PR #90 froze a published football row the moment its own game starts — correct, and the fix rule 302 asked for. ⛔ **The first NCAAF card written after it merged went from 25 picks / 20 top plays at 15:52Z to 34 / 26**, and four of the five frozen sections breached the cap the card DECLARES ON ITSELF: `picks 34>25 · top_plays 26>20 · parlays[2] 11>8 · parlays[3] 15>8 · parlays[4] 16>8 · sgp[2] 10>6 · sgp[3] 8>6`. **Every other card in `picks/` was clean**, which is what pinned it to that commit rather than to a theory.
    🔴 `_merge_list` returned `keep + fresh` — every started row off the published card, plus a complete, freshly-capped new build. **Two lists of ≤N make one list of ≤2N.** The page prints `board_max` so a reader can check the rule without the source, and it was printing 25 above 34 rows.
    ✅ **THE RESOLUTION, AND THE ORDER IS THE WHOLE FIX:** the cap applies to the MERGED list and is filled from the FROZEN rows first. A published row is never dropped to make room — that is the record changing after the fact, which is the offence #90 exists to prevent. If the frozen rows alone fill the cap, no fresh row is added.
    ⚠️ **WHAT IT DOES NOT UNDO, STATED RATHER THAN QUIETLY LEFT:** 13 of 15 three-leg and all 16 four-leg parlays had already started. Those stay. `picks` and `top_plays` self-heal to 25 and 20 on the next build because only 16 and 11 had started.
    📌 **THE GENERAL FORM:** whenever a repair MERGES a preserved set with a freshly computed one, every invariant the computed set satisfied must be re-asserted on the union. Length caps, share ceilings, per-market limits — none of them survive a concatenation.

312. 🆕 ⛔ **A FIX'S GUARD ASKS THE FIX'S QUESTION. THE INVARIANTS THE FIX COULD BREAK ARE A DIFFERENT QUESTION, AND NOBODY ASKS IT.** `[2026-09-19, the same night]`
    `test_card_frozen.py` shipped with #90 and is a good test — it drives the real function, on the real corrupted card, and pins the per-row behaviour in both directions. **It asks only “did the freeze freeze?”** ⛔ It never asks whether the document that comes out still satisfies anything the builder guaranteed going in, and the answer was no within one production run.
    ⚠️ **AND THE LIVE CHECKS THAT DID CATCH IT WERE INCOMPLETE:** `test_board_cap.py` and `test_shadow_fb.py` reddened on `picks` and `top_plays`. **`parlays` and `sgp` had no live assertion at all and were breached worse** — 16 against a cap of 8. Rule 78 again: the sections nobody asserted are the sections nobody knew about.
    ✅ The new §3 asks the cap question of **every name in `FREEZE_SECTIONS`, derived rather than listed**, so a section added later cannot escape it.
    ➡️ **THE HABIT:** when a change rewrites a published artifact, list what that artifact promised before the change and assert each promise on the output. A guard written from the changelog entry will only ever re-ask the bug.

313. 🆕 🔴🔴 **TWO FOLDERS, ONE FILENAME: A DELIVERY THAT SHARES A NAME WITH A SUPERSEDED ONE IS A REVERT WAITING FOR A TIRED HAND — AND IT WAS MINE TO PREVENT.** `[2026-09-20 04:46Z, proved by hash]`
    Sam uploaded `card_fb.py` and `test_card_frozen.py` under the correct commit message. ⛔ **The bytes came from `Downloads\\gizmos-fix-2026-09-19\\1-root-files\\` — the SUPERSEDED folder — not from the new one.** `main`'s `card_fb.py` and the superseded copy are both `6717a8cd72c201f15480e4b4bf79faa0`. **Identical file.**
    🔴 **THE EFFECT WAS A SILENT REVERT OF A MERGED PR.** `freeze_published` went from 3 references to **0 across every `.py` in the repo**, and the **whole-card freeze I had written, been shown was wrong, and deleted (rule 305) went LIVE** — the version that locks the entire dated card at its first kickoff and would stop an NFL Sunday repricing its 4pm and night games every week.
    ⚠️ **NOTHING IN THE REPO COULD HAVE CAUGHT IT AS A REVERT.** The upload was well-formed, the commit message was right, the tests that shipped with it were the matching old ones, so the tree was SELF-CONSISTENT at the wrong version. ⛔ A revert that arrives as a complete matched pair is invisible to every check that asks whether the code and its test agree.
    📌 **THE CAUSE IS THE DELIVERY MECHANISM, NOT THE HAND.** `Downloads` holds **~150 `gizmos-*` folders** from this project, many carrying a file called `card_fb.py`, `nfl.py` or `collect.py`. **File Explorer shows no way to tell them apart.** I created that hazard one delivery at a time and then sent a same-named file into it.
    ✅ **THE PROTOCOL, FROM NOW:** every UPLOAD doc opens with **Step 0 — delete the superseded folder by name**, and every verification step is **CONTENT-based** (“open the file on GitHub and search for `<symbol>`”), never “it looks right”. A filename and a commit message are not evidence about bytes.

314. 🆕 ⛔ **WHEN A FIX IS DELIVERED IN PIECES, DELIVER THE PIECES TOGETHER — A TWO-STEP UPLOAD IS A WINDOW IN WHICH THE PRODUCT IS IN NEITHER STATE.** `[2026-09-20]`
    Two correct deliveries were outstanding (`nfl.py`, `card_fb.py`). One went up from the wrong folder, the other never went up at all, and for ~9 hours `main` was **a version that had never been tested as a whole**: no per-row freeze, no cap fix, no `ahead_out` fix.
    ➡️ **The recovery was a SINGLE folder containing all four files and one instruction to select all of them.** Nothing to choose between is the only instruction that cannot be followed wrongly. ⚠️ This does not contradict “one PR per task” — that rule is about reviewable units in git; this is about what a human drags out of a folder at midnight.

315. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A WALL OF RED RUNS IS A COUNT, NOT A DIAGNOSIS — AND THE USER CANNOT SEE THE DIFFERENCE.** `[2026-09-20]`
    Sam: *"there is so many red x's for failed collect runs its making me mad … i dont even know whats failing."* ⛔ **He was looking at ~24 consecutive ❌ and the page told him nothing.** Reading the annotations of three consecutive failures — different crons, different hours — the error lists were **near-identical**, and only **TWO** `::error::` lines in `collect.yml` redden a run at all.
    ⚠️ **THE REST OF EACH ANNOTATION LIST IS NOISE THAT LOOKS LIKE FAILURE:** a `::warning::` about a push race, a line that says *"soft, not failing the run"* in its own text, and two GitHub platform notices. **Nine "errors" on the run page, two causes underneath.**
    📌 **THE LESSON IS ABOUT PRESENTATION, NOT CODE.** Every one of those lines is individually correct and well-written. Stacked on a runs page with no grouping, they read as *"everything is broken, constantly"* — which is what a person acts on. ➡️ **A repeated failure needs a CAUSE COUNT, not an error count. Group before reporting: N runs, M distinct causes.**

316. 🆕 ⛔ **A STALE ARTIFACT FROM A FINISHED SLATE FAILS FOREVER — NOTHING REBUILDS YESTERDAY.** `[2026-09-20]`
    `picks/fb-ncaaf-2026-09-19.json` was left at 34 picks under its own declared cap of 25 by the merge bug of rule 311. The card builder writes a **dated** file, so once that slate ended nothing ever touched it again — and `test_board_cap` and `test_shadow_fb` run on **every** scheduled `collect`. ➡️ **One stale file, reddening every run in the project, indefinitely.**
    ✅ **THE REPAIR IS NOT A REWRITE, AND THE DISTINCTION IS THE WHOLE POINT.** Measured **at the moment of the bad write (19:10Z)**, only **9 of 34** picks had started. So the fixed trim reconstructs exactly what `freeze_published` would have produced — every section lands at cap (25 / 20 / 8,8,8 / 6,6) and **every started row is kept**.
    🔴 **AND THE RULE IS CHECKED, NOT ARGUED:** the script asserted every removed row against its own kickoff — *"EVERY dropped row had NOT started: True"*. Sam's rule protects rows whose games have BEGUN; the removed rows had not, and were still freely repriceable when the bug added them. ⛔ **A permanent-record rule is about the rows that were real, not about the file's byte count.**

317. 🆕 ⚠️ **RAISING A LIMIT IS A GUESS. FIND OUT WHAT IT IS SPENDING THE LIMIT ON FIRST.** `[2026-09-20]`
    `self-repair` runs #29 and #30 both died on *"Claude execution failed: Reached maximum number of turns (40)"*. ⛔ **The obvious move — raise `--max-turns` — was NOT made.** The agent is most likely burning its turns on the two causes above, which were being fixed the same night; a higher ceiling would have bought more turns of the same churn and a bigger bill.
    ⚠️ **AND THAT FILE CARRIES ITS OWN REASON NOT TO BE TOUCHED:** GitHub attributes a scheduled run to whoever last changed the cron, so a bot actor makes `self-repair.yml`'s own actor check reject it — **the workflow dies with no red run and no alert.** ➡️ **Re-read it once the real causes are clear. A limit that is hit is evidence about the work, not about the limit.**


318. 🆕 🔴🔴 **AN ERROR MESSAGE THAT NAMES THE WRONG MODE SENDS EVERY READER TO THE WRONG FILE.** `[2026-09-21]`
    For a week the runs page said *"mode 'news' failed"*, *"mode 'gamelines' failed"*, *"mode 'props-player' failed"*. ⛔ **None of those modes failed.** Read from the job logs (#1633, #1634, #1638): the named mode succeeded (gamelines logged *"17 games, spent 6"*). Then the converge pass planned `props-board`, and `run_mode` dispatched it to the **MLB-only** `collect_props_board()`, which raised *"no game logs at all — run the hitters and pitchers jobs first"*. The workflow reports any converge failure under **the mode the cron launched**. ✅ Fixed: football calls `collect_props_board_fb()`, and MLB's call is unchanged. `test_props_board_dispatch.py` drives the real `run_mode` per league. ➡️ **Before fixing what an annotation names, read the log line it came from.** Three "different" failures were one line of code.

319. 🆕 ⛔ **A STARTED ROW FROM A SLATE THE LIST HAS LEFT IS RECORD, NOT LIST.** `[2026-09-21]`
    The college card stays dated 09-19 all week while `game_lines` falls forward to the next day with open lines (rule 255). `freeze_published` kept the started Saturday lines **inside** `game_lines`, so a list labelled *"Thursday Sept 24"* held 11 Saturday rows. `test_game_lines_day` went red on every run. The watchdog's card-fb repair failed 3x a pass, and that repair loop is what used up **self-repair's 40 turns** (rule 317's guess, now confirmed: raising the limit would have bought more of the same churn). ⛔ **Dropping the rows would break the permanent-record rule.** ✅ They move verbatim to `game_lines_earlier_slates`, keyed by their own ET day. It is a dict, so no list-day check reads it and the page does not render it. ➡️ **When two rules collide (one-day list vs never delete a started row), the answer is usually a second place to put the row, not a choice between the rules.**

320. 🆕 🔴 **A TEST THAT ASSERTS WHAT THE CALENDAR LOOKS LIKE EXPIRES WITHOUT WARNING.** `[2026-09-21]`
    Three of the five causes of the week's red runs were tests or verifiers asserting a *moment*, not a property:
    - `test_dossier_fb`: "§6 is UNAVAILABLE". It went red the day §6 shipped.
    - `test_verifiers_run`: "something is not yet measurable", "the logs are behind". It went red when week 2 arrived. One of these checks had also been passing **by accident**, because the text it searched for is the name of a check and appears on the PASS line too.
    - `verify_nfl`: one pooled snap fraction. It went red when a half-played week 2 (6.4%) dragged week 1 (99.7%) down to 54%.

    ✅ **Every replacement is harder:**
    - §6 is checked against the disk, and the absent case is driven on a tree with the table removed.
    - The not-yet-measurable behaviour is driven on a fixture that cannot drift.
    - Snap coverage is per complete week. A missing week can no longer hide behind full ones (4×100% + 1×0% pools to exactly 80%).

    ➡️ **If a check's truth depends on today's date, it belongs on a fixture, not on the live tree.** This is rule 227's family.

321. 🆕 🔴🔴 **A PUSH LOOP THAT CAN FAIL SILENTLY LOSES DATA AND MONEY, AND LOOKS LIKE A SCHEDULER PROBLEM.** `[2026-09-22]`
    The "dark crons" DID fire. `collect.yml` pushed with `git pull --rebase --autostash && git push` three times. A conflicted rebase was left half-done, and the loop fell through without an error, so the whole converge pass never reached `main`. **The same loss explains the ~50% of Odds spend that was never recorded** (09-19: 1,632 of 2,750 credits): the snapshot carrying `credits_used` was the thing lost, and the next run re-bought the same pulls. ✅ Fixed with `push_retry.sh`: 5 attempts; a stuck rebase is aborted and this run's changed paths are replayed onto the fresh tip; if the push still fails, the run goes **red**. `test_push_retry.py` races two clones on real files. ✅ Guard: `watchdog.check_credit_reconciliation` compares the Odds balance drop against recorded spend (warn 30, broken 200 credits in 24h; it reproduces 09-19's 1,632 on history). ➡️ **"The job didn't run" and "the job's output never landed" look the same from the page. Check the commit, not the schedule.**

322. 🆕 ⛔ **A CHECK THAT SKIPS ITSELF IN CI HAS NEVER RUN.** `[2026-09-22]`
    `test_repo_watch.py`'s live size check skipped on GitHub's shallow clone, so it passed everywhere and ran nowhere. ✅ `budget.yml` now repacks a full clone and runs it with `REPO_WATCH_LIVE_REQUIRED=1`, where a skip is a failure. ➡️ **Every skip path needs a place where skipping is forbidden.**

323. 🆕 🔴 **WHEN SAM REVERSES A POLICY, THE GUARD FOR THE OLD POLICY BECOMES A GUARD FOR THE WRONG QUESTION.** `[2026-09-22]`
    Sam lifted the MLB freeze (*"unfreeze all mlb… kept up to date from now on"*). `test_watchdog` asserted "MLB IS CLOSED FOR WORK" is in CLAUDE.md, and the self-repair prompt still said "MLB IS FROZEN". ✅ The check now asks the real question and is harder: exactly one live MLB policy in CLAUDE.md, cited to Sam with a date, and the self-repair prompt must say the SAME thing (it fails if they disagree, which the old check never noticed). ✅ "Kept up to date" is automatic: `mlb_tables.py` rebuilds the pitcher and opponent tables after every `pitchers` pull, with freshness rows. ⛔ **The model coefficients are still fitted, not tuned; unfrozen means the code may change.**

324. 🆕 🔴 **DRAGGING A FOLDER ONTO GITHUB PUTS ITS NAME IN FRONT OF EVERY FILE — AND THE RUN STAYS GREEN.** `[2026-09-22]`
    The 09-22 fix was uploaded twice into `upload-to-repo-root/` and `.github/workflows/upload-to-github-workflows/`. The real files never changed, so every run was green on the OLD code, and Sam's content search found nothing. ✅ Delivery now uses GitHub's **"choose your files"** button, which can only pick files; dragging is kept only where a folder path is wanted (`docs/`). ✅ Verification is by me, byte for byte against `main`, never by a green run. ➡️ **A green run after an upload proves the old code still works, not that the new code arrived.**

325. 🆕 ✅ **CLAUDE CODE SHIPS BY PULL REQUEST, AND THE SUITE RUNS BEFORE MERGE.** `[Sam, 2026-09-22]`
    Sam: *"when we used claude code for this it did end up causing alot of failed recurring runs, i would like to avoid that"*. ✅ `pr-tests.yml` (no cron) runs the collector's own test loop on every PR; `test_pr_tests.py` fails if the two loops differ. Rule in `CLAUDE.md`: branch → PR → green → Sam merges; cron-bearing workflow files are still hand-uploaded by Sam; one agent at a time. Handoff: `docs/HANDOFF-2026-09-22.md` in the repo.

(2) I told it to "pick a coverage floor with the p10 in hand" — **the experiment says no floor exists.**
  🔴 **THE EXPERIMENT, AND IT CHANGES THE ACCEPTANCE RULE:** 29 fixture games at coverage ≥ 0.95 as ground truth, degraded by dropping **WHOLE DRIVES** (the realistic missingness shape, not random plays). **12-game season p95 error: 68s at 0.95 coverage, 81s at 0.90, 193s at 0.60 — over the 60s bar at EVERY level.** 🟢 **BUT THE ERROR IS ZERO-MEAN** — signed means **−15.6s to +18.4s against sd 120–352s** — so it is **NOISE, NOT BIAS**, and averages out over games. ⛔ **That is the opposite of the raw-seconds defect, which IS biased and which no number of games removes. Share fixes the bias; it does not remove the noise.**
  📌 **THE RULE THIS EARNED: A BAR THAT VALIDATES A *READ* CANNOT VALIDATE A *DERIVATION*.** NFL's 60s bar is right because `drive_time_of_possession` is **recorded** — coverage 100% by construction, **269 of 269 games sum to exactly 3600**. CFB **derives** its number and carries derivation noise a recorded field does not. ➡️ **Two bars, each stating what it checks.** ⛔ **Widening NFL's bar so one number covers both is weakening a check to make it pass**, and it was the tempting move.
